Rhubarb

Interrogationes

Questions the code answers: how do I, can I, why, what is

Each unit declares the questions it answers in lib/S.interrogationes.stml (canon interrogationes.canon). Every answer is anchored to a symbol, a path or a command, and the gate interrogationes turns red when an anchor dies. From the terminal: bin/fabrica quid -interrogationes -quaere "how do I ...", or -symbolum xar_addere for the questions that name a symbol.

actio

include/actio.h · lib/actio.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I make a widget do something when it is clicked or receives a key?
    Name the action in the tree with componens_ponere_actio(c, name) and register a handler for the same name with actio_registrare(reg, name, fn, ctx). The dispensator walks the ascent from the target upward; at each componens that names an action with a handler registered in its spatium it calls the ActioFn, and VERUM means consumed: the ascent stops. FALSUM lets the ancestors try. The nodus argument is the componens whose action matched, which may be an ancestor of the event's target.
    symbolum componens_ponere_actio actio_registrare ActioFn · via lib/dispensator.c
  2. possumnecan I register the same action name twice or replace a handler?
    No. actio_registrare returns FALSUM for a name already registered in the host spatium, for an empty name (interning returns NIHIL for an empty string) and for a NIHIL fn. There is no single unregister: actio_registrum_vacare empties the whole registry (keeping its memory) so it can be rebuilt.
    symbolum actio_registrare actio_registrum_vacare
  3. quidwho owns the action name strings, and must a lookup use an interned chorda?
    actio_registrare interns the C string into the registry's internamentum, so a string literal or a temporary buffer is fine. Lookups compare by content (chorda_aequalis), so actio_invenire accepts any chorda, interned or not. Merging copies the source's name chordas by reference, so the source's internamentum must outlive the target registry (the header asks for the same internamentum).
    symbolum actio_registrare chorda_internare_ex_literis actio_invenire actio_registrum_miscere
  4. possumnecan a widget inside a mounted pane trigger a handler registered in the host?
    Not one registered in a different spatium. The dispensator looks the name up with actio_invenire_in_spatio in the componens' effective spatium (componens_spatium: the nearest ancestor that sets one), and the lookup is strict. If nothing matches there it skips that componens and keeps ascending, so an ancestor in another spatium can still handle its own action. Put a pane's handlers into the host registry with actio_registrum_miscere_in_spatio; entries that already carry a spatium keep it. The spatium chorda is stored as given and must outlive the registry.
    symbolum actio_invenire_in_spatio actio_registrum_miscere_in_spatio componens_spatium
  5. quomodohow do I find action names in my tree that have no handler, or handlers nobody uses?
    actio_non_registratae returns the names the tree references with no handler in the componens' spatium (an error for lint L10), and actio_non_relatae the registered names no componens references (a warning). Both return a Xar of chorda, each name once per spatium, allocated from the piscina you pass. The chordas carry the name only, not its spatium. A NIHIL registry or piscina gives NIHIL; a NIHIL tree gives an empty Xar (and every handler counts as unreferenced).
    symbolum actio_non_registratae actio_non_relatae
  6. curwhy are insula writes made inside an action handler attributed to the action's name?
    The dispensator sets the insula writer to the componens' action name with insula_scriptorem_ponere just before calling the handler and clears it right after, so every write the handler makes is owned by that action. Handlers do no I/O (lint L5): they change state through the InsulaRepositorium and the motus they are handed, and effects travel as data.
    symbolum insula_scriptorem_ponere ActioFn · via lib/dispensator.c

aedilis

include/aedilis.h · lib/aedilis.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I build and run a program or test without writing a build script?
    Run bin/aedilis on the file that has main, from the repository root: it derives the closure, writes build/aedilis/BASIS/manifestum.stml and a bash script, and with --currere runs the script, which leaves the binary at build/aedilis/BASIS/BASIS. --varians picks a platform variant; --enumerare (print the objects) and --ordo (print the headers in dependency order) print and exit without writing the manifest or the script. If derivation fails it refuses loudly (AEDILIS RECUSAT on stderr, exit 1, cause named) and writes nothing; with --currere a failing script also gives exit 1.
    via tools/aedilis.c · imperium ./bin/aedilis probationes/probatio_stml.c --currere
  2. quomodohow do I derive a closure from C code instead of the command line?
    Read aedilis.stml with aedilis_configurationem_legere, make the real extractor with aedilis_silva_creare, and call aedilis_derivare with aedilis_silva_extrahere and that extractor as its datum (radix_thesauri NIHIL runs without the cross-run cache; with a cache, the key comes from aedilis_thesauri_praefixum). The engine itself never touches silva: the extractor is injected, so tests pass a fixture extractor instead. aedilis_configurationem_legere and aedilis_derivare return NIHIL and fill *causa_out on refusal; there is never a partial result. aedilis_silva_creare has no causa_out: it just returns NIHIL.
    symbolum aedilis_configurationem_legere aedilis_derivare aedilis_silva_creare aedilis_silva_extrahere
  3. quidwhat object file does aedilis pick for a header I include?
    By convention: include/x.h maps to lib/x, any other header to its own directory. For each preferred variant in order (only the given one with --varians, otherwise the praelatio of aedilis.stml, macos then posix) it tries x_VARIANT.m, then x_VARIANT.c; then plain x.c. A plain x.m is never tried: declare it with a corpus annotation. A header with none of these simply has no object. An irregularia rule in aedilis.stml replaces the convention for that header.
    symbolum aedilis_derivare
  4. quidwhat annotations does aedilis read from source comments?
    Elements of the form <aedilis VERB="ARG"/> in comments of any file the derivation walks (so never in an unwalked object): obiectum (link this source, never walked), corpus (in a header: its implementation, walked, must exist), vexillum (a LINK flag), nexus (only the value purus), facultas (fenestra, rete or repositorium; scope file only) and instrumentum (a bin/ binary the scope runs; scope file only, under bin/, no duplicates, no ..). Any other verb or value, or obiectum/corpus/vexillum with an empty argument, refuses the whole derivation.
    symbolum aedilis_derivare · via include/modulus.h
  5. curwhy are the includes of some objects never walked?
    Two cases are compiled and linked but not walked. Objects declared by an obiectum annotation: their dependencies belong to their generator (parsing 9 MB of generated capsules took 17 s for nothing). And sources over 1 MB, which are treated as data written as code (a 6.1 MB Bible source made silva run out of memory). A declared object missing from disk (absens, e.g. not generated yet) is not walked either. Use a corpus annotation when you DO want the implementation walked; the 1 MB limit still applies to it.
    symbolum aedilis_derivare
  6. quidwhat is the difference between systemata and inresolutae in a manifest?
    An include that resolves nowhere under the root (as written, beside the including file, or under the -I directories of aedilis.stml) goes into systemata. If it was written in quoted form it is also listed in inresolutae: probably one of our headers that the include roots miss, so the manifest is incomplete. Angle-bracket includes are only systemata. The inresolutae section is written only when it is not empty.
    symbolum aedilis_derivare aedilis_manifestum_scribere
  7. quomodohow do I promise that a header pulls in no framework (pure link)?
    Put <aedilis nexus="purus"/> in the header's comment. aedilis_nexum_purum_probare derives the header as if it were a scope and fails, naming the include chain and the flags, if its own implementation or any object in its closure has a nexus rule (e.g. fenestra_macos -> Cocoa). A header without the promise passes. bin/aedilis --nexus-purus checks every header under the include roots.
    symbolum aedilis_nexum_purum_probare · via include/modulus.h
  8. quomodohow do I get a bash build script from a derivation?
    aedilis_scriptum_scribere returns the script text. With solitarius FALSUM objects are shared in build/aedilis/obiecta and rebuilt when missing, older than their source, or older than ANY header of the closure; with VERUM they go to a private solitarius directory and are always rebuilt (vendor objects excepted: in both modes they are rebuilt only when missing or older than their source). Objects are named by flattened path (lib/tcp_posix.c becomes lib__tcp_posix.o, with the flags of a compilatio rule appended, so lib__sigillum__O2.o). The flattening is not one-to-one despite the comment in lib/aedilis.c: lib/a__b.c and lib/a/b.c would both become lib__a__b.o. The script must run from the repository root. aedilis_scriptum_scribere_ad sends outputs and objects to other directories.
    symbolum aedilis_scriptum_scribere aedilis_scriptum_scribere_ad
  9. possumnecan a vendored C file get our warning flags?
    No: that is deliberate. An include under vendor/ makes X.c beside X.h (or the file itself) a vendor object, compiled with ONLY the flags of its vendor rule in aedilis.stml: neither the shared VEXILLA nor the -I directories. It is rebuilt only when its own source is newer. A vendor header without a matching .c is treated as header-only.
    symbolum aedilis_scriptum_scribere_ad aedilis_configurationem_legere
  10. quomodohow do I list headers so that every header comes after the ones it includes?
    aedilis_capita_ordinare returns a Xar of AedilisCaput pointers into the derivation (stable, because xar never moves elements). Within one round the discovery order is kept, so the result is deterministic. An include cycle returns NIHIL and causa_out names every header not yet emitted (the cycle plus anything that depends on it). Edges exist only for headers (a header's inclusa list); implementation files can be in any order.
    symbolum aedilis_capita_ordinare
  11. quidwhat is the extractor cache key from aedilis_thesauri_praefixum?
    The prefix under which extraction records are cached. When ingressus is a valid digest (64 lowercase hex, built into the binary) the key is the seal of extractor: plus that digest plus the bytes of aedilis.stml, so every binary built from the same extractor sources shares the cache. Otherwise (NIHIL, bootstrap, malformed) it seals the binary file plus the configuration. *fons_out says which was used; FALSUM if a file needed could not be read.
    symbolum aedilis_thesauri_praefixum

aemulator

include/aemulator.h · lib/aemulator.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I feed program output into the terminal emulator and read the screen back?
    Fill an AemulatorConfiguratio with aemulator_configuratio_initiare (always start from it, so fields added later get defaults), change what you need, and call aemulator_creare. Pass the program's bytes to aemulator_scribere, then read cells with aemulator_cellula(a, x, y, &cellula) or the whole screen as plain text with aemulator_textum_effundere. aemulator_creare returns NIHIL if a side is outside 1..IV * MXXIV cells or the piscina runs out.
    symbolum aemulator_configuratio_initiare aemulator_creare aemulator_scribere aemulator_cellula aemulator_textum_effundere
  2. quomodohow do I send the emulator's replies (DA, DSR, cursor position) back to the program?
    Set effectus.responsum (with effectus.datum) in the configuration. It is called synchronously from inside aemulator_scribere with the reply bytes, and you must not call back into the emulator from it. If the field is NIHIL, replies are simply dropped. BEL and OSC 0/2 titles arrive the same way through campana and titulus (a CSI 23 t title-stack pop re-announces the restored title through titulus too); the title chorda is valid only during that call. aemulator_hospes takes over effectus.responsum and queues the replies to the child's Pseudoterminale; campana and titulus still go to you.
    symbolum AemulatorEffectus aemulator_scribere aemulator_hospes_creare
  3. possumnecan I pass bytes in arbitrary chunks, splitting escape sequences or UTF-8 characters?
    Yes. Parser state is kept between calls: a sequence may be split anywhere, and up to III trailing bytes of a split UTF-8 rune are carried over to the next call. If a control or escape sequence arrives while a rune is still incomplete, the rune becomes U+FFFD and the sequence is then handled normally. One known flaw: the carried rune is completed with the next call's first bytes without checking that they are continuation bytes, so "\xE6" then "AB" shows a single U+FFFD and loses A and B (sent in one call, the same bytes show U+FFFD A B).
    symbolum aemulator_scribere
  4. quidwhat does a cell's latitudo mean, and what does an empty graphema mean?
    AEMULATOR_ANGUSTA is one cell. AEMULATOR_LATA is a wide character, followed by an AEMULATOR_CAUDA cell that must not be painted. AEMULATOR_CAPUT is the empty last-column cell left when a wide character wrapped to the next line. An empty graphema means no text: never written OR erased (ED/EL/ECH), since the two are not distinguished. The graphema points into the emulator's own cell storage: it is not NUL-terminated and is valid only until the next aemulator_scribere or aemulator_amplitudo.
    symbolum aemulator_cellula AemulatorCellula AemulatorLatitudo
  5. possumnecan a cell hold a character with combining marks or another multi-codepoint grapheme?
    Not yet: a cell holds exactly one rune (up to IV UTF-8 bytes). Zero-width runes such as combining accents are dropped when printed, so e followed by U+0301 shows as a plain e. Multi-codepoint graphemes are planned for a version II of the core.
    symbolum aemulator_scribere aemulator_cellula
  6. quomodohow do I let the user scroll back through history?
    aemulator_visum_movere(a, delta): delta greater than 0 moves up into history, less than 0 moves down, clamped to [0, aemulator_historia(a)] (the sum is formed in s32 before clamping, so a huge positive delta overflows: to jump to the top pass aemulator_historia(a) - aemulator_visus(a), to the bottom pass the negated aemulator_visus(a)). aemulator_visus returns the current offset (0 = live screen). Paint with aemulator_visus_cellula. aemulator_cellula, the cursor and aemulator_textum_effundere always read the LIVE screen. While scrolled back (offset above 0), new output does not move what you see: each line pushed into history raises the offset by one; at 0 the view follows the live screen. Entering the alternate screen resets the offset to 0. Only the primary screen has history. A line enters it when it scrolls off the TOP row of a scroll region whose top is row 0 (the full screen or a DECSTBM region from the top, as in Ghostty): LF or IND at the region's bottom, SU, or a height shrink. A region that starts lower, IL/DL, ED 2 and the alternate screen never add any. The header comment's 'whole screen only' wording is stale; the worklog (C2) has the rule.
    symbolum aemulator_visum_movere aemulator_visus aemulator_historia aemulator_visus_cellula aemulator_visum_effundere
  7. quomodohow do I bound how much memory the emulator uses?
    Set historia_octeti: history lives in pages of LXIV * MXXIV bytes, the limit is rounded up to whole pages (at least one), the default is X MB, and 0 means no history. Once the limit is reached the oldest page is recycled and nothing more is allocated. The screen keeps its capacity: resizing within it allocates nothing, and growing past it makes the capacity half again larger, or exactly the new size if that is bigger, capped at IV * MXXIV (never smaller); the old line storage is not given back, since everything comes from the caller's piscina.
    symbolum aemulator_configuratio_initiare aemulator_amplitudo aemulator_scribere
  8. quomodohow do I know which keyboard and mouse encodings the program asked for?
    aemulator_modi returns a snapshot by value (application cursor keys and keypad, mouse mode and format, bracketed paste, focus reports, LNM, kitty keyboard flags, wheel-as-arrows, reverse screen). Read it again after every aemulator_scribere. aemulator_modus(a, numerus, privatus) gives the raw bit of one mode as DECRQM reports it; modes outside the table read FALSUM.
    symbolum aemulator_modi AemulatorModi aemulator_modus
  9. quomodohow do I find out whether the emulator ignored sequences the program sent?
    aemulator_ignota counts every sequence consumed but not understood since creation. Unknown input is never fatal and is never printed as text. An OSC whose body (number, ';' and text together) exceeds the lexer's SERIES_CHORDA_MAXIMA (II * MXXIV bytes) is dropped whole and counted, not truncated. CSI 21 t and DECRQCRA with lectio_schirmi FALSUM are counted here too. The tessera loopback harness requires the count to stay 0 (worklog A3), since tessera emits nothing the emulator should not understand.
    symbolum aemulator_ignota aemulator_scribere
  10. quomodohow do I resize the emulator, and does text reflow?
    aemulator_amplitudo(a, latitudo, altitudo). There is no reflow: wider lines are padded and narrower ones cut. Shrinking first drops blank rows below the cursor, and the rest of the excess scrolls into history. Growing pulls lines back from history only when the cursor is on the bottom row. Rows that scroll off the alternate screen are lost (only the primary has history). The scroll region and tab stops are reset. On FALSUM (bad size, or the piscina ran out) the state is unchanged.
    symbolum aemulator_amplitudo
  11. curwhy is screen readback (DECRQCRA) off by default, and why is the title report CSI 21 t never answered?
    Two separate decisions. DECRQCRA lets a program checksum screen cells; lectio_schirmi is FALSUM by default (decision IX: Ghostty does not have it, iTerm2 ships it closed) and only the esctest driver (tools/aemulator_esctest.c) and the unit tests turn it on; closed, the sequence is unknown and gets no reply. CSI 21 t would echo the window title back as if typed, and a title echoed as input is an injection path, so it is refused on purpose and counted as unknown.
    symbolum aemulator_configuratio_initiare aemulator_creare · via lib/aemulator.worklog.md include/aemulator.h
  12. curwhy does the emulator core do no I/O and keep no clock?
    The core is PURE (decision X): bytes in, screen state out, replies and events through the effect callbacks; no files, no time, no threads, no mutable statics, all memory from the caller's piscina, so it can be built for wasm. The read loop and reply queue live in aemulator_hospes (aemulator_hospes_pulsare), which touches the child only through a Pseudoterminale table, so it is pure too; the real pty is behind that table.
    symbolum aemulator_scribere aemulator_hospes_pulsare · via lib/aemulator_hospes.c
  13. quomodohow do I read the colors the program set with OSC 4, 10, 11 or 12?
    aemulator_color(a, index) returns 0xRRGGBB: 0-CCLV is the palette, AEMULATOR_COLOR_LITTERAE, AEMULATOR_COLOR_FUNDI and AEMULATOR_COLOR_CURSORIS the foreground, background and cursor. A cursor without its own color reports the current foreground. An index above AEMULATOR_COLOR_CURSORIS returns 0, which is indistinguishable from black. Resets (OSC 104/110/111/112) go back to the configured values (OSC 110/111/112 with a parameter do nothing, as in Ghostty).
    symbolum aemulator_color AemulatorConfiguratio
  14. possumnecan I read the current window title back from the emulator?
    No: there is no getter. The title reaches you only through effectus.titulus, called for OSC 0 and OSC 2 (even an empty title) and again when CSI 23 t pops the title stack; keep your own copy, since the chorda is valid only during the call. The callback gets the whole OSC text, while the core keeps only the first CCLVI bytes for its own title stack (X deep). A program cannot read it back either: CSI 21 t is refused.
    symbolum AemulatorEffectus aemulator_creare

anulus

include/anulus.h · lib/anulus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I write an algebra routine once so it works over Z, Q, Laurent polynomials and Z/n?
    Take a constans Anulus* and treat elements as opaque blocks of anulus->mensura bytes. Every operation is a function pointer that takes the descriptor first: anulus->adde(anulus, &a, &b, piscina, &c). The ready rings are ANULUS_INTEGRORUM (elements Magnus), ANULUS_RATIONALIUM (Fractio), ANULUS_POLYNOMIORUM (Polynomium, text letter 't') and anulus_residuorum(n, piscina) for Z/n (elements i32). matrix is written this way.
    symbolum Anulus ANULUS_INTEGRORUM ANULUS_RATIONALIUM ANULUS_POLYNOMIORUM anulus_residuorum
  2. possumnecan I call divisor_communis, divide_cum_residuo, compara_normam or signum on any ring?
    No: they are optional and NIHIL where the ring lacks them, so test the pointer first. The three Euclidean hooks exist only for Z (NIHIL for Q, Z[t,t^-1] and Z/n). signum exists for Z and Q (and ordered extensio fields), NIHIL for Z[t,t^-1], Z/n and cyclotomia. Algorithms that need a missing hook return FALSUM.
    symbolum Anulus ANULUS_INTEGRORUM ANULUS_RATIONALIUM
  3. quomodohow do I make the ring Z/n, and how are its rings compared?
    anulus_residuorum(n, piscina) copies a descriptor into the piscina with the modulus in contextus and a title like "Z/7"; it returns NIHIL if n < 2. corpus and integrum are VERUM only when n is prime. Matrices compare rings by POINTER, so two calls with n = 7 give two different rings: create one descriptor per modulus and share it.
    symbolum anulus_residuorum
  4. possumnecan I put an unreduced value like 9 into a Z/7 element directly?
    Do not: Z/n elements are i32 residues 0 <= x < n and aequalis compares the raw numbers, so a stored 9 is not equal to 2. Build elements with the ring's own operations, or with ex_chorda, which accepts any decimal integer (negative too) and reduces it modulo n.
    symbolum anulus_residuorum Anulus
  5. curwhy does Anulus have an integrum flag separate from corpus?
    Bareiss elimination (determinant, rank, kernel) is only sound over an integral domain. Over composite Z/n a zero-divisor pivot gave WRONG answers instead of refusing: [0,3,3; 4,4,5; 5,5,5] over Z/6 came out det 0, truly 3. So matrix_determinans, matrix_gradus and matrix_nucleus refuse when integrum is FALSUM. Z is integrum but not corpus; Z/n is both exactly when n is prime.
    symbolum Anulus anulus_residuorum · via lib/anulus.worklog.md
  6. quomodohow do I keep a ring element after resetting the scratch piscina it was computed in?
    Copy it with anulus->transcribe(anulus, &a, piscina_durabilis, &exitus) before piscina_reficere. transcribe makes a DEEP copy (Magnus limbs, Fractio parts and polynomial arrays are copied), so the value survives losing the source piscina. A plain struct copy would still point into the scratch memory.
    symbolum Anulus piscina_notare piscina_reficere
  7. possumnecan the output of an anulus operation be the same element as an input?
    Yes: the wrappers read the inputs by value before writing the output, so adde(anulus, &x, &y, piscina, &x) is safe and matrix relies on it. When an operation returns FALSUM (division not exact or by zero, polynomial exponent out of range, bad text) the output is left untouched.
    symbolum Anulus · via lib/anulus.worklog.md
  8. quidwhat does the parvum hook of an Anulus mean?
    parvum(a) is VERUM when the element lives entirely inside its struct with no external memory: Z when it fits in s64, Q when numerator and denominator both fit, Z[t,t^-1] only for the zero polynomial, Z/n always. Algorithms use it to take a scratch-free fast path.
    symbolum Anulus

canon

include/canon.h · lib/canon.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I check an STML document against its schema from C?
    Load the schema with canon_legere(fons, piscina, intern, &causa), parse the document with stml_legere (canon_examen shares one internamentum for both; plain canon_iudicare compares names by content, but the _expansum and _distributum variants need the tree's own), and call canon_iudicare(canon, r.radix, piscina). It returns a Xar of CanonVitium, and an empty Xar means the document is sound. Print each with canon_nuntius(v->genus) plus v->elementum and v->detail (chorda pointers that may be NIHIL); the line is v->nodus->linea when v->nodus is not NIHIL. A NIHIL canon or piscina gives NIHIL, not an empty Xar.
    symbolum canon_legere stml_legere canon_iudicare canon_nuntius CanonVitium
  2. quomodohow do I check a .stml or .canon file against its canon from the shell?
    Run bin/canon_examen FILE... (the canon is looked up in canones.registrum) or bin/canon_examen -canon X.canon FILE; add -machina for TSV output. Run it from the repository root: it reads canones.registrum from the current directory, and -canon applies to the files named after it. Exit 0 = sound, 1 = vitia (a file that does not parse also counts), 2 = no file at all was judged. A file no registry line covers is only reported on stderr and skipped, so with other files judged the exit can still be 0. A .canon file is itself judged against canon.canon (registry keys <canon> and .canon), which is how typos inside a canon are caught.
    via tools/canon_examen.c canones.registrum · imperium bin/canon_examen -machina canon.canon
  3. quomodohow do I find which canon applies to a file?
    Read canones.registrum yourself and pass its text as catalogus: the library does no I/O. The registry has two kinds of key, '<radix>' (the document's root element) and '.ext' (the extension, taken from the last '.' anywhere in the path). Ask canon_registrum_quaerere_radice first and fall back to canon_registrum_quaerere only when it returns an empty chorda: the root wins because .stml carries several dialects. The library does not combine the two for you (canon_examen does). The answer is a slice of the catalogue text (not a piscina copy, whatever the header says), not NUL-terminated and alive only as long as the catalogue, so use chorda_ut_cstr before opening it.
    symbolum canon_registrum_quaerere_radice canon_registrum_quaerere · via canones.registrum tools/canon_examen.c
  4. quidwhat does a canon check, and what does it deliberately not check?
    It checks a closed vocabulary (unknown elements and attributes are vitia), child counts per element (minimum, maximum, order ignored), attribute value genera, required attributes, where text is allowed, uniqueness (unicitas) and key references (citatio). It does not check co-occurrence rules ("a or b", "a <= b"), inheritance, or references into other files except through a claves-externae block. There is no expression language on purpose: that road turns a schema into a programming language, as XSD did. Those rules belong to the caller.
    symbolum canon_iudicare CanonVitiumGenus · via lib/canon.worklog.md
  5. quomodohow do I find out why canon_legere returned NIHIL?
    Pass a chorda* causa: on failure it holds the reason, for example the text does not parse ("canon parsari nequit"), the root is not <canon>, a citatio ad= has no '/', a unicitas or citatio names an attribute no element declares ("porta quae nihil custodit"), an ordinarius= default that does not fit its own genus or fines, fines on a non-numerus attribute, inverted or malformed fines, or a broken fragment or transclusion. Be careful the other way too: the reader tolerates unknown elements and attributes in a canon, so a misspelt keyword silently weakens it. Judge every .canon with canon.canon.
    symbolum canon_legere canon_ex_nodo
  6. quomodohow do I validate a document that carries its own embedded canon?
    An embedded canon counts only as the FIRST element child of the root, named canon. canon_iudicare with the registry canon just treats it as invisible; it does not apply it. To honour it do what canon_examen does: canon_infixum_invenire(root), judge that node against canon.canon, build it with canon_ex_nodo, and judge the document a second time with it. Both judgments must pass (the registry canon is a floor the document cannot remove; the embedded one can only add rules). Not every tool does this: tools/silex.c judges with the embedded canon INSTEAD of the registry one and skips the canon.canon check.
    symbolum canon_infixum_invenire canon_ex_nodo canon_iudicare · via tools/canon_examen.c tools/silex.c
  7. quomodohow do I validate a document that uses templates, fragments or distribution wrappers?
    canon_iudicare judges the file as written: fragments and transclusions are resolved transparently, but template calls are quoted material it does not see into. canon_iudicare_expansum runs stml_expandere first and judges what the calls produce; canon_iudicare_distributum also runs stml_distribuere, so it judges what a consumer finally sees. Both must get the tree's own internamentum (identities are compared by interned pointer, and a foreign one makes every call unknown). A failed expansion gives one CANON_EXPANSIO_FRACTA with nodus NIHIL, numerus = StmlExpansioVitium and limes = line.
    symbolum canon_iudicare canon_iudicare_expansum canon_iudicare_distributum stml_expandere
  8. quidwhat attribute value genera can a canon declare?
    textus (anything), nomen ([a-zA-Z0-9_*]), compositum (nomen plus '-'), titulus (compositum with one optional leading dot), numerus (optional sign and digits; fractio="verum" allows a decimal point with digits on both sides; minimum=/maximum= are inclusive bounds compared digit by digit after stripping leading and trailing zeros, never converted to floats), veritas (verum or falsum), dies, electio (one of the <optio> children), identitas (&name;) and referentia (&name; or .genus). The reader takes an unknown genus word as textus without complaint; only judging the .canon against canon.canon (where genus= is an electio) catches the typo.
    symbolum CanonGenusValoris · via canon.canon
  9. possumnecan a dies attribute reject impossible dates like 2026-02-30?
    No. dies accepts yyyy, yyyy-mm and yyyy-mm-dd and checks only the month (1-12) and the day (1-31), so 2026-02-30 passes. A leading '-' writes a year before Christ in the historical sense ("-312" is 312 BC, not the astronomical year -312), with I to IV digits for a bare year. Year zero is not rejected either: "0000" and "-0" pass although the source comment says that reading has no year zero.
    symbolum CanonGenusValoris canon_iudicare
  10. possumnecan a unicitas rule use super="*" or the parent/child form like citatio does?
    No, and nothing warns you. citatio super= understands '*' and 'parens/titulus', but unicitas compares each super= entry with element titles literally. The loader accepts both forms, because its check that the attribute exists understands them, so such a rule loads cleanly and then never matches a node: duplicates pass silently. Write plain element titles in unicitas super=.
    symbolum canon_iudicare canon_legere
  11. quomodohow do I get typed C structs and a reader generated from a canon?
    Add a line to cocta.registrum (canon, prefix, header, source) and run tools/canon_coquere.sh; -probare only checks that the committed readers are fresh. bin/canon_coquere turns each element into a struct (chorda* for textual genera, s64 for numerus, b32 for veritas, each with a presence flag, an enum for electio, a pointer for a child of maximum 1, Xar* for unbounded children) plus a _legere_nodum reader. The generated reader applies ordinarius= defaults; canon_iudicare never does, since the judge only states the default.
    via tools/canon_coquere.c cocta.registrum · imperium tools/canon_coquere.sh -probare

capsula

include/capsula.h · lib/capsula.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I embed asset files in a binary and read them at runtime?
    List the files in a TOML file as top-level keys X_files = [...] (paths or * glob patterns, relative to the TOML's directory) and X_compress = true|false (default false). Not [X] tables with files/compress, whatever the generator's top comment and usage text say: those generate nothing. bin/capsula_generare writes capsula_X.c/.h next to the TOML, defining constans CapsulaEmbed capsula_X; tools/capsula_regenerare.sh runs it. At runtime capsula_aperire(&capsula_X, piscina) then capsula_legere(cap, "path/relative/to/the/toml", piscina), checking .status == CAPSULA_OK.
    symbolum capsula_aperire capsula_legere CapsulaEmbed · via tools/capsula_generare.c probationes/test_capsula.toml tools/capsula_regenerare.sh
  2. quidwhat do I get back from capsula_legere, and who owns the bytes?
    A CapsulaFructus: status plus datum, a chorda (NOT NUL-terminated, use chorda_ut_cstr if you need a C string). Every call allocates a fresh copy (or a fresh decompression) in the piscina you pass, even for uncompressed entries, because the embedded blob is const. Reading the same file in a loop therefore grows that piscina; read once and keep the chorda.
    symbolum capsula_legere capsula_legere_chorda CapsulaFructus
  3. quomodohow do I serve assets from a directory during development without rebuilding?
    capsula_aperire_e_disco(radix, piscina) returns a Capsula with the same handle API, reading the directory instead of the blob (atrium uses it for -radix). capsula_legere, _chorda and capsula_habet go to disk on every call, so an edited file is seen at once. It returns NIHIL if radix is not a directory. Paths that normalize outside radix ("../x") are refused as CAPSULA_NON_INVENTUM. Nothing is compressed. An existing empty file is CAPSULA_OK with mensura 0. Note that in this mode capsula_habet builds the full path in the piscina given to capsula_aperire_e_disco on every call (it takes no piscina of its own), so calling it per request slowly grows that piscina. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): if a file appears under radix while capsula_aperire_e_disco is opening it (between its counting walk and its filling walk), the fill writes one CapsulaIndexum past the end of the index allocation, a heap overflow in the piscina.
    symbolum capsula_aperire_e_disco capsula_legere capsula_habet · via lib/atrium.c
  4. possumnecan I list files that were added after capsula_aperire_e_disco opened the directory?
    No. capsula_numerus, capsula_indexum, capsula_iter and capsula_invenire use the index taken when the directory was opened. A file added later can be read with capsula_legere but is not listed until you open it again. The index also skips hidden entries (dot-files), though capsula_legere still reads them by path.
    symbolum capsula_aperire_e_disco capsula_numerus capsula_indexum capsula_iter capsula_invenire capsula_legere
  5. possumnecan I find out why capsula_aperire failed?
    No: capsula_aperire returns only NIHIL, whether embed or piscina is NIHIL, the blob is under XII bytes, or the magic or version is wrong. CAPSULA_FRACTA_MAGICA and CAPSULA_FRACTA_VERSIO are never produced anywhere, and CAPSULA_FRACTA_DATUM only by the separate capsula_caudae library; in capsula.c only capsula_legere and capsula_legere_chorda return a status (CAPSULA_NON_INVENTUM, CAPSULA_FRACTA_INFLATIO, CAPSULA_FRACTA_ALLOCATIO). capsula_status_nuntium turns a status into text. The TOC is not bounds-checked against the blob, so only open blobs that capsula_generare produced.
    symbolum capsula_aperire capsula_status_nuntium CapsulaStatus
  6. quomodohow do I walk every file in a capsula?
    Loop i from 0 to capsula_numerus(cap) - 1, take capsula_indexum(cap, i) and read it with capsula_legere_chorda(cap, entry->via, piscina), as lib/speculum.c does. capsula_iter and capsula_iter_proximus also exist, but after proximus the current entry is capsula_indexum(cap, iter.positus - 1): positus is 1-based once advanced.
    symbolum capsula_numerus capsula_indexum CapsulaIndexum capsula_legere_chorda · via lib/speculum.c
  7. quomodohow do I tell whether an entry is stored compressed, and what is mensura_compressa for an uncompressed file?
    Read the compressa flag, not mensura_compressa. The header says mensura_compressa is 0 when uncompressed, but for an embedded capsula the generator writes it EQUAL to mensura_cruda and capsula_aperire sets compressa = (mensura_compressa != mensura_cruda); only disk mode stores 0. mensura_cruda is the uncompressed size (in disk mode, the size when the index was taken; capsula_legere returns the file as it is now).
    symbolum CapsulaIndexum capsula_aperire capsula_aperire_e_disco · via tools/capsula_generare.c
  8. curwhy does disk mode exist instead of serving assets over a local HTTP server?
    The page keeps the same scheme, URL and origin (capsula://radix/...) and cannot tell which mode it runs in, so development and release differ only in which files exist. An HTTP server would also change port, origin, transport and cwd. And nothing listens: no port that any process on the machine could read (header comment on capsula_aperire_e_disco).
    symbolum capsula_aperire_e_disco

chorda

include/chorda.h · lib/chorda.interrogationes.stml

  1. possumnecan I pass chorda.datum to printf, strcmp or fopen?
    No: a chorda is mensura plus datum with NO terminating NUL, so C string functions read past the end. chorda_ut_cstr copies the bytes plus a NUL into the piscina; for printing without a copy use printf("%.*s", (int)s.mensura, s.datum). chorda_ut_cstr returns NIHIL (not "") when datum is NIHIL, which is how many functions here represent an empty result, so check before handing it to printf.
    symbolum chorda_ut_cstr chorda_aequalis_literis
  2. quomodohow do I make a chorda from a C string literal, and does it copy?
    chorda_ex_literis copies the bytes into the piscina (an empty literal gives {0, NIHIL}, because piscina_allocare of zero bytes returns NIHIL). chorda_ex_buffer wraps an existing i8 buffer without copying, but takes a non-const pointer, so a literal needs a cast that -Wcast-qual refuses; several files keep a private _chorda_ex_cstr view helper for that. To merely compare against a literal, chorda_aequalis_literis needs no chorda at all.
    symbolum chorda_ex_literis chorda_ex_buffer chorda_aequalis_literis
  3. quidwhat counts as an empty chorda, and why do {0, NIHIL} and {0, pointer} behave differently?
    Both have mensura 0, and chorda_vacua is VERUM for either, so test emptiness with it rather than with datum == NIHIL. The search functions do not treat them alike: with a NIHIL-datum needle chorda_continet and chorda_incipit say FALSUM and chorda_invenire finds nothing, while a zero-length needle with a real pointer matches at once (chorda_invenire then returns the whole haystack). chorda_ut_cstr gives NIHIL for the first and "" for the second.
    symbolum chorda_vacua chorda_continet chorda_incipit chorda_invenire chorda_ut_cstr
  4. possumnecan I keep a slice or a trimmed chorda after the original buffer is gone?
    No: chorda_sectio, chorda_praecidere (and _sinistram, _dextram), chorda_invenire and the elements from chorda_fissio all point into the original bytes. chorda_praecidi_laterale also returns a view even though it takes a piscina (and returns empty if that piscina is NIHIL). Copy with chorda_transcribere into a piscina that lives long enough.
    symbolum chorda_sectio chorda_praecidere chorda_praecidi_laterale chorda_fissio chorda_transcribere
  5. quomodohow do I find a substring and get the text after it?
    chorda_invenire returns only the MATCHED slice (mensura = needle length), not the rest of the string the way strstr does. For the rest, take chorda_invenire_index (an s32, -1 when absent) and cut with chorda_sectio(s, index + acus.mensura, s.mensura). For a plain yes/no use chorda_continet; the _ultimum variants search from the end.
    symbolum chorda_invenire chorda_invenire_index chorda_sectio chorda_continet chorda_invenire_ultimum_index
  6. quomodohow do I split a string on a delimiter and join the pieces back?
    chorda_fissio splits on one character, chorda_fissio_chorda on a multi-byte delimiter; both return a chorda_fissio_fructus (elementa, numerus) whose elements are views into the input. Empty fields are kept: "a,,b," gives four elements, the last one empty. An empty input gives zero elements, and an empty delimiter to chorda_fissio_chorda gives the whole input as one element. chorda_iungere joins an array with a separator into a new chorda. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): chorda_fissio_chorda reads past the end of the input and crashes whenever the delimiter is longer than the input ("a" split on "abc"), and chorda_fissio never matches a delimiter byte of 0x80 or above (0xFF, 0xC3) because character is signed on arm64 macOS, so such an input comes back as one element.
    symbolum chorda_fissio chorda_fissio_chorda chorda_iungere
  7. quomodohow do I parse an integer or a float out of a chorda safely?
    Use chorda_ut_s32, chorda_ut_i32, chorda_ut_s64 or chorda_ut_f64: each returns b32 and writes the result only on success. The whole chorda must be the number, so "12x", an embedded NUL byte, an empty chorda and out-of-range values are refused. Small differences: _s32 and _i32 go through strtol/strtoul and accept leading whitespace, _s64 is hand-written and accepts only an optional sign and digits, and _i32 refuses a leading '-'. For a number at the front of a longer text, cut it out with chorda_sectio first. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): _i32 checks for '-' only at the first byte, so after leading whitespace a minus is accepted and wrapped modulo 2^64: " -0" gives VERUM 0 and "\t-18446744069414584321" gives VERUM 4294967295, while "-0" is refused.
    symbolum chorda_ut_s32 chorda_ut_i32 chorda_ut_s64 chorda_ut_f64
  8. quomodohow do I turn a number into a chorda?
    chorda_ex_s32 and chorda_ex_s64 write decimal integers. chorda_ex_f64(n, praecisio, piscina) writes fixed decimals and returns an EMPTY chorda when praecisio is above L or the text would not fit its CXXXII-byte buffer (1e300 at six places does not), so large values vanish silently. chorda_ex_f64_exacta writes %.17g, which reads back to the same double. Inside a larger text, append with the chorda_aedificator functions instead.
    symbolum chorda_ex_s32 chorda_ex_s64 chorda_ex_f64 chorda_ex_f64_exacta chorda_aedificator_appendere_s32
  9. quomodohow do I compare chordae for equality or for sorting?
    chorda_aequalis compares length then bytes; chorda_aequalis_literis compares with a C string without allocating; chorda_aequalis_case_insensitivus folds through tolower, byte by byte, so only ASCII letters fold. For ordering, chorda_comparare is memcmp over the common prefix, then the shorter string first; it returns memcmp's own value when the bytes differ, so test its sign rather than comparing with -1 or 1.
    symbolum chorda_aequalis chorda_aequalis_literis chorda_aequalis_case_insensitivus chorda_comparare
  10. quomodohow do I convert an identifier between snake_case, camelCase, PascalCase and kebab-case?
    Use chorda_serpens, chorda_camelus, chorda_pascalis, chorda_kebab or chorda_pascalis_serpens. All split the input into words the same way: any non-alphanumeric byte separates, a lower-to-upper step starts a word, a letter after a digit starts a word, and a run of capitals followed by a lowercase letter gives its last capital to the next word, so "XMLParser" becomes xml_parser and "abc123def" becomes abc123_def. Case is changed with toupper and tolower, so this is ASCII only. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): non-ASCII letters are not kept but dropped as separators, so UTF-8 "café au lait" becomes caf_au_lait, CafAuLait and caf-au-lait (chorda_kebab slugs for "Café" and "Caf" collide).
    symbolum chorda_serpens chorda_camelus chorda_pascalis chorda_kebab chorda_pascalis_serpens
  11. possumnecan I use chorda_ex_bytes_legibilis for binary (KiB, MiB) sizes?
    No: it divides by M, which is 1000, so the units are decimal: 1000 bytes gives "1.0 KB" and 1023 bytes is also "1.0 KB", not "1023 B". The header's examples 1024 and 1048576 only look binary because they round to the same text, and its 1073741824 example is wrong: that value prints "1.1 GB". Values under one hundred get one decimal place, larger values none.
    symbolum chorda_ex_bytes_legibilis
  12. quomodohow do I hash a chorda, and is the hash stable across runs?
    chorda_friare is 32-bit FNV-1a over the bytes (i32, unsigned) with no seed, so the same bytes give the same hash in every run and on every machine. For a string-keyed map use tabula_dispersa rather than hashing by hand.
    symbolum chorda_friare tabula_dispersa_creare_chorda
  13. quomodohow do I replace every occurrence of a substring?
    chorda_substituere(s, antiquum, novum, piscina) scans left to right and replaces non-overlapping matches into a new chorda (chorda_numerare_occurrentia counts the same way: "aaaa" holds two "aa"). An empty antiquum returns a plain copy; a result that comes out empty is {0, NIHIL}. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): the result length is never checked against i32, so a growing replacement whose result exceeds 4 GiB (65536 'a' with antiquum "a" and a 65537-byte novum) wraps, allocates too little and writes past the buffer.
    symbolum chorda_substituere chorda_numerare_occurrentia

chorda_aedificator

include/chorda_aedificator.h · lib/chorda_aedificator.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I build a string piece by piece when I don't know its final length?
    Create a builder with chorda_aedificator_creare(piscina, capacitas) (capacitas must be above zero or it returns NIHIL), append with the _appendere_ family (_literis for a C string, _chorda for a chorda, _character, _s32, _i32, _f64, _hex_i32, _repetita, _lineam_novam), then chorda_aedificator_finire returns the text as a chorda. The header example calls chorda_aedificator_appendere_integer, which does not exist: use _s32 for signed and _i32 for unsigned numbers (i32 is unsigned in the house types).
    symbolum chorda_aedificator_creare chorda_aedificator_appendere_literis chorda_aedificator_appendere_chorda chorda_aedificator_appendere_s32 chorda_aedificator_appendere_i32 chorda_aedificator_finire
  2. possumnecan I pass the chorda from chorda_aedificator_finire to printf or fopen?
    Not directly: like every chorda it carries a length and is NOT NUL-terminated. Print it with "%.*s" and its mensura, or copy it with chorda_ut_cstr(fructus, piscina) to get a C string. chorda_aedificator_spectare has the same shape and points at the same live buffer; the header calls it valid only until the next change to the builder.
    symbolum chorda_aedificator_finire chorda_ut_cstr chorda_aedificator_spectare
  3. quidwhat does chorda_aedificator_finire do to the builder, and who owns the result?
    It only returns a chorda pointing at the builder's current buffer; nothing is copied and nothing is destroyed, whatever the header comment says. The bytes belong to the piscina and live until that piscina is destroyed or rolled back. Appending after finire is harmless to the result (new bytes land past its end, or in a new buffer when the builder grows), but chorda_aedificator_reset or _truncare followed by appending writes over the bytes your finished chorda still points at. chorda_aedificator_destruere does nothing.
    symbolum chorda_aedificator_finire chorda_aedificator_reset chorda_aedificator_truncare chorda_aedificator_destruere
  4. quomodohow do I write a JSON string value with the builder?
    Write the quotes yourself with chorda_aedificator_appendere_character and put the body through chorda_aedificator_appendere_evasus_json (or _literis_evasus_json for a C string). It escapes quote, backslash, \n, \r and \t, writes other bytes below 0x20 and DEL as \u00xx (lowercase hex), and copies bytes of 0x80 and above unchanged, so UTF-8 passes through without being checked. An empty input appends nothing and returns VERUM, also for an empty chorda whose datum is NIHIL.
    symbolum chorda_aedificator_appendere_evasus_json chorda_aedificator_appendere_literis_evasus_json chorda_aedificator_appendere_character
  5. quomodohow do I undo text I appended if I change my mind?
    Save the position with chorda_aedificator_longitudo before writing, and if you don't want the result call chorda_aedificator_truncare with that saved length. truncare only ever shortens (a larger value is ignored) and keeps the capacity; it does not touch the indentation level. The stml writer (lib/stml.c) uses this for optimistic emission.
    symbolum chorda_aedificator_longitudo chorda_aedificator_truncare
  6. quomodohow do I indent nested output (pretty printing) with the builder?
    The builder keeps an indentation level: push_indentationem raises it (capped at M), pop_indentationem lowers it (never below zero), and indentatio_gradus reads it. Nothing is indented automatically. At the start of each line call chorda_aedificator_appendere_indentationem(a, chorda_aedificator_indentatio_gradus(a)), which writes II spaces per level (CHORDA_AEDIFICATOR_INDENTATIO_SPATIA). reset clears the level too.
    symbolum chorda_aedificator_push_indentationem chorda_aedificator_pop_indentationem chorda_aedificator_indentatio_gradus chorda_aedificator_appendere_indentationem
  7. possumnecan I rely on the b32 return value of the append functions to catch out-of-memory?
    Not for memory. Creation and growth use piscina_allocare, which exits the process when no memory is left or a fixed-size piscina is full, so FALSUM never means out-of-memory (the builder does not use piscina_conari_allocare). FALSUM does mean bad input: a NIHIL builder or C string, _f64 with decimales above XXX or a value whose printed form is CXXXII bytes or longer, and chorda_aedificator_appendere_chorda on an empty chorda whose datum is NIHIL (what chorda_ex_literis("") returns), even though nothing needed appending.
    symbolum chorda_aedificator_appendere_chorda chorda_aedificator_appendere_f64 piscina_allocare piscina_conari_allocare
  8. curwhy does a builder use several times more piscina memory than the final string?
    A piscina cannot free one allocation, so each growth (the capacity doubles until it fits) allocates a new buffer and leaves the old one behind until the piscina dies: all buffers together come to about twice the final capacity, which is itself up to twice the text. The JSON escapers also allocate scratch from the same piscina and never give it back: six bytes per input byte for strings longer than LXXXIII bytes, plus a copy of every non-empty input in the _literis_ variant. Start with a realistic capacity, or take a piscina_notare mark, build, copy the result into ANOTHER piscina (chorda_transcribere), then piscina_reficere back to the mark.
    symbolum chorda_aedificator_creare chorda_aedificator_appendere_evasus_json piscina_notare piscina_reficere chorda_transcribere

color

include/color.h · lib/color.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I turn a Color into a pixel for a TabulaPixelorum and back?
    color_ad_pixelum packs it as a << 24 | b << 16 | g << 8 | r (ABGR in the 32-bit word), which on this little-endian machine lies in memory as the bytes R, G, B, A - the same layout as an Imago. color_ex_pixelum unpacks it. A literal written as 0xRRGGBBAA is NOT a pixel: build it with color_ex_rgba and convert.
    symbolum color_ad_pixelum color_ex_pixelum Color
  2. quomodohow do I blend or fade between two colours?
    color_interpolate(a, b, t) with t from 0 (all a) to 256 (all b), applied to all four channels including alpha, in signed integer arithmetic that truncates toward zero. t is not clamped: outside 0..256 the result is cast to i8 and wraps. It mixes the sRGB bytes directly; for a perceptually even mix go through linear light.
    symbolum color_interpolate
  3. curwhy should colour mixing for dithering or gradients happen in linear light?
    sRGB bytes are gamma-encoded: a black and white checkerboard looks like sRGB 188, not 128. Convert bytes with color_octetus_ad_linearem (a 256-entry table filled on first use), average there, and go back with color_linearis_ad_srgb, which returns 0..1 (multiply by 255 and round yourself - there is no helper back to a byte). The conversions do not clamp values outside 0..1.
    symbolum color_octetus_ad_linearem color_srgb_ad_linearem color_linearis_ad_srgb · via lib/color.worklog.md
  4. quomodohow do I find which palette colour is perceptually closest to a given colour?
    Convert both to OKLab with color_ad_oklab (sRGB bytes) or color_linearis_ad_oklab (linear floats; negative channels clamp to 0) and compare with color_oklab_distantia, a plain Euclidean distance: black to white is about 1.0. Alpha is ignored. The dithering_exemplaria library precomputes palette OKLab values and distances this way.
    symbolum color_ad_oklab color_oklab_distantia color_linearis_ad_oklab · via lib/dithering_exemplaria.c
  5. possumnecan I round-trip linear RGB through OKLab exactly?
    Not bit-exactly: Ottosson's published 10-digit matrices are not exact inverses, and the round trip errs by up to about 2.6e-7 (tests use 1e-6). That is far below 8-bit precision. color_oklab_ad_linearem does not clamp, so an OKLab point outside the gamut comes back with channels below 0 or above 1.
    symbolum color_linearis_ad_oklab color_oklab_ad_linearem · via lib/color.worklog.md
  6. quidwhat does color_ad_cinereum compute?
    An ITU-R BT.601 luma from the raw sRGB bytes, in integers: (30 R + 59 G + 11 B) / 100, truncated, alpha ignored. It is not a linear-light luminance; for perceptual lightness use the L of color_ad_oklab.
    symbolum color_ad_cinereum
  7. possumnecan color_ex_palette read colours from the current theme palette?
    No: it always reads the fixed Aquinas palette from thema_palette_aquinas (16 entries of 6-bit components, scaled by CCLV / LXIII, alpha CCLV), whatever thema_ponere_palette set. The index is not checked: 16 or more reads past the 48-byte table.
    symbolum color_ex_palette thema_palette_aquinas thema_ponere_palette

componens

include/componens.h · lib/componens.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I build a component tree for a widget?
    Create each node with componens_creare(piscina, intern, id, partes), give it bounds with componens_ponere_fines (in the PARENT's coordinate space) and attach it with componens_addere_liberum. componens_ponere_actio names the action it triggers and componens_ponere_titulum sets its accessible label. A node is logical only (id, action, kind, bounds, hit predicate, focus, children); drawing is derived from the tree elsewhere.
    symbolum componens_creare componens_ponere_fines componens_addere_liberum componens_ponere_actio componens_ponere_titulum
  2. curwhy do the componens setters take a C string, and do I have to keep it alive?
    No: id, actio, titulus and spatium are interned through the node's InternamentumChorda when set, so the node keeps its own canonical copy. Aliasing the caller's literal would need a const cast that -Wcast-qual refuses. Because the interner refuses "", an empty string becomes a zero-length chorda over a static byte (not NIHIL), so test these fields with chorda_vacua.
    symbolum componens_ponere_actio internare_aut_vacua chorda_internare_ex_literis · via lib/componens.worklog.md
  3. possumnecan I pass NIHIL as the interner to componens_creare?
    You can, but every string is then lost without a warning: the setters go through chorda_internare_ex_literis, which returns NIHIL when there is no interner, and that NIHIL is stored as an empty chorda, so id, actio, titulus and spatium all come out empty and componens_invenire_per_id cannot find a node by name. Pass a real interner (internamentum_globale or your own); each node keeps the one it was created with.
    symbolum componens_creare internare_aut_vacua chorda_internare_ex_literis internamentum_globale
  4. quomodohow do I find a component by its id?
    componens_invenire_per_id searches depth first, parent before children, and returns the first node whose id has the same bytes, or NIHIL. It ignores spatia, so when the same app is mounted twice use componens_invenire_in_spatio(arbor, spatium, id): it finds the root of that spatium and searches inside it without entering nested spatia (an empty spatium means the whole tree).
    symbolum componens_invenire_per_id componens_invenire_in_spatio
  5. quidwhat is a spatium on a component?
    A namespace for one mounted application (vicus side panes): the host marks the root of each mount with componens_ponere_spatium, and ids, actions and shapes inside resolve within it, so two copies of the same app do not collide. componens_spatium returns the nearest spatium walking up through the parents, or "" for the host.
    symbolum componens_ponere_spatium componens_spatium componens_invenire_in_spatio
  6. possumnecan I round-trip a component tree through STML without losing anything?
    Not quite. componens_scribere_stml writes id, partes, actio, titulus, bounds, praedicatum, focusabilis, sectio, translation, scala, puncta and children, and componens_legere_stml reads them back, but the claudit and spatium fields are not written, and componens_aequalis does not compare them either, so their loss is invisible. An unknown partes reads as PARTES_NULLUM and an unknown praedicatum as PRAEDICATUM_RECTANGULUM.
    symbolum componens_scribere_stml componens_legere_stml componens_aequalis
  7. possumnecan I make a component hit-test as a polygon or a mask?
    Not yet. PRAEDICATUM_POLYGONUM and PRAEDICATUM_MASCA are stored and serialized, but the hit test in destinatio checks only the rectangle in fines (plus sectio clipping, translation and scala). PRAEDICATUM_PROPRIUS is what scriba and pictor use: the rectangle is hit and the component resolves the local point itself. There is no setter for the polygon; callers fill puncta and numerus_punctorum directly.
    symbolum componens_ponere_praedicatum componens_ponere_fines · via lib/destinatio.c
  8. quomodohow do I scroll or zoom the children of a component?
    componens_ponere_transformatio(c, x, y, scala) sets a translation and an integer scale applied to the children (a scala below I is raised to I), and componens_ponere_sectio(c, VERUM) clips children to the node's bounds, so points outside them never reach a child.
    symbolum componens_ponere_transformatio componens_ponere_sectio

credo

include/credo.h · lib/credo.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I write a new test suite with credo?
    Use credo v2: a static CredoSectio table of rows {titulus, probare, parare, purgare, signa, exspectata_fracta} ending in a row whose titulus is NIHIL, and principale returns credo_suitam_currere("suita", SECTIONES). Each section gets its own piscina in c->piscina (destroyed after purgare), runs parare, probare, then purgare (always), and the function returns 0 only if every section that ran passed. CREDO_SECTIO=titulus runs one section; an unknown title fails the suite before anything runs.
    symbolum credo_suitam_currere CredoSectio CredoContextus · via probationes/probatio_admonitiones.c
  2. quomodohow do I end an old-style suite so the exit code reflects failures?
    Ask credo_omnia_praeterierunt() BEFORE credo_claudere, print credo_imprimere_compendium, and return 0 or 1 from that answer. credo_claudere resets all counts, so asking after it sees zero assertions, which now reads as FALSUM ("NIHIL PROBATUM"). An assertion before credo_aperire prints FATALE and exits the process with 1.
    symbolum credo_aperire credo_omnia_praeterierunt credo_imprimere_compendium credo_claudere · via probationes/probatio_piscina.c lib/credo.worklog.md
  3. curwhy does a test with zero assertions fail?
    Because "nothing failed" is not "something passed": a moved fixture directory or a corpus loop that finds nothing used to exit green. credo_omnia_praeterierunt returns FALSUM when the total is 0, a v2 section with no assertions is VACUA (not passing), and a suite in which no section ran (all OMISSA, say) also returns I.
    symbolum credo_omnia_praeterierunt credo_suitam_currere · via lib/credo.worklog.md
  4. possumnecan I compare two chorda values with CREDO_CHORDAE_AEQUALES?
    No: despite the name, CREDO_CHORDAE_AEQUALES takes two C strings (constans character*). For chordae use CREDO_CHORDA_AEQUALIS(a, b), which wraps chorda_aequalis in CREDO_VERUM, so a failure prints only the expression text, never the two values; for a chorda against a literal use CREDO_CHORDA_AEQUALIS_LITERIS(s, "text"), which prints the received chorda (first 255 bytes) next to the expectation.
    symbolum _credo_notare_chorda _credo_notare_chorda_literis chorda_aequalis
  5. quomodohow do I read the Speratus and Receptus lines of a failure?
    The comparison macros take (received, expected): CREDO_AEQUALIS_S32(a, b) prints a as Receptus and b as Speratus, so put the computed value first and the expected constant second. Strings are quoted and escaped (\t, \n, \xNN for control bytes, NUL included) with their length in octets; a value longer than 160 octets shows a window around the first difference, named as "fenestra [a, b)", never a silent cut. Equality of strings or bytes adds a third line, Differentia, naming the first differing octet on both sides (or "finit" where one side ends); float comparisons name the difference and the tolerance there. Every failure line is flushed immediately, so it survives a crash a few lines later even through a pipe. Passing assertions print a dot with no newline, so a failure's F, or any line the test prints itself, follows the dots on the same line: never grep output with a leading ^.
    symbolum _credo_notare _credo_notare_integrum _credo_notare_chordae
  6. possumnecan I trust the numbers CREDO_AEQUALIS_I32 prints for large unsigned values?
    Yes, since credo III (2026-10-19): every _I32 helper prints with %u, so a value at or above 2^31 no longer shows as negative (before that the equality and inequality helpers used %d). For 64 bits there are full families: CREDO_*_S64 (signed) and CREDO_*_I64 (unsigned), each with AEQUALIS, INAEQUALITAS, MAIOR, MINOR and the AUT_AEQUALIS forms; a memoriae_index goes into the _I64 forms without a cast.
    symbolum _credo_notare_integrum_i32 _credo_notare_integrum_inaequalitas_i32 _credo_notare_i64
  7. quomodowhich assertion should I use for strings, byte buffers and floats?
    Chordas: CREDO_CHORDA_AEQUALIS, _CONTINET, _INCIPIT, _TERMINATUR, and CREDO_CHORDA_AEQUALIS_LITERIS against a C literal. Two C strings: CREDO_LITERAE_AEQUALES (NIHIL-safe; CREDO_CHORDAE_AEQUALES takes C strings too, despite its name, and is kept for old suites). Raw bytes: CREDO_OCTETI_AEQUALES(a, b, n), which names the first differing offset in hex. Floats: CREDO_F64_PROXIMUS and CREDO_F32_PROXIMUS for an absolute epsilon; CREDO_F64_RELATIVE(a, b, r) when magnitudes are very large or very small (|a-b| <= r * max(|a|, |b|)); CREDO_F64_ULP(a, b, n) for "within n representable doubles" (-0.0 equals 0.0, NaN never equals anything). Prefer these over CREDO_VERUM(strcmp(...) == 0): a failing CREDO_VERUM can only print its expression, these print both values.
    symbolum _credo_notare_chordae _credo_notare_literae _credo_notare_octeti _credo_notare_f64_relative _credo_notare_f64_ulp
  8. quomodohow do I see how much memory each section used?
    Run the suite with CREDO_VERDICTA=file (fabrica always does): every SECTIO line ends with three numbers in KiB - the peak use of the section's own piscina, the process footprint when the section ended, and how much the process's lifetime peak rose while it ran (the footprint counts compressed pages, which RSS hides). The lifetime peak never resets, so a light section after a heavy one shows a rise of 0, not the heavy one's peak. Nothing is printed to the human output: officina compares stdout with golden files. fabrica copies the three numbers into the annals as facts on each section's run; bin/mensor quaestio graves lists the heaviest sections across the tree.
    symbolum credo_suitam_currere memoria_processus_legere
  9. quomodowhy did a section fail with credo_memoria, and how do I let a heavy section through?
    After each section credo compares the larger of its arena peak and the rise in the process's lifetime peak against a ceiling: CREDO_MEMORIA_ORDINARIA_MB (256) normally, CREDO_MEMORIA_GRAVIS_MB (1024) for a section whose signa include "gravis". Going over is an ordinary failure named credo_memoria, so the section is FRACTA and the suite exits 1. If the section really needs the memory, add "gravis" to its signa field; CREDO_SIGNA_OMITTE=gravis then skips such sections. CREDO_MEMORIA_MB=N replaces the ordinary ceiling for one run (0 turns it off). The check runs only when a section ends, so it can't stop a runaway loop. When credo is compiled under AddressSanitizer only the arena peak is judged: ASan's quarantine (256 MB by default) and shadow memory sit in the process footprint, so the rise there isn't the section's.
    symbolum credo_suitam_currere CREDO_MEMORIA_ORDINARIA_MB CREDO_MEMORIA_GRAVIS_MB
  10. quomodohow do I stop a section early when a precondition fails instead of crashing on NIHIL?
    CREDO_NECESSE(x) or CREDO_NECESSE_NON_NIHIL(p): it records the assertion and, if it failed, longjmps out of the section (marked ABORTA; purgare still runs). Outside a section it behaves like CREDO_VERUM. For a bail-out site with no expression (fixture missing, file not opened), CREDO_CULPA("reason") records an unconditional failure so the summary and exit code agree; it does not jump, so you still return yourself.
    symbolum _credo_necesse credo_suitam_currere
  11. quomodohow do I test that code crashes, or does not crash or hang?
    CREDO_NON_RUIT(expr), CREDO_NON_PENDET(expr, mora_ms) and CREDO_RUIT_CUM(expr, SIGSEGV) (or _INTRA with a deadline) run expr in a forked child; the parent waits, kills it with SIGKILL past the deadline (default CREDO_MORA_ORDINARIA_MS, 5000), and judges how it ended. They are statement macros on purpose: as a function argument, expr would be evaluated, and crash, in the parent. Assertions made inside the child are NOT counted by the parent.
    symbolum credo_processus_incipere credo_processus_iudicare credo_processus_filium_finire
  12. possumnecan CREDO_NON_RUIT catch a call that exits with an error code?
    No. With no expected signal the check passes whenever the child exited normally, whatever its exit status, so exit(I) inside the expression (for example a fatal piscina_allocare) or a failed CREDO_NECESSE in the child (which calls _exit(I)) still passes. Also the deadline counts 1 ms sleep iterations, not wall time, so real waits run long (the worklog measured about 25%).
    symbolum credo_processus_iudicare _credo_necesse
  13. quomodohow do I give a test a scratch directory that is cleaned up?
    credo_area(c) inside a v2 section returns TMPDIR/suite.n.pid (n = the row's position in the table, from 1; in parare_suitae/purgare_suitae it is TMPDIR/suite.suita.pid), created on the first call; the whole tree is deleted after purgare, even after NECESSE or when a CREDO_NON_RUIT child created it. It returns NIHIL outside a running context or if the directory cannot be made.
    symbolum credo_area CredoContextus
  14. quomodohow do I share an expensive fixture across sections or skip slow ones?
    credo_suitam_currere_cum(suita, sectiones, parare_suitae, purgare_suitae) runs parare_suitae once (its datum reaches every section as c->suita, read-only) and purgare_suitae always; if the fixture fails no section runs. Tag rows with signa from the closed set "lentus rete fenestra repositorium" and CREDO_SIGNA_OMITTE="rete" reports them OMISSA; an unknown word fails the suite. CREDO_ORDO=mixtus shuffles the sections (seed printed) to find hidden shared state.
    symbolum credo_suitam_currere_cum CredoSectio CredoContextus
  15. quomodohow do I mark a section as a known failure without turning the suite red?
    Write the reason in the row's exspectata_fracta field (an empty string is refused before anything runs). A failing or aborted section is then NOTA_FRACTA and counts as passing; if it starts passing it becomes INOPINATA and fails the suite, telling you to delete the line. CREDO_GRADUS("step") names the step inside a section so the first failure says which one.
    symbolum CredoSectio credo_suitam_currere

css_lexema

include/css_lexema.h · lib/css_lexema.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I tokenize a CSS source and walk the tokens?
    css_lexare(piscina, fons, mensura) returns a Xar of CssLexema stored BY VALUE, so xar_obtinere(lexemata, k) gives a CssLexema* directly. The last token is always CSS_LEX_FINIS with an empty valor and offset == mensura, so a loop can stop on the kind instead of counting. css_lexema_genus_nomen gives a printable kind name for debugging.
    symbolum css_lexare CssLexema xar_obtinere css_lexema_genus_nomen
  2. possumnecan I free or reuse the source buffer after css_lexare returns?
    No. Nothing is copied: every valor points into fons (a view, not NUL-terminated), so the buffer must outlive the tokens. Never write through a valor either; the cast away from const is only there because chorda holds an i8*.
    symbolum css_lexare CssLexema
  3. quomodohow do I get the original text back from the tokens?
    css_lexemata_emittere(piscina, lexemata) concatenates every valor. Because the lexer is total and keeps whitespace and comments as tokens, the result equals the input byte for byte for ANY input, including invalid CSS and NUL bytes. The tests use it as the fidelity oracle.
    symbolum css_lexemata_emittere css_lexare
  4. curwhy does the lexer emit whitespace and comment tokens when the CSS spec discards comments?
    For byte fidelity and so tools can query comments. CSS_LEX_SPATIA, CSS_LEX_COMMENTARIUM and CSS_LEX_COMMENTARIUM_IMPERFECTUM stay in the stream; a consumer that wants spec behaviour filters them out. css_arbor_parsare (css/fontes) keeps them too: between rules it attaches them as trivia to the neighbouring token, while inside preludes and declaration values every space and comment stays a content leaf of its own (moving a comment between two kept values onto a token would reorder bytes).
    symbolum css_lexare · via include/css_lexema.h css/fontes/css_arbor.c
  5. quidwhat are the IMPERFECTA token kinds?
    Tokens that are cut short or malformed. CSS_LEX_CHORDA_IMPERFECTA is a string ended by a raw newline (the newline is NOT consumed, it starts the next token) or by end of input. CSS_LEX_URL_IMPERFECTA is a bad url( or one open at EOF. CSS_LEX_COMMENTARIUM_IMPERFECTUM is a comment open at EOF. For a string or url left open at EOF the spec returns a valid token plus a parse error; this lexer names the problem in the token kind instead. Here newline means \n, \r or \f. No input makes the lexer fail.
    symbolum css_lexare
  6. possumnecan I get the decoded value of an escaped identifier or string?
    Not from this library: escapes are understood only far enough to find token boundaries, and valor stays verbatim (\41 stays three bytes, plus the one whitespace byte after a hex escape, which belongs to it). The header assigns decoding to the tree stage, but nothing in css/fontes decodes escapes yet either: css_arbor_parsare keeps valores verbatim. One consequence: url( is recognised by its raw three bytes (any case), so an escaped spelling such as u\72l( lexes as CSS_LEX_FUNCTIO, not CSS_LEX_URL.
    symbolum css_lexare · via css/fontes/css_arbor.c
  7. quidwhat units are the linea and columna of a CssLexema in?
    Both are 1-based and count BYTES, not characters: a multi-byte UTF-8 letter advances columna by its byte length. offset is the 0-based byte offset. Only \n starts a new line in this count, although the lexer itself treats \r and \f as newlines inside strings, so a file with bare \r line ends reports everything on line 1.
    symbolum css_lexare CssLexema

delineare

include/delineare.h · lib/delineare.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I draw lines, rectangles and circles into a pixel buffer?
    Make a TabulaPixelorum (fenestra_creare_tabulam_pixelorum for a window, tabula_pixelorum_creare_nuda without one), wrap it with delineare_creare_contextum(piscina, tabula), and call the primitives with a Color struct (color_ex_rgb), not a packed pixel. The RGB(...) macro in the header's example yields a packed i32 and does not fit the Color parameters. Nothing is shown until you present the table (fenestra_praesentare_pixela). Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): the outline delineare_rectangulum with altitudo 1 at y = 0 hangs, because its side edges run to y + altitudo - 2, which wraps to UINT_MAX in unsigned i32.
    symbolum delineare_creare_contextum tabula_pixelorum_creare_nuda fenestra_creare_tabulam_pixelorum delineare_rectangulum_plenum delineare_circulum
  2. possumnecan I pass negative coordinates, for a shape partly off the left or top edge?
    Not safely. Coordinates are i32, which is unsigned, so -5 arrives as about 4e9. Outlines of circles and ellipses survive (off-table pixels are skipped). delineare_rectangulum_plenum silently drops the whole rectangle, visible part included, and the radial gradients draw nothing once the circle crosses the top or left edge. The rest can hang: delineare_lineam (so triangles and polygons) picks its direction with an unsigned compare and never reaches its end, and rectangle outlines and the other filled shapes draw through delineare_lineam_horizontalem or _verticalem, which loop from the smaller value up to the wrapped one - billions of steps, forever when an end is exactly -1. Clip to the table yourself, or emit Mandata and rasterize with delineare_mandata, which clips in signed space.
    symbolum delineare_lineam delineare_lineam_horizontalem delineare_lineam_verticalem delineare_rectangulum_plenum delineare_circulum_plenum delineare_gradientum_radialem_dithered delineare_mandata · via lib/delineare_mandata.worklog.md
  3. quomodohow do I restrict drawing to a rectangle (clipping)?
    delineare_ponere_praecisionem(ctx, x, y, latitudo, altitudo) - x and y are s32 - and delineare_tollere_praecisionem to clear it. A negative x or y is raised to 0 but the width or height is NOT reduced by the same amount, so a clip at x = -5, width 10 covers columns 0 to 9 rather than 0 to 4. delineare_vacare ignores the clip and fills the whole table; delineare_restituere_contextum resets clip, mode and pattern.
    symbolum delineare_ponere_praecisionem delineare_tollere_praecisionem delineare_vacare delineare_restituere_contextum
  4. quomodohow do I fill with a classic Mac pattern or draw in XOR mode?
    Set MODUS_EXEMPLAR with delineare_ponere_modum, then the 8x8 bitmap with delineare_ponere_exemplar (8 bytes, one per row, high bit = leftmost pixel) or a built-in one with delineare_ponere_exemplar_internum (ids below EXEMPLAR_NUMERUS; others are ignored). Set bits get the colour, clear bits are left alone; the pattern is anchored at the offset from delineare_ponere_offset_exemplaris. MODUS_XOR XORs the whole packed pixel, alpha included, so XOR with an opaque colour over an opaque pixel leaves alpha 0.
    symbolum delineare_ponere_modum delineare_ponere_exemplar delineare_ponere_exemplar_internum delineare_ponere_offset_exemplaris exemplar_obtinere
  5. possumnecan I fill a rounded rectangle with delineare_rectangulum_rotundum_plenum and get the same footprint as a plain filled rectangle?
    No. With a radius above 0 it is meant to sit inside the border drawn by delineare_rectangulum_rotundum: the leftmost and rightmost columns stay empty, so draw the outline as well. With radius 0 it calls delineare_rectangulum_plenum and fills everything. The radius is capped at half the width and half the height.
    symbolum delineare_rectangulum_rotundum_plenum delineare_rectangulum_rotundum delineare_rectangulum_plenum
  6. quomodohow do I draw a dithered gradient limited to a palette?
    The _cum_palette functions take an AlgorithusDithering and a Color array (NIHIL = pure black and white). They work in grey: both end colours and every palette entry are reduced to BT.601 luma, so a colour palette is matched by brightness only. The radial version always dithers with Bayer (8x8 when asked, otherwise 4x4), whatever algorithm you pass. The plain _dithered functions quantize each RGB channel to 0 or 255 with Floyd-Steinberg. Today only brightening gradients work: the shared interpolation runs in unsigned i32, so when the end value is below the start every pixel past the first clamps to full brightness. DITHERING_ATKINSON also drops its two-rows-down neighbour and spreads 5/8 of the error, not Atkinson's 6/8.
    symbolum delineare_gradientum_linearem_dithered_cum_palette delineare_gradientum_radialem_dithered_cum_palette delineare_gradientum_linearem_dithered
  7. possumnecan I draw gradients every frame without the context's piscina growing?
    Not on its own: the Floyd-Steinberg and Atkinson paths allocate their error rows from the piscina given to delineare_creare_contextum on every call and never release them. Bracket the drawing with piscina_notare and piscina_reficere on that piscina, or give the context a per-frame piscina. Bayer paths allocate nothing.
    symbolum delineare_gradientum_linearem_dithered delineare_creare_contextum piscina_notare piscina_reficere

delineare_mandata

include/delineare_mandata.h · lib/delineare_mandata.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I render a Mandata list to pixels without opening a window?
    tabula_pixelorum_creare_nuda(piscina, latitudo, altitudo) gives a window-free table (scale 1), then delineare_mandata(m, tabula, fons, ctx). imago_ex_tabula views the result as an Imago without copying (the packed pixels already lie in memory as R, G, B, A), ready for imago_png_scribere. The new table is zeroed, i.e. black with alpha 0, so paint a background rectangle first if the PNG should not be transparent.
    symbolum tabula_pixelorum_creare_nuda delineare_mandata imago_ex_tabula imago_png_scribere
  2. quomodohow do I get pictures drawn for MANDATUM_IMAGO elements?
    Mandata never carry pixels: mandata_imago stores a name in textus, and the ImagoFons callback you pass to delineare_mandata turns that name into a constans Imago* (pictor uses the projection's sigillum). A NIHIL fons or a NIHIL result skips the element. The picture is cropped to the element's fines, scaled by the group's integer scale, pixels with alpha 0 are skipped, and every other pixel is copied as is - no blending.
    symbolum ImagoFons delineare_mandata mandata_imago
  3. quidwhat colour does a ColorMandati turn into when rasterized?
    color_ex_mandato decides: COLOR_MANDATI_THEMA goes through thema_color, so thema_initiare must have run; COLOR_MANDATI_INDEX through thema_color_ex_indice_colorationis, so its value is a syntax colouring index (ColoratioIndex), not a raw palette slot; COLOR_MANDATI_RGBA (and any other genus) is the packed table pixel a << 24 | b << 16 | g << 8 | r read by color_ex_pixelum - not a literal 0xRRGGBBAA.
    symbolum color_ex_mandato thema_color thema_color_ex_indice_colorationis color_ex_pixelum
  4. possumnecan text and lines follow a group's clip, scale and line thickness?
    Not in this V1 rasterizer.
