Text is placed at the group's origin and scale but drawn at the font's native cell size by tabula_pixelorum_pingere_chordam_fonte, and it ignores the group's sectio clip. Lines are always one pixel thick (crassitudo is ignored) and use only their first two points. Rectangles, lines, polygons and images do honour the clip, and their geometry follows the scale.
    symbolum delineare_mandata tabula_pixelorum_pingere_chordam_fonte · via lib/delineare_mandata.worklog.md
  5. possumnecan mandata have negative coordinates, for content panned off the left or top?
    Yes for the primitives: rectangles, lines and polygons that reach into negative space are clipped in signed arithmetic before the unsigned delineare calls (which would vanish or hang otherwise). One gap remains: a group with sectio whose clip box starts left of or above 0 hands that origin to delineare_ponere_praecisionem, which raises it to 0 without shrinking the box, so the clip comes out wider or taller than the group by the overhang.
    symbolum delineare_mandata delineare_ponere_praecisionem · via lib/delineare_mandata.worklog.md
  6. quomodohow do I rasterize only some of the primitives in a Mandata list?
    delineare_mandata_selecta with a DelineareFiltrum: return VERUM to paint an element. The filter judges primitives only; groups are always walked, so transforms and clips stay exact for whatever you keep. A NIHIL filter paints everything, the same as delineare_mandata.
    symbolum delineare_mandata_selecta DelineareFiltrum
  7. possumnecan I nest groups (coetus) as deeply as I like?
    Up to 63 levels below the root (the frame stack is LXIV). A deeper group is not pushed, without any warning: its contents are drawn in the enclosing group's origin, scale and clip, while the element counting still pops the outer frames correctly.
    symbolum delineare_mandata mandata_coetus_incipere
  8. possumnecan I rasterize the same Mandata every frame without its piscina growing?
    Not for free: every call allocates a ContextusDelineandi from the Mandata's own piscina (m->piscina), and every polygon three scratch arrays, none released. Rebuild the list per frame in a piscina you empty, or bracket the call with piscina_notare and piscina_reficere on that piscina.
    symbolum delineare_mandata delineare_creare_contextum mandata_vacare piscina_notare piscina_reficere
  9. quidwhat are delineare_mandata_modulus and delineare_mandata_mensor for?
    They declare this rasterizer's native scope for tessellation: the Modulus has the window's text cell as its cell and the table as its extent, and the ModulusMensor says text is measured by the font, one cell per rune (fons_6x8), grapheme policy. Text this rasterizer paints stays inside what that module measures.
    symbolum delineare_mandata_modulus delineare_mandata_mensor modulus_creare

dispensator

include/dispensator.h · lib/dispensator.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I wire an app's event loop through the dispensator?
    Create it once with dispensator_creare(piscina, intern, repo, actiones, componere, ctx, quies_ms) - NIHIL if any of the first five is NIHIL - which composes frame 0 at once. Feed every input event to dispensator_tractare, call dispensator_pulsare(d, now) once per frame, paint dispensator_arbor(d), and call dispensator_finire after the loop ends. ludus_fenestra.c is the window glue that does exactly this.
    symbolum dispensator_creare dispensator_tractare dispensator_pulsare dispensator_arbor dispensator_finire · via lib/ludus_fenestra.c
  2. possumnecan I keep the Componens* from dispensator_arbor across events?
    No. Trees live in two ping-pong piscinae and each recomposition empties the older one; every handled event or pulse recomposes at least once (twice when derived events were delivered), so a node pointer is good only until the next dispensator_tractare, dispensator_pulsare or dispensator_recomponere. Keep the id instead: component ids are interned and outlive the tree. dispensator_numerus_compositionum counts compositions.
    symbolum dispensator_arbor dispensator_recomponere dispensator_numerus_compositionum
  3. quidwhat is the staleness rule, and how often does my componere function run?
    After every dispatched event, including pulses and events no action consumed, the tree is recomposed from the island and Motus, and again after any derived events are delivered. Painting is separate and happens once per frame outside this library. A Componere must only read: it builds the tree into the piscina it is given and writes nothing to the island.
    symbolum dispensator_tractare dispensator_recomponere Componere · via lib/dispensator.worklog.md
  4. quomodohow do I set, push and pop keyboard focus from code?
    dispensator_focus_ponere writes a component id; dispensator_focus_impellere pushes the current focus and sets a new one; dispensator_focus_extrahere pops it back. They are the attributes focus and focus_acervus (a comma-separated list) in the innermost branch of the focus chain, so an island restore restores them. Setting an empty id removes the attribute, since the interner refuses empty strings. A focus id missing from the new tree is offered to the root as focus_petitus, and cleared if it is still missing after that.
    symbolum dispensator_focus dispensator_focus_ponere dispensator_focus_impellere dispensator_focus_extrahere insula_attributum_tollere
  5. curwhy do focus_captus, mus_intravit and other derived events arrive after the tree is recomposed?
    They are queued and delivered at the boundary (limen): after the current event's actions and the staleness recompose, against the NEW tree, so a derived event sees the world its cause produced. The queue is copied before delivery, so addressing from inside a handler is safe and waits for the next boundary. The cost is a second composition for events that produce derived ones.
    symbolum dispensator_addressare dispensator_tractare · via lib/dispensator.worklog.md
  6. quomodohow do I send an event to one component by its id?
    dispensator_addressare(d, id, genus, tempus). The innermost focus space is taken at the call; the id is looked up in that space at the next boundary, against the new tree, and an id that is empty or not found there is dropped silently. The id chorda is stored, not copied, so pass an interned or otherwise long-lived one. Events delivered at the boundary skip the ante/post middleware hooks.
    symbolum dispensator_addressare componens_invenire_in_spatio
  7. possumnecan I intercept every event before the actions see it?
    Yes, with one pair of hooks: dispensator_medium_ponere(d, ante, post, ctx). ante runs before the actions and returning VERUM consumes the event (no action runs); post runs after the action that consumed it, with that component as actor. NIHIL removes a hook. They see the raw event and what derivare makes from it, but not the events queued for the boundary (hover, focus captured or lost, focus_petitus, dispensator_addressare).
    symbolum dispensator_medium_ponere DispensatorAnte DispensatorPost
  8. curwhy must I call dispensator_finire when the app exits?
    Pan/zoom and the app's gesture are flushed into the island at a quiet seat: when an event or pulse arrives quies_ms (or the gesture's own interval) after the last movement, measured on event time so a replay flushes at the same events (pan/zoom also flushes when focus moves into another nested space). Whatever is still pending when the loop ends is lost unless dispensator_finire flushes it without waiting.
    symbolum dispensator_finire motus_effundere motus_gestum_effundere · via lib/dispensator.worklog.md
  9. quidwhat happens with Tab and Escape that no action consumed?
    The dispatcher handles them itself. Tab (Shift+Tab backwards) cycles focus over every focusable component under the pane's root (motus.spatium), nested spaces included, wrapping at the ends. Escape while focus is inside a nested space moves focus to the first focusable of the nearest enclosing level. Consume the key in an action to override either.
    symbolum dispensator_tractare dispensator_focus
  10. quomodohow does my app learn the window size?
    On EVENTUS_MUTARE_MAGNITUDINEM the dispatcher writes superficies_latitudo and superficies_altitudo (our pixels) into the island root as writer "dispensator", before targeting and composition, and the componere function reads them there. The app's canon must declare both attributes as numbers owned by "dispensator", otherwise the island refuses the write.
    symbolum dispensator_tractare insula_ramus_attributum
  11. quomodohow do I record the raw events a dispensator receives, for replay or tests?
    dispensator_notarium_ponere(d, xar) with a Xar of EventusNotatum: every raw event, pulses included, is appended (not the events derivare makes from it). Mouse down, up and move also get scopus, the id of the component they were targeted at (copied into the dispatcher's piscina), and scopus_x/y in its local space; other events have no scopus. eventus_notata_scribere_stml writes the list; NIHIL stops recording.
    symbolum dispensator_notarium_ponere EventusNotatum eventus_notata_scribere_stml
  12. possumnecan I destroy a dispensator and free its memory?
    No: there is no dispensator_destruere. dispensator_creare makes three piscinae of its own (arbor_a and arbor_b, LXIV * M bytes each to start, and destinatio_scratch, XVI * M) that nothing frees, besides what it takes from the piscina you pass. Create one per app and keep it for the process lifetime.
    symbolum dispensator_creare piscina_destruere

dispositio

include/dispositio.h · lib/dispositio.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I lay out a widget tree (header, growing body, footer) without hand pixel arithmetic?
    Create a Dispositio from a piscina, fill a DispositioForma per node (start from dispositio_formam_initiare, then set directio, the latitudo and altitudo DispositioMensura, spacing, gap and alignment), add it with dispositio_addere under its parent's index (-1 for the root), call dispositio_computare with the surface size in cells, then read each node's rectangle with dispositio_fines(d, index). It is the Clay model: fit, grow, fixed and percent sizes per axis.
    symbolum dispositio_creare dispositio_formam_initiare dispositio_addere dispositio_computare dispositio_fines · via lib/kalendarium.c
  2. quidwhat units does dispositio work in, and how do I get pixel rectangles for a window?
    Everything is integer CELLS, so every edge lands on a cell boundary by construction. dispositio_ad_pixela(fines, columnae, lineae, latitudo, altitudo, cw, ch) multiplies by the cell size, and a node that reaches the right or bottom edge of the cell grid is stretched to the surface's real pixel edge, since a window is usually not an exact multiple of the cell size.
    symbolum dispositio_ad_pixela dispositio_fines Fines
  3. quidwhat is the implicit root, and what does parent -1 mean?
    As in Clay there is a hidden root: a row (DISPOSITIO_LINEA) fixed at the surface size given to dispositio_computare, with no padding and no gap. Nodes added with parent -1 are its children, laid out left to right. Usually you add a single top-level node there with CRESCENS on both axes and build under it.
    symbolum dispositio_addere dispositio_computare
  4. quomodohow do I make a child take the remaining space, or a fixed or percentage size?
    Set the axis DispositioMensura: DISPOSITIO_CRESCENS grows into what is left (bounded by minimum and maximum, 0 meaning no maximum), DISPOSITIO_FIXA takes valor cells, DISPOSITIO_PARS takes valor hundredths of the parent's inner size (after padding and, along the parent's direction, gaps; rounded down), and DISPOSITIO_APTA fits the content. minimum and maximum apply to APTA and CRESCENS only. Several growing children share the space Clay-style: the smallest grow first, and an uneven remainder is spread across them, not given to the first.
    symbolum DispositioMensura DispositioGenus
  5. possumnecan a node hold text and size itself to fit it?
    Yes, one line of it: set forma.textus and pass a DispositioMensor to dispositio_computare that returns the width of a chorda in cells (wide runes may count II in a terminal). The text becomes a virtual first child one line high, and its minimum is the widest space-separated word. With a NIHIL mensor the text is ignored. Newlines and wrapping are not supported yet.
    symbolum dispositio_computare DispositioMensor DispositioForma
  6. possumnecan I free the text I put in forma.textus right after dispositio_addere?
    No. dispositio_addere copies the DispositioForma, but the chorda in textus is copied by value, so it still points at your bytes, and the mensor reads them during dispositio_computare. Keep the text alive until computare returns.
    symbolum dispositio_addere dispositio_computare
  7. quomodohow do I re-run layout every frame without the piscina growing?
    dispositio_vacare removes all nodes and keeps the memory, so the next frame's dispositio_addere calls reuse it; indices restart at 0. Calling dispositio_computare again on the same tree with a new surface size simply recomputes every rectangle.
    symbolum dispositio_vacare dispositio_computare
  8. quomodohow do I know dispositio_addere failed, and what does dispositio_fines give for a bad index?
    dispositio_addere returns -1 when d or forma is NIHIL, when parens is below -1, or when the parent index does not exist yet (a parent must be added before its children); otherwise it returns the node's index in order of addition. dispositio_fines for an unknown index returns an all-zero Fines rather than failing, so check the index first (dispositio_numerus gives the count).
    symbolum dispositio_addere dispositio_fines dispositio_numerus
  9. quidwhat happens when children do not fit in their parent?
    Along the parent's direction, APTA and CRESCENS children shrink (largest first) down to their content minimum; FIXA and PARS never shrink. If the parent has praecidere set on that axis nothing is compressed and the children simply overflow. Along the main axis an overflowing row is placed from the start even when alignment is MEDIUM or FINIS; across it, a too-big child centred with MEDIUM gets a negative offset (rounded down, as -3/2 gives -2).
    symbolum dispositio_computare DispositioForma
  10. curwhy are dispositio results not identical to Clay's?
    Clay uses floats; dispositio is integer and names its divergences in the header: centring rounds down (even when negative), percent sizes round down, and when grow or shrink space does not divide evenly each edge is the floor of the exact edge, so the remainder is spread instead of going to the first children. Everything else is meant to match, and tools/dispositio_oraculum.sh runs the real Clay (a ../clay clone pinned at e6cc369; exit 2 if it is missing) on the same STML tree as the oracle.
    symbolum dispositio_computare · via include/dispositio.h tools/dispositio_oraculum.sh

dithering

include/dithering.h · lib/dithering.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I dither an RGBA image to the Aquinas palette and get displayable pixels back?
    Fill a b32 colores[XVI] with dithering_praeparare_omnes (or another preset), call dithering_atkinson_colorum(rgba, latitudo, altitudo, colores, piscina) and check fructus.successus (FALSUM for a NIHIL rgba or piscina, a zero side, no active color, or a failed allocation). The result is one palette index (0-15) per pixel in fructus.indices, allocated in the piscina you passed. To show it, give dithering_indices_ad_rgba a buffer of latitudo * altitudo * 4 bytes that you allocated yourself; it writes the palette RGB and alpha 255 for every pixel.
    symbolum dithering_praeparare_omnes dithering_atkinson_colorum dithering_indices_ad_rgba DitheringFructus
  2. possumnecan I limit the dither to a subset of the sixteen colors?
    Yes: colores_activi is XVI b32 flags, one per palette entry. The presets fill it for you: griseum (0 1 3 4 5 15), calidus (0 5 6 9 10 11 15), frigidus (0 1 5 7 12 13 14) and monochromaticus (0 5). The indices in the result are still full-palette numbers, not positions in your subset. With no flag set, both dither functions return successus FALSUM and indices NIHIL. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): dithering_atkinson_griseum emits at most four of the active colors, so with the six-color griseum preset colors 3 and 4 never appear and the output tone is biased.
    symbolum dithering_praeparare_griseum dithering_praeparare_calidus dithering_praeparare_frigidus dithering_praeparare_monochromaticus dithering_atkinson_colorum
  3. possumnecan I dither an image with transparency and keep the transparent pixels?
    Not by itself: the alpha byte of every input pixel is ignored and dithering_indices_ad_rgba always writes alpha 255. Masking is the caller's job. pictor_imaginem_aptare, for example, dithers with dithering_atkinson_colorum and then overwrites the index with 0xFF (CCLV) wherever the scaled pixel's alpha is below CXXVIII. Do not feed such indices back into dithering_indices_ad_rgba: it clamps any index above XV to XV, so 0xFF comes out as opaque Griseus Calidus.
    symbolum dithering_atkinson_colorum dithering_indices_ad_rgba pictor_imaginem_aptare
  4. quidwhat is the difference between dithering_atkinson_colorum and dithering_atkinson_griseum?
    colorum picks the nearest active color by squared RGB distance and diffuses the RGB error. griseum works on luminance only ((77R + 150G + 28B) >> 8): it sorts the active colors by luminance and assigns buckets with the three thresholds in puncta. A pixel below puncta[j] gets sorted color j, otherwise it gets the brightest. So griseum can reach at most IV colors: with the six-color griseum preset, colors 3 and 4 are never emitted. Its error is measured against an evenly spaced target (bucket * 255 / (n - 1)), not the chosen color's real luminance. Both spread 6/8 of the error over six neighbours (Atkinson).
    symbolum dithering_atkinson_colorum dithering_atkinson_griseum
  5. possumnecan I pass NIHIL for puncta to get the default thresholds {64, 128, 192}?
    No. The header's "default" is only the recommended value: the code reads puncta[0..2] without checking for NIHIL, so NIHIL crashes whenever two or more colors are active. Pass your own array, as importatio_visus does ({LXIV, CXXVIII, CXCII}). The thresholds should rise, because the first one that the pixel is below wins.
    symbolum dithering_atkinson_griseum importatio_visus_creare
  6. quomodohow much memory does a dither call take, and how do I re-dither repeatedly without the piscina growing?
    Each call allocates the index buffer (latitudo * altitudo bytes) plus scratch in the same piscina, and frees nothing. colorum's scratch is three rows of s16 RGB error (latitudo * 18 bytes). griseum's is a whole-image s32 luminance buffer (latitudo * altitudo * 4 bytes). The scratch sits after the indices, so you cannot roll it back alone. For interactive re-dithering, use a dedicated piscina and piscina_vacare it (or take a mark with piscina_notare and piscina_reficere) before each call, once the previous indices are no longer needed.
    symbolum dithering_atkinson_colorum dithering_atkinson_griseum piscina_vacare piscina_notare piscina_reficere
  7. quidwhat is in AQUINAS_PALETTE and how do I read a color's RGB safely?
    AQUINAS_PALETTE is XVI rows of {r, g, b} as i8 (unsigned character in this house, so values read 0-255 without sign trouble), and AQUINAS_NOMINA holds the Latin name of each entry for debugging. dithering_color_ex_indice(index, &r, &g, &b) copies one entry, and any output pointer may be NIHIL. It silently clamps an out-of-range index to 0 or XV, so a bad index returns black or warm gray instead of failing.
    symbolum AQUINAS_PALETTE AQUINAS_NOMINA dithering_color_ex_indice AQUINAS_COLORUM_NUMERUS
  8. possumnecan I dither a sub-rectangle or an image whose rows have padding (a stride)?
    No. Neither function takes a stride: rgba must be latitudo * altitudo pixels packed row after row, 4 bytes each, with pixel (x, y) at byte (y * latitudo + x) * 4. To dither part of an image or a padded buffer, copy the pixels into a packed buffer first. The result is packed the same way: one index byte per pixel, row after row.
    symbolum dithering_atkinson_colorum dithering_atkinson_griseum DitheringFructus

entitas

include/entitas.h · lib/entitas.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I create an entity and set and read its properties?
    entitas_creare(piscina, id, genus) with chorda* that are already interned, then entitas_proprietas_ponere(e, clavis, valor) (an existing key is overwritten and its parse cache reset) and entitas_proprietas_capere(e, clavis), which returns the stored chorda* or NIHIL. Lookup is a linear scan. The returned value is a chorda, so it is NOT NUL-terminated.
    symbolum entitas_creare entitas_proprietas_ponere entitas_proprietas_capere chorda_internare
  2. curwhy does entitas_proprietas_capere return NIHIL for a key I know is set?
    Keys, tags, relation ids and relation kinds are compared by POINTER, not by content: the library assumes every chorda* came from the same InternamentumChorda. A key built with chorda_ex_literis, or interned in a different table, never matches. Intern it with chorda_internare or chorda_internare_ex_literis in the table that interned the stored key. Only entitas_titulum_capere and entitas_nota_cum_praefixo_habet compare by content.
    symbolum entitas_proprietas_capere entitas_nota_habet chorda_internare chorda_internare_ex_literis
  3. quomodohow do I read a property as a number, boolean or timestamp?
    Use the typed accessors: they parse the text on demand, cache the result in the Proprietas, and return FALSUM if the key is missing or the text does not parse. b32 accepts true, false, 1, 0, verum and falsum (exact case). tempus accepts only a raw millisecond integer (no ISO 8601 yet). Known limits: the s32 and f64 parsers copy at most 63 and 127 bytes before strtol/strtod, so a longer text is judged on its first bytes only; the s64 parser rejects the most negative s64, -9223372036854775808, as overflow.
    symbolum entitas_proprietas_capere_s32 entitas_proprietas_capere_s64 entitas_proprietas_capere_f64 entitas_proprietas_capere_b32 entitas_proprietas_capere_tempus
  4. possumnecan I store binary data in an entity property?
    Yes: entitas_proprietas_ponere_blobum gzips the bytes with flatura into the piscina you pass, and entitas_proprietas_capere_blobum inflates them into a piscina. entitas_proprietas_capere on that key returns the COMPRESSED bytes. Empty data (mensura 0) is refused. The blob mark is stored in the same typus_literalis field the parse cache uses: setting the key again with entitas_proprietas_ponere, or calling proprietas_parsare_ut_typum(prop, TYPUS_CHORDA or TYPUS_NIHIL) on it, overwrites the mark, and capere_blobum then returns FALSUM.
    symbolum entitas_proprietas_ponere_blobum entitas_proprietas_capere_blobum entitas_proprietas_capere
  5. quomodohow do I add a relation to another entity and list relations of one kind?
    entitas_relatio_addere gives the relation a fresh UUIDv7 id (interned); entitas_relatio_addere_cum_id takes an existing id when replaying events. entitas_relationes_generis_capere returns a NEW Xar of Relatio VALUES (copies, read with (Relatio*)xar_obtinere), and an empty Xar when none match - despite its header comment, which says Xar of Relatio* and NIHIL when none.
    symbolum entitas_relatio_addere entitas_relatio_addere_cum_id entitas_relationes_generis_capere Relatio
  6. possumnecan I rely on the order of properties, relations or tags after a delete?
    No. All three deletes are swap-and-pop: the last element is copied into the deleted slot and the Xar is truncated. Order is insertion order only until the first delete. A Proprietas* or Relatio* you held to the deleted slot now sees the moved element, and one to the old last element points past xar_numerus. Adding never moves elements (Xar segments are stable).
    symbolum entitas_proprietas_delere entitas_relatio_delere entitas_nota_delere
  7. quomodohow do I check whether an entity has any tag in a namespace like "#project::"?
    entitas_nota_cum_praefixo_habet(e, "#project::") compares the bytes of every tag against a C string prefix, so it needs no interning. entitas_nota_habet tests one exact tag by pointer, and entitas_nota_addere does nothing (and returns VERUM) if that pointer is already there.
    symbolum entitas_nota_cum_praefixo_habet entitas_nota_addere entitas_nota_habet
  8. quomodohow do I get a display name for an entity?
    entitas_titulum_capere returns the value of the property "name", else "title", else the entity id. These two keys are matched by CONTENT, unlike every other lookup. It returns the stored chorda* itself, not a copy.
    symbolum entitas_titulum_capere
  9. quomodohow do I see which type a property was last parsed as, or parse it by a type name?
    entitas_proprietas_capere_plena returns the Proprietas* itself: typus_literalis and parsitus_validus tell what the cache holds (TYPUS_NIHIL and FALSUM after a fresh entitas_proprietas_ponere; TYPUS_BLOBUM for a blob). To parse by name, map the text with typus_literalis_ex_chorda ("chorda", "s32", "s64", "f64", "b32", "tempus", and also "blobum", which the header omits; anything else gives TYPUS_NIHIL) and call proprietas_parsare_ut_typum. It caches on success and returns FALSUM on a parse failure, and also for TYPUS_BLOBUM, which it does not handle.
    symbolum entitas_proprietas_capere_plena Proprietas proprietas_parsare_ut_typum typus_literalis_ex_chorda

eventus_stml

include/eventus_stml.h · lib/eventus_stml.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I save a sequence of input events to a file and replay it later?
    Collect the events in a Xar of Eventus and call eventus_scribere_stml(eventus, piscina, intern, pulchrum): it returns one STML document as a chorda, an <eventus_index> with one <eventus genus=".." tempus=".."/> per event. eventus_legere_stml parses such a document back into a new Xar of Eventus allocated in your piscina. pulchrum VERUM indents the output; FALSUM gives one line, which is what tools/auscultator.c prints per event. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): the replay is not exact for a mouse or pen event with pressio exactly 0, which is not written and reads back as EVENTUS_PRESSIO_IGNOTA (-1).
    symbolum eventus_scribere_stml eventus_legere_stml · via tools/auscultator.c probationes/probatio_iteratio.c
  2. possumnecan I pass the chorda I read from a file straight to eventus_legere_stml?
    No: both readers take a NUL-terminated constans character*, and a chorda is not NUL-terminated. Convert first with chorda_ut_cstr(fons, piscina), as probatio_iteratio.c does. They return NIHIL only when the STML does not parse or has no root element.
    symbolum eventus_legere_stml eventus_notata_legere_stml chorda_ut_cstr · via probationes/probatio_iteratio.c
  3. quidwhat happens to an event whose genus name the reader does not know?
    It is still read, as genus EVENTUS_NIHIL: eventus_genus_ex_titulo maps every unknown title to EVENTUS_NIHIL, and the reader turns every child element of the root into one Eventus, whatever its tag. In the other direction eventus_genus_titulus returns "ignotum" for a genus beyond the title table, which reads back as EVENTUS_NIHIL. The table follows the order of eventus_genus_t in eventus.h, so new genera must be added at the end of both together.
    symbolum eventus_genus_ex_titulo eventus_genus_titulus eventus_legere_stml
  4. possumnecan tempus values beyond the s32 range survive a round trip?
    Yes, up to 2^53. tempus is s64 and is written through f64 with no decimals; an earlier draft used chorda_ex_s32, which truncated after about 24 days of uptime. The test plants a value beyond s32 and reads it back.
    symbolum eventus_scribere_stml eventus_legere_stml · via lib/eventus_stml.worklog.md
  5. curwhy are some attributes missing from the written events?
    Attributes added after the first format are written ONLY when they differ from their default (indicator, indicator_genus, pressio, runa, codex, actio, and the wheel's dx/dy/genus_rotulae, x/y and modificantes), so old replay files are rewritten byte for byte. The reader supplies the default for each absent attribute: 0 for numbers, the first title for enums, and EVENTUS_PRESSIO_IGNOTA (-1) for a missing pressio. So a pressio of exactly 0, although valid (the range is 0..M), is not written and reads back as -1: that round trip is lossy.
    symbolum eventus_scribere_stml · via probationes/probatio_eventus_stml.c
  6. possumnecan I still read old replay files that use typus, delta_x and delta_y?
    Yes. For a key event the reader takes producta, and falls back to the old typus attribute (the ASCII code) when producta is absent. For a wheel event it takes dx/dy, and falls back to rounding the old f32 delta_x/delta_y to the nearest integer (halves away from zero). The writer only produces producta and dx/dy, so the example in the header comment, which still shows typus and delta_x, is the old format.
    symbolum eventus_legere_stml
  7. quomodohow is text with quotes, newlines or control bytes stored in an attribute?
    The text fields (contentum of a text event, viae of a drop event) are percent-escaped: '%', '"' and bytes below 0x20 or equal to 0x7F become %XX in upper-case hex; everything else, UTF-8 included, is kept verbatim. This is needed because an STML attribute value cannot contain a double quote. On reading, the text is unescaped into a fresh copy in the piscina.
    symbolum eventus_scribere_stml eventus_legere_stml
  8. quomodohow do I record which widget each event was aimed at?
    Use the notata pair with a Xar of EventusNotatum: an Eventus plus scopus (the target component's id) and scopus_x/scopus_y (the point in its space). The scopus attributes are written only when scopus is non-empty, so a file without targets is identical to a plain one, and a plain file reads as notata with empty scopus. The scopus chorda read back is the attribute value itself (the code comments it as interned), not a fresh copy.
    symbolum eventus_notata_scribere_stml eventus_notata_legere_stml EventusNotatum · via probationes/probatio_notarius.c
  9. quomodohow do I convert a physical key code to a name like "KeyA"?
    eventus_codex_titulus returns the W3C name ("KeyA", "ArrowLeft", "F12", "CapsLock"), and "Unidentified" for EVENTUS_CODEX_IGNOTUS or any code past the table. eventus_codex_ex_titulo maps a name back, giving EVENTUS_CODEX_IGNOTUS for an unknown one. Key events store the code as that name in the codex attribute, omitted when IGNOTUS.
    symbolum eventus_codex_titulus eventus_codex_ex_titulo EventusCodex

exemplaria

include/exemplaria.h · lib/exemplaria.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I test whether a pixel of a classic Mac 8x8 pattern is painted?
    Call exemplar_punctum(id, x, y): it tiles the pattern over the whole plane (x and y are taken modulo VIII with & VII, so negative coordinates wrap too: -1 is column 7) and returns VERUM where the pattern paints. To get the raw rows instead, exemplar_obtinere(id) returns the eight bytes.
    symbolum exemplar_punctum exemplar_obtinere
  2. quidwhat is the bit layout of the eight bytes exemplar_obtinere returns?
    One byte per row, row 0 first; inside a byte the HIGH bit (0x80) is x 0 and the low bit is x 7. A set bit means painted (the foreground colour). exemplar_punctum reads exactly e[y & VII] & (I << (VII - (x & VII))).
    symbolum exemplar_obtinere exemplar_punctum
  3. possumnecan I pass any number as a pattern id?
    Yes, it is checked: an id at or above EXEMPLAR_NUMERUS (38 patterns, 0 to 37) makes exemplar_obtinere return NIHIL and exemplar_punctum return FALSUM (so a bad id paints nothing rather than crashing). The id is i32, which is unsigned, so there is no negative case. Callers that memcpy the bytes must check for NIHIL first.
    symbolum exemplar_obtinere exemplar_punctum exemplar_internum_t
  4. quidwhich pattern ids are solid black and plain white?
    EXEMPLAR_SOLIDUS (0) has every bit set, so everything is painted; EXEMPLAR_ALBUS (19) has none set. They are not at the two ends of the table: the order follows the classic Mac pattern palette, not darkness.
    symbolum exemplar_internum_t exemplar_obtinere
  5. curwhy is the pattern table its own library instead of living in delineare?
    It was moved verbatim out of lib/delineare.c on 2026-10-09 so the command rasterizers (the vicus focus indicator) could use the same patterns without the legacy painter. delineare still reads it through exemplar_obtinere. Adding a library the legacy painter uses means updating three hand-written or generated build lists (ludus_tessera and tessera compile_probationes.sh, tessera/musivum_fontes_generata.sh).
    symbolum exemplar_obtinere exemplar_punctum · via lib/exemplaria.worklog.md

fabrica

include/fabrica.h · lib/fabrica.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I check whether generated files and installed binaries are up to date?
    Run bin/fabrica iudicare (quick) or bin/fabrica iudicare -plenus (full) from the repository root, optionally naming artifacts. Each artifact gets RECENS, STALUM, IGNOTUM, NON IUDICATUM or FORTASSE with a cause. Exit 0 = all fine, 1 = something STALUM, IGNOTUM or FORTASSE, 2 = nothing judged (an unknown flag, declarations missing or broken, or no artifact matched). Quick mode skips regeneration unless the action is celer="verum", so many generated files say NON IUDICATUM; that is a chosen mode, not a failure, and does not affect the exit code.
    symbolum fabrica_iudicare FabricaStatus · imperium bin/fabrica iudicare -plenus
  2. quidwhat does fabrica mean by "stale", and does it ever look at mtimes?
    Never mtimes and never "the file exists". An artifact is stale when it is not what its declared inputs produce TODAY. The input digest is, per input in path order, "path NUL sha256(bytes)". A manifest contributes the files it lists, not itself (it carries timestamps). A directory contributes its sorted name list under "path/" so it cannot collide with a file of the same path. The build/fabrica/provenientia/ directory is left out (fabrica_actionem_sigillare).
    symbolum fabrica_iudicare fabrica_actionem_sigillare fabrica_ingressus_sigillare
  3. quidwhat are the provenientia strategies (regeneratio, relatio, ignota, verdictum)?
    How freshness is decided per output. regeneratio: re-run the generator into an empty scratch directory and compare the bytes with the committed file. relatio: ask the installed binary for -provenientia and compare its "ingressus HEX" line with today's input digest. ignota: never judged, only realized as a precondition (cannot be a composite part or another action's input). verdictum: a gate's verdict file plus its recorded read trace, never re-run by the judge. fabrica_strategia_invenire looks them up by name.
    symbolum fabrica_strategia_invenire FabricaStrategia
  4. quomodohow do I declare a new generated file so fabrica judges and heals it?
    Add an <actio titulus genus="generator"> to the subsystem's aedificatio.stml (subsystems are listed in fabrica.stml) with a <mandatum> of <verbum! (> lines (ONE argument per line, because a raw capture eats to end of line), <ingressus genus via> for each input, and <exitus via provenientia="regeneratio"/>. Titles must be unique across all subsystems (scratch directories are per title). The reader refuses unknown kinds, missing title, input or output, with "via:linea" in the cause.
    symbolum fabrica_declarationes_legere_cum_sutura fabrica_subsystemata_legere · via aedificatio.stml aedificatio.canon fabrica.stml
  5. curwhy must my generator write into FABRICA_SCRIPTURA, and why is that directory emptied first?
    Under judgment the generator runs with FABRICA_SCRIPTURA set to build/fabrica/scriptura/TITULUS and must write its output there (at the exitus 'scriptura' path, default = the output path), not over the committed file. The seam empties it before every run: a mute generator (exit 0, writes nothing) would otherwise be compared with the previous run's leftovers and wrongly judged RECENS. Nothing written there = IGNOTUM "generator nihil scripsit" (worklog T1).
    symbolum fabrica_iudicare FabricaSutura · via lib/fabrica.worklog.md tools/fabrica_sutura.c
  6. quomodohow do I find out why an artifact is STALUM or IGNOTUM?
    Read the cause printed next to it (FabricaIudicium.causa): "regeneratio differt (lineae differentes: N)", "ingressus mutati post institutionem", "ingressus mutatus: a, b, c +N" (N as a Roman numeral) or "lectio transitus mutata: X" for gate verdicts, "generator fractus: ...", "ingressus build/ sine domino: X". FORTASSE means the artifact itself matches but something above it is stale; its cause names the upstream root ("supra: TITULUS STATUS (cause)"). FORTASSE is added by bin/fabrica's chain pass (_iudicare_catenam in tools/fabrica.c, worklog 2026-10-15), not by fabrica_iudicare, which judges an artifact against its direct inputs only; a library caller of fabrica_iudicare never sees it. bin/fabrica causae [titulus] shows why actions were run.
    symbolum FabricaIudicium fabrica_iudicare · via tools/fabrica.c lib/fabrica.worklog.md · imperium bin/fabrica causae
  7. quomodohow do I rebuild everything that is stale, in the right order?
    bin/fabrica sanare [artifact...] walks the dependency order over the selected artifacts and everything above them: judge now, run the action in place, judge again (SANATUM or FRACTUM; a dependent of a broken action is OMISSUM). -siccum only reports (AGENDUM, FORTASSE). The log of each run is build/fabrica/acta/TITULUS.log (fabrica_acta_via). An action that writes outside its declared vestigium is FRACTUM with "scripsit extra vestigium: ...". Gate actions (genus iudicium) are never part of a sweep: a plain bin/fabrica sanare skips them, they run only when named.
    symbolum fabrica_sanare FabricaEventus fabrica_acta_via · imperium bin/fabrica sanare -siccum
  8. quomodohow do I test code that uses fabrica without touching the disk or running processes?
    The library touches disk and processes only through a FabricaSutura. Call fabrica_suturam_parare first (it sets EVERY member to NIHIL), then fill in what you provide; most members are optional and documented "NIHIL licet". probationes/fabrica_mundus_fictus.c is the in-memory world used by the tests; tools/fabrica_sutura.c is the real one. Skipping fabrica_suturam_parare on a stack sutura once left a garbage pointer in a newly added member (Bus error).
    symbolum fabrica_suturam_parare FabricaSutura · via probationes/fabrica_mundus_fictus.c tools/fabrica_sutura.c
  9. quidwhat are memorabilis and lectiones, and how do they let the judge skip a regeneration?
    Both apply to regeneratio outputs, in quick mode too (they are checked before the quick-mode skip). lectiones="verum": the generator runs with FABRICA_LECTIONES (fabrica_liber_via: build/fabrica/lectiones/TITULUS.tsv) and records what it read; if every recorded read still has the same digest, the output is RECENS without running. memorabilis="verum": a stored verification under a key of input digest plus argv is reused. Both are written only after a regeneration that matched. Under -plenus with auditus set (iudicare -audit) a hit is regenerated anyway, and a mismatch is reported as AUDITUM DISCORS.
    symbolum fabrica_liber_via fabrica_iudicare
  10. possumnecan a generator with lectiones="verum" read files outside the tree and still be traced?
    Not for generators: when the read log is turned into a trace, paths under the tree root are made relative, and the remaining absolute paths (plus reads of the scratch directory, the read logs and the object store) are dropped, so a change to a file outside the tree never invalidates the trace. Declare such a file as an ingressus. Gate verdicts (verdictum) are stricter: absolute reads are kept except system and temporary roots, and reading a file under build/ that no declared action produces makes the trace unusable (IGNOTUM "ingressus build/ sine domino").
    symbolum fabrica_iudicare fabrica_liber_via
  11. quidwhat is a compositum and how is its verdict computed?
    A named flat list of parts (an artifact, all outputs of an action, or another composite) declared with <compositum> and <pars> elements; no expressions. fabrica_compositum_explicare flattens it (cycles, unknown parts and parts whose strategy is never judged are refused), and the verdict is the worst part: RECENS < NON_IUDICATUM < SUPRA < IGNOTUM < STALUM (fabrica_iudicia_coniungere), naming up to III of the worst artifacts and then "+N".
    symbolum fabrica_composita_legere fabrica_compositum_explicare fabrica_iudicia_coniungere fabrica_compositum_verdictum
  12. quidwhat is a gradus member, the "actio/membrum" names I see in verdicts?
    A gradus kind (probationes_c) expands one declared action into one synthetic action per member, titled "ACTIO/MEMBRUM", each with its own area build/fabrica/area/ACTIO/MEMBRUM/ (fabrica_area_via), a recorded read trace and a verdict file. The parent becomes a composite of its members (fabrica_gradus_composita), so a single changed test re-runs only that member. bin/fabrica probare COMPOSITUM [FILTRUM...] runs the stale members and reuses fresh ones; -vis re-runs every selected member.
    symbolum fabrica_gradus_explicare FabricaGradus fabrica_area_via fabrica_gradus_composita · imperium bin/fabrica probare -vis
  13. curwhy was a test member killed with "memoria maxima excessa"?
    fabrica runs every gradus member (link and run) with a whole-process memory cap: FABRICA_MEMORIA_MB from fabrica's own environment (not the member's), default FABRICA_MEMORIA_ORDINARIA_MB = 16 GiB, 0 = none. Over it the member gets SIGKILL with exit 137, and the cause names the footprint seen. For a sectioned credo suite the verdict also names the section that was running ("sectio 'X' RUIT"), and the sections before it keep their verdicts. The cap only stops runaways. A heavy section that finishes is judged by credo's own per-section ceiling (256 MiB, or 1024 with signum gravis).
    symbolum fabrica_sectiones_legere · via tools/fabrica_sutura.h

fasti

include/fasti.h · lib/fasti.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I get today's date and print it?
    fasti_dies_hodie returns a Dies and fasti_nunc a DiesHora, both read from the system clock through localtime, so they are LOCAL wall-clock time. Turn a Dies into text with fasti_formare_diem(dies, FASTI_FORMA_ISO, piscina), or append it to a builder with fasti_scribere_diem; the FASTI_FORMA_* constants name the styles (ISO, European, American, Latin long and short, liturgical, English long).
    symbolum fasti_dies_hodie fasti_nunc fasti_formare_diem fasti_scribere_diem
  2. possumnecan I pass fasti_nunc() to fasti_ad_iso or fasti_ad_unix to get the current UTC time?
    No: fasti_nunc is local time, while fasti_ad_unix and fasti_ad_iso treat the DiesHora they are given as UTC, so the result is off by your UTC offset and fasti_ad_iso still writes a 'Z'. fasti has no UTC "now"; take the epoch from mensura_momentum_nunc (milliseconds) and convert it: fasti_ex_unix(mensura_momentum_nunc() / M). Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): fasti_ad_unix and fasti_ex_unix currently take 1980-01-01 as their epoch (MCMX + LXX in lib/fasti.c, where MCMLXX was meant), so their results are ten years off until that is fixed.
    symbolum fasti_nunc fasti_ad_iso fasti_ad_unix fasti_ex_unix mensura_momentum_nunc
  3. quomodohow do I add days or months to a date, and how many days lie between two dates?
    fasti_addere_dies goes through the Julian day number (fasti_ad_iulianum), so any count crosses months and years correctly. fasti_addere_menses and fasti_addere_annos clamp the day to the target month: 31 January 2024 plus one month is 29 February 2024, and 29 February plus one year is 28 February. fasti_differentia(prima, secunda) returns an Intervallum whose dies_totales is prima minus secunda (positive when prima is later).
    symbolum fasti_addere_dies fasti_addere_menses fasti_addere_annos fasti_differentia fasti_ad_iulianum
  4. possumnecan I build a Dies like 30 February, and what does the library do with it?
    fasti_dies and fasti_hora never validate; call fasti_dies_valida and fasti_hora_valida yourself. Arithmetic simply runs the day through the Julian number, so 30 February 2024 plus zero days comes back as 1 March. Validate before formatting: the month-name styles of fasti_formare_diem index their name tables with mensis - 1 unchecked, so a month outside 1..12 reads past the table. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): validating is not enough for FASTI_FORMA_LITURGICA and FASTI_FORMA_ANGLICA_LONGA, because a valid Dies in year -4713 or earlier (negative Julian day number) gets a negative weekday and reads before the start of the weekday name table.
    symbolum fasti_dies fasti_hora fasti_dies_valida fasti_hora_valida fasti_formare_diem
  5. quomodohow do I parse or write an ISO-8601 date or timestamp?
    fasti_parsare_iso reads YYYY-MM-DD (four-digit year, optional leading '-') and validates the date; it reads only the first ten bytes (eleven with the sign), so trailing text such as "2024-03-15xyz" is not refused. fasti_ex_iso reads a full instant ending in 'Z' or a +HH:MM / -HH:MM offset and converts it to UTC; fractional seconds are refused on purpose. fasti_ad_iso writes the 20-byte YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ form and returns an empty chorda for invalid values or a year outside 0..9999. fasti_parsare_diem currently tries ISO only.
    symbolum fasti_parsare_iso fasti_ex_iso fasti_ad_iso fasti_parsare_diem
  6. possumnecan I read back the text that fasti_formare_diem wrote with FASTI_FORMA_ISO?
    Only when the year has exactly four digits (1000..9999, or -1000..-9999 with the sign). The ISO style writes the year as a plain number without zero padding, so 25 December 800 comes out as 800-12-25 and 15 March -44 as -44-03-15, and fasti_parsare_iso refuses both because it wants exactly four year digits; a five-digit year is refused too. For a fixed-width form that always round-trips (years 0..9999), use fasti_ad_iso with fasti_ex_iso.
    symbolum fasti_formare_diem fasti_parsare_iso fasti_ad_iso fasti_ex_iso
  7. quidwhat numbering do fasti use for weekdays, months, weeks and years?
    Weekdays run 0 = Sunday (FASTI_DOMINICA) to 6 = Saturday, the Roman and liturgical order, from fasti_dies_hebdomadis. Months are 1-indexed (FASTI_IANUARIUS = I). fasti_hebdomada_anni is the ISO week (Monday start, 1..53) and fasti_dies_anni the day of the year (1..366). Years are proleptic Gregorian with a year 0 (a leap year), i.e. astronomical numbering, so year 0 is 1 BC and -1 is 2 BC. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): for dates in year -4713 and earlier fasti_dies_hebdomadis returns -6..0 instead of 0..6, since it takes C's % of a negative Julian day number.
    symbolum fasti_dies_hebdomadis fasti_hebdomada_anni fasti_dies_anni fasti_est_bissextilis
  8. quomodohow do I compute the date of Easter and the moveable feasts?
    fasti_computus(annus) gives Easter Sunday by the anonymous Gregorian algorithm (Meeus, Jones, Butcher). Good Friday (-2), Ash Wednesday (-46), Palm Sunday (-7) and Pentecost (+49) are offsets from it, and fasti_nomen_festivitatis returns an English name for those five days or NIHIL. For the full liturgical calendar (ranks, seasons, colours) use calendarium_liturgicum, which is built on fasti.
    symbolum fasti_computus fasti_feria_vi fasti_feria_iv_cinerum fasti_dominica_palmarum fasti_pentecoste fasti_nomen_festivitatis calendarium_obtinere_diem
  9. quidwhat are Momentum, MomentumSec, Mora and DiesIulianus?
    Signed s64 typedefs that carry a time unit. Momentum (a point) and Mora (a duration) are milliseconds, the house's canonical unit; MomentumSec and MoraSec are Unix seconds (fasti_ad_unix returns a MomentumSec); DiesIulianus and MoraDierum count days. Each typedef carries a contractus annotation, so the house checker reports mixing scales (the classic x1000 bug) as a type error. Point minus point is a duration of the same scale. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): the MomentumSec from fasti_ad_unix (and the input of fasti_ex_unix) is counted from 1980-01-01, not the Unix epoch 1970-01-01, so real Unix seconds are off by 3652 days.
    symbolum Momentum MomentumSec Mora DiesIulianus fasti_ad_unix fasti_ad_iulianum

figura

include/figura.h · lib/figura.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I make a widget draw itself?
    Register a FiguraFn for the widget's partes and a thema with figura_registrare, then call pingere on the logical tree. pingere opens one coetus per componens (fines, sectio, translatio, scala, provenientia = the componens id), calls the registered figura, then walks the children, so a parent always paints UNDER its children. The figura draws in the componens' own space: the origin is the top-left corner of its fines. figura_registrare returns FALSUM for a NIHIL fn or an already registered (partes, thema) pair; a componens with no figura still gets its coetus and its children are still painted.
    symbolum figura_registrare pingere FiguraFn
  2. quidwhat is the thema argument of figura_registrare and pingere, and does it pick the colours?
    It is only a key: an i32 that chooses which set of registered figurae draws the tree (probatio_figura registers figura_finium under thema I next to its thema ZEPHYRUM figura). The figura also receives it, but nothing here turns it into colours. A figura names colours as ColorMandati, normally through figura_color_thematis (genus COLOR_MANDATI_THEMA), and the rasterizer resolves them later in color_ex_mandato through thema_color, which reads the single process-wide semantic map of thema.h.
    symbolum pingere figura_color_thematis color_ex_mandato thema_color
  3. possumnecan a widget inside a mounted pane use figurae registered in the host registry?
    No. Lookup is strict per spatium: pingere uses the componens' effective spatium (the nearest ancestor that sets one, see componens_spatium), and figura_invenire_in_spatio only matches entries of that exact spatium, with no fallback to the host spatium. Merge the pane's own registry into the host with figura_registrum_miscere_in_spatio. Entries that already carry a spatium (nested panes) keep theirs. The spatium chorda is stored as given, not copied, so it must live as long as the registry (pass an interned one).
    symbolum figura_invenire_in_spatio figura_registrum_miscere_in_spatio componens_spatium
  4. possumnecan I merge two figure registries that both define the same (partes, thema)?
    No. Merging is all or nothing: if any entry of the source collides with an entry already in the target (same partes, thema and spatium), the merge returns FALSUM and adds nothing. Collisions among entries of the source itself are not checked. To rebuild a registry from scratch, figura_registrum_vacare empties it and keeps its memory.
    symbolum figura_registrum_miscere figura_registrum_miscere_in_spatio figura_registrum_vacare
  5. curwhy do the figura helpers copy text into the Mandata piscina before drawing it?
    mandata_textus stores the chorda you pass by reference, it does not copy the bytes. If the text lives in a temporary buffer, the command list points at stale memory by the time it is rasterized. Every text helper here (figura_globulum_pingere, figura_segmentum_pingere, figura_textum_audacem) first copies the text with chorda_transcribere into md->piscina. Do the same when you call mandata_textus yourself.
    symbolum mandata_textus chorda_transcribere figura_textum_audacem figura_globulum_pingere
  6. quomodohow do I draw a standard button, checkbox or segmented control in the house style?
    Use the V1 helpers: figura_globulum_pingere (button), figura_sagittam_pingere (arrow button, directio -1 left, +1 right), figura_segmentum_pingere (one segment, positio FIGURA_SEGMENTUM_PRIMUM, _MEDIUM or _ULTIMUM) and figura_capsam_pingere(md, x, y, factum) (a IX by IX checkbox at x, y, ticked when factum). For the first three, status is a mask of FIGURA_ELECTUM (filled with COLOR_SELECTION), FIGURA_UMBRA and FIGURA_OBSCURUM (dim text). With FIGURA_UMBRA the body shrinks by II pixels (a segment only in height, except _ULTIMUM) and the shadow stays inside the given fines, so nothing is painted outside the component. Text is centred assuming a 6x8 glyph per byte of the chorda.
    symbolum figura_globulum_pingere figura_sagittam_pingere figura_segmentum_pingere figura_capsam_pingere
  7. quomodohow do I paint a pane background with the focus indicator?
    Call figura_mensam_pingere(md, fines, focatum): it fills the fines with COLOR_SUPERFICIES and, when focatum is VERUM, lays the FIGURA_EXEMPLAR_FOCI pattern in COLOR_BORDER over it (surface first, pattern second). It takes a plain b32 so the figura stays pure; decide focus with motus_ramus_focatus before painting.
    symbolum figura_mensam_pingere motus_ramus_focatus FIGURA_EXEMPLAR_FOCI

filum

include/filum.h · lib/filum.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I read a whole file into memory?
    filum_legere_totum(via, piscina) returns a chorda allocated in the piscina, NOT NUL-terminated. On failure it returns mensura 0 and datum NIHIL, and filum_error_recens() names the step that failed ('stat fracta', 'fopen fracta', 'fread fracta - non omnia legere'). An EMPTY file also comes back as mensura 0 and datum NIHIL, and today it even goes down the failure path (piscina_allocare of 0 bytes returns NIHIL): filum_error_recens() says 'piscina_allocare fracta' and the read is not recorded in the lectiones ledger. So test filum_existit first when 'absent' and 'empty' must be told apart. The length is stored as i32, so a file of 4 GiB or more is reported with a truncated mensura. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): on a FIFO with no writer both filum_existit and filum_legere_totum block indefinitely inside fopen (filum_status returns at once on the same path).
    symbolum filum_legere_totum filum_error_recens
  2. curwhy should I call filum instead of fopen, stat or open directly?
    Nearly every filum call records what it read, probed or wrote in the lectiones ledger (L, A, X, S lines), which is how fabrica learns a program's real inputs and outputs. (Gaps today: filum_copiare does not record its SOURCE, and an empty file read with filum_legere_totum is not recorded.) A new raw fopen, freopen, open, opendir, stat, lstat or access call is examen code 96 (CANALIS_PRAETERITUS) and is refused at commit unless it carries a tolera with a reason; files listed in canalis_debita.tsv predate the rule, and that list only shrinks.
    symbolum filum_legere_totum lectiones_notare · via project-specs/canalis-lectionum-spec.md silva/probationes/fixa/examinis/canalis_debita.tsv
  3. quomodohow do I read a file line by line?
    Open with filum_lector_aperire(via, piscina), loop while filum_lector_lineam_proximam(lector, &linea) is VERUM, and close with filum_lector_claudere (it closes the FILE; the lector itself stays in the piscina). Each line is copied into the piscina without its trailing '\n' and any trailing '\r'; lines longer than the 4096-byte buffer are joined. An empty line returns VERUM with mensura 0 and datum NIHIL, so loop on the return value, not on datum. filum_lector_numerus_versus gives the 1-based number of the line just read. Known bug: a '\r' that fgets leaves as the last byte of a full buffer is taken as a line end, so a CRLF straddling the 4095-byte boundary yields an extra empty line and shifts the count by one. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): a lone interior '\r' on that same boundary (4094 'A' + "\rX\n") is deleted and splits the line in two, and calling filum_lector_lineam_proximam after filum_lector_claudere crashes (SEGV in fgets) instead of returning FALSUM.
    symbolum filum_lector_aperire filum_lector_lineam_proximam filum_lector_numerus_versus filum_lector_claudere
  4. possumnecan the line reader handle NUL bytes inside a line?
    No. It reads with fgets and measures with strlen, so everything after an embedded NUL in that buffer is silently dropped. For binary or untrusted data read the whole file with filum_legere_totum and split the chorda yourself. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): the short strlen is also taken as 'line complete', so 10 'A' + NUL + 5000 'B' + "\nZ\n" comes back as three lines [10] [916] [Z]: the part of the line beyond the first fgets chunk becomes an extra line and numerus_versus is off.
    symbolum filum_lector_lineam_proximam filum_legere_totum
  5. quomodohow do I write or replace a file in one call?
    filum_scribere(via, chorda) creates or truncates the file and writes the bytes; filum_scribere_literis does the same for a C string. filum_appendere and filum_appendere_literis add to the end (creating the file if needed). All return FALSUM with filum_error_recens set on failure. A chorda with datum NIHIL is refused, even when mensura is 0, so writing an empty chorda from some APIs (json_ad_chorda, an empty line from filum_lector) leaves the old file in place; use filum_scribere_literis(via, "") to empty a file. Success is judged from fwrite only: the fclose result is ignored, so an error that surfaces when stdio flushes at close (disk full) still returns VERUM.
    symbolum filum_scribere filum_scribere_literis filum_appendere filum_appendere_literis
  6. quomodohow do I append a log line that is really on disk before I continue?
    filum_appendere_firmiter opens with O_APPEND|O_CREAT (mode 0644), does ONE write, then fsync, then close, and returns FALSUM if any of them fails. With O_APPEND every write lands at the current end of file, so records that several processes write this way are not interleaved (the header cites PIPE_BUF 4096, but that is Linux's figure and a pipe rule; macOS defines PIPE_BUF as 512). An empty chorda (even with datum NIHIL) creates the file and still fsyncs. filum_scriptor_sync is only fflush: it reaches the kernel, not the disk. Not available under _WIN32.
    symbolum filum_appendere_firmiter filum_scriptor_sync
  7. quomodohow do I write a file piece by piece?
    Open a FilumScriptor with filum_scriptor_aperire(via, FILUM_MODUS_CREARE or FILUM_MODUS_APPENDERE, piscina), call filum_scriptor_scribere / _scribere_literis / _lineam_scribere (adds '\n'), and always filum_scriptor_claudere at the end: the writer wraps a stdio FILE, so unflushed bytes are written at close and the handle leaks if you forget it. claudere returns nothing, so a flush error at close is lost: call filum_scriptor_sync first (FALSUM if fflush fails) when you must know the bytes left the process. Unlike filum_scribere, an empty chorda here is a no-op success.
    symbolum filum_scriptor_aperire filum_scriptor_scribere filum_scriptor_lineam_scribere filum_scriptor_sync filum_scriptor_claudere
  8. quomodohow do I create a nested directory path like mkdir -p?
    filum_directorium_creare_cum_parentibus creates every missing segment (mode 0755) and is idempotent. The other three creators make ONE level only: filum_directorium_creare fails if the directory exists, _si_necesse succeeds if it already exists (also under a concurrent creator), and _cum_modo sets the given mode and chmods an existing directory to it too (use it for 0700 secrets). _cum_parentibus does not roll back: segments created before a failure stay.
    symbolum filum_directorium_creare_cum_parentibus filum_directorium_creare_si_necesse filum_directorium_creare filum_directorium_creare_cum_modo
  9. possumnecan I delete a whole directory tree safely, without following symlinks?
    Yes: filum_arborem_delere is rm -r with lstat, so a symbolic link is removed as a link and its target is never touched. It refuses NIHIL, "" and the filesystem root by identity (so "//" and "/tmp/.." too), and an absent path is VERUM. filum_delere is remove(): one file or one EMPTY directory. POSIX only. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): a symlink to a directory spelled with a trailing slash ('nexus/') or as 'nexus/.' IS followed, because lstat resolves through it: the target's contents are deleted, and with 'nexus/' the target directory itself is removed and VERUM returned.
    symbolum filum_arborem_delere filum_delere
  10. possumnecan filum_copiare keep the executable bit when copying a binary?
    No: it is a plain byte copy through fopen "wb", so a new destination gets the default mode (0666 minus the umask, typically 0644) and an existing one keeps its own mode. Call filum_modum_ponere(via, 0755) afterwards, and check with filum_status, whose potest_exsequi reports the user +x bit. Also note that a read error on the source ends the copy like end of file and still returns VERUM. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): when source and destination are the same file (same path, 'd/./b.txt', a hardlink or a symlink to the source) the source is truncated to 0 bytes and VERUM is returned, and a directory as via_fons truncates the destination and returns VERUM with filum_error_recens NIHIL.
    symbolum filum_copiare filum_modum_ponere filum_status
  11. quomodohow do I hold an exclusive lock across read, check and append between processes?
    filum_seram_capere(via, mora_ms, piscina) takes an flock on the lock file (created if absent, never deleted), retrying every 10 ms until mora_ms runs out (0 = try once); NIHIL means busy or unopenable. Hold it for the whole read-check-append sequence and release with filum_seram_liberare (NIHIL-tolerant, repeatable). The kernel drops it when the process dies, and the descriptor is FD_CLOEXEC, so programs started with exec (processus_exsequi) do not inherit it. Two captures in the same process also exclude each other. It neither sets nor clears filum_error_recens.
    symbolum filum_seram_capere filum_seram_liberare
  12. quidwhat does filum_error_recens return, and when is it reliable?
    A static, process-global message (not thread-safe, do not free) for the last failure, or NIHIL. Most functions clear it on entry and set it on failure, but filum_existit, filum_mensura, filum_directorium_existit and filum_seram_capere never touch it, so after those it may still describe an older error. Read it immediately after the call that returned FALSUM. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): filum_legere_totum sets it to 'piscina_allocare fracta' on an empty file or /dev/null although nothing failed, and leaves it NIHIL when a file of 4 GiB or more comes back silently truncated.
    symbolum filum_error_recens filum_existit filum_mensura filum_seram_capere

flatura

include/flatura.h · lib/flatura.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I gzip a buffer and get it back?
    flatura_gzip_deflare(datum, mensura, FLATURA_COMPRESSIO_ORDINARIA, piscina) and flatura_gzip_inflare(datum, mensura, piscina). Both return a FlaturaFructus by value: check status == FLATURA_STATUS_OK before using datum and mensura; on failure datum is NIHIL and flatura_status_nuntium names the status. The output is allocated in the piscina you pass and lives as long as it does. Output bytes are binary, not a NUL-terminated string.
    symbolum flatura_gzip_deflare flatura_gzip_inflare flatura_status_nuntium
  2. quomodohow do I inflate zlib data (PNG, git objects) rather than gzip?
    There is no zlib wrapper here. Check and skip the 2-byte zlib header yourself (a preset dictionary, FDICT, cannot be handled), call flatura_inflare on the raw DEFLATE data, and check the Adler-32 in the last 4 bytes yourself: _zlib_inflare in lib/git.c does all three, and lib/imago_png.c is the writing side (flatura_deflare plus header and Adler-32). flatura_inflare stops at the final block and ignores bytes after it, but it does not tell you how many input bytes it consumed, so the Adler-32 can only be found when the stream ends exactly at the end of your buffer. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): on zlib data from another encoder where a stored block follows a dynamic-Huffman block, flatura_inflare can drop a byte of the stored block's LEN/NLEN and fail a valid PNG or git object with FLATURA_STATUS_FRACTA_DATUM (see the untrusted-sources answer below).
    symbolum flatura_inflare flatura_deflare · via lib/git.c lib/imago_png.c
  3. possumnecan I stream data through flatura, or tell truncated input from corrupt input?
    No. Each call takes the whole input and produces the whole output in one go. FLATURA_STATUS_INCOMPLETUS and FLATURA_STATUS_FRACTA_ALLOCATIO exist in the enum but are never returned (only flatura_status_nuntium names them): input that ends early comes back as an ordinary error, usually FLATURA_STATUS_FRACTA_DATUM (FLATURA_STATUS_FRACTA_DISTANTIA if it ends inside a distance code), exactly like garbage, and an allocation failure exits the process inside piscina_allocare. lib/git.c waits for INCOMPLETUS to grow its pack window, so that growth never happens.
    symbolum flatura_inflare flatura_gzip_inflare
  4. possumnecan I limit how much a decompression may produce?
    No: there is no output limit parameter. The output buffer starts at IV * MXXIV bytes and doubles, each old buffer staying in the arena, so a decompression bomb grows the piscina until piscina_allocare gives up and exits the process. A piscina from piscina_generare_certae_magnitudinis caps it, but the process still exits instead of getting an error.
    symbolum flatura_inflare piscina_allocare piscina_generare_certae_magnitudinis
  5. curwhy does flatura compress worse than gzip -9 even at level 9?
    flatura_deflare always writes a single block with the FIXED Huffman code (no dynamic Huffman tables). The level only changes how far the LZ77 search follows the hash chain: VIII candidates for level 1, CXXVIII for levels 2 to 8, IV * MXXIV for 9 and up. FLATURA_COMPRESSIO_NULLA writes stored blocks of at most 65535 bytes.
    symbolum flatura_deflare
  6. possumnecan I size an output buffer with flatura_maxima_magnitudo_compressa?
    Only for FLATURA_COMPRESSIO_NULLA. The bound assumes stored blocks (5 bytes per 65535 plus 18 for gzip framing), but the compressing levels use the fixed Huffman code, where bytes 144 to 255 cost 9 bits each, so incompressible input can exceed the bound by up to about an eighth of its size (1000 such bytes gzip to 1145 against a bound of 1023). Even with NULLA, empty input breaks it: the bound for 0 bytes is 18, but the empty stored block makes a 23-byte gzip file. You never need it for flatura itself, which allocates its own output.
    symbolum flatura_maxima_magnitudo_compressa flatura_deflare
  7. quomodohow do I compute a CRC-32 over data that arrives in pieces?
    Start with 0xFFFFFFFF, pass it through flatura_crc32_continuare for each piece, and XOR the final value with 0xFFFFFFFF. flatura_crc32 does all three for one buffer, so its result is already finalized: do not feed it back into flatura_crc32_continuare. It is the IEEE 802.3 CRC used by gzip and PNG.
    symbolum flatura_crc32_continuare flatura_crc32 · via lib/imago_png.c
  8. possumnecan flatura_gzip_inflare read multi-member gzip files or files with trailing bytes?
    No. It reads the CRC-32 and size from the LAST 8 bytes of the buffer and inflates only the first member, so a second member or any padding after the trailer makes the check fail with FLATURA_STATUS_GZIP_FRACTA. The header's optional name, comment and extra fields are skipped; the header CRC (FHCRC) is skipped, not checked. Input shorter than 18 bytes is refused.
    symbolum flatura_gzip_inflare
  9. possumnecan I trust flatura_inflare on deflate data from other encoders or from untrusted sources?
    Only with care. A back-reference distance is not checked against the bytes produced so far, so corrupt input that points before the start of the output still returns FLATURA_STATUS_OK with bytes from the unwritten window instead of FLATURA_STATUS_FRACTA_DISTANTIA. The extra-bit reads of the 16/17/18 repeat codes in a dynamic header are not checked for end of input. And a stored block discards the whole bit buffer to reach a byte boundary, which is only right when fewer than 8 bits are buffered. After a fixed block that always holds; after a dynamic-Huffman block whose end-of-block code is at least 4 bits shorter than its longest literal/length code, a whole byte of LEN/NLEN is dropped and a VALID stream fails with FLATURA_STATUS_FRACTA_DATUM. zlib rarely emits that shape (its end-of-block code is usually among the longest), but a hand-built stream that zlib itself decodes fails. flatura's own output never mixes the two (one fixed block, or only stored blocks).
    symbolum flatura_inflare

fons

include/fons.h · lib/fons.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I turn a Unicode code point into a glyph index for the 6x8 font?
    Decode the text with utf8_decodere and pass each code point to fons_codepoint_ad_glypham: ASCII and drawn Latin-1 slots map to themselves (U+00AD to its deliberately blank slot), U+00A0 becomes a space, curly and low-9 quotes, dashes, the ellipsis and the minus sign become their ASCII look-alikes, the bullet becomes slot 0x07, and Greek capitals that look Latin (and small omicron) become the Latin letter. Everything else, negative input included, is FONS_TOFU (0x7F). tabula_pixelorum_pingere_chordam does all of this for a whole chorda.
    symbolum fons_codepoint_ad_glypham utf8_decodere tabula_pixelorum_pingere_chordam
  2. possumnecan I tell in advance whether a character will draw as tofu?
    For the 6x8 font, yes: fons_est_mappatum is true when the lookup is not FONS_TOFU. Because tofu is slot 0x7F, U+007F itself also reports as unmapped. There is no such predicate for the 8x16 font; compare fons_8x16_codepoint_ad_glypham with 0x7F yourself (U+2302 legitimately maps there too).
    symbolum fons_est_mappatum fons_codepoint_ad_glypham fons_8x16_codepoint_ad_glypham
  3. quidwhat are FONS_PARVUS and FONS_TITULI, and how big is a glyph?
    The two font ids a Mandatum.fons field can hold: FONS_PARVUS (0, the 6x8 HP 100LX + Alcuin face, the default) and FONS_TITULI (1, the 8x16 Nix8810 header face in CP437 order). fons_latitudo and fons_altitudo give the glyph cell in pixels (6x8 or 8x16); any unknown id is treated as FONS_PARVUS. Every glyph has the same advance, tofu included.
    symbolum fons_latitudo fons_altitudo Mandatum tabula_pixelorum_pingere_chordam_fonte
  4. curwhy does the 8x16 font need its own code point lookup instead of reusing the 6x8 one?
    The 8x16 glyphs stay in native CP437 order, while the 6x8 high slots are a hybrid (Latin-1 at its own code points). So the 8x16 lookup inverts the CP437 tables (0x80-0xFF and the 0x01-0x1F dingbats), maps Latin-1 letters CP437 lacks to their base letter (A grave becomes A), applies the same quote and dash substitutions, and returns 0x7F for anything else.
    symbolum fons_8x16_codepoint_ad_glypham fons_codepoint_ad_glypham · via lib/fons.worklog.md
  5. possumnecan the small font draw Greek or other non-Latin scripts?
    Only the Greek letters that look Latin (Alpha, Beta, Epsilon, ... and small omicron) are mapped, to the Latin letter; alpha, pi, Gamma and the rest are tofu in the 6x8 font. The 8x16 font also has the Greek letters CP437 carries (alpha, Gamma, pi, Sigma, ...). The terminal draws other symbols itself through glyphae_ductae.
    symbolum fons_codepoint_ad_glypham fons_8x16_codepoint_ad_glypham · via include/glyphae_ductae.h

fractio

include/fractio.h · lib/fractio.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I do exact arithmetic with fractions like 1/3?
    Build integers with fractio_ex_s64 and ratios with fractio_ex_s64_s64(n, d, piscina, &f), which returns FALSUM for a zero denominator. fractio_adde, fractio_subtrahe and fractio_multiplica return the result directly; fractio_divide and fractio_inversa return b32 because the divisor may be zero. Every result is canonical (denominator positive, gcd 1), and fractio_ad_chordam prints "a" or "a/b". Numerator and denominator are Magnus, so nothing overflows and no floating point is used.
    symbolum fractio_ex_s64 fractio_ex_s64_s64 fractio_adde fractio_multiplica fractio_divide fractio_ad_chordam
  2. possumnecan I zero-initialize a Fractio with memset or a static declaration and get zero?
    No. All-zero bytes make 0/0, not 0, and it propagates silently (0/0 plus 1/2 is 0/0), unlike Magnus where zero bytes are a valid zero. Always create values with a fractio_ex_* function or an operation; zero is fractio_ex_s64(0).
    symbolum fractio_ex_s64
  3. quomodohow do I compare two fractions?
    fractio_aequalis compares the parts, which is exact because the form is canonical, and needs no piscina. For order, fractio_compara(a, b, piscina) returns -1, 0 or +1 by cross-multiplying (no division), so it allocates for large values. fractio_signum gives the sign and fractio_est_integra is VERUM when the denominator is 1.
    symbolum fractio_aequalis fractio_compara fractio_signum fractio_est_integra
  4. quomodohow do I parse a fraction from text?
    fractio_ex_chorda accepts "a" or "a/b" in decimal, with a '-' allowed only before the numerator and no spaces. Non-canonical input is reduced ("6/4" gives 3/2); "3/-4", "3/0" and malformed text return FALSUM and leave the output untouched.
    symbolum fractio_ex_chorda
  5. curwhy does fractio_rotunda round 5/2 to 2 instead of 3?
    It rounds halves to the even neighbour (1/2 to 0, 3/2 to 2, 5/2 to 2, -1/2 to 0), the IEEE default and what Python's round does. Exact rationals land exactly on halves often, so round-half-up would bias many roundings. For the schoolbook rule combine fractio_pavimentum (floor) and fractio_tectum (ceiling).
    symbolum fractio_rotunda fractio_pavimentum fractio_tectum · via lib/fractio.worklog.md
  6. possumnecan I reset the scratch piscina and keep a Fractio computed in it?
    Not without copying. Results may share limbs with their arguments and live in the piscina passed to the operation, so they are valid only while those piscinae live. fractio_transcribe(a, piscina) deep-copies numerator and denominator into the piscina you keep. Small values use magnus's inline fast path and often allocate nothing.
    symbolum fractio_transcribe magnus_transcribe
  7. possumnecan I raise a fraction to a negative power?
    Yes: fractio_potentia takes a signed exponent and computes a to the minus n as 1 over a to the n. It returns FALSUM only for a zero base with a negative exponent; 0 to the power 0 is 1.
    symbolum fractio_potentia fractio_inversa
  8. quomodohow do I get the numerator and denominator out as ordinary integers?
    The fields are private; read them with fractio_numerator and fractio_denominator (always positive), then convert each with magnus_ad_s64, which returns FALSUM when the value does not fit. There is no conversion to a floating-point number.
    symbolum fractio_numerator fractio_denominator magnus_ad_s64

friatio

include/friatio.h · lib/friatio.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I hash a string key for a hash table?
    friatio_fnv1a takes a chorda; friatio_fnv1a_literis takes a pointer and an explicit length (it does not look for a NUL, so pass strlen yourself). friatio_djb2 is the other choice. tabula_dispersa already wraps FNV-1a as tabula_friare_fnv1a, so a table needs no hashing of its own. Both are unseeded and the header marks them non-cryptographic, so whoever chooses the keys can force collisions.
    symbolum friatio_fnv1a friatio_fnv1a_literis friatio_djb2 tabula_friare_fnv1a
  2. quomodohow do I compute a SHA-256 over data that arrives in pieces?
    Call sha256_initiare on a SHA256Contextus, sha256_addere once per piece (any sizes), then sha256_finire to get the 32 raw bytes (SHA256_DIGEST_MENSURA). sha256_friare does all three for one buffer. finire does not reset the context: call initiare again before reusing it. SHA-1 (sha1_*) has the same shape. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): sha256_addere and sha1_addere write past the context buffer (heap overflow, SEGV) when a piece of 2^32 - j bytes or more arrives while j = 1..63 bytes are already buffered, and an empty piece with a NIHIL pointer (e.g. sha256_friare_chorda or sha1_friare_chorda on the empty chorda {0, NIHIL}) is undefined behavior that UBSan flags, though the digest is correct.
    symbolum sha256_initiare sha256_addere sha256_finire sha256_friare
  3. possumnecan I get a hex string or a constant-time comparison from friatio's SHA-256?
    No: friatio only fills a raw i8 digest array, and it has no HMAC. sigillum.h is a second SHA-256 that does: sigillum_computare returns a Sigillum, sigillum_hex writes 64 lowercase hex characters plus a NUL, sigillum_aequale compares in constant time, sigillum_hmac does HMAC-SHA256, and its lengths are memoriae_index (sha256_addere takes an i32 per call). Prefer sigillum where the hash is an identity or guards a secret.
    symbolum sha256_finire sigillum_computare sigillum_hex sigillum_aequale sigillum_hmac
  4. curwhy are there two SHA-256 implementations, friatio and sigillum?
    They serve different jobs, as the sigillum.h header explains: friatio hashes to spread keys over a table, while sigillum SEALS, a digest that is a content identity (tabularium entities, CAS) where collision resistance carries the weight. Hence sigillum's Sigillum type, hex output, constant-time sigillum_aequale and HMAC. friatio.h does not point to sigillum, so a new caller has to know it exists.
    symbolum sha256_friare sigillum_computare · via include/sigillum.h
  5. possumnecan I use sha1_friare for anything security related?
    No. The header marks SHA-1 obsolete for security; it is here for legacy checksums and for UUIDv5, which the standard defines with SHA-1 (lib/uuid.c is its consumer). Use SHA-256 for anything new.
    symbolum sha1_friare sha1_finire uuidv5_creare
  6. quomodohow do I compute a CRC32 incrementally over a stream?
    crc = crc32_initiare(); then crc = crc32_addere(crc, data, n) for each piece; finally crc32_finire(crc) inverts the bits. Feed addere the running value, never the finished one. crc32_calculare does it in one call. The polynomial is the IEEE one (0xEDB88320, reflected), as in ZIP, PNG and Ethernet.
    symbolum crc32_initiare crc32_addere crc32_finire crc32_calculare
  7. quidwhat type do the friatio functions return, signed or unsigned?
    Unsigned: i32 is unsigned in this house (s32 is the signed one), so the 32-bit results wrap with defined arithmetic and print with %u or 0x%08X. Digests are arrays of i8, which is unsigned char. The input bytes are hashed as unsigned too, so results do not depend on whether the platform's char is signed.
    symbolum friatio_fnv1a crc32_calculare sha256_friare

historia

include/historia.h · lib/historia.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I give a document undo and redo backed by an append-only log?
    Fill a HistoriaProiectio (memset it first, then memoria + mensura of a fixed-size state you own, plus vacare to reset it and applicare to apply one act, and ctx for both), and call historia_creare(piscina, intern, volumen, spatium, genus, origo_checkpoint, intervallum, proiectio) - scriba passes "" as spatium, "mutatio" as genus and "scriba:checkpoint" as origin. It returns NIHIL if a required argument or hook is missing. Each edit is historia_actum(h, act) with the act as a chorda (it returns the act's seq, or 0 if refused); undo is historia_revocare, redo historia_reficere. The projection memory always shows the state at the cursor.
    symbolum historia_creare historia_actum historia_revocare historia_reficere HistoriaProiectio · via lib/scriba_documentum.c
