  2. quomodohow do I reopen a document from an existing volume?
    Use historia_aperire with the same spatium, genus and projection: it finds the last live act, rebuilds the projection from the newest live checkpoint plus the acts after it, and returns to the last saved undo position. historia_creare is only for a NEW history: it does not read the volume, so on a volume that already holds acts it starts at cursor 0 while the old acts stay live in the log.
    symbolum historia_aperire historia_creare
  3. quidwhat happens to undone acts when I make a new edit after undo?
    The log is never truncated. When the cursor is behind the end, historia_actum first appends a branch marker (ramus ab=cursor), and every act between that seq and the marker becomes dead: replays skip it and historia_reficere can no longer reach it. historia_finis then equals the new act's seq.
    symbolum historia_actum historia_reficere historia_finis
  4. quomodohow do I make one undo step cover several acts, like a debounced insert?
    Append the first act with historia_actum and each later chunk with historia_actum_coniunctum: it writes a coniunctio marker before the act, and revocare and reficere then move over the whole joined group in one step. With no live act before it, a joined act is just a plain act.
    symbolum historia_actum_coniunctum historia_revocare historia_reficere
  5. possumnecan several documents share one volume?
    Yes: give each a different spatium. With spatium s1 the act genus becomes s1/genus, the markers s1/ramus, s1/coniunctio and s1/cursor, and checkpoint keys s1/checkpoint/seq, and each history reads only its own. An empty (or NIHIL) spatium uses the bare names, which is what older volumes contain.
    symbolum historia_creare historia_aperire · via lib/historia.worklog.md
  6. possumnecan historia_verificare detect that my code scribbled on the projection memory?
    Only if the scribble was sealed. historia_verificare replays the log from nothing (no checkpoint) and compares the result with the sigillum recorded after the last act. A divergence that a later act or checkpoint sealed is caught. Memory changed AFTER the last seal is not detected: the replay matches the recorded seal and silently overwrites your change.
    symbolum historia_verificare historia_sigillum_hex
  7. quomodohow do I keep checkpoints small when my state is not one fixed block?
    Set all three hooks: sigillare (hash your raw canonical state; it runs after every act), codificare (the checkpoint bytes; length 0 skips the checkpoint) and decodificare (restore; FALSUM means unusable, e.g. an old format, and historia replays from nothing). Then memoria may be NIHIL and mensura 0. historia_creare returns NIHIL if only some of the three are set.
    symbolum HistoriaProiectio historia_creare · via lib/historia.worklog.md
  8. quidwhat does the intervallum argument control?
    A checkpoint is written when a NEW act (historia_actum or historia_actum_coniunctum) brings the number of live acts to a multiple of intervallum (0 means LXIV); undo and redo never write one. Checkpoints are found by listing the volume's files, not by seq arithmetic, because the volume interleaves its own bookkeeping acts; a checkpoint inside dead (undone and branched-over) history is never used as a base.
    symbolum historia_creare historia_numerus_vivorum
  9. curwhy does undo append to the log instead of only moving a cursor?
    So the undo position survives a reopen (insula-rami-plan R5): undo and redo append a cursor marker, and historia_aperire returns to the last one unless an act, a branch or a join marker came after it. Before this, undo then quit brought the undone text back.
    symbolum historia_revocare historia_reficere historia_aperire · via lib/historia.worklog.md
  10. possumnecan I keep a historia open for a long editing session without its piscina growing?
    No: everything is allocated from the piscina given to historia_creare or historia_aperire and never released. Every undo and every edit after undo re-reads the volume's acts from seq 0 into that piscina (undo also lists the volume's files to find a checkpoint), and every redo reads the acts after the cursor, so memory grows with log length times the number of undos. Use a piscina whose lifetime is the document's; to reclaim it, reopen with historia_aperire into a fresh piscina.
    symbolum historia_revocare historia_reficere historia_actum
  11. quomodohow do I get notified when the document changes?
    Register a callback with historia_observatorem_addere(h, fn, ctx); observers run in the order added after every successful historia_actum, historia_actum_coniunctum, historia_revocare and historia_reficere. A refused act (empty chorda, failed append) and historia_aperire do not call them.
    symbolum historia_observatorem_addere HistoriaObservator historia_actum_coniunctum

html_lexema

include/html_lexema.h · lib/html_lexema.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I tokenize an HTML document?
    Call html_lexare(piscina, fons, mensura). It returns a Xar of HtmlLexema stored BY VALUE (xar_obtinere gives an HtmlLexema*), always ending with one HTML_LEX_FINIS token whose valor is empty and whose offset is mensura. Each token has genus, valor (the exact bytes), a byte offset, and a 1-based linea and columna (columns count bytes). html_lexema_genus_nomen names a genus for debugging. It returns NIHIL only when memory runs out; the tree builder in html/fontes/html_arbor.c is the main consumer.
    symbolum html_lexare HtmlLexema html_lexema_genus_nomen · via html/fontes/html_arbor.c
  2. possumnecan I free the source buffer after lexing?
    No. The source is not copied: every valor points into fons, so the buffer must live as long as the tokens. fons need not be NUL-terminated (only mensura bytes are read), and the valor chordas are not NUL-terminated either. They are read-only views even though chorda.datum is non-const.
    symbolum html_lexare HtmlLexema
  3. quidwhat does the lexer do with malformed or truncated HTML?
    It never fails and never drops a byte. A construct cut off by EOF gets the matching _IMPERFECTUS/_IMPERFECTUM/_IMPERFECTA genus (an unclosed comment, doctype, CDATA, quoted value or raw text); anomalies such as '</>', '</3>', '<?x>' or '<!x>' become one HTML_LEX_COMMENTARIUM_PRAVUM token up to and including the first '>' (or to EOF); for '</>' the spec would emit nothing at all. A tag left open at EOF ('<div cl') gives complete tokens with no TAG_FINIS. A bare '<' or '&' that starts nothing stays in TEXTUS.
    symbolum html_lexare html_lexemata_emittere
  4. quomodohow do I check that the lexer did not lose or change any bytes?
    html_lexemata_emittere concatenates every valor into a new chorda in the piscina; for any input it equals the source byte for byte. The tokens tile the source because every lexer step consumes at least one byte. Note this oracle cannot see a wrong token boundary, such as a script cut short, which still concatenates to the same bytes.
    symbolum html_lexemata_emittere html_lexare
  5. possumnecan I get decoded attribute values and resolved character references from the lexer?
    No: everything stays verbatim. Tag and attribute names keep their original case, an attribute value token includes its quotes, and references are not decoded. HTML_LEX_REFERENTIA is split out only in data mode and only in the strict form '&name;', '&#dd;' or '&#xhh;' (the name or digits are scanned at most 64 bytes ahead); without the ';' it stays text. Inside attribute values and raw text the references remain part of the larger token. Decoding is the tree's job.
    symbolum html_lexare HtmlLexema
  6. quidwhat is raw text, and which elements switch the lexer into it?
    After the start tag of script, style, xmp, iframe, noembed, noframes (RAWTEXT) or title, textarea (RCDATA), everything up to the matching '</name' followed by space, '/' or '>' is ONE HTML_LEX_TEXTUS_CRUDUS token (case-insensitive; '</scripty' does not close). RAWTEXT and RCDATA are lexed the same; they differ only in decoding. Empty content gives no raw token at all, just the close tag. '<script/>' still enters raw mode, as in browsers. After '<plaintext>' the rest of the file is one raw token, PERFECT rather than imperfect, since no close tag exists.
    symbolum html_lexare · via lib/html_lexema.worklog.md
  7. curwhy does a script containing an HTML comment with a nested script tag not end at the first close tag?
    HTML5 keeps escape states inside script: '<!--' opens the escaped state, where a '<script' opens the double-escaped state in which '</script>' is just text, and '-->' returns. So '<script><!--<script></script></script>' gives one raw token that runs up to the last close tag. Before this was added (2026-09-15) the html5lib oracle showed such scripts cut short, which the byte-fidelity check cannot see; real pages carry '<!--' in scripts as a browser-hiding idiom. Only script has escape states: in style, title, textarea and the other raw elements the first matching close tag ends the text.
    symbolum html_lexare · via lib/html_lexema.worklog.md
  8. curwhy is <a href=/> not a self-closing tag?
    Inside a tag the lexer tests for an attribute value (after '=') BEFORE it tests for '/>'. So '<a href=/>' gives the value '/' and a normal TAG_FINIS, and '<a href=x/>' gives the value 'x/', because the unquoted-value loop stops only at a space or '>' ('/' is legal in an unquoted value). This matches the spec and browsers. Testing '/>' first would turn '<a href=/>' into a self-closing tag with no value; the 'x/' case rests on the value loop, not on the order of the checks.
    symbolum html_lexare · via lib/html_lexema.worklog.md
  9. quidwhat tokens does the inside of a tag produce?
    '<div' is HTML_LEX_TAG_APERTURA and '</div' HTML_LEX_TAG_CLAUSURA (the angle bracket plus the name). Then come SPATIA runs, ATTRIBUTUM_NOMEN, AEQUALE for '=', ATTRIBUTUM_VALOR (the token after an AEQUALE, spaces aside, is always a value unless it is '>', so 'b==c' gives AEQUALE then the value '=c'; quoted values may contain newlines; only EOF truncates them to ATTRIBUTUM_VALOR_IMPERFECTUS), DELIM for a stray '/', and finally TAG_FINIS '>' or TAG_FINIS_SOLUS '/>'. Close tags are lexed the same way, attributes included.
    symbolum html_lexare HtmlLexema

http

include/http.h · lib/http.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I make an HTTPS request and read the response body?
    Build the request with http_petitio_creare(piscina, HTTP_POST, url) (NIHIL if the URL has no "://"), add headers with http_petitio_caput_addere and a body with http_petitio_corpus_ponere (copied), then call http_exsequi(petitio, piscina). On res.successus, read res.responsum->status and res.responsum->corpus. Host, Content-Length (only when the body is not empty) and "Connection: close" are added for you. An https URL goes through tls.h; anything else is plain TCP. A request holds at most LXIV headers (http_petitio_caput_addere drops further ones silently), and the automatic Host header never carries a non-default port.
    symbolum http_petitio_creare http_petitio_caput_addere http_petitio_corpus_ponere http_exsequi HttpResultus
  2. possumnecan I treat res.successus as "the server said 200"?
    No. successus means the exchange worked and the response parsed: a 404 or 500 still comes back with successus VERUM, so check res.responsum->status yourself. When successus is FALSUM, res.responsum is NIHIL and res.error is the HttpError (CONNEXIO, TIMEOUT, IO, PARSE, URL, REDIRECTIO), with a description in res.error_descriptio; http_error_descriptio names the code.
    symbolum http_exsequi http_error_descriptio HttpError
  3. possumnecan I pass responsum->corpus.datum to a function that expects a C string?
    Not safely. Whether a NUL follows the body depends on the path: the Content-Length and read-to-EOF paths of http_exsequi copy it with a trailing NUL, but a chunked body and every body from http_exsequi_rivus are not guaranteed to be terminated, and an empty body has datum NIHIL. Use corpus.mensura, or chorda_ut_cstr when you really need a C string.
    symbolum http_exsequi http_exsequi_rivus chorda_ut_cstr
  4. quomodohow do I set a timeout on a request?
    http_petitio_tempus_ponere(petitio, ms) before executing; 0 keeps the default of XXX seconds. It applies to plain TCP and, since 2026-10-07, to TLS as well. On the plain path it becomes SO_RCVTIMEO and SO_SNDTIMEO on the socket after connect, so it bounds each wait for data, not the whole request (a server that trickles bytes never times out), and does not bound DNS or the connect itself. When it expires http_exsequi returns HTTP_ERROR_TIMEOUT and throws away any partial body on purpose: a truncated body presented as success is worse than an honest error. For the same reason a body shorter than its Content-Length, or a chunked body without the final 0 chunk, is HTTP_ERROR_IO (RFC 9112 6.3). http_exsequi always sends "Connection: close", reads until the server closes and has no size cap; http_exsequi_rivus stops at the end of the message instead.
    symbolum http_petitio_tempus_ponere http_exsequi · via lib/http.worklog.md
  5. quomodohow do I stream a response body as it arrives, for example a model's output?
    Call http_exsequi_rivus(petitio, rivus, datum, piscina) with an HttpRivus callback (datum, octeti, n). The callback receives the decoded body bytes (Content-Length, chunked or until close) as they arrive, but ONLY for a 2xx status; the whole body is also collected in responsum->corpus (up to HTTP_LECTOR_CORPUS_MAXIMUM, XVI * M * M bytes; past that the call fails with HTTP_ERROR_PARSE "corpus nimium"). It stops reading as soon as the message is complete instead of waiting for the server to close. Use HttpVecturaRivi and http_vectura_rivi_exsequi where tests must replace the network.
    symbolum http_exsequi_rivus HttpRivus http_vectura_rivi_exsequi
  6. possumnecan I use http_exsequi_rivus without a callback, as a drop-in for http_exsequi?
    Yes: pass NIHIL as the rivus and the reader only collects the body into responsum->corpus. The differences: it stops reading at the end of the message (http_exsequi waits for the server to close), it understands chunk extensions ("5;name=value"), which the older chunked decoder in http_exsequi misreads (it copies the extension bytes as data and can then report success with a wrong body), and it caps the body at XVI * M * M bytes where http_exsequi has no cap. Its body is not guaranteed to be NUL-terminated; malformed framing comes back as HTTP_ERROR_PARSE, a body cut short at EOF as HTTP_ERROR_IO.
    symbolum http_exsequi_rivus http_exsequi http_lector_creare
  7. quomodohow do I parse an HTTP response from bytes I read myself, for example from a reactor callback?
    Use the incremental reader, which does no I/O: http_lector_creare(piscina, methodus, rivus, datum), then feed each chunk to http_lector_adicere until it returns HTTP_LECTOR_FINITUS or HTTP_LECTOR_ERROR; chunk boundaries may fall anywhere. Call http_lector_finire at EOF (it finishes a read-until-close body, otherwise reports "corpus truncatum"). http_lector_responsum is NIHIL until the headers are complete; http_lector_error gives the reason. Limits: LXIV * M bytes of headers, XVI * M * M bytes of body; bytes after the end of the message are ignored. An interim 1xx response (100 Continue, 103 Early Hints) is taken as the final response: the reader finishes on it, so the real response that follows is ignored (http_exsequi likewise reports the first status line it sees).
    symbolum http_lector_creare http_lector_adicere http_lector_finire http_lector_responsum http_lector_error
  8. possumnecan I rely on http_exsequi_cum_redirectionibus to follow redirects?
    Not yet: it has no test and no caller. It follows only 301, 302, 307 and 308 (not 303); it keeps the method on 301 and 302 but drops the body; it copies every original header except Host to the new URL, Authorization included, even to another host; and the new request loses the timeout set with http_petitio_tempus_ponere. A one-character Location such as "/" also makes its absolute-URL scan run past the end of the string, and a relative Location that contains "://" anywhere (say "/login?next=https://x/") is taken as absolute, so the request goes to host x. Handle 3xx yourself with http_exsequi for now.
    symbolum http_exsequi_cum_redirectionibus http_exsequi
  9. quidwhat URL forms does http_petitio_creare understand?
    scheme://host[:port][/path?query]. The scheme is compared case-sensitively: only "https" gets TLS and port 443, every other scheme (including "HTTPS" and "ftp") gets plain TCP on port 80. There is no userinfo, no IPv6 literal and no check that the port is below 65536, and the path is sent as written (no escaping). The host ends only at ':' or '/', so "http://host?q=1" makes "host?q=1" the host: write "http://host/?q=1". http_petitio_visus shows how a URL was split.
    symbolum http_petitio_creare http_petitio_visus
  10. quomodohow do I test code that makes HTTP calls without touching the network?
    Take an HttpVectura {exsequi, datum} instead of calling http_exsequi directly, and send through http_vectura_exsequi. Production passes http_vectura_ordinaria() (which is http_exsequi); a test passes its own exsequi function that inspects the request with http_petitio_visus (method, scheme, host, port, path, body, headers, timeout) and returns a scripted HttpResultus. A vectura whose exsequi is NIHIL gives HTTP_ERROR_CONNEXIO, never a crash. herbarium wraps a vectura the same way.
    symbolum HttpVectura http_vectura_ordinaria http_vectura_exsequi http_petitio_visus
  11. quomodohow do I parse incoming requests on the server side?
    Create a parser with http_parser_creare_cum_limitibus(piscina, petitio_maxima, uri_maxima) (0 = M * M and VIII * M bytes) and feed each read to http_parser_adicere. While res.successus is VERUM and res.completa FALSUM, wait for more data; on completa read res.petitio (via, quaestio, capita, corpus, keep_alive). On failure answer with res.status_suggestus: 400 (bad line or version, duplicate Content-Length, Content-Length with Transfer-Encoding), 411 (chunked request bodies are refused), 413, 414, 501 (unknown method). Bare LF line ends are accepted. http_parser_reliquiae counts pipelined bytes left over; hospitium closes the connection after answering in that case. Stop feeding after the first failure: a later http_parser_adicere on a failed parser returns successus VERUM and completa FALSUM, and data fed after completa is dropped. Only the first LXIV headers are kept, and the Content-Length, Transfer-Encoding and Connection checks run only on those, so a Content-Length after the 64th header is ignored (no body, no 400).
    symbolum http_parser_creare_cum_limitibus http_parser_adicere HttpParseResultus http_parser_reliquiae
  12. curwhy does hospitium create a new parser for every request instead of calling http_parser_reset?
    Its parser lives in the connection's piscina, which is rolled back with piscina_reficere after each request; the parser's buffer and request struct would be dangling after the rollback. Note also that http_parser_reset keeps the limits but empties the buffer, so any pipelined bytes still in it are lost, and the reset reuses the same HttpPetitioServeri, overwriting a request you still hold.
    symbolum http_parser_reset http_parser_creare_cum_limitibus piscina_reficere · via lib/hospitium.c
  13. quomodohow do I serialize a response on the server, and who writes Content-Length?
    http_responsum_serialize_cum_conexione(responsum, keep_alive, piscina) adds "Connection: keep-alive" or "close"; http_responsum_serialize adds no Connection header. Both own the framing: they always write Content-Length (even 0) except for 1xx, 204 and 304, and skip any Content-Length or Connection header you put in capita. The reason phrase comes from http_status_descriptio(status), not from responsum->status_descriptio, so an unlisted code (206, 418, ...) goes out as "Unknown". A body you set is written even for 1xx, 204 and 304, where no Content-Length frames it, so leave corpus empty for those.
    symbolum http_responsum_serialize_cum_conexione http_responsum_serialize http_status_descriptio

imago

include/imago.h · lib/imago.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I load a PNG or JPEG file into pixels I can draw?
    Call imago_caricare_ex_file(via, piscina) (a NUL-terminated C path, not a chorda) or imago_caricare_ex_memoria(datum, mensura, piscina) and check fructus.successus before touching fructus.imago. The pixels are always 4 bytes per pixel, R, G, B, A, not premultiplied, rows top to bottom, whatever the file held (grey, palette and 16-bit images are converted to 8-bit RGBA). They live in your piscina; there is nothing to free.
    symbolum imago_caricare_ex_file imago_caricare_ex_memoria ImagoFructus Imago · via tools/icones_instrumentum.c
  2. quomodohow do I get the width and height of an image without decoding it?
    imago_mensuras_ex_file or imago_mensuras_ex_memoria read only the header (stbi_info) and allocate no pixels. They return FALSUM and set both outputs to 0 when the file is missing or the format unknown. Both output pointers are written unconditionally, so they must not be NIHIL.
    symbolum imago_mensuras_ex_file imago_mensuras_ex_memoria · via lib/imago.worklog.md
  3. quomodohow do I find out why an image failed to load?
    Read fructus.error, a chorda (not NUL-terminated) copied into the piscina you passed: stb_image's short English failure reason, or "Argumenta invalida" for a NIHIL path, NIHIL data or zero length. With a NIHIL piscina the error is an empty chorda, since there is nowhere to copy the text. On success error is empty.
    symbolum ImagoFructus imago_caricare_ex_file
  4. possumnecan I include imago.h just to use the Imago type without pulling in the decoder?
    Include imago_typus.h instead: it declares Imago alone. imago.h brings lib/imago.c and vendor/stb_image.h into the link closure of every program that includes it, which once dragged the decoder into every app through vitrea. Writers (imago_png) and comparers (imago_collatio) use the type header only.
    symbolum Imago · via include/imago_typus.h
  5. possumnecan a very large image make loading fail gracefully instead of exiting?
    Only if stb_image itself refuses it. The decoded buffer is malloc'd by stb_image and then copied into your piscina with piscina_allocare, which exits the process when it cannot get memory, so the "Non possum allocare memoriam" branch never returns in practice. Peak memory is about twice the RGBA size during the copy. Check the size first with imago_mensuras_ex_file.
    symbolum imago_caricare_ex_file piscina_allocare
  6. quomodohow do I save pixels as a PNG file?
    Not with this library, which only decodes. imago_png_scribere and imago_png_codificare write an Imago; imago_ex_tabula (delineare_mandata.h) views a TabulaPixelorum as an Imago without copying.
    symbolum imago_png_scribere imago_png_codificare imago_ex_tabula

imago_opus

include/imago_opus.h · lib/imago_opus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I resize an image to an exact width and height?
    Call imago_scalare(fons, latitudo, altitudo, modus, piscina). It returns a NEW Imago whose RGBA pixels live in the piscina you pass; the source is not touched. A NIHIL source, NIHIL pixels or a zero dimension give an empty Imago (pixela NIHIL, 0 x 0), so test .pixela before using the result. The source's own size is not checked, so do not pass a source with pixels but a zero width or height. An unknown modus falls back to nearest neighbour.
    symbolum imago_scalare ImagoScalaModus
  2. quomodohow do I choose between PROXIMUS, BILINEARIS and AREA?
    Use IMAGO_SCALA_AREA to shrink: it averages every source pixel under each output pixel, weighted by alpha. Bilinear reads only a 2 x 2 neighbourhood whatever the scale, so 1024 to 16 would sample 4 of 4096 pixels. Use BILINEARIS to enlarge smoothly (it is exact at 1:1) and PROXIMUS for fast or pixel-art scaling. pictor_documentum picks AREA when the new width is smaller, bilinear otherwise.
    symbolum imago_scalare ImagoScalaModus · via lib/pictor_documentum.c
  3. curwhy is AREA scaling alpha-weighted, and why does a transparent pixel not tint its neighbours?
    Imago pixels are RGBA and NOT premultiplied. A plain average would let invisible pixels pull their colour into the result (the halo around icon edges). AREA therefore averages alpha by coverage and averages RGB weighted by alpha times coverage, rounding half up: opaque red beside transparent white at 2:1 gives (255, 0, 0, 128). A block that is fully transparent comes out as all zeros.
    symbolum imago_scalare Imago · via lib/imago_opus.worklog.md include/imago_typus.h
  4. possumnecan I rely on imago_scalare_ad_limites filling the limit exactly in one dimension?
    No. The fit-within ratio is computed in 16-bit fixed point and truncated twice, so the side that should touch the limit is often one pixel short (a 1000 px crop asked for 16 gives 15; a 3 x 3 image fitted to 100 x 100 gives 99 x 99), and then the whole image is resampled. It also always scales, enlarging small images too. When the size must be exact, compute it yourself and call imago_scalare, as icones and pictor_documentum do.
    symbolum imago_scalare_ad_limites imago_extrahere_et_scalare imago_scalare · via lib/imago_opus.worklog.md
  5. quomodohow do I crop an image without resampling it?
    Use imago_excidere(fons, x, y, latitudo, altitudo, piscina): it copies the intersection of the region with the source, pixel for pixel, into a new Imago (a 5 x 5 region at (3, 3) of a 4 x 4 image gives 1 x 1). A region wholly outside gives an empty Imago. imago_extrahere_et_scalare always runs a resampler over the region (and fits it within limits), which is the reason specimen gives for cropping by hand.
    symbolum imago_excidere imago_extrahere_et_scalare · via lib/specimen.c
  6. possumnecan I alpha-blend one image onto another with imago_transcribere?
    No: it is a copy, not a composite. Destination pixels are REPLACED, alpha included, even where the source is fully transparent. dx and dy may be negative and every edge is clipped; an image wholly outside changes nothing. It uses memcpy per row, so do not pass the same Imago as source and destination (overlapping rows). Start a canvas with imago_creare, which fills one RGBA colour.
    symbolum imago_transcribere imago_creare
  7. quomodohow do I draw a rectangle outline or a filled rectangle into an Imago?
    imago_rectangulum(dest, x, y, latitudo, altitudo, crassitudo, r, g, b, a) draws the outline with its thickness INSIDE [x, x + latitudo) x [y, y + altitudo), clipped to the image; x and y may be negative. 0 draws nothing. A thickness from half the shorter side (rounded up) up to the shorter side fills it. Never go above the shorter side, whatever the header says: the bands are not clamped to the rectangle, so a thicker value paints outside it (w 4, h 20, thickness 5 paints columns x - 1 and x + 4).
    symbolum imago_rectangulum
  8. possumnecan these functions handle very large images?
    Only moderately. The AREA path computes in s64, but the nearest and bilinear paths index the source in s32 and wrap near a 23170 x 23170 source; buffer sizes are latitudo * altitudo * 4 in i32, which is UNSIGNED and wraps above 4 GB; and the fit-within paths multiply the limit by 65536 in s32, so limits must stay below 32768.
    symbolum imago_scalare imago_scalare_ad_limites imago_creare

imago_png

include/imago_png.h · lib/imago_png.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I save an Imago to a PNG file?
    Call imago_png_scribere(imago, via, piscina) with a NUL-terminated path and check successus on the PngFructus it returns. On failure error usually names the cause (for example Imago abest, Imago vacua, Via abest, Plagula aperiri non potest, Scriptio incompleta); it is a chorda, so print it with %.*s, not %s. On success datum and mensura are the bytes that were written, held in the piscina. Only the fwrite count is checked: the result of fclose is ignored, so an error that appears only when the file is flushed at close still reports success.
    symbolum imago_png_scribere PngFructus
  2. quomodohow do I get the PNG bytes in memory without touching the filesystem?
    imago_png_codificare(imago, piscina) is pure: no file is read or written. The bytes (datum, mensura) are allocated in the piscina and need no freeing; they live until the piscina is destroyed, emptied, or rolled back past them with piscina_reficere. imago_png_scribere is only this call followed by lectiones_fopen, one fwrite and fclose.
    symbolum imago_png_codificare piscina_reficere
  3. possumnecan I read or decode a PNG with imago_png?
    No, it only encodes. Decoding goes through imago_caricare_ex_file or imago_caricare_ex_memoria (lib/imago.c, vendored stb_image). The asymmetry is deliberate: the encoder writes one fixed layout, while a decoder must accept every bit depth, palette, interlace mode and filter type.
    symbolum imago_caricare_ex_file imago_caricare_ex_memoria
  4. quidwhat pixel layout does imago_png_codificare expect?
    An Imago: RGBA, IV bytes per pixel, NOT premultiplied, rows packed with no stride or padding (row y starts at pixela + y * latitudo * IV). There is no RGB or grayscale input; convert to RGBA first. imago_typus.h holds the type alone, so including imago_png.h does not pull in the decoder.
    symbolum imago_png_codificare Imago · via include/imago_typus.h
  5. curwhy are the PNG files larger than other encoders produce?
    Every row uses filter None, there is one IDAT and no interlacing. The filters PNG offers improve compression, mostly on photographic content; screenshots (flat regions) barely suffer. In exchange the code is small and the output is deterministic: the same image always gives the same bytes, which tests depend on. Adding filters later changes only the implementation, not the interface.
    symbolum imago_png_codificare · via lib/imago_png.worklog.md
  6. possumnecan I pass a NIHIL piscina or an empty image to the encoder?
    You get successus FALSUM either way. A NIHIL image or NIHIL pixela gives Imago abest, a zero latitudo or altitudo gives Imago vacua. A NIHIL piscina also gives successus FALSUM, but error is EMPTY, because there is nowhere to allocate the message, despite the header's promise that error is always named. The encoder's own buffers come from piscina_allocare, which is fatal on failure, so running out of memory exits the process and the Allocatio fracta messages in the code never appear.
    symbolum imago_png_codificare piscina_allocare
  7. possumnecan I encode a very large image (tens of thousands of pixels on a side)?
    Only while the raw scanline buffer, (1 + latitudo * IV) * altitudo bytes, stays well below 4 GiB (the compressed stream and the file size are i32 as well). Every size and offset is computed in i32 (unsigned 32 bit) with no overflow check, so a larger image wraps: for 32768 x 32769 the buffer comes out about 160 KiB and the row copy writes far past it. There is no refusal for oversized input; check the dimensions yourself before calling.
    symbolum imago_png_codificare

insula

include/insula.h · lib/insula.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I change a value in the insula state tree?
    Write an InsulaMutator and pass it to insula_mutare (whole tree) or mutare_ramum (one branch); inside it call insula_attributum_ponere(nodus, piscina, intern, titulus, valor) with the piscina and intern the mutator was given. The mutator runs on a DUPLICATE (the live tree written to text and parsed into the other piscina); owners and canons judge it, and only then does it replace the live tree, so a write is all or nothing. Use insula_attributum_ponere rather than stml_attributum_addere: the latter always appends, and stml_attributum_capere would keep returning the old value.
    symbolum insula_mutare mutare_ramum insula_attributum_ponere InsulaMutator
  2. possumnecan I change the tree through the pointer insula_radix gives me?
    No. insula_radix and insula_ramus_nodus are for reading only. The gate keeps the text of the last honest write; insula_restituere serializes the live tree, compares, and on a mismatch sets insula_mendacium, puts "mendacium: insula extra portam mutata" in insula_causa and rebuilds the tree from the honest text, discarding your change. mendacium is decided anew by each restituere, not sticky. Only restituere checks: the next gate write copies the live tree as it stands, so an out-of-gate change that reaches it first is carried into the new honest text unjudged.
    symbolum insula_radix insula_restituere insula_mendacium insula_causa
  3. possumnecan I keep a StmlNodus pointer from the insula across writes?
    Not safely. The tree lives in one of two alternating piscinas, and every write attempt (even a refused one) and every insula_restituere starts by emptying the piscina that is NOT live. A refused write leaves your pointer valid; an accepted write or a restituere moves the live tree to the other piscina, so your pointer then reads the stale old copy, and the next attempt or restituere empties it. Look nodes up again after each write. Attribute values are different: the chorda* from insula_attributum points into the interner and outlives the swap.
    symbolum insula_radix insula_mutare insula_restituere insula_attributum
  4. quomodohow do I find out why a write was refused?
    Read insula_causa(repo) right after a FALSUM return. It names the reason: an owner refusal ("dominus: X possidetur a ..."), a canon refusal with the canon's first vitium, "ramus abest" for a missing branch, or "mutatio extra ramum" when a branch mutator touched nodes outside its subtree. A successful write does NOT clear it, so a non-empty causa alone does not mean the last write failed.
    symbolum insula_causa mutare_ramum insula_mutare
  5. curwhy does every insula write serialize and re-parse the whole tree?
    The round trip through text IS the rehydration discipline: a tree that cannot survive being written and read back cannot be written at all, and the mutator working on a copy is what makes a refused write leave the live tree untouched. The worklog measured the cost (S8d, S8e): at size the time goes to owner and canon judging and to persistence, not to the copy.
    symbolum insula_mutare insula_restituere · via lib/insula.worklog.md
  6. quomodohow do I give an app or widget its own part of the tree?
    A branch: insula_ramus(repo, elementum, id) names the root's child <elementum id="id"/>, and insula_ramus_liberi(&parens, e, id) a child of another branch, at any depth. The path ("a:1/b:2") is interned, so a branch is a cheap value to copy. insula_ramum_initiare(&ramus, stml) adds the element under its parent if it is absent (the parent must exist), and mutare_ramum hands the mutator the branch's node in the duplicate and refuses changes outside it. insula_ramus_ex_via goes back from a path string to a branch.
    symbolum insula_ramus insula_ramus_liberi insula_ramum_initiare mutare_ramum insula_ramus_ex_via
  7. possumnecan insula_ramum_initiare create the branch from any element I pass?
    Only if the element's name and id match the branch. The function appends whatever element the STML holds and does not check it against the branch's elementum and id, so <b id="2"/> for branch "a:1" returns VERUM while insula_ramus_nodus stays NIHIL, and each later call appends another <b/>. A malformed STML string also commits an empty write (versio goes up) before returning FALSUM.
    symbolum insula_ramum_initiare insula_ramus_nodus
  8. quomodohow do I make an attribute writable only by one action or system?
    Register an owner with insula_dominum_ponere (root) or insula_ramus_dominum_ponere (branch), or read a <domini><dominus attributum scriptor systemata/></domini> table with insula_dominos_legere. A write that adds, changes or removes that attribute is refused unless the current scriptor (insula_scriptorem_ponere, set around the call; empty = anonymous) is the owner or one of the systemata. The usual idiom saves repo->scriptor, sets it, writes, and restores it. Register owners AFTER insula_ramum_initiare, or the anonymous initial write is refused.
    symbolum insula_dominum_ponere insula_ramus_dominum_ponere insula_dominos_legere insula_scriptorem_ponere
  9. quomodohow do I validate the tree's shape on every write?
    insula_ponere_canonem sets (and replaces) the root canon; insula_ramus_canonem_ponere gives a branch its own canon. Each canon judges its node minus descendant branches that have their own canon, and a write is judged by the nearest canon above its path plus the canons below it. Set a branch canon BEFORE initiating the branch. Note that insula_ramus_canonem_ponere appends: calling it again for the same branch does not replace the first canon, which keeps winning for writes through that branch, while a root write is judged by both.
    symbolum insula_ponere_canonem insula_ramus_canonem_ponere canon_iudicare
  10. possumnecan I set an attribute to the empty string?
    No. Values are interned and chorda_internare_ex_literis returns NIHIL for "", so the attribute is stored with a NIHIL value and insula_attributum reads NIHIL, as if absent. Worse, the STML writer emits a NIHIL value as a bare attribute name, which the parser reads as "true", so after the next round trip (the next gate write or insula_restituere) the attribute reads "true". Remove it with insula_attributum_tollere instead (swap-remove; attribute order is not meaningful).
    symbolum insula_attributum_ponere insula_attributum_tollere chorda_internare_ex_literis stml_scribere
  11. quomodohow do I persist or log every change to the tree?
    Register an InsulaActarius with insula_ponere_actarium: it is called after every successful write with the new root and the written path (empty = root, so anything may have changed). Nothing outside that path changed, so it may skip other parts. insula_scribere(repo, piscina) gives the whole tree as pretty STML in your piscina, and insula_versio counts accepted writes. An accepted write counts even if it changed nothing: InsulaMutator returns vacuum and cannot cancel, so decide before calling the gate.
    symbolum insula_ponere_actarium InsulaActarius insula_scribere insula_versio
  12. possumnecan I free an InsulaRepositorium when I am done with it?
    No. insula_repositorium_creare makes two private dynamic piscinas (insula_a and insula_b, XVI * M bytes initially) for the alternating trees, and there is no function that destroys them. It also returns NIHIL for malformed source STML, so the reason it set in causa cannot be read.
    symbolum insula_repositorium_creare piscina_generare_dynamicum

internamentum

include/internamentum.h · lib/internamentum.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I intern strings so I can compare them by pointer?
    chorda_internare (or chorda_internare_ex_literis for a C string) returns a chorda* to the canonical copy; interning the same bytes again through the SAME interner returns the same pointer, so two interned names are equal exactly when the pointers are equal. chorda_internare_ex_literis looks the literal up before allocating, so repeats cost nothing.
    symbolum chorda_internare chorda_internare_ex_literis internamentum_globale
  2. possumnecan I intern the empty string?
    No: both functions return NIHIL for a zero-length chorda or "", because the hash table refuses empty keys. This is a written contract, not a bug: actio, insula and stml treat that NIHIL as "empty name", and a 2026-09-22 attempt to change it broke them. If "" must be a real value, keep your own canonical empty chorda (json and componens do).
    symbolum chorda_internare chorda_internare_ex_literis · via lib/internamentum.worklog.md
  3. quomodohow do I choose between internamentum_globale and my own interner?
    internamentum_globale is a lazily created singleton with its own dynamic piscina (64 KB to start) that is never freed; use it when several libraries must share pointers. internamentum_creare(piscina) makes a private one whose copies live and die with that piscina. Pointers from two different interners never compare equal, even for the same bytes.
    symbolum internamentum_globale internamentum_creare
  4. possumnecan I free the memory of interned strings with internamentum_vacare?
    No. internamentum_vacare only empties the lookup table; the copies stay in the piscina, so old pointers still read correctly but are no longer canonical: interning the same bytes afterwards gives a NEW pointer that does not equal the old one. Memory comes back only when the interner's piscina is destroyed or reset.
    symbolum internamentum_vacare tabula_dispersa_vacare piscina_destruere
  5. possumnecan chorda_est_internata tell me whether my pointer is the canonical copy?
    No: it looks the BYTES up, so it is VERUM for any chorda with the same content as an interned one, canonical or not. To get the canonical pointer, intern the chorda again with chorda_internare (no new copy is made when it is already present).
    symbolum chorda_est_internata chorda_internare

internuntius

include/internuntius.h · lib/internuntius.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I expose a C function that page JavaScript can call?
    Create the bridge once with internuntius_creare(piscina, missor, datum), register each method with internuntius_praebere(inx, "titulus", tractator, datum), and feed every incoming message to internuntius_tractare(inx, nuntium, piscina_vocationis). The message is JSON {"id": 7, "methodus": "...", "argumenta": {...}}; the reply {"id": 7, "fructus": ...} or {"id": 7, "culpa": {"nuntius": "..."}} goes out through the missor.
    symbolum internuntius_creare internuntius_praebere InternuntiusTractator internuntius_tractare
  2. quomodohow does a handler report an error instead of a result?
    Set *culpa_exitus to a non-empty chorda and return NIHIL (the return value is ignored once culpa has length > 0); the caller gets {"culpa": {"nuntius": ...}} and the culpae counter goes up. Returning NIHIL with culpa left empty is a SUCCESS whose fructus is JSON null. argumenta is NIHIL when the message had no "argumenta" key, so the handler must check it.
    symbolum InternuntiusTractator internuntius_tractare
  3. quidwhat is the piscina_vocationis and what lives in it?
    The per-call arena: the parsed message tree, everything the handler builds for its result, and the serialized reply all live there, and the caller marks and resets it. The long-lived piscina given to internuntius_creare holds only the bridge and its method registry (method names are copied into it). Allocate the handler's result in the piscina argument it receives, never in a static.
    symbolum internuntius_tractare internuntius_eventum_mittere internuntius_creare
  4. possumnecan my missor keep the textus it is given?
    Only by copying it. The text lives in the per-call piscina and is valid only during the missor call; it is a chorda (length + bytes, not NUL-terminated). lib/vitrea_servus.c copies it with chorda_transcribere into its request piscina; the WebKit missor (vitrea_missor in lib/vitrea_macos.m) escapes and evaluates it at once.
    symbolum InternuntiusMissor chorda_transcribere vitrea_missor · via lib/vitrea_servus.c lib/vitrea_macos.m
  5. possumnecan I register the same method name twice, for example to override a handler?
    No: internuntius_praebere returns FALSUM for a duplicate name (and for an empty name or a NIHIL handler). The registry is meant as code written once, so a duplicate is treated as a wiring error. There is no unregister. Most callers cast the result to (vacuum), so a refused duplicate goes unnoticed; check it when names are computed.
    symbolum internuntius_praebere
  6. possumnecan the request id be a string, as in JSON-RPC?
    No. Only a JSON integer id is recognized (json_est_integer). A string, float or null id is treated as no id: the call is still handled and a reply is still sent, but without "id", so the JS side cannot route it. Malformed JSON or a non-object message gets an error reply with no id.
    symbolum internuntius_tractare json_est_integer
  7. quomodohow do I push an event from C to the page without a request?
    internuntius_eventum_mittere(inx, "titulus", datum, piscina_vocationis) sends {"eventus": titulus, "datum": datum} through the same missor (datum NIHIL becomes null) and counts it in eventus_missi.
    symbolum internuntius_eventum_mittere
  8. curwhy does the praebitor callback take an InternuntiusModus parameter?
    So that the same registration function serves both the local window (INTERNUNTIUS_MODUS_LOCALIS, full trust) and a socket transport (INTERNUNTIUS_MODUS_PUBLICUS, where test-harness methods must be left out). It is a parameter rather than a config field on purpose: the provider sees the mode in its signature and cannot forget to check it. lib/vitrea_servus.c calls it with INTERNUNTIUS_MODUS_PUBLICUS; apps/forum/forum.c is a provider that registers some methods only when modus is INTERNUNTIUS_MODUS_LOCALIS.
    symbolum InternuntiusPraebitor InternuntiusModus · via lib/vitrea_servus.c apps/forum/forum.c
  9. quomodohow do I put arbitrary text safely inside a JavaScript string literal?
    internuntius_effugere_js(textus, piscina) escapes backslash, double quote, control bytes (\n \r \t short, others \uXXXX), '<' (so '</script' cannot close an inline script) and U+2028/U+2029, which are legal in JSON but not in a raw JS literal. It does not add the surrounding quotes; you write those yourself (vitrea_missor wraps the result in internuntius._accipere("...")). Returns an empty chorda if piscina is NIHIL.
    symbolum internuntius_effugere_js vitrea_missor · via lib/vitrea_macos.m lib/speculum.c
  10. quomodohow do I list the registered methods or see how many calls failed?
    internuntius_methodi(inx, piscina) returns a new Xar of chorda (method names, in registration order; the names themselves stay in the bridge's piscina). internuntius_fructus(inx) returns the counters by value: vocationes_tractatae (successful dispatches), culpae (error replies sent, including malformed messages and unknown methods) and eventus_missi. A NIHIL inx gives all zeros.
    symbolum internuntius_methodi internuntius_fructus InternuntiusFructus

iter_directoria

include/iter_directoria.h · lib/iter_directoria.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I list the entries of one directory?
    Open it with directorium_iterator_aperire(via, piscina) (NIHIL if it cannot be opened), loop on directorium_iterator_proximum until it returns NIHIL, then call directorium_iterator_claudere. "." and ".." are skipped; the order is whatever readdir gives, not sorted. The returned entry, and its titulus, live in a buffer inside the iterator that the next call overwrites, so copy a name you keep with chorda_transcribere.
    symbolum directorium_iterator_aperire directorium_iterator_proximum directorium_iterator_claudere chorda_transcribere
  2. quomodohow do I walk a directory tree recursively and visit only the files I want?
    Start from directorium_filtrum_omnia(), set the fields you need (genera_accepta and genera_numerus, exemplar such as "*.c", profunditas_max, includere_occultos) and call directorium_ambulare(via, &filtrum, functio, ctx, piscina). The callback gets the full path (via_iungere of parent and name) and the entry. The pattern is tested on regular files only (a directory always passes it), and genera_accepta decides what reaches the callback but never stops the walk from descending into directories.
    symbolum directorium_ambulare directorium_filtrum_omnia DirectoriumFiltrum DirectoriumAmbulatorFunctio
  3. possumnecan I pass directorium_filtrum_omnia() and NIHIL interchangeably as the filter?
    No. A NIHIL filter accepts everything, hidden names included, and descends into hidden directories. directorium_filtrum_omnia() sets includere_occultos to FALSUM, so names starting with '.' are neither reported nor descended into. Set includere_occultos to VERUM when you really want everything.
    symbolum directorium_filtrum_omnia directorium_ambulare
  4. possumnecan I limit how deep directorium_ambulare goes?
    Yes, with profunditas_max: 0 means no limit (still capped at DIRECTORIUM_PROFUNDITAS_MAXIMA, C levels), and N visits the starting directory plus N levels below it (profunditas_max = I gives the top level and the contents of its subdirectories). Do not use -1, which the header documents as "no recursion": the check runs before the starting directory is read, so the walk visits nothing and returns 0.
    symbolum directorium_ambulare DirectoriumFiltrum
  5. quomodohow do I stop a directory walk early and tell that apart from an error?
    Return a non-zero value from the callback; directorium_ambulare stops, closes its open iterators and returns that same value unchanged (not -1 as the header suggests). It returns -1 when an argument is NIHIL or a directory cannot be opened. So stop with a value other than -1, say I, if you need to tell the two apart.
    symbolum directorium_ambulare DirectoriumAmbulatorFunctio
  6. curwhy does my whole walk fail when one subdirectory is unreadable?
    Each level opens its directory with directorium_iterator_aperire and returns -1 if that fails, and the parent treats any non-zero result from a subdirectory as a reason to stop. One subdirectory without read permission therefore ends the walk with -1 after only part of the tree has been visited, and the caller gets the same -1 as for a bad argument. A filter cannot route around it: genera_accepta and exemplar never stop the descent (only a hidden name with includere_occultos FALSUM does), and a callback that returns non-zero on that directory ends the whole walk. To skip unreadable directories, walk level by level with directorium_iterator_aperire yourself.
    symbolum directorium_ambulare directorium_iterator_aperire
  7. quidwhat genus does a symbolic link get, and are link cycles a danger?
    The entry's type comes from stat, which follows links: a link to a file is INTROITUS_FILUM, a link to a directory is INTROITUS_DIRECTORIUM (and the walk descends into it), and a broken link is INTROITUS_IGNOTUS. So INTROITUS_ALIUS covers devices, sockets and pipes, not links, despite its comment. A link cycle is stopped only by the DIRECTORIUM_PROFUNDITAS_MAXIMA cap of C levels. mensura is the target's st_size for every genus, directories included (a directory reports its stat size, not the 0 the header promises). An entry whose full path would reach 1024 bytes is not stat'ed and comes back INTROITUS_IGNOTUS.
    symbolum directorium_iterator_proximum IntroitusGenus
  8. curwhy use iter_directoria instead of calling opendir and readdir myself?
    It records what it reads in the lectiones log for fabrica: a 'D' line when a directory is enumerated, 'A' when it is missing, and 'X' from directorium_existit, so a build step's inputs include the directories it listed. Raw opendir is invisible to that log. The per-entry stat is not recorded; the 'D' line covers names, but a file that becomes a directory under the same name is a known gap.
    symbolum directorium_iterator_aperire directorium_existit lectiones_notare
  9. possumnecan I walk a very large tree in a small piscina?
    Only if the piscina can grow: nothing is released during the walk. Every entry's full path is built with via_iungere in the given piscina, and every directory visited allocates its iterator there too, so memory grows with the number of entries in the tree. For a huge tree, use a dedicated piscina and destroy it after the walk.
    symbolum directorium_ambulare via_iungere directorium_iterator_aperire

json

include/json.h · lib/json.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I parse a JSON text and read a field out of it?
    Call json_legere (a chorda) or json_legere_literis (a C string) with a piscina and check res.successus before touching res.radix. Read fields with json_objectum_capere plus a json_ad_* accessor, or in one step with json_capere_integer / json_capere_chorda / json_capere_boolean, which take a default returned when the object is NIHIL, the key is absent, the value is null or of another type.
    symbolum json_legere json_legere_literis json_objectum_capere json_capere_integer json_capere_chorda
  2. quomodohow do I find out why and where a parse failed?
    On failure successus is FALSUM, radix is always NIHIL (never a partial tree), and res.error, res.linea and res.columna give the cause and position (both 1-based; columns count characters, not bytes). When the parser stopped on a lexer error the cause is appended, e.g. 'Clavis chorda expectata: Chorda non terminata'. Only a NIHIL piscina (or a NIHIL input to json_legere_literis) fails with an empty error and position 0.
    symbolum json_legere JsonResultus
  3. possumnecan json_legere accept two values on one line, trailing garbage, comments or trailing commas?
    No. The whole input must be ONE value with only space, tab, CR or LF around it; anything after it ('1 2', '{}}', '01', a NUL byte) is refused as 'Contentum post valorem radicis'. Trailing commas, comments, duplicate keys in one object, invalid escapes, raw control characters in strings and invalid UTF-8 are all refused (RFC 8259 strict). Split newline-framed input yourself and parse each line.
    symbolum json_legere
  4. quomodohow do I read a number that may be written either as 3 or as 3.0?
    Branch on the type: json_est_integer(v) ? (f64)json_ad_integer(v) : json_ad_fluitans(v). A number with '.', 'e' or 'E' is parsed as JSON_FLUITANS and json_ad_integer returns 0 for it (and json_capere_integer returns its default), so '3.0' is not an integer. There is no json_capere_fluitans; lib/norma.c uses the branch above.
    symbolum json_est_integer json_ad_integer json_ad_fluitans · via lib/norma.c
  5. possumnecan I parse integers bigger than 2^63 or numbers like 1e400?
    No, they are refused instead of silently wrapped or rounded: an integer outside -2^63 .. 2^63-1 fails with 'Integer extra s64', and a float that overflows f64 fails with 'Numerus extra f64'. Underflow (1e-400) is accepted as the nearest value. Large ids that must survive exactly should travel as JSON strings.
    symbolum json_legere json_integer_creare
  6. quomodohow do I reach a nested value by a dotted path like a.b.0.c?
    json_via(radix, "a.b.0.c"): a segment of digits indexes an array (0-based), any other segment is an object key, compared byte for byte (json_via interns nothing). It returns NIHIL if any segment is missing, an index is out of range, a segment is empty ("a..b", "a.", ".a") or the path walks through a scalar. Known bug: the index overflow guard is vacuous, so a digit string of 2^32 or more wraps around ("a.4294967296" on ["zero"] returns "zero"). A key that itself contains a '.' cannot be reached this way; use json_objectum_capere for it.
    symbolum json_via json_objectum_capere
  7. quomodohow do I iterate over all key/value pairs of an object?
    Make an iterator with json_objectum_iterator(obj) and loop while json_objectum_iterator_proxima(&it, &clavis, &valor) is VERUM; pairs come back in insertion order. Or index directly with json_objectum_numerus and json_objectum_par_obtinere. The key is an interned chorda: treat it as NOT NUL-terminated and compare by mensura (the empty key has mensura 0).
    symbolum json_objectum_iterator json_objectum_iterator_proxima json_objectum_par_obtinere json_objectum_numerus
  8. quidwhat memory does a parsed JSON tree live in, and does it point into my input?
    Every value, string and container is allocated in the piscina you pass, and strings are copied (and unescaped), so the input buffer can be freed after json_legere returns. Object keys are the exception: they are interned in internamentum_globale(), which lives for the whole process and outlives every piscina. A lookup by key (json_objectum_capere, _capere_chorda, _habet, json_capere_*) interns the key even when it is absent; json_via compares bytes and interns nothing. Built trees differ: json_chorda_creare keeps the chorda you pass without copying it (json_chorda_creare_literis copies).
    symbolum json_legere internamentum_globale
  9. quomodohow do I build a JSON document and serialize it?
    Create containers with json_objectum_creare / json_tabulatum_creare and leaves with json_integer_creare, json_chorda_creare_literis and friends, link them with json_objectum_ponere and json_tabulatum_addere, then call json_scribere (compact) or json_scribere_pulchrum (two-space indentation) to get a chorda in the piscina. Keys keep insertion order; a NIHIL element is written as null.
    symbolum json_objectum_creare json_tabulatum_creare json_objectum_ponere json_tabulatum_addere json_scribere json_scribere_pulchrum
  10. possumnecan json_objectum_ponere add a second value under a key that already exists?
    No: the builder overwrites the existing value in place (the key keeps its position). The parser is stricter and refuses a document that repeats a key in one object ('Clavis duplicata'), so what you build and what you can read back agree.
    symbolum json_objectum_ponere json_objectum_ponere_chorda json_legere
  11. possumnecan I write NaN, infinity or arbitrary bytes through json_scribere?
    NaN and infinities are written as null (JSON has neither). A float that looks like an integer gets '.0' appended so it re-reads as JSON_FLUITANS, and the shortest exact form is chosen (0.1, not 0.10000000000000001). Strings are NOT checked for valid UTF-8 on the way out: bytes put into a chorda are emitted as given, so a builder can produce output that json_legere itself refuses.
    symbolum json_scribere json_fluitans_creare json_chorda_creare
  12. curwhy does the parser stop at 256 levels of nesting but the builder does not?
    The parser is recursive, so JSON_PROFUNDITAS_MAXIMA (CCLVI) stops hostile input like two million '[' from exhausting the stack ('Nidificatio nimis profunda'); every MCP resident parses client JSON with it. The limit guards reading only. The builder checks neither depth nor cycles: a value placed inside itself sends json_scribere into infinite recursion.
    symbolum json_legere json_tabulatum_addere · via lib/json.worklog.md

lapifex_generare

include/lapifex_generare.h · lib/lapifex_generare.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I turn a grammar into a parse table?
    Read the STML grammar with lapifex_grammaticam_legere, run lapifex_first_computare, build the item sets with lapifex_collectio_construere (canonical LR(1)) or lapifex_collectio_lalr_construere (LALR(1)), and turn them into an ACTION/GOTO table with lapifex_tabulam_construere. Every step returns NIHIL or FALSUM on failure. lib/nuntium_schema.c is the live consumer and runs exactly this chain (it also calls lapifex_follow_computare) before handing the table to the lapifex parser.
    symbolum lapifex_grammaticam_legere lapifex_first_computare lapifex_collectio_construere lapifex_collectio_lalr_construere lapifex_tabulam_construere · via lib/nuntium_schema.c
  2. quidwhat does the STML grammar format look like?
    A root with three required children: <terminalia> holding <terminalis titulus=".." exemplum=".." genus=".."/> (titulus required; exemplum is the literal or a $identificator/$numerus class used by the lexer; genus is optional), <regulae> holding <regula titulus="lhs"> with one <productio>A B C</productio> per alternative (symbols separated by spaces), and <initium>start</initium>. Every name in a production must be a declared terminal or a regula titulus.
    symbolum lapifex_grammaticam_legere LapifexSymbolum · via lib/nuntium_schema.c
  3. possumnecan I write an empty (epsilon) production?
    No. The reader skips a <productio> whose text is empty after trimming, silently, so an empty alternative simply disappears from the grammar. FIRST and FOLLOW do handle epsilon (habet_epsilon, productions with an empty right side), but no STML grammar can produce one. Write an optional part out as two alternatives instead: A -> B C and A -> C rather than A -> opt_B C with an empty opt_B.
    symbolum lapifex_grammaticam_legere lapifex_first_computare
  4. curwhy is there a separate LALR(1) builder, and when should I use it?
    The canonical LR(1) builder makes a separate state for every lookahead combination and blew up on the C89 expression grammar (95 productions): it ran indefinitely. The LALR(1) builder makes the LR(0) automaton first (about 155 states there), propagates lookaheads to a fixed point, and returns the same LapifexCollectio type, so it is a drop-in replacement; that table took about two seconds. Small grammars such as nuntium_schema's still use the canonical builder.
    symbolum lapifex_collectio_lalr_construere lapifex_collectio_construere · via lib/lapifex_generare.worklog.md archivum/lib/lapifex_c89_grammatica.c
  5. quidwhat are __initium__, EOF and the -1 terminal?
    The reader adds an augmented non-terminal __initium__ with one last production: __initium__ -> start EOF when a terminal titled exactly "EOF" is declared, otherwise __initium__ -> start, and makes it the grammar's initium_index. Terminal index -1 is the end marker $ used as lookahead; ACCEPT is the action on -1 in the state where the augmented production is complete. A terminal named anything else ("END", "eof") gets no special treatment.
    symbolum lapifex_grammaticam_legere lapifex_tabulam_construere LapifexActioIntroitus
  6. quomodohow do I know which production number my reduce callback receives?
    Productions are numbered from 0 in document order: regula by regula, productio by productio (an empty <productio> is skipped and takes no number), with the augmented __initium__ production last. Symbols are numbered the same way: terminals first in declaration order, then the regula non-terminals, then __initium__. lapifex_grammaticam_imprimere prints both lists (P0, P1, ...), and nuntium_schema.c switches on exactly those numbers (its P15 is __initium__ -> schema EOF).
    symbolum lapifex_grammaticam_imprimere LapifexProductio · via lib/nuntium_schema.c
  7. quomodohow do I see the conflicts in my grammar?
    The table keeps ALL actions for a (state, terminal) pair, because the GLR parser forks on them: conflicting entries get conflictus_intentus VERUM, the state habet_conflictum, and LapifexTabula.numerus_conflictuum counts conflicting PAIRS (three actions on one terminal count as three). lapifex_conflictus_imprimere lists them on stdout, lapifex_tabulam_imprimere prints the whole table, and lapifex_actiones_quaerere returns every action for a pair.
    symbolum lapifex_conflictus_imprimere lapifex_tabulam_imprimere lapifex_actiones_quaerere LapifexTabula
  8. possumnecan I keep one table and parse many inputs with it?
    Yes, archivum/lib/lapifex_c89_grammatica.c (now archived) built its table once into a static piscina. But every call to lapifex_actiones_quaerere allocates a NEW Xar from the grammar's piscina (tabula->grammatica->piscina) for its result, and the parsers call it once per step, so that piscina grows with every token parsed for the life of the table. lapifex_goto_quaerere allocates nothing.
    symbolum lapifex_actiones_quaerere lapifex_goto_quaerere · via archivum/lib/lapifex_c89_grammatica.c lib/lapifex_parsere.c
  9. quidwhat does lapifex_grammaticam_legere do with a bad grammar?
    It prints a message to stderr ("lapifex: ...") and returns NIHIL for unparsable STML, a missing <terminalia>, <regulae> or <initium>, a terminalis or regula without titulus, or a production or initium naming an unknown symbol. It does NOT reject a regula whose titulus equals a terminal's: the productions are attached to the terminal as their left side, silently; likewise two regulae with the same titulus are merged into one non-terminal.
    symbolum lapifex_grammaticam_legere
  10. possumnecan I build the table without computing FOLLOW sets?
    Yes. Both collection builders use only FIRST (they compute FIRST(beta a) for the lookaheads), so lapifex_first_computare is required and lapifex_follow_computare is not; FOLLOW sets are only read by lapifex_follow_obtinere and lapifex_follow_imprimere. Both sets are Xar of s32 terminal indices (the header comment on lapifex_first_obtinere says i32; the elements are s32). The end marker -1 appears only in FOLLOW sets (lapifex_follow_computare seeds it into the start symbol's FOLLOW); a symbol's FIRST set holds real terminal indices only.
    symbolum lapifex_follow_computare lapifex_first_computare lapifex_collectio_construere

lectiones

include/lectiones.h · lib/lectiones.interrogationes.stml

  1. quidwhat is the lectiones read ledger and when does it record anything?
    It is the list of what a process read, looked for, found, listed, wrote and asked the environment, which fabrica uses to decide whether a generated file is stale. Each event appends one line, a letter, a tab and the path (L read, A absent, X exists, D directory listed, S written, E environment), with one write() in O_APPEND mode so parallel children never split lines. Nothing at all happens unless the environment variable FABRICA_LECTIONES is set to a non-empty file name; the path is written as given, so a relative path stays relative.
    symbolum lectiones_notare LectioGenus · via lib/fabrica.worklog.md
  2. quomodohow do I open or stat a file so the build layer sees the dependency?
    New code should go through filum (filum_legere_totum, filum_existit, filum_scriptor_aperire), which records its reads and writes itself. lectiones_fopen and lectiones_stat are stopgaps for the raw stdio and stat calls that remain. lectiones_fopen records L or A for a read mode ('r' or 'rb' without '+'), and S for any other mode but only when the open succeeded; lectiones_stat records X or A. Paths under /dev/ are never recorded.
    symbolum filum_legere_totum filum_existit filum_scriptor_aperire lectiones_fopen lectiones_stat
  3. possumnecan I read a secret such as an API key with lectiones_ambitus?
    No. lectiones_ambitus writes the value itself into the ledger file (E, tab, name, tab, value), so whenever the ledger is on (for a generator, fabrica points it at a file under build/fabrica/lectiones/) the secret lands on disk in plain text. Read secrets with plain getenv. There is a second cost for generators: a generator trace that holds any E line is not stored, so the generator gets no cached verdict for that run.
    symbolum lectiones_ambitus lectiones_notare · via include/fabrica_interna.h
  4. curwhy does lectiones use raw open and write instead of filum?
    lectiones is the channel itself: filum, via_existit and iter_directoria call lectiones_notare, so recording through filum would recurse and record the ledger as a read. The descriptor is kept open per process while FABRICA_LECTIONES names the same file, is inherited by forked children, and is closed as soon as a call finds the variable unset, so a ledger deleted and recreated while the variable was off is not written through a stale inode (one recreated while it stays set still would be).
    symbolum lectiones_notare filum_existit via_existit · via lib/filum.c
  5. possumnecan lectiones_fopen or lectiones_stat take a NIHIL path or mode safely?
    No. Both call fopen (or stat) first and test for NIHIL only afterwards, so the test guards the recording, not the call: a NIHIL modus or via reaches fopen directly, which is undefined behaviour. Check your arguments before calling.
    symbolum lectiones_fopen lectiones_stat
  6. possumnecan any path or environment value be recorded in the ledger?
    Not every one. Lines are written raw, with no escaping, so a path or an environment value containing a newline splits into two ledger lines and the second may be read back as a bogus event. A line that would pass the IV * MXXIV byte buffer is silently not recorded (lectiones_ambitus: name plus value of 4094 bytes or more), and so is everything while FABRICA_LECTIONES itself names a path of 4096 bytes or more.
    symbolum lectiones_notare lectiones_ambitus

ludus_fenestra

include/ludus_fenestra.h · lib/ludus_fenestra.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I run an app's dispatcher in a real window?
    Create the window, make its pixel table with fenestra_creare_tabulam_pixelorum, then call ludus_fenestra_creare(piscina, d, figurae, thema, fons, fons_ctx, tabula) and ludus_fenestra_currere(lf, fenestra, ZEPHYRUM). creare returns NIHIL if piscina, d, figurae or tabula is NIHIL. The loop runs until EVENTUS_CLAUDERE or the window asks to close. It then calls dispensator_finire, prints one timing line to stdout and returns ZEPHYRUM (I only when lf or fenestra is NIHIL). Destroying the window is still your job, as apps/pictor/pictor.c shows.
    symbolum ludus_fenestra_creare ludus_fenestra_currere fenestra_creare_tabulam_pixelorum fenestra_destruere · via apps/pictor/pictor.c
  2. quomodohow do I render frames or take a screenshot without opening a window?
    Make a bare table with tabula_pixelorum_creare_nuda, pass it to ludus_fenestra_creare, call ludus_quadrum(lf, nunc) once per frame, then ludus_fenestra_imaginem_scribere(lf, via) to write the last frame as a PNG. That function returns FALSUM before the first frame and when the write fails. The worklog records a real-window PNG that was byte-identical to the headless one, so headless frames are faithful screenshots. The apps expose this as -imago with -fumus.
    symbolum tabula_pixelorum_creare_nuda ludus_fenestra_creare ludus_quadrum ludus_fenestra_imaginem_scribere · via probationes/probatio_ludus_imago.c
  3. possumnecan I keep lf->mandata or anything from the previous frame across ludus_quadrum calls?
    No. Each ludus_quadrum pulses the dispatcher, then calls piscina_vacare on the per-frame piscina and builds a fresh Mandata there, so lf->mandata and everything allocated for the last frame (including the PNG buffers of ludus_fenestra_imaginem_scribere) is valid only until the next frame. Copy out anything you need first.
    symbolum ludus_quadrum ludus_fenestra_imaginem_scribere piscina_vacare
  4. curwhy does the window loop sleep when idle, and does that delay input?
    Until 2026-10-05 it polled without waiting, and an idle pictor window pinned a CPU core. Now it blocks in fenestra_expectare_eventus for ludus_fenestra_mora(lf): ZEPHYRUM before the first frame, then the dispatcher's quies_ms. An arriving event wakes the wait at once, so drags and strokes are not delayed. The quiet flush still runs in each frame's dispensator_pulsare.
    symbolum ludus_fenestra_mora fenestra_expectare_eventus dispensator_pulsare · via lib/ludus_fenestra.worklog.md
  5. quomodohow do I keep a live app (a terminal, a clock) updating when no events arrive?
    Install a pulse with ludus_fenestra_pulsum_ponere(lf, fn, ctx); NIHIL removes it. In the normal loop (quadra_maxima ZEPHYRUM) fn(ctx) runs once per iteration, inside ludus_fenestra_pingendum, and with a pulse every wait is XVI ms at most (also the first one, which would otherwise be ZEPHYRUM). The loop draws only when ludus_fenestra_pingendum says so: there were events, the pulse returned VERUM, no frame has been drawn yet, or (after the dispatcher alone is pulsed for its timers) the store version differs from the one at the last drawn frame. So return VERUM from the pulse whenever it changed something visible. Without a pulse every iteration draws, as before.
    symbolum ludus_fenestra_pulsum_ponere ludus_fenestra_pingendum LudusPulsator · via apps/vicus/vicus.c
  6. curwhy does the frame skip compare the store version instead of counting recompositions?
    The dispatcher recomposes on EVERY event, including the empty pulse (the staleness rule), so recompositions do not mean anything changed. insula_versio goes up once per accepted write. It is compared with versio_picta, which ludus_quadrum records, rather than with the value before this pulse, because a click handled in an earlier iteration may still be undrawn.
    symbolum ludus_fenestra_pingendum insula_versio ludus_quadrum · via lib/ludus_fenestra.worklog.md
  7. possumnecan I use quadra_maxima for a smoke run of an idle app that installs a pulse?
    Yes. With quadra_maxima > ZEPHYRUM the frame skip is bypassed and every iteration draws, so the loop stops after exactly that many frames. Before the 2026-10-08 fix an idle vicus -fumus drew one frame and hung. Two consequences: in smoke mode ludus_fenestra_pingendum is never called, so your pulse function never runs (vicus -fumus does not poll its reactor); and with a pulse each idle wait is at most XVI ms, while an app without one waits the dispatcher's quies (300 ms in pictor) per idle frame, which made pictor's 30-frame smoke run take about 9 s.
    symbolum ludus_fenestra_currere ludus_fenestra_pingendum
  8. quidwhat coordinates does the dispatcher see for window resizes and the first size?
    Table pixels, never window points. On EVENTUS_MUTARE_MAGNITUDINEM, ludus_fenestra_tractare refits the table with tabula_pixelorum_ad_fenestram (scale kept, buffer grown in the piscina only when it gets bigger) and rewrites the event's size to the table's before passing it on. The very first event or frame first sends one synthetic MUTARE_MAGNITUDINEM with the table's size. An event with tempus ZEPHYRUM is stamped with nunc.
    symbolum ludus_fenestra_tractare tabula_pixelorum_ad_fenestram
  9. possumnecan I destroy a LudusFenestra and free its frame memory?
    Not through the API: there is no destroy function. The struct lives in your piscina, but ludus_fenestra_creare also makes its own dynamic piscina for frames ("ludus_quadrum", LXIV * M initial), and nothing ever destroys it. Destroying your piscina does not release it. The field is public, so code that creates many (such as tests) can call piscina_destruere(lf->piscina_quadri) itself once it is done with lf; nothing in the tree does today. Apps create one per process.
    symbolum ludus_fenestra_creare piscina_generare_dynamicum piscina_destruere

magnus

include/magnus.h · lib/magnus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I compute with integers too big for s64 without silent overflow?
    Wrap inputs with magnus_ex_s64 (or parse them), combine them with magnus_adde, magnus_subtrahe, magnus_multiplica, magnus_divide and magnus_potentia, and print with magnus_ad_chordam. Values are immutable like chorda: each operation returns a new Magnus. Values that fit in s64 stay inside the struct and need no allocation; larger ones keep 32-bit limbs in the piscina you pass. Always pass a real piscina: small inputs can still give a large result (S64 overflow, negating the most negative s64).
    symbolum magnus_ex_s64 magnus_adde magnus_multiplica magnus_potentia magnus_ad_chordam
  2. possumnecan I reset or destroy a scratch piscina and keep a Magnus computed in it?
    Only after copying it out with magnus_transcribe(valor, piscina_mea), which gives the value fresh limbs (values that fit in s64 need none). Limbs are never written after creation, so results may SHARE limbs with their arguments: magnus_nega only flips the sign over the same limbs, and magnus_absolutum can return its argument as is. A result is valid only while the piscinae of its arguments and of the result are alive.
    symbolum magnus_transcribe magnus_nega magnus_absolutum
  3. curwhy does magnus_divide give a positive remainder for a negative dividend?
    Division is Euclidean: a = quotiens * divisor + residuum with 0 <= residuum < |divisor|, always. C89 leaves the sign of % with negative operands to the implementation, so magnus defines it: -7 divided by 2 gives quotient -4 and remainder 1. It returns FALSUM for a zero divisor without touching the outputs, and either output pointer may be NIHIL.
    symbolum magnus_divide magnus_residuum_parvum · via lib/magnus.worklog.md
  4. quomodohow do I convert between a Magnus and decimal text or an s64?
    magnus_ex_chorda accepts decimal digits with an optional leading '-' (no '+', no spaces, no other bases) and returns FALSUM, leaving the output untouched, for anything else. Parsing always allocates limbs, even for small values. magnus_ad_chordam prints decimal into the piscina (the chorda is not guaranteed to be NUL-terminated). magnus_ad_s64 returns VERUM and the value only when it fits in s64.
    symbolum magnus_ex_chorda magnus_ad_chordam magnus_ad_s64
  5. possumnecan I pass a negative exponent to magnus_potentia?
    No. The exponent is an i32, which is UNSIGNED in this house, so -1 becomes 4294967295 and, for any base other than 0, 1 and -1, the call tries to compute a power with billions of bits (repeated squaring, no size check). There are no rational results here (fractio builds them on top of magnus); 0^0 is defined as 1.
    symbolum magnus_potentia
  6. quomodohow do I get a gcd together with Bezout coefficients?
    magnus_divisor_communis(a, b, piscina) returns the gcd, always >= 0, with gcd(0, 0) = 0. magnus_divisor_communis_testatus also fills u and v with g = u*a + v*b, a certificate anyone can check without this library (u and v may be NIHIL). For large operands (more than IV limbs, about 38 digits: both of them for the plain gcd, either one for the witness variant) Euclid runs in two internal scratch piscinae used alternately, so the caller's piscina only receives the result; smaller ones run in the caller's piscina. magnus_apex_alternarum reports the peak scratch usage of the most recent gcd call (every call resets it; 0 when the caller's piscina was used). It is one static counter for the whole process.
    symbolum magnus_divisor_communis magnus_divisor_communis_testatus magnus_apex_alternarum
  7. quomodohow do I reduce a big integer modulo a word-sized number without allocating?
    magnus_residuum_parvum(a, n) returns a mod n (Euclidean, 0 <= r < n) for 1 <= n < 2^32, walking the limbs with no piscina; n == 0 returns 0. For a size bound without allocation, magnus_bitorum gives the bit length of |a| (0 for 0).
    symbolum magnus_residuum_parvum magnus_bitorum
  8. possumnecan I compare two Magnus values with == or memcmp?
    No: use magnus_aequalis or magnus_compara (-1, 0, +1). The form is canonical and unique (a value that fits in s64 is always stored inline, a larger one has at least two limbs and a non-zero top limb), but equal large values usually sit in different limb arrays, so comparing the struct bytes compares pointers. The fields are private.
    symbolum magnus_aequalis magnus_compara magnus_signum
  9. possumnecan magnus do shifts, bit operations, square roots or modular exponentiation?
    Not yet. The worklog lists shifts and bit operations, square root, modular exponentiation, Karatsuba multiplication and an in-place API as deferred, each addable without changing the existing API. Today, multiply or divide by a power of two from magnus_potentia instead of shifting.
    symbolum magnus_potentia magnus_divide · via lib/magnus.worklog.md
  10. curwhy does magnus use no floating point, even for sizing the decimal output?
    Fran's explicit rule, so that the result is identical on every machine and at every optimization level. Decimal output divides repeatedly by 10^9 in integers and sizes its buffer from the number of nine-digit chunks instead of a log10; quotient digits in long division are estimated by integer division (Knuth's algorithm D).
    symbolum magnus_ad_chordam · via lib/magnus.worklog.md

mandatum

include/mandatum.h · lib/mandatum.interrogationes.stml
