  1. quomodohow do I build a list of draw commands and put it on the screen?
    Create a Mandata with mandata_creare(piscina, intern), emit primitives in painting order (mandata_rectangulum, mandata_linea, mandata_polygonum, mandata_imago, mandata_textus), then hand the list to a rasterizer such as delineare_mandata, which walks it once into a TabulaPixelorum. mandatum itself only records: it never draws and resolves no colours.
    symbolum mandata_creare mandata_rectangulum mandata_textus mandata_linea delineare_mandata
  2. quomodohow do I clip, translate or scale a group of draw commands?
    Wrap them in a coetus: i32 c = mandata_coetus_incipere(m, fines, sectio, translatio_x, translatio_y, scala, provenientia), emit the children, then mandata_coetus_finire(m, c) with the SAME index. Clip, translation and integer scale are properties of the group, not push/pop commands. In delineare_mandata the children's coordinates are relative to the group: origin += fines + translatio, scale multiplies, and sectio clips to fines. A scala below I is stored as I.
    symbolum mandata_coetus_incipere mandata_coetus_finire delineare_mandata
  3. possumnecan I forget mandata_coetus_finire or pass it a different index?
    No, and nothing checks it. A coetus starts with magnitudo_arboris I, so an unclosed group owns no children: the rasterizer and mandata_scribere_stml treat everything after it as siblings and the clip and translation silently do not apply. finire sets magnitudo_arboris to (count now - index) on whatever element is at index, without checking it is a coetus, and an index past the end makes mandata_obtinere return NIHIL, which finire then writes through.
    symbolum mandata_coetus_finire mandata_coetus_incipere mandata_scribere_stml
  4. possumnecan I free or reuse the text buffer after calling mandata_textus?
    No. mandata_textus and mandata_imago store the chorda you pass as is (pointer and length, no copy), so its bytes must live as long as the Mandata. Points are different: mandata_linea and mandata_polygonum copy them into the Mandata's piscina, so the caller's array may be reused at once.
    symbolum mandata_textus mandata_imago mandata_polygonum mandata_linea
  5. quidwhat does the valor of a ColorMandati mean?
    It depends on genus: COLOR_MANDATI_THEMA is a theme token resolved through thema.h, COLOR_MANDATI_INDEX a palette index, and COLOR_MANDATI_RGBA a framebuffer pixel, a<<24 | b<<16 | g<<8 | r, made with color_ad_pixelum(color_ex_rgba(r, g, b, a)). It is NOT a literal 0xRRGGBBAA (the header comment says it once claimed that). Only the rasterizer resolves it, through color_ex_mandato.
    symbolum ColorMandati color_ex_rgba color_ad_pixelum color_ex_mandato
  6. quomodohow do I fill a rectangle with a classic Mac pattern instead of a solid colour?
    mandata_rectangulum_exemplar(m, fines, color, exemplar) with a pattern id from exemplaria.h: only the set bits are painted in color, the background shows through, and the pattern is anchored to framebuffer coordinates so neighbouring fills line up. For a two-colour fill emit a solid mandata_rectangulum first, then the pattern. exemplar 0 means solid.
    symbolum mandata_rectangulum_exemplar exemplar_obtinere
  7. quomodohow do I snapshot draw commands as text and compare them in a test?
    mandata_scribere_stml(m, piscina, pulchrum) writes STML (groups as nested elements), mandata_legere_stml parses it back (NIHIL if the STML does not parse; unknown element names are skipped), and mandata_aequalia compares two lists field by field, including provenientia. Note that the writer builds its child nodes and attribute text in the Mandata's own piscina even though m is constans; only the root element and the final chorda go into the piscina you pass. An empty textus or imago fons does not round-trip: "" interns to NIHIL, the STML writer emits a NIHIL value as a bare attribute, and the reader gets "true".
    symbolum mandata_scribere_stml mandata_legere_stml mandata_aequalia stml_attributum_addere
  8. curwhy do draw commands carry no ids or click handlers?
    Draw commands are the pure output of a paint function: identity, actions and parts live in the component tree (componens.h). The only id is a coetus's optional provenientia, which is for the inspector; hit targeting never reads it.
    symbolum mandata_coetus_incipere Mandatum · via lib/mandatum.worklog.md

manus_ludus

include/manus_ludus.h · lib/manus_ludus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I drive a widget from a test, clicking and typing, without a window?
    Create a hand over the app's dispensator with manus_ludus_creare(piscina, d), then call manus_ludus_premere(m, "#id") to click the centre of a component, manus_ludus_scribere(m, "text") to type, and manus_ludus_clavem for one key. Every call is synchronous: the events have been handled by dispensator_tractare when it returns. Assert with CREDO_MANUS_LUDUS_EXISTIT, _ABEST and _FOCUS.
    symbolum manus_ludus_creare manus_ludus_premere manus_ludus_scribere manus_ludus_clavem CREDO_MANUS_LUDUS_FOCUS · via probationes/probatio_vicus_paginae.c
  2. quidwhat selectors does manus_ludus understand?
    Four forms over the LOGICAL component tree (never over the drawn mandata): '#id', '[partes=x]', '[actio=x]' and '[titulus=x]'. Matching is exact and depth-first from the root, and the first match wins. Anything else (no '#' or '[', missing ']', another key) is a bad selector: manus_ludus_invenire returns NIHIL and manus_ludus_causa says so. There are no combinators, descendants or wildcards.
    symbolum manus_ludus_invenire
  3. quidwhat clock does manus_ludus use, and how do I make a timeout or the quiet seat fire?
    A virtual one: m->tempus starts at M ms and advances by gradus_ms (L = 50 ms) after every mouse event and every key press or release the hand sends (a typed character's text event shares the key-down's time). Besides that only replay moves it, up to the latest recorded event time, so nothing time-based happens on its own. Call manus_ludus_exspectare(m, ms) to advance the clock and pulse the dispensator with dispensator_pulsare; that is what lets the quiet seat flush or a timer fire.
    symbolum manus_ludus_exspectare manus_ludus_creare dispensator_pulsare
  4. possumnecan I send arrow keys, function keys or other non-ASCII keys with manus_ludus_clavem?
    No. manus_ludus_clavem takes a single character, so it can only produce key codes 0 to 255; CLAVIS_SINISTER, CLAVIS_SURSUM, CLAVIS_F1 and the other named keys (256 and up) cannot be expressed. Build the Eventus yourself and pass it to dispensator_tractare, as probatio_vicus_paginae.c does for Ctrl+Shift+arrow; note that such an event does not advance the hand's clock.
    symbolum manus_ludus_clavem dispensator_tractare · via probationes/probatio_vicus_paginae.c
  5. possumnecan manus_ludus_scribere type non-ASCII UTF-8 text such as an accented letter?
    Not as a real source would. It walks the string BYTE by byte: each byte of 0x20 or above (except 0x7F) gets key-down, a one-byte EVENTUS_TEXTUS committed, then key-up, so a two-byte character is delivered as two one-byte text events, neither valid UTF-8 on its own. Control characters get the key events only, no text.
    symbolum manus_ludus_scribere
  6. quomodohow do I drag inside a component with manus_ludus?
    manus_ludus_trahere(m, selector, puncta, n): press at puncta[0], motion through the rest, release at the last, all in the component's LOCAL coordinates, all with MUS_SINISTER. It maps each point to the screen with manus_ludus_ad_schirmum, the exact inverse of the targeting transform (fines, then per ancestor scala and translatio). n must be at least I.
    symbolum manus_ludus_trahere manus_ludus_ad_schirmum
  7. quomodohow do I replay a recorded event file and detect that the layout changed under it?
    manus_ludus_iterare(m, notata, modus, divergentiae) feeds a Xar of EventusNotatum to the dispensator. MANUS_ITERATIO_CRUDA replays positions as recorded; MANUS_ITERATIO_SEMANTICA re-aims mouse events at their recorded target's id in the CURRENT tree. In both modes a mouse event whose raw position now hits a different component than its recorded scopus is counted (the return value) and, if divergentiae is not NIHIL, recorded as a ManusDivergentia. Only mouse events that carry a recorded scopus are checked or re-aimed; a scopus missing from the current tree falls back to the raw position. Events keep their recorded tempus, and the hand's clock is raised to the latest one. manus_ludus_iterare_per routes each event through a ManusTraditio (e.g. the terminal encoder) instead.
    symbolum manus_ludus_iterare manus_ludus_iterare_per ManusDivergentia
  8. possumnecan I trust manus_ludus_causa to explain the most recent failure?
    Not always. causa is never cleared, not even by a success, and manus_ludus_premere writes 'selector nihil invenit' only when causa is still empty, so after an earlier bad selector a later premere that finds nothing keeps reporting the old cause (manus_ludus_trahere, by contrast, overwrites it on every failure). A dispensator with no tree makes manus_ludus_invenire return NIHIL without setting any cause. Use a fresh hand per scenario when you assert on the cause.
    symbolum manus_ludus_causa manus_ludus_premere manus_ludus_invenire manus_ludus_trahere

matrix

include/matrix.h · lib/matrix.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I build a matrix of integers, rationals or Laurent polynomials?
    Pick a ring descriptor (&ANULUS_INTEGRORUM for Z with Magnus elements, &ANULUS_RATIONALIUM for Q with Fractio, &ANULUS_POLYNOMIORUM for Z[t, t^-1] with Polynomium, or anulus_residuorum(n, piscina) for Z/n with i32 residues) and either parse text with matrix_ex_chorda, e.g. [t - 1, 1; -t, t - 1] (commas between entries, semicolons between rows, [] is 0 x 0), or start from matrix_nulla / matrix_identitas and fill it with matrix_pone. matrix_ad_chordam prints the same format back.
    symbolum matrix_ex_chorda matrix_nulla matrix_identitas matrix_pone ANULUS_INTEGRORUM ANULUS_RATIONALIUM ANULUS_POLYNOMIORUM anulus_residuorum
  2. possumnecan I change an entry of a matrix after I have used it in a computation?
    Do not: the contract is build then freeze. matrix_pone is meant only while you are constructing a matrix; every operation returns a NEW matrix and never changes its arguments. matrix_pone copies the element struct (it does not deep-copy a Magnus or Polynomium), and an index out of range silently does nothing. Read entries with matrix_elementum, which returns a pointer to the ring's element type, or NIHIL out of range.
    symbolum matrix_pone matrix_elementum
  3. quomodohow do I compute a determinant, for example an Alexander polynomial?
    matrix_determinans(m, piscina, &valor) writes an element of the ring (a Magnus, a Fractio or a Polynomium you declare) and returns FALSUM if the matrix is not square or the ring is not an integral domain (Z/n with n composite: integrum is FALSUM); 0 x 0 gives 1. It is fraction-free Bareiss elimination, so it works exactly over Z and Z[t, t^-1] without fractions. The worklog builds det(V - t V^T) from a Seifert matrix with transpose, multiply and subtract.
    symbolum matrix_determinans matrix_transposita matrix_subtrahe matrix_multiplica
  4. quomodohow do I find out why a matrix operation returned FALSUM?
    Every b32 function returns FALSUM, and leaves its output untouched, when dimensions or rings do not match (rings are compared by descriptor address), when an element operation refuses (a Polynomium exponent out of range, an inexact division), or when the ring lacks what the algorithm needs (no integrum for determinant, rank and kernel; no Euclidean hooks for Hermite and Smith). There is no error code; check those three things.
    symbolum matrix_adde matrix_multiplica matrix_determinans matrix_forma_hermite
  5. possumnecan I destroy the piscina of the input matrix after computing a result from it?
    Only for some results. Determinant, kernel, product and the normal forms are always deep-copied into the piscina you pass, so they own their memory. matrix_transposita, matrix_adde, matrix_subtrahe and matrix_pone copy element structs and may SHARE storage with the arguments, so those results stay valid only while the arguments' piscinae live.
    symbolum matrix_determinans matrix_nucleus matrix_multiplica matrix_transposita matrix_adde matrix_subtrahe
  6. quidwhat kind of basis does matrix_nucleus return, and when do I need matrix_reticulum_nuclei instead?
    matrix_nucleus gives columns spanning the kernel over the fraction field, with entries in the ring (they are minors, so their size is bounded), but over Z they are not primitive and not a lattice basis: for [2, 4] it gives (-4, 2). matrix_reticulum_nuclei (Euclidean rings, Z today) gives a basis of the integer kernel lattice, (-2, 1) up to sign, so every integer solution is an integer combination of its columns. Both have columns minus rank columns.
    symbolum matrix_nucleus matrix_reticulum_nuclei
  7. quomodohow do I get the Smith or Hermite normal form with the transformation matrices?
    matrix_forma_hermite(a, piscina, &h, &u) gives H = U A by row operations (positive pivots, entries above reduced, zero rows last); matrix_forma_smith(a, piscina, &d, &u, &v) gives D = U A V with each d_i dividing the next. Pass NIHIL for u or v when you do not need the certificate; it saves time and memory. Only rings with divisor_communis, divide_cum_residuo and compara_normam work, which today means Z; Q and Z[t, t^-1] return FALSUM.
    symbolum matrix_forma_smith matrix_forma_hermite
  8. curwhy can the determinant or rank of a Z[t, t^-1] matrix return FALSUM when the answer is well defined?
    Elimination makes intermediate exponents grow: the rank of [t^(2^29), 1; 1, t^(2^29)] needs t^(2^30), which is past Polynomium's exponent bound, so the element operation refuses and the whole call returns FALSUM with the output untouched. Huge sparse exponents also need Polynomium's dense memory. This is documented and pinned by a test, not a bug to work around.
    symbolum matrix_determinans matrix_gradus · via include/matrix.h lib/matrix.worklog.md
  9. curwhy does matrix create its own scratch piscinae, and how do I measure them?
    Elimination produces many intermediate values; they live in four internal scratch piscinae (two alternating, one per-entry temporary, one stable) that are rolled back as the work proceeds, so only the result is deep-copied into your piscina. Determinant, rank and kernel skip the scratch when the matrix has at most XXV entries AND every entry is stored inline (anulus->parvum: a Polynomium only when it is zero); multiplication uses the same rule with at most CXXV element products. Hermite, Smith and the kernel lattice always use scratch. A 2 x 2 determinant cost 0.5 us with four piscinae against 0.007 us without (worklog A2). matrix_apex_officinarum reports the peak scratch bytes of the last multiplication, elimination or normal form (0 when that call took the small path).
    symbolum matrix_apex_officinarum matrix_determinans matrix_multiplica · via lib/matrix.worklog.md
  10. possumnecan a matrix with zero rows or zero columns round-trip through text?
    No: any matrix with a zero dimension (r x 0 or 0 x c) prints as [], and [] reads back as 0 x 0, so the dimensions are lost. Keep the dimensions separately if you serialize such a matrix, for example an empty kernel.
    symbolum matrix_ad_chordam matrix_ex_chorda

mensura

include/mensura.h · lib/mensura.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I record a measurement during a build without slowing the build down?
    Fill a Mensura (sessio from mensura_sessionem_novam, id from mensura_id_novum, momentum from mensura_momentum_nunc, a titulus, a valor and a unitas such as "secunda") and append it with mensura_annotare: one TSV line in a plain diary file, no sqlite. After the run, mensura_condere moves the whole diary into the volumen in one transaction and then truncates the diary. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): a line that volumen_actum_appendere fails to store (e.g. another writer holds the volume past busy_timeout) is counted as malformed, the transaction still commits and the diary is still truncated, so that line is lost while successus is VERUM.
    symbolum mensura_annotare mensura_condere mensura_sessionem_novam mensura_id_novum mensura_momentum_nunc
  2. curwhy does mensura write to a diary file first instead of straight into the volumen?
    A sqlite write opens the database and commits a transaction, perhaps ten milliseconds. Across CL files that adds about a second to the very build being measured, and an instrument that changes what it measures gets switched off. Appending a line is nearly free; the fold into the volumen happens once, after the run.
    symbolum mensura_annotare mensura_condere · via lib/mensura.worklog.md
  3. possumnecan a measurement title or unit contain a tab or a newline?
    No. mensura_ad_lineam returns an empty chorda (and mensura_annotare FALSUM) when sessio, id, parens, titulus or unitas contains a TAB, LF or CR: a format that lets a field break it corrupts data silently, so it refuses instead of escaping. Run lines are the same, except that argv and nota are escaped (\\ \t \n \r) because a command is data.
    symbolum mensura_ad_lineam mensura_annotare mensura_cursus_initium_ad_lineam
  4. possumnecan I fold a diary with mensura_condere while other processes are still appending to it?
    Not safely: mensura_condere reads to the end, commits, then truncates the file, with no lock, so a line appended between the last read and the truncation is lost. bin/mensor's run fold (_cursus_condere) avoids this by renaming the diary first and folding the renamed copy (later appenders create a new file; it removes that copy afterwards even when the fold failed); 'mensor condere' folds the live measurement diary directly and has the race. Truncation after success is what stops the next fold from counting everything twice.
    symbolum mensura_condere · via tools/mensor.c
  5. quidwhat does MensuraFructus report after mensura_condere?
    successus, conditae (lines stored, measurement and run lines together) and causa. successus is VERUM even when some lines were malformed or could not be appended: those are skipped and counted in causa ("N lineae malformatae praeteritae"), never silently dropped - but they are gone once the diary is truncated. successus is FALSUM if an argument is NIHIL, the diary cannot be opened (also when it does not exist yet: causa "Diarium aperiri non potest"), or the transaction cannot start or commit; then nothing is truncated. A failed truncation is NOT reported: successus stays VERUM and the next fold stores the same lines again.
    symbolum MensuraFructus mensura_condere
  6. possumnecan a diary line be arbitrarily long?
    No. mensura_condere reads lines with fgets into a buffer of IV * MXXIV bytes (MENSURA_LINEA_MAXIMA in lib/mensura.c), so a line with more than IV * MXXIV - I bytes before its newline arrives in pieces; the pieces fail to parse, are counted malformed, and are then truncated away with the rest of the diary. The writers do not enforce this: nota is capped at CURSUS_NOTA_MAXIMA bytes, but argv is not capped at all, so a run started with a very long command line loses its start line at fold time, and mensura_cursus_legere then skips its end line too (an end without a start is dropped).
    symbolum mensura_condere mensura_cursus_initium_ad_lineam
  7. quomodohow do I record a run with a start, an end and an exit code so killed runs still show up?
    Write a CursusInitium with mensura_cursum_incipere when the run starts and a CursusFinis with the same id with mensura_cursum_finire when it ends; both are appended to the same diary. After mensura_condere, mensura_cursus_legere pairs them by id into a Xar of Cursus in start order: exit 0 is CURSUS_TRANSIIT, any other is CURSUS_FRACTUS, and a start with no end is CURSUS_INTERRUPTUS (killed, hung, or still running). Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): argv is not length-capped, so a run whose start line exceeds IV * MXXIV - I bytes is split by mensura_condere, counted malformed and truncated away, and its end line is then skipped: the run does not show up at all.
    symbolum mensura_cursum_incipere mensura_cursum_finire mensura_cursus_legere CursusStatus
  8. possumnecan I keep the Mensura that mensura_ex_linea gives me after the line buffer is gone?
    No: its chorda fields point INTO the line you passed (nothing is copied), so keep the line alive or copy the fields. In mensura_cursus_ex_linea only argv and nota are new chordae in the piscina (they are unescaped); the other fields alias the line too. Note that mensura_ex_linea only rejects a valor or momentum field with no leading number: "1.5abc" reads as 1.5.
    symbolum mensura_ex_linea mensura_cursus_ex_linea
  9. quomodohow do I read stored measurements back out of the volumen?
    mensura_mensuras_legere(volumen, praefixum, piscina) returns a Xar of Mensura whose titulus starts with the prefix (an empty prefix gives all), in stored order; NIHIL if the volume cannot be read. It reads every event in the volume each call. For questions about runs (history, failures, trends) use the named queries of bin/mensor quaestio.
    symbolum mensura_mensuras_legere mensura_cursus_legere · imperium bin/mensor quaestio
  10. curwhy is momentum an s64 and not an unsigned type?
    The clock is gettimeofday, which is not monotonic: NTP can step it back, so the difference of two readings can be negative. A signed type shows that; an unsigned one would wrap it to a huge positive number and make a branch silently never run. For a run's duration CursusFinis carries duratio_ms from a monotonic clock.
    symbolum mensura_momentum_nunc

mensura_quaestio

include/mensura_quaestio.h · lib/mensura_quaestio.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodowhich test sections use the most memory?
    bin/mensor quaestio graves [n=N] [titulus=MEMBRUM] [-machina]. Every section that fabrica ran with a current credo leaves three facts on its own run, in octets: credo.arena_apex (peak use of the section's piscina), credo.apex_auctus (how much the process's lifetime peak rose while it ran) and credo.vestigium (footprint at its end). cursus_quaerere_graves takes the maximum of each across runs per section title and orders by the larger of the first two, the number a section's memory ceiling judges. titulus= without '#' means the sections of that member; n defaults to XX in mensor. Sections run outside fabrica, or by a credo older than the fourteen-field SECTIO line, have no facts and do not appear.
    symbolum cursus_quaerere_graves CursusGravis · imperium bin/mensor quaestio

modulus

include/modulus.h · lib/modulus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quidwhat is a modulus, and which of its three coordinate spaces do drawing commands use?
    A Modulus is the cell size in our pixels plus the surface extent (also in our pixels), and two screen ratios schirmus_x and schirmus_y. Drawing commands are in OUR pixels; cells are derived from them, and screen pixels would come from the ratios, which are reserved: modulus_creare sets them to 1/1 and no function reads them yet. The window takes its modulus from its font (delineare_mandata_modulus, VI x VIII); the terminal accepts any.
    symbolum modulus_creare delineare_mandata_modulus
  2. quomodohow do I convert a pixel coordinate to a cell column or line?
    For the cell that CONTAINS a point use modulus_columna / modulus_linea: they take the floor, so x = -1 gives column -1, not 0. For an EDGE (filled rectangles, clipping) use modulus_columna_proxima / modulus_linea_proxima, which round to the nearest cell boundary, half up: with a cell width of 6, x = 9 gives 2 and x = 8 gives 1. Both are exact for negative coordinates because the library does the floor division by hand instead of trusting C89 division of negatives. The _proxima functions compute 2x + cell in s32, so keep coordinates well below 2^30 or the sum overflows.
    symbolum modulus_columna modulus_linea modulus_columna_proxima modulus_linea_proxima
  3. quomodohow do I measure how big a string will be on screen?
    Call modulus_textum_metiri with a ModulusMensor. Width = columns of the longest line times cellula_latitudo; height = number of lines times cellula_altitudo, where lines = 1 + the count of newlines (so text ending in a newline has an extra empty line, and empty text is 0 x 0). Take the mensor from the target scope: delineare_mandata_mensor for the window (one cell per decoded UTF-8 step, invalid bytes included) or tessellatio_mensor for the terminal (rune widths under a RunaePolitica, wide = 2). The text is a chorda, so it need not be NUL-terminated.
    symbolum modulus_textum_metiri delineare_mandata_mensor tessellatio_mensor
  4. curwhy does the same CJK character measure 6 pixels in the window but 12 in the terminal?
    Text is measured by the scope that draws it. The window's 6x8 font paints every rune in exactly one cell (ASCII and Latin-1 only, everything else is a TOFU box, combining marks included), so the FONTIS mensor counts one cell per rune. The terminal gives wide runes two cells, so the RUNARUM mensor uses runae_latitudo_textus. Measuring with the wrong mensor makes layout disagree with what is painted.
    symbolum modulus_textum_metiri runae_latitudo_textus
  5. possumnecan I fill in a Modulus struct by hand instead of calling modulus_creare?
    You can, but then nothing protects you: modulus_creare clamps a cell size of 0 or less to 1 and a negative extent to 0, and the conversion functions (modulus_columna, modulus_linea, the two _proxima functions and modulus_extensio_cellularum) divide by the cell size without checking. A hand-built Modulus with cellula_latitudo 0 divides by zero in all of them. The library allocates nothing and keeps no state, so modulus_creare is cheap to call.
    symbolum modulus_creare modulus_columna modulus_extensio_cellularum
  6. quomodohow do I find how many whole cells fit on the surface?
    Call modulus_extensio_cellularum(m, &columnae, &lineae): it divides the extent by the cell size, so a partial cell at the right or bottom edge is not counted (a 100-pixel-wide surface with 6-pixel cells gives 16 columns). After modulus_creare the extent is never negative and the cell size is at least 1, so this plain division is the floor.
    symbolum modulus_extensio_cellularum modulus_creare

motus

include/motus.h · lib/motus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quidwhat is a Motus and why does it not write to the insula on every event?
    The mobile tier: state that changes on every event (pan, zoom, pointer capture, the pending stroke, an app's gesture) lives in a plain struct in memory, and only the quiet point writes it to the insula. Painting happens per event, writing happens IN QUIET: mutare_motum is the one gate for changes and never touches the insula; motus_effundere is the one quiet-time write (lint L4 allows writes of the mobile fields only from its quies seat).
    symbolum Motus mutare_motum motus_effundere · via lib/motus.worklog.md
  2. quomodohow do I change pan or zoom from an event handler so it gets saved later?
    Write a MotusMutator (vacuum fn(Motus*, vacuum* ctx)) that edits motus->pan or motus->zoom, and run it through mutare_motum(motus, fn, ctx, ev->tempus): it records the time and marks the Motus dirty. The dispensator then checks motus_quies with its own quies_ms on each event and calls motus_effundere, which writes pan_x, pan_y and zoom with insula_attributum_ponere (replacing, so a second flush does not double them) and clears the dirty flag when the write was accepted. Get the dispatcher's Motus with dispensator_motus.
    symbolum mutare_motum MotusMutator motus_quies motus_effundere dispensator_motus
  3. quomodohow do I save an in-progress edit debounced, with its own quiet interval, and never lose it on quit?
    Attach the app's state once with motus_gestum_ponere(motus, status, effusor, ctx, quies_ms) (scriba uses SCRIBA_QUIES_MS, one second). Change it only through mutare_gestum, which does nothing if no gesture is attached. The dispatcher calls motus_gestum_effundere when motus_gestus_quies says the gesture's own interval has passed, and again in dispensator_finire. The gesture's dirty flag is separate from pan/zoom's: changing one never dirties the other.
    symbolum motus_gestum_ponere mutare_gestum motus_gestus_quies motus_gestum_effundere dispensator_finire scriba_gestum_ponere
  4. curwhy does my gesture stay dirty after motus_gestum_effundere?
    The effusor returned FALSUM. A refusing effusor leaves the gesture dirty on purpose so the next quiet point tries again (and vicus refuses to switch away from an app whose dirty gesture will not flush, since the text would be lost). The call also returns FALSUM, and does nothing, when no gesture is attached, no effusor was given, or nothing changed since the last flush.
    symbolum motus_gestum_effundere MotusGestusEffusor · via lib/vicus.c
  5. possumnecan I flush the Motus from inside an action without losing the action's writer name?
    For the gesture, yes: motus_gestum_effundere sets the writer to "gestus" for the effusor and then restores the previous writer (fixed 2026-10-05, because scriba flushes on Esc and dd inside an action). motus_effundere does not: it writes as "motus" and then sets the writer to an empty chorda, so later writes in the same action are anonymous and owned attributes refuse them silently. Set the writer again after calling it. The dispatcher also calls motus_effundere itself when focus moves into another branch while pan/zoom is dirty.
    symbolum motus_gestum_effundere motus_effundere insula_scriptorem_ponere · via lib/motus.worklog.md
  6. possumnecan I rely on the pending stroke in ictus_pendens being saved at quiet time?
    No. motus_effundere writes only pan_x, pan_y and zoom. The pending stroke (a Xar of Punctum created by motus_initiare) never reaches the insula; a finished stroke leaves through a document action, which goes into history. Capture and stroke are thrown away when vicus switches away from an app (xar_vacare on ictus_pendens).
    symbolum motus_effundere motus_initiare
  7. quomodohow do I know which insula branch motus_effundere writes into?
    It takes the first one set: motus->ramus_intimus (the innermost focused branch the dispatcher found along the focus chain), else motus->ramus (the active branch the host set, for example the focused side in vicus), else insula_ramus_radix(repo). A branch counts as set when its repo is not NIHIL; motus_initiare zeroes both, so a standalone app writes to the root.
    symbolum motus_effundere insula_ramus_radix mutare_ramum
  8. quomodohow does a component tell whether its branch has focus?
    Call motus_ramus_focatus(motus, ramus). It is VERUM when there is no Motus, when the branch is the root (empty via, an app on its own), or when motus->spatium equals the branch's via. The scriba, pictor, kalendarium, nomenclator, colloquia and admonitiones panes use it to decide whether to draw their focus indication.
    symbolum motus_ramus_focatus

norma

include/norma.h · lib/norma.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I declare a schema for a JSON object and check a parsed value against it?
    Build nodes with the builders (norma_objectum, then norma_campus(obj, titulus, valor, requiritur) per field, with norma_integer, norma_textus and the rest as values; modifiers like norma_intra return the node so they nest). Then norma_iudicare(n, valor, piscina) returns a NormaIudicium: validum, and vitia listing EVERY problem (not just the first) as NormaVitium with a path such as $.content[2].input.n, a causa and a nuntius (up to CCLVI, see the cap). The xars, paths and messages are allocated in the piscina you pass; the one exception is a NORMA_CAUSA_SCHEMA_PRAVA nuntius, which is the node's own string in the builders' piscina.
    symbolum norma_objectum norma_campus norma_integer norma_intra norma_iudicare · via tools/norma.c
  2. quomodohow do I find out that I misused a builder, like norma_intra on a norma_numerus?
    Builders never fail loudly: they record the first misuse on the node they were called on and return it (a bad norma_campus or norma_variatio marks the object or union, not the field). norma_iudicare walks the whole schema first and, if any node is marked, returns a single NORMA_CAUSA_SCHEMA_PRAVA at $ (the nuntius says what) and judges nothing. norma_visus(n).error_schematis shows the mark on one node. norma_json_schema does NOT check the marks: it exports a misbuilt schema without complaint.
    symbolum norma_iudicare norma_visus norma_intra norma_json_schema
  3. possumnecan a JSON value like 7.0 pass a norma_integer?
    No: INTEGER accepts only values the JSON parser read as integers, and the parser reads any number containing '.', 'e' or 'E' as a float, so 7.0, 7.5 and 1e3 all fail with NORMA_CAUSA_GENUS. Use norma_numerus to accept both. Bounds are typed to match: norma_intra (s64) works only on an integer node and norma_intra_fluitans (f64) only on a numerus node; the wrong pairing marks the schema as misbuilt.
    symbolum norma_integer norma_numerus norma_intra norma_intra_fluitans
  4. quomodohow do I let an object have keys the schema does not declare?
    norma_modus(obj, m). NORMA_CLAUSUM (the default) makes each extra key an error (NORMA_CAUSA_EXTRA). NORMA_APERTUM allows them silently. NORMA_NOTANDUM allows them but reports each in j.notae, and the value stays valid. The same modes govern unknown tags on a discriminated union. Notes stop silently after CCLVI.
    symbolum norma_modus norma_iudicare NormaIudicium
  5. quomodohow do I validate a tagged union like content blocks with a type field?
    norma_discrimen(p, "type"), then norma_variatio(d, "text", obj) once per tag value. Each variant must be a norma_objectum, and the tag key counts as declared in it, so CLAUSUM does not reject it. Extra keys inside a matched variant follow the VARIANT object's own norma_modus; the union's modus governs only unknown tags. A missing or non-string tag gives NORMA_CAUSA_DISCRIMEN, and an unknown tag gives NORMA_CAUSA_VARIATIO (subject to norma_modus); both point at the tag's path. vates judges Anthropic responses this way (lib/vates_responsum_norma.c).
    symbolum norma_discrimen norma_variatio norma_modus · via lib/vates_responsum_norma.c
  6. quomodohow do I turn a norma into a JSON Schema for a tool's input_schema?
    norma_json_schema(n, p) returns a JsonValor with a fixed key order and no $schema key. additionalProperties false is written only for CLAUSUM objects, and norma_aut_nullum becomes "type": [X, "null"] (on a union, an extra {"type": "null"} branch in oneOf). A discriminated union becomes oneOf over its variants with the tag as a const, whatever its modus: an unknown tag that an APERTUM or NOTANDUM judge accepts is still rejected by the exported schema. Same kind of gap for norma_electio plus norma_aut_nullum: the judge accepts null, but the export writes "type": ["string", "null"] next to an enum that does not list null, so a JSON Schema validator rejects null.
    symbolum norma_json_schema VatesInstrumentum norma_electio norma_aut_nullum
  7. possumnecan a schema contain itself, for a recursive JSON shape?
    No: the header says recursion is not supported yet. A node may be shared by several parents (a DAG), but nothing detects a cycle. norma_campus(o, "x", o, FALSUM) is accepted, and norma_iudicare on it then recurses without end in its schema walk (so does norma_json_schema).
    symbolum norma_campus norma_tabulatum norma_iudicare norma_json_schema
  8. quidwhat does norma_longitudo count, bytes or characters?
    For a textus it counts runes (code points, via utf8_numerare_runas; malformed UTF-8 is counted, not rejected: a stray byte counts as one rune); for a tabulatum it counts elements. It is an error on any other genus. Bounds are i32 and inclusive.
    symbolum norma_longitudo utf8_numerare_runas
  9. quidwhich norma_forma names does the judge actually check?
    Four: date-time (must parse with fasti_ex_iso), uuid (8-4-4-4-12 hex), email (exactly one @, not first; the last dot after it is neither right after the @ nor the final character) and uri (a scheme, a colon, then something). Any other name is never judged and is only a hint for the generator (norma_gignere). These are shape checks, not full RFC validation.
    symbolum norma_forma fasti_ex_iso norma_gignere
  10. curwhy does a norma error message never show the bad value, and why does judging stop at 256 errors?
    The judged value may be huge or secret, so nuntius describes the problem and its path but never echoes the value. The cap of CCLVI means a hostile value cannot produce a hostile number of issues: after it, one NORMA_CAUSA_LIMES at $ is added and judging stops (a 1000-element wrong-typed array gives 257 records, not 1000).
    symbolum norma_iudicare NormaVitium · via project-specs/norma-spec.md lib/norma.worklog.md
  11. possumnecan a field be missing or null without failing?
    Missing and null are different. An absent field fails (NORMA_CAUSA_DEEST) only if norma_campus was called with requiritur VERUM. A field that is present with JSON null fails with NORMA_CAUSA_GENUS unless its node has norma_aut_nullum, or is norma_nullum or norma_liberum (liberum accepts anything).
    symbolum norma_campus norma_aut_nullum norma_liberum
  12. quidhow is a key that is not a plain identifier written in a NormaVitium path?
    A key matching [A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]* is appended as .clavis; any other key (empty, with a dot, a quote or a non-ASCII rune) is written as ["..."] with JSON escaping, so $["a.b"] and $["q\""]. Array elements are [i]. DEEST and EXTRA point at the key's path, DISCRIMEN and VARIATIO at the tag's, LIMES and SCHEMA_PRAVA at $.
    symbolum NormaVitium norma_iudicare chorda_aedificator_appendere_evasus_json

numerus_romanus

include/numerus_romanus.h · lib/numerus_romanus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I check whether a word like XIV or xii is a roman numeral and get its value?
    numerus_romanus_legere(s, &valor) returns VERUM and stores the sum when s is a numeral; pass NIHIL for valor to only test. An empty chorda is FALSUM. All upper case or all lower case is accepted (the case is taken from the first character), mixed case such as Xii is not. capitula and paginatio use it to tell chapter and preface page labels from ordinary words. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): non-canonical forms with an additive letter before a subtractive pair of the same place, such as VIX, VIV, LXC, DCD and MMMDCM (4400, above 3999), still return VERUM with their summed value.
    symbolum numerus_romanus_legere
  2. curwhy does numerus_romanus_legere reject DID, CIVIC and MIMIC when every letter is in IVXLCDM?
    On purpose: a loose all-letters-are-numerals rule would read English words (DID, MILD, CIVIC, VIM, DIM, LIVID, MIMIC) as numbers in a chapter index or a page label. The reader only allows the subtractive pairs IV IX XL XC CD CM, at most three repeats, no repeated V L D, and each group must start with a letter smaller than the one that started the group before. MIX is still accepted, because 1009 really is MIX. The check is per letter, not per value: it never checks the larger letter of a subtractive pair against the group before, so non-canonical forms such as VIX (14), VIV (9), LXC (140) and even MMMDCM (4400, above MMMCMXCIX) also pass. Lower-case vix is therefore read as 14.
    symbolum numerus_romanus_legere · via include/numerus_romanus.h
  3. quomodohow do I turn an integer into its roman numeral?
    numerus_romanus_scribere(n, piscina) gives the canonical upper-case subtractive form (106 -> CVI, 3999 -> MMMCMXCIX) in the piscina. It only covers 1 to 3999: 0, n > 3999 and a NIHIL piscina return an empty chorda (datum NIHIL, mensura 0), so test mensura before using it. Like every chorda the result is not NUL-terminated.
    symbolum numerus_romanus_scribere
  4. quomodohow do I write a number above 3999 (or zero) as a latina.h expression in generated C or a message?
    numerus_romanus_exprimere(n, &compositum, piscina) works for any i64 n >= 0: 0 gives ZEPHYRUM, up to 3999 the numeral, a multiple of 1024 that is not a round thousand gives IV * MXXIV, everything else groups of thousands such as V * M + CCLXXX. A term too big for int is prefixed (i64). compositum is set to VERUM when the result contains an operator, so the caller can add parentheses where precedence needs them (x / (IV * MXXIV)). Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): the (i64) prefix is missing or misplaced when the sum of thousand groups exceeds int, so n = 2147483649 gives MMCXLVII * M * M + CDLXXXIII * M + DCXLIX (int overflow, clang -Werror rejects it) and n = 2199023256576 gives (i64)(MMCXLVII * M * M + CDLXXXIII * M + DCXLIX) * MXXIV, whose inner sum overflows before the cast.
    symbolum numerus_romanus_exprimere · via tools/latina_numeri.c
  5. possumnecan I pass a negative number to numerus_romanus_exprimere?
    No, and nothing warns you. A negative n takes the n <= 3999 branch and is cast to i32, which is unsigned in this house, so numerus_romanus_scribere sees a value above 3999 and the result is an empty chorda with compositum FALSUM (only an n below about minus four billion could wrap round to a small value and print a numeral). There is no minus-sign form: write the sign yourself and pass the magnitude.
    symbolum numerus_romanus_exprimere numerus_romanus_scribere
  6. curwhy does 128000 come out as CXXVIII * M and not CXXV * MXXIV?
    The binary form is chosen only for a multiple of 1024 that is NOT also a round thousand; round thousands always stay thousands. The tie-break was decided when the function was written (worklog 2026-09-26), so 4096 is IV * MXXIV but 128000 is CXXVIII * M.
    symbolum numerus_romanus_exprimere · via lib/numerus_romanus.worklog.md
  7. quidwhat are the roman numeral macros like CVI and MXXIV, and where do they come from?
    latina.h defines every numeral from ZEPHYRUM to MMMCMXCIX as a macro for its value, so the house writes constants as XXIV or IV * MXXIV instead of digits. That section is GENERATED by tools/latina_numeri.c from numerus_romanus_scribere, and the generata gate checks it. Because every numeral is a macro, no identifier may be spelled like one.
    symbolum numerus_romanus_scribere · via include/latina.h tools/latina_numeri.c

organum

include/organum.h · lib/organum.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I load and validate a widget manifest from its text?
    organum_legere(fons, piscina, intern, &vitia) parses the STML, judges it against the embedded organum canon, then applies the loader rules. It returns NIHIL if there is ANY vitium, and *vitia is always set to a Xar of chorda messages (empty when the manifest is sound), each prefixed 'linea N:' (only a broken embedded canon gives an unprefixed message). Beware: today the loader formats several messages with sprintf into fixed stack buffers, so an over-long value (an absens of a few hundred bytes that does not fit its typus) overflows the stack; keep manifest values short. Apps normally do not call it directly: organum_legere_genus in organum_montatio reads the embedded manifest plus the common base and prints the vitia.
    symbolum organum_legere organum_legere_genus
  2. possumnecan organum_legere read the manifest file for me?
    No: the loader never touches the disk; the caller passes the text. Runtime manifests are embedded in lib/organa_infixa.c and come from organum_infixum_textus(genus, piscina); organum_legere_viam reads from disk for tests and tools. The organum canon itself is embedded too, so the loader needs no file at all.
    symbolum organum_legere organum_infixum_textus organum_legere_viam · via lib/organa_infixa.c
  3. curwhy are some manifest errors reported by the canon and others by the loader?
    The canon judges shape (closed vocabulary, choice lists, unique names, scriptor citing a declared actio). Rules that involve several attributes at once or split a value are the loader's, because the canon dialect deliberately does not express them: sedes="motus" needs rhythmus="mobilis" and vita="transiens", typus="series" needs sedes="motus", orbatus needs referentia, referentia must be 'ligamen/genus' with a declared ligamen, absens must fit its typus, and optio appears only (and at least once) under typus="electio". The loader rules run only when the canon found nothing. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): these loader messages are sprintf'd without a bound into fixed stack buffers (256 bytes for the absens and unknown-ligamen messages, 512 for the campus-title prefix), so a rule violation whose absens, referentia or campus nomen is a few hundred bytes long overflows the stack instead of being reported.
    symbolum organum_legere · via organum.canon lib/organum.worklog.md
  4. quidwhat do vita, rhythmus and sedes on a campus mean?
    They classify one piece of widget state. vita (required): persistens (saved), transiens (never reaches disk) or probatio (test-only fields). rhythmus: discretus (discrete edits, the default) or mobilis (kinetic, changes continuously during a gesture). sedes: insula (an attribute of the widget's insula branch, the default) or motus (in-flight gesture state kept in memory structs next to Motus, never in the insula; the loader demands rhythmus mobilis and vita transiens for it). Only sedes="insula" fields go into the generated canon, starting values and owners table.
    symbolum OrganumCampus organum_campus · via organum.canon project-specs/status-genera-census.md
  5. quomodohow do I look up one field of a loaded manifest and its default?
    organum_campus(o, titulus) returns the OrganumCampus or NIHIL; organum_campus_index and organum_numerus_camporum walk them in manifest order (base fields first after organum_basim_addere). All chorda members are NOT NUL-terminated and an absent attribute is an empty chorda (mensura 0, datum NIHIL); use habet_absens to tell absens="" from no absens at all.
    symbolum organum_campus organum_campus_index organum_numerus_camporum
  6. quomodohow do I merge the common base fields into a widget manifest?
    organum_basim_addere(organum, basis) puts the base campi BEFORE the widget's own and adds the base actiones the widget does not already declare. It returns FALSUM and adds nothing if any base campus title already exists in the widget. Base ligamina and portae are not merged, and the loader rules are not run again on the combined set.
    symbolum organum_basim_addere organum_legere_viam
  7. quomodohow do I get the insula canon and starting values generated from a manifest?
    organum_canonem_generare builds a canon whose root element is the organum genus, one attributum per sedes="insula" campus (electio options copied and escaped); NIHIL with *causa set if canon_legere refuses it. organum_initium returns the starting attributes as text, ' titulus="absens"' for each insula campus with an absens (leading space, values not escaped). organum_dominos_generare builds the <domini> owners table from campi with a scriptor.
    symbolum organum_canonem_generare organum_initium organum_dominos_generare
  8. quidwhat is the difference between ligamina, portae and actiones in a manifest?
    ligamina are entities the widget shows (props; a="parens" means the mounting parent supplies the value) and are what referentia 'ligamen/genus' points at. portae are the observed outside world, never stored, with modus petitio or eventus. actiones are the names a campus scriptor may cite; systema="verum" marks one the system performs. Each has a numerus/index accessor pair.
    symbolum organum_ligamen_index organum_porta_index organum_actio_index

organum_montatio

include/organum_montatio.h · lib/organum_montatio.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I mount a widget on an insula branch from its manifest?
    Two calls: organum_legere_genus(piscina, intern, "genus", praefixum) gets the embedded manifest merged with organum_commune, then organum_ramum_parare(organum, &ramus, systemata, attributa_addita, clavis, piscina, intern) puts the generated canon on the branch, creates the starting element, restores saved state, installs owners and registers with dimissio. lib/colloquia_visus.c mounts on insula_ramus(repo, "colloquia", id) or, without an id, on insula_ramus_radix(repo).
    symbolum organum_legere_genus organum_ramum_parare insula_ramus insula_ramus_radix · via lib/colloquia_visus.c
  2. quidwhat is the difference between organum_legere_genus and organum_legere_viam?
    organum_legere_genus reads the manifest COMPILED INTO the binary (lib/organa_infixa.c, generated by tools/organa_infigere.sh from every apps/GENUS/organum.stml); apps use it, so an installed binary runs from any directory. organum_legere_viam reads a manifest from disk (radix + "/" + via) plus radix/apps/organum_commune.stml, for tests and tools; with radix NIHIL both paths are relative to the cwd. Both return NIHIL on failure and print parse errors or a missing (or empty) manifest to stderr with your praefixum ("organum" when NIHIL); a NIHIL argument and a base-field collision return NIHIL silently.
    symbolum organum_legere_genus organum_legere_viam organum_infixum_textus · via lib/organa_infixa.c tools/organa_infigere.sh
  3. curwhy does my edited apps/X/organum.stml not change the running app?
    Apps read the embedded copy, never the disk: two sources of truth would let an installed binary silently differ from the tree, so there is no fallback. Regenerate lib/organa_infixa.c with tools/organa_infigere.sh (the fabrica action organa_infixa) and rebuild. probatio_organum_montatio (section infixa_fontes) names any stale manifest (worklog S7c-1).
    symbolum organum_legere_genus · via tools/organa_infigere.sh lib/organa_infixa.c probationes/probatio_organum_montatio.c lib/organum.worklog.md
  4. quomodohow do I add my own attributes to the widget's starting element?
    Pass them as attributa_addita, raw STML attribute text that BEGINS WITH A SPACE, e.g. " focus=\"admonitiones\"" (lib/admonitiones_visus.c). It is pasted unchanged after the manifest defaults (organum_initium) in <genus id="..." defaults addita/>, so a missing space or an unescaped quote breaks the element and the mount fails with "elementum initiale". NIHIL = none. On a child branch an element that already exists is left untouched (your attributes are not applied); on the root branch only the attributes the root lacks are added.
    symbolum organum_ramum_parare organum_initium · via lib/admonitiones_visus.c
  5. quidwhat are StatusSystemata and the clavis argument for?
    StatusSystemata holds the repository-wide systems: conservatio (saved state), relatio, dimissio. A NIHIL field means that system is absent, and a NIHIL pointer means none (tests). With conservatio and a non-NIHIL clavis, organum_ramum_parare restores the branch's saved fields under that key; dimissio registers the branch's popover fields. relatio is NOT touched: binding the document stays with the widget. For nested mounts pass the branch path as clavis, since the same id under different parents would share one saved record.
    symbolum StatusSystemata organum_ramum_parare conservatio_ramum_addere dimissio_ramum_addere
  6. possumnecan a failed organum_ramum_parare leave the branch partly set up?
    Yes. The canon is set first (on the repository for the root branch, otherwise on the branch), then the element is initiated; if initiation fails it returns FALSUM with the canon already in place. After a successful initiation the restore, owners and dimissio steps ignore their own results, so VERUM does not prove that state was restored.
    symbolum organum_ramum_parare insula_ponere_canonem insula_ramus_canonem_ponere insula_ramum_initiare
  7. possumnecan I tell why organum_legere_viam returned NIHIL when the manifest parsed fine?
    Not from stderr: parse errors and a missing file are printed, but when organum_basim_addere refuses the merge (a field in your manifest has the same name as one in organum_commune) both readers return NIHIL and print nothing. Rename the colliding field.
    symbolum organum_legere_viam organum_legere_genus organum_basim_addere

pecunia

include/pecunia.h · lib/pecunia.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I split a bill so the cents add up?
    Use pecunia_dividere(total, n, PECUNIA_GRANUM_UNITAS, piscina, parts) for equal shares, or pecunia_partiri with an array of ratios for weighted ones. Both use largest remainder: the parts always sum to the total exactly (USD 10.00 / 3 = 3.34, 3.33, 3.33), ties go to the earlier part, and a zero ratio never gets a leftover cent. With PECUNIA_GRANUM_UNITAS the total must already be payable, otherwise PECUNIA_VITIUM_NON_SOLVIBILIS.
    symbolum pecunia_dividere pecunia_partiri
  2. quomodohow do I store an amount in an event or a file?
    Write it with pecunia_ad_textum ("USD -12.3400": upper-case code, one space, always four decimals) and read it back with pecunia_ex_textu, which accepts ONLY that exact form. Every value has one spelling, so equal text means equal value and diffs stay clean. Never store the display form from pecunia_formare.
    symbolum pecunia_ad_textum pecunia_ex_textu
  3. curwhy is an amount someone typed refused?
    pecunia_ex_ingressu is lenient only by fixed rules and returns a named PecuniaVitium - pass it to pecunia_vitium_titulus for a message. Group separators must sit in real positions ("1,234.56" yes, "12,34" no - that could mean 1234 or 12.34, and the library refuses rather than guesses), only one sign is allowed, at most four decimals, and a currency code in the text must match the expected currency.
    symbolum pecunia_ex_ingressu pecunia_vitium_titulus
  4. quomodohow do I round an amount to cents (or to the currency's minor unit)?
    pecunia_ad_unitatem(amount, modus, &result) rounds to the minor unit from the ISO table (USD 3.4590 -> 3.46 with PECUNIA_PAR; JPY to whole yen). The mode is always explicit: PECUNIA_PAR (banker's), PECUNIA_DIMIDIUM_SURSUM (half away from zero), PECUNIA_VERSUS_NIHIL, PECUNIA_SOLUM, PECUNIA_TECTUM. pecunia_solvibilis tells you whether an amount is already a whole number of minor units.
    symbolum pecunia_ad_unitatem pecunia_solvibilis
  5. quomodohow do I convert an amount to another currency?
    Pass the rate as an exact Fractio (units of the target per one unit of the source, greater than zero) to pecunia_convertere with a rounding mode. The product is exact and rounded once to four decimals; call pecunia_ad_unitatem afterwards for a payable amount. pecunia never fetches rates and never mixes currencies silently - adding USD to EUR is PECUNIA_VITIUM_MONETAE_DIVERSAE.
    symbolum pecunia_convertere
  6. quomodohow do I read and write amounts in Lunch Money's API format?
    Lunch Money sends amounts as strings matching ^-?\d+(\.\d{1,4})?$ in any currency. pecunia_ex_numero(text, moneta, &amount) reads exactly that (leading zeros allowed, five decimals refused as PECUNIA_VITIUM_DECIMALES); pecunia_numerus writes "-12.3400". The currency comes separately, from pecunia_moneta_ex_codice, which accepts Lunch Money's lower-case codes.
    symbolum pecunia_ex_numero pecunia_numerus
  7. possumnecan I use crypto amounts such as BTC or ETH?
    No. Every amount is an s64 count of ten-thousandths (PECUNIA_SCALA), which covers all ISO currencies but not 18-decimal crypto units, so pecunia_moneta_ex_codice refuses BTC and ETH as PECUNIA_VITIUM_MONETA_IGNOTA. Withdrawn currencies (BGN, HRK...) and GGP/IMP/JEP are in the table, marked by status.
    symbolum pecunia_moneta_ex_codice PECUNIA_SCALA

pictor_applicatio

include/pictor_applicatio.h · lib/pictor_applicatio.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I start the pictor drawing app from a new main, window or terminal?
    Call thema_initiare yourself (glue's job), open the volume with pictor_volumen_aperire(piscina, argc, argv, &fumus) (-fumus = temporary volume, -volumen PATH, default "pictor.volumen"), then pictor_applicatio_aedificare with the surface size in our pixels. Hand app.d, app.figurae and &app.montatio.figurae_ctx to your glue (ludus_fenestra or ludus_tessera). pictor_volumen_aperire returns NIHIL if the volume cannot be opened; pictor_applicatio_aedificare returns FALSUM if any part (repository, mount, dispensator) fails or an argument is NIHIL. The header promises a reason on stderr, but lib/pictor_applicatio.c itself prints one only when the document cannot be opened or created ("pictor: documentum").
    symbolum pictor_volumen_aperire pictor_applicatio_aedificare PictorApplicatio · via apps/pictor/pictor.c apps/pictor/pictor_terminalis.c
  2. quomodohow do I mount pictor as one pane inside a host repository such as vicus?
    pictor_montare(m, piscina, intern, volumen, repo, id, latitudo, altitudo, systemata) creates the branch <pictor id> if absent and opens (or creates) the drawing stored in spatium id; id NIHIL mounts at the repository root, which must already be a pictor element. The PictorMontatio holds the contexts its registries point to, so give it a stable address and never copy it. systemata may be NIHIL (tests).
    symbolum pictor_montare PictorMontatio · via lib/vicus_applicatio.c
  3. quomodohow do I show a different drawing in a mounted pictor pane, or create a new one?
    pictor_picturam_ponere(m, spatium) shows an existing drawing; it returns FALSUM unless the drawing is already in the pane's document table or listed by pictor_documenta_enumerare (manifest origin "pictor:documentum"), so a scriba document is never opened as a canvas. pictor_picturam_condere(m, spatium) creates a blank one at the pane's new-canvas size and shows it; it returns FALSUM if the name is empty or already listed by pictor_documenta_enumerare. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): when the pane is a PictorApplicatio's montatio, either call leaves app.doc pointing at the old document, so read app.montatio.doc afterwards.
    symbolum pictor_picturam_ponere pictor_picturam_condere pictor_documenta_enumerare
  4. possumnecan I keep using PictorApplicatio.doc after the shown drawing changes?
    No: despite its header comment "= montatio.doc", PictorApplicatio.doc is a copy taken once in pictor_applicatio_aedificare. pictor_picturam_ponere re-points montatio.doc and the doc of the action, figure and composition contexts, but not app.doc, so read app.montatio.doc for the drawing on screen. app.actiones and app.figurae stay valid: a swap changes the contexts, never the registries. The standalone app never swaps by itself (worklog: only vicus panes do).
    symbolum PictorApplicatio pictor_picturam_ponere pictor_applicatio_aedificare
  5. quidwhat size does a new pictor canvas get?
    The surface minus one cell (VI pixels) on each side in width, and minus (III status lines + II) cells of VIII pixels in height, with a floor of LXIV pixels each way. The surface is the latitudo and altitudo given to pictor_montare (kept in montatio.compositio.fenestra_latitudo/altitudo), so a canvas made later by pictor_picturam_condere uses the mount-time size even after the window was resized. A drawing that already exists keeps its own size whatever the pane size.
    symbolum pictor_montare pictor_picturam_condere
  6. curwhy does PictorMontatio keep a table of documents by spatium?
    Each PictorDocumentum is about a megabyte of pixels plus history in the arena, so reopening it on every swap would leak. The table also guarantees one document object per drawing; vicus shares one table across all pictor panes so two panes on the same drawing write one history. The root spatium "" is stored under key "." because tabula_dispersa takes no empty key.
    symbolum PictorMontatio pictor_picturam_ponere · via lib/pictor_applicatio.worklog.md

pictor_componentia

include/pictor_componentia.h · lib/pictor_componentia.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I get pictor's component tree on screen; who calls pictor_componere?
    You normally do not call it yourself: it has the dispensator's componere shape, so pictor_montare fills a PictorCompositio and pictor_applicatio passes pictor_componere and that compositio as ctx to dispensator_creare (vicus does the same through its VicusFacies). Each frame it reads the insula state and the Motus and returns a fresh Componens tree in the piscina it is given. Any of repo, piscina, intern or ctx NIHIL returns NIHIL.
    symbolum pictor_componere dispensator_creare pictor_montare PictorCompositio · via lib/pictor_applicatio.c lib/vicus_applicatio.c
  2. quomodohow do I set up a PictorCompositio for a test or a new host?
    memset the WHOLE struct to zero first (the header asks for it: ramus.repo NIHIL means use the repository root), then set fenestra_latitudo and fenestra_altitudo (the surface used until the state carries superficies_latitudo and superficies_altitudo), cellula_latitudo and cellula_altitudo (layout is in cells; 0 is treated as I), status_lineae (the bar height in cells) and, if you want the layers button and panel, doc.
    symbolum PictorCompositio pictor_componere · via probationes/probatio_pictor_componentia.c
  3. quidwhat nodes does the pictor tree contain and what are they for?
    radix (action instrumentum.eligere, where keys bubble) holds prospectus (the clipped viewport with the pan and zoom transform), status (the tool bar at the bottom, a fixed number of cell lines, which holds the bar squares), the quadratum.strata button when a document is set and no paste is pending, and the open palette as the LAST child so it is drawn on top and hit first. prospectus holds tabula, the canvas, one cell in from its edge so the page border shows. Roles are data (ids, titles, actions), not per-component types; the golden tree is pictor.arbor.stml.
    symbolum pictor_componere · via probationes/pictor/pictor.arbor.stml
  4. curwhy does the canvas get its action name from the current tool instead of one handler that switches on the tool?
    The action is chosen at compose time as a pure function of state: pictor_actio_instrumenti maps penicillus, aspergillum, spongia and linea to name.ictus and anything else to the empty string (no action), so handlers never branch on the tool. During a pending paste the canvas gets glutinatio.agere instead.
    symbolum pictor_actio_instrumenti pictor_componere · via lib/pictor_componentia.worklog.md
  5. curwhy is the stroke in progress copied into tabula.puncta instead of read from Motus when painting?
    So that drawing and every figura read the tree alone (decision 2026-09-05), and the tree snapshot shows a stroke in flight. The copy is made only while the mouse capture is tabula, otherwise a layer-row drag in the layers panel would draw a stray line. Previews that need more than points (spray seed and radius, brush diameter and colour, eraser side) are written into the canvas titulus as data.
    symbolum pictor_componere · via lib/pictor_componentia.worklog.md
  6. quomodohow does a palette open, and how do its options know which state to change?
    The bar squares (and quadratum.strata) carry action palette.aperire, which sets the transient field palette (instrumentum, color_primus, color_secundus, exemplar, magnitudo or strata). The composer then adds a PARTES_DIALOGUS node with id palette just above that square as the last root child. Each option of the first five is an optio.genus.valor square whose action is the scriptor of the attribute in apps/pictor/organum.stml (color_primus.ponere, color_secundus.ponere, instrumentum.eligere, exemplar.ponere, magnitudo.ponere); the current value is titled with :electum and the option closes the palette on click. The strata panel is different: oculus.id and stratum.id rows plus strata.novum and strata.deletum, all with action strata.agere. During a pending paste only the magnitudo palette can open, and the eraser's size square has no action, so it opens nothing.
    symbolum pictor_componere · via lib/pictor_componentia.worklog.md apps/pictor/organum.stml
  7. quidwhat squares does the pictor status bar show (the header comment lists only three)?
    Five 20 x 20 squares, a cell apart: quadratum.instrumentum, quadratum.color_primus, quadratum.color_secundus, quadratum.exemplar and quadratum.magnitudo (its title names the current tool's size; for spongia it is the fixed side and has no action). The status node's own title is the tool name, nihil when none. With a document set, quadratum.strata (LXX wide, titled strata:stratum id) sits at the right edge as a ROOT child, not a status child. During a pending paste the squares are replaced by glutinatio.A, glutinatio.B, glutinatio.confirmare and glutinatio.abicere (all action glutinatio.agere) plus a quadratum.magnitudo for the brush size. The header comment still describes the three-square P1a bar.
    symbolum pictor_componere · via lib/pictor_componentia.worklog.md

pictor_documentum

include/pictor_documentum.h · lib/pictor_documentum.interrogationes.stml

  1. quidwhat is a pictor document, and where is the picture actually stored?
    The truth is the append-only log of acts in a volumen (strokes, layer changes, pasted images); the bitmap is a projection rebuilt from the nearest checkpoint plus the live acts after it. The log machinery (branches, checkpoints, cursor, undo, redo, verify) is historia; this library only knows how to paint an act into RGBA layers.
    symbolum PictorDocumentum pictor_documentum_proiectio historia_actum · via lib/pictor_documentum.worklog.md include/historia.h
  2. quomodohow do I open an existing drawing, or create one if it does not exist yet?
    Try pictor_documentum_aperire(piscina, intern, volumen, spatium) first; it reads the size from the plagula SPATIUM/documentum (or documentum for an empty spatium) and returns NIHIL if that is missing or gives a zero size. Only then call pictor_documentum_creare with a size and checkpoint interval (0 or less means LXIV): it does not check for an existing document and rewrites the manifest. pictor_documenta_enumerare lists every spatium in a volume.
    symbolum pictor_documentum_aperire pictor_documentum_creare pictor_documenta_enumerare · via lib/pictor_applicatio.c
  3. quomodohow do I record a brush stroke so it is saved and can be undone?
    Pass the stroke as STML text to pictor_documentum_actum: an ictus element with instrumentum, color (palette index 0-15), magnitudo and optional color_secundus, exemplar, semen and stratum, holding punctum x y [t] children. It appends, paints and checkpoints when due, returning the seq (greater than 0) or 0 if refused. pictor_documentum_revocare and _reficere are undo and redo; a new act after an undo starts a branch, so nothing is ever truncated.
    symbolum pictor_documentum_actum pictor_documentum_revocare pictor_documentum_reficere · via lib/pictor_actiones.c
  4. quidwhat tools can an ictus use, and how big is each one?
    Absent or unknown instrumentum is the brush: a disc of diameter magnitudo at every point of the Bresenham line between points (sizes up to III are full squares). linea is the brush with two points. aspergillum sprays single pixels in a disc of radius PICTOR_ASPERGILLI_RADIUS x magnitudo, PICTOR_GUTTAE_PUNCTO x magnitudo per point plus more for dwell time, seeded by semen so replay is identical. spongia erases a PICTOR_SPONGIAE_LATUS x magnitudo square to transparent and ignores colour and pattern.
    symbolum pictor_disci_pixelum pictor_lineam_ambulare pictor_gutta
  5. possumnecan a stroke that goes off the edge of the canvas wrap around or hang?
    No longer. Point coordinates are signed, and every segment is clipped to the canvas (Cohen-Sutherland in s64, widened by the stamp size) before it is walked, so a drag off the edge stops there. Before 2026-10-08 negative points were cast to the UNSIGNED i32 and a line ran to the opposite edge, or effectively forever.
    symbolum pictor_documentum_actum pictor_lineam_ambulare · via lib/pictor_documentum.worklog.md
  6. quomodohow do I add, delete, reorder or hide layers?
    Append stratum acts with pictor_documentum_actum: actio novum id supra, deletum id, ordo ids (bottom first, must be a permutation of every layer), visibile id valor. Layers are named by id, not index; supra unknown or 0 puts the new layer on top. Invalid acts (duplicate id, deleting the last layer, more than PICTOR_STRATA_MAXIMA = XVI layers) are logged but change nothing. Read layers bottom first with pictor_documentum_stratum(doc, index).
    symbolum pictor_documentum_actum pictor_documentum_stratum pictor_documentum_numerus_stratorum PictorStratum
  7. curwhy did my stroke get a sequence number but nothing appeared?
    Acts are appended first and painted second, and painting ignores what it cannot apply: an ictus whose stratum id does not exist (absent means layer 1), a colour outside 0-15 (it means leave the pixel untouched), or an act whose root element is not ictus, stratum or imago. A stroke into a hidden layer IS painted but is not composited until the layer is shown. The seq only says the act was logged.
    symbolum pictor_documentum_actum PictorStratum
  8. quidwhat does pictor_documentum_proiectio return, and does it blend layers?
    The COMPOSITE as an Imago that shares memory with doc->tabula, so it changes with the next act; copy it if you need a snapshot. It is the theme background colour, then each visible layer from the bottom, where any pixel with non-zero alpha replaces the one below: no alpha blending.
    symbolum pictor_documentum_proiectio pictor_documentum_sigillum_compositi_hex
  9. curwhy are there two seals, and why can the composite seal change with the theme?
    pictor_documentum_sigillum_hex hashes the document state (layer count, ids, visibility and every layer's pixels); pictor_documentum_verificare replays from nothing and compares it. pictor_documentum_sigillum_compositi_hex hashes the visible pixels, which include the background: that is thema_color(COLOR_BACKGROUND), not stored in the acts, so every drawing renders on today's theme background.
    symbolum pictor_documentum_sigillum_hex pictor_documentum_sigillum_compositi_hex pictor_documentum_verificare · via lib/pictor_documentum.worklog.md
  10. quomodohow do I paste an image into a drawing as a new layer?
    pictor_imaginem_aptare fits the RGBA image inside the canvas keeping the aspect (small images are scaled UP), centres it, dithers it to the 16 Aquinas colours with Atkinson and marks alpha below CXXVIII as 0xFF (transparent). pictor_documentum_imaginem_inserere then stores the deflated indices as a volume massa and logs stratum novum plus imago as one conjoined group (one undo removes both), returning the new layer id (highest id + 1) or 0, which includes a full document of PICTOR_STRATA_MAXIMA layers. Only the first act's result is checked: if the imago act fails to append, the id is still returned. Replay never reruns the dithering. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): when that imago act fails, the log keeps an empty layer and a dangling coniunctio note, so the user's NEXT unrelated act is conjoined to the layer creation and one undo removes both.
    symbolum pictor_imaginem_aptare pictor_documentum_imaginem_inserere
  11. quomodohow does a pending paste with an A/B choice work before it is committed?
    pictor_documentum_glutinationem_parare fits the image once and dithers it twice (A Atkinson, B dithering_exemplaria) into doc->glutinatio, OUTSIDE the log: no acts, no undo. A failed parare leaves any earlier pending paste alone. _radere paints a mask that shows the other mode; _confirmare(modus, supra) inserts the mixed result as a layer; _abicere drops it. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): if the imago act inside the insert fails to append, _confirmare still gets a non-zero layer id, drops the pending paste and leaves an empty layer instead of the image.
    symbolum pictor_documentum_glutinationem_parare pictor_documentum_glutinationem_confirmare pictor_documentum_glutinationem_abicere pictor_documentum_glutinationem_radere PictorGlutinatio

piscina

include/piscina.h · lib/piscina.interrogationes.stml

  1. possumnecan I free a single allocation from a piscina?
    No: a piscina is an arena and frees everything at once. piscina_destruere releases all of it, piscina_vacare keeps the blocks and empties them for reuse, and piscina_notare with piscina_reficere rolls back everything allocated after the mark.
    symbolum piscina_destruere piscina_vacare piscina_notare piscina_reficere
  2. quomodohow do I use temporary scratch memory in a loop without the piscina growing every iteration?
    Take a mark with piscina_notare before the temporaries and call piscina_reficere after. Everything allocated after the mark becomes invalid, but the memory is neither freed nor cleared, so a stale read usually still sees the old values and the bug is silent. Build with -DPISCINA_VENENUM=1 to fill released bytes with 0xA5 (the venenum gate does this).
    symbolum piscina_notare piscina_reficere · via tools/venenum_probare.sh
  3. quomodohow do I handle running out of memory instead of the program exiting?
    piscina_allocare is fatal: if no block can be found or created it prints the piscina's name and exits the process. piscina_conari_allocare (and _ordinatum) return NIHIL in the same situation, so the caller can recover.
    symbolum piscina_allocare piscina_conari_allocare piscina_conari_allocare_ordinatum
  4. possumnecan I put a hard cap on how much memory a piscina uses?
    Yes: piscina_generare_certae_magnitudinis makes one buffer that never grows; an allocation that does not fit is fatal through piscina_allocare or NIHIL through piscina_conari_allocare. piscina_potesne_allocare asks first. piscina_generare_dynamicum instead adds blocks (twice the initial size, or the request size plus the initial size when larger).
    symbolum piscina_generare_certae_magnitudinis piscina_potesne_allocare piscina_generare_dynamicum
  5. quidwhat alignment does piscina_allocare give, and how do I pack bytes tightly?
    Eight bytes (PISCINA_ORDINATIO_ORDINARIA), enough for every house type including pointers, i64 and f64, like malloc. For byte-only data such as text, piscina_allocare_ordinatum(piscina, mensura, I) packs with no padding. Before 2026-10-05 the default was one byte, which left wider members misaligned.
    symbolum piscina_allocare piscina_allocare_ordinatum

polynomium

include/polynomium.h · lib/polynomium.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I build a polynomial from text and print it back?
    polynomium_ex_chorda(chorda_ex_literis("3t^2 - t + 1 - 2t^-1", piscina), 't', piscina, &p) returns FALSUM on bad text and leaves p untouched. A term is an optional decimal coefficient, then optionally the letter with ^ and an exponent that may start with '-'; terms are joined by + or -, only the first may carry a leading '-' (a leading '+', "3*t" or "+ -3" is refused), spaces and tabs may sit between sign, coefficient and letter but not around ^ ("t ^2" is refused), terms come in any order and repeated exponents are added. The letter must be ASCII a-z or A-Z. polynomium_ad_chordam prints the canonical form, highest degree first, "0" for zero.
    symbolum polynomium_ex_chorda polynomium_ad_chordam
  2. curwhy do multiplica, potentia, translata and friends return b32 instead of the polynomial?
    Exponents are bounded by POLYNOMIUM_EXPONENS_MAXIMUS, 2^30 - 1, so that every span fits i32 and every exponent product fits s64; with the full s32 range a sum's coefficient count wrapped to 0 and corrupted memory. Any operation that takes or moves an exponent returns FALSUM instead of overflowing, and leaves *exitus untouched. nega, adde, subtrahe, multiplica_scalari and inversum cannot leave the range and return the value directly.
    symbolum polynomium_multiplica polynomium_potentia polynomium_monomium · via lib/polynomium.worklog.md
  3. possumnecan I keep a polynomial after rolling back or destroying the piscina its inputs came from?
    Only after polynomium_transcribe. Results may share coefficient values (and their magnus limbs) with the arguments, and polynomium_translata shares the whole coefficient array without allocating, so a result can point into the inputs' piscina. polynomium_transcribe copies the array and every coefficient through magnus_transcribe into the piscina you name.
    symbolum polynomium_transcribe polynomium_translata magnus_transcribe
  4. quidwhat is the difference between polynomium_translata and polynomium_translatum?
    polynomium_translata(p, k) is p times t^k: it only moves the exponents (shares the coefficients, refuses if out of range). polynomium_translatum(p, c) is the Taylor shift p(t + c) with a magnus c: the coefficients change, and it refuses any negative exponent.
    symbolum polynomium_translata polynomium_translatum
  5. possumnecan I store a sparse polynomial like t^1000000 + 1 cheaply?
    No: the representation is dense, summus - imus + 1 coefficients, so t^1000000 alone is one coefficient but t^1000000 + 1 is 1000001. polynomium_ex_chorda refuses a span above POLYNOMIUM_AMPLITUDO_LECTIONIS (2^20) because a 30-byte text could otherwise ask for gigabytes; the arithmetic itself has no such limit, so a wide adde or multiplica simply allocates.
    symbolum polynomium_ex_chorda polynomium_ex_coefficientibus · via lib/polynomium.worklog.md
  6. quomodohow do I turn a Kauffman bracket in A into a Jones polynomial in t?
    With writhe w, the factor (-A^3)^(-w) is the single monomial (-1)^w A^(-3w): build it with polynomium_monomium and multiply the bracket by it with polynomium_multiplica, then call polynomium_contrahe(f, -IV, piscina, &jones), since A = t^(-1/4). contrahe returns FALSUM if some exponent is not divisible by 4. The tests turn the right trefoil's -A^5 - A^-3 + A^-7 (w = 3, factor -A^-9) into -t^4 + t^3 + t this way.
    symbolum polynomium_monomium polynomium_multiplica polynomium_contrahe polynomium_ex_chorda · via lib/polynomium.worklog.md
  7. quomodohow do I evaluate a polynomial at a number?
    polynomium_valor(p, x, piscina, &exitus) with x a Fractio (fractio_ex_s64 for an integer): Horner over exact rationals, then times x^imus. It returns FALSUM only when x = 0 and p has a negative exponent.
    symbolum polynomium_valor fractio_ex_s64
  8. quomodohow do I divide one polynomial by another?
    polynomium_divide_exacte gives the quotient when b divides a exactly in Z[t, t^-1] (t is a unit there) and FALSUM otherwise, or when b is zero. There is no division with remainder or pseudo-division in the public API; polynomium_divisor_communis computes a gcd internally with pseudo-remainders.
    symbolum polynomium_divide_exacte polynomium_divisor_communis
  9. possumnecan I take the gcd or square-free part of a Laurent polynomial?
    No: polynomium_divisor_communis and polynomium_pars_libera accept only ordinary polynomials (no negative exponent) and return FALSUM otherwise, because in Z[t, t^-1] a gcd is only defined up to a unit t^k. Shift first with polynomium_translata. The gcd is over Q[t], returned primitive with a positive leading coefficient; gcd(0, 0) is 0.
    symbolum polynomium_divisor_communis polynomium_pars_libera polynomium_translata
  10. quidwhat is the canonical form, and how do I tell the zero polynomial apart?
    Zero has no coefficients and gradus 0, the same degrees as the constant 1, so test polynomium_est_nullum.
