Every other result has non-zero lowest and highest coefficients, which is why polynomium_aequalis can compare parts. polynomium_normale shifts to imus 0 with a positive lowest coefficient (Alexander's form); polynomium_est_symmetricum asks whether the coefficients read the same reversed with the SAME sign. The struct members are private: use the accessors.
    symbolum polynomium_est_nullum polynomium_gradus_imus polynomium_gradus_summus polynomium_aequalis polynomium_normale polynomium_est_symmetricum
  11. quidwhat does polynomium_apex_officinarum measure?
    The peak bytes used in the internal scratch piscinae (officinae) by the most recent multiplica or divide_exacte call, including the ones potentia, divisor_communis and pars_libera make internally: every call resets it, so it is 0 when that call ran in the caller's piscina. Officinae are used only when the term-by-term work is at least LXIV products and some partial sum could leave s64, so the caller keeps only the result. It exists so tests can assert scratch is rolled back.
    symbolum polynomium_apex_officinarum polynomium_multiplica polynomium_divide_exacte · via lib/polynomium.worklog.md

praeconium

include/praeconium.h · lib/praeconium.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I record a Claude Code hook event in the session log?
    Parse the hook's stdin JSON with praeconium_ex_hamo (pass TMUX_PANE, TMUX and the time in ms), then praeconium_scribere to praeconium_via(piscina). This is exactly what 'praeco hamus' does. praeconium_ex_hamo returns FALSUM for malformed JSON or a non-object root, and then nothing should be written.
    symbolum praeconium_ex_hamo praeconium_scribere praeconium_via · via tools/praeco.c
  2. quidwhere is the session event log, and how do I point a test at another one?
    praeconium_via returns $RHUBARB_COLLOQUIA/eventa.jsonl when that variable is set and non-empty, otherwise $HOME/.rhubarb/colloquia/eventa.jsonl (with HOME unset it falls back to ./.rhubarb/...). One file is shared by every worktree. Tests set RHUBARB_COLLOQUIA to a temporary directory; praeconium_scribere creates the directory if needed. Never set it to the empty string: the buffer is then sized for the empty value (plus LXIV) but the HOME path is written into it, which overflows once HOME is longer than 31 bytes.
    symbolum praeconium_via
  3. possumnecan several hooks append to the log at the same time without corrupting lines?
    Yes: each event is one JSON line written by a single O_APPEND write() (then fsync) through filum_appendere_firmiter, so concurrent writers never interleave inside a line. The test forks four writers of 50 events and reads back all 200 lines intact. To keep lines short, nuntius is cut to 200 bytes, never inside a UTF-8 character.
    symbolum praeconium_scribere filum_appendere_firmiter
  4. quomodohow do I follow the log incrementally, reading only new events?
    Keep an s64 byte position and call praeconium_legere_ab(via, &positio, piscina) on each tick. Start at 0 to replay the whole file; to start from now, do what colloquia_visus does: set positio to the file size (fseek/ftell) FIRST, then seed your state once with praeconium_caudam_legere(via, octeti, piscina), which returns no position of its own (an event written in between is seen twice rather than lost). praeconium_legere_ab returns only complete lines and advances positio to the end of the last one, so a half-written line is read next time. If the file shrank below positio (deleted or rotated) it restarts from 0; if it is missing it returns an empty Xar and sets positio to 0.
    symbolum praeconium_legere_ab praeconium_caudam_legere · via lib/colloquia_visus.c
  5. quidwhat do the genus and nuntius fields of a PraeconiumEventus hold?
    They depend on hook_event_name: Notification gives notification_type and message; PreToolUse, PostToolUse, PostToolUseFailure and PermissionRequest give tool_name and the first non-empty of tool_input description, command, file_path, pattern, url; UserPromptSubmit puts the prompt in nuntius; Stop and SubagentStop the last_assistant_message; StopFailure the error; SessionStart sets genus to trigger (or the older source); SessionEnd to reason. servitor is only the socket part of TMUX (before the first comma).
    symbolum PraeconiumEventus praeconium_ex_hamo · via lib/praeconium.worklog.md
  6. possumnecan I keep the chorda fields of an event after resetting the piscina I read it with?
    No. Every chorda in a PraeconiumEventus points into JSON values allocated in the piscina you passed; resetting it (colloquia_visus calls piscina_vacare on its scratch piscina every tick) leaves them dangling. Copy what you keep. They are chorda, not NUL-terminated: use chorda_ut_cstr before printf or strcmp. Missing fields are empty chorda (datum NIHIL).
    symbolum praeconium_ex_hamo praeconium_caudam_legere praeconium_legere_ab chorda_ut_cstr

processus

include/processus.h · lib/processus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I kill a child that uses too much memory?
    memset a ProcessusOptiones to zero, set memoria_maxima in bytes and call processus_exsequi_cum. The child's footprint (compressed pages included, not RSS) is read in the same loop that enforces mora_maxima_ms, at most once every 20 ms, so a child that loops without printing is caught too. Over the limit it gets SIGKILL and the result has successus FALSUM, error PROCESSUS_ERROR_MEMORIA and an error_descriptio naming the footprint seen; output captured so far is kept. 0 means no limit. processus_incipere (the incremental path) takes no options, so it has no memory limit. Always memset the options: a field added later would otherwise be read as stack garbage.
    symbolum processus_exsequi_cum ProcessusOptiones PROCESSUS_ERROR_MEMORIA

provenientia

include/provenientia.h · lib/provenientia.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I make a new house binary answer -provenientia?
    Declare the generated record and link its object through an aedilis annotation, as tools/stml.c does: a comment <aedilis obiectum="build/fabrica/provenientia/T.c"/>, externus constans ProvenientiaRelatio provenientia_T; and as the FIRST statement of principale: si (provenientia_respondere(argc, argv, &provenientia_T)) redde ZEPHYRUM. The installer runs tools/provenientia_scribere.sh T ARTIFICIUM before building, which writes that .c file.
    symbolum provenientia_respondere ProvenientiaRelatio · via tools/stml.c tools/provenientia_scribere.sh
  2. quidwhat does the -provenientia report contain and who reads it?
    Four lines: 'provenientia 1', 'artificium X' (e.g. bin/manus), 'ingressus H' (the fabrica digest of the inputs of the action that built it) and 'commissum C' (short HEAD hash, with ' SORDIDUM' when the tree had uncommitted changes). bin/fabrica runs the installed binary with -provenientia and compares the digest with the one it computes today: equal means the install is fresh. A non-zero exit or empty output counts as no report.
    symbolum provenientia_textus provenientia_respondere · via tools/fabrica_sutura.c
  3. curwhy is the record passed as an argument instead of the library linking a well-known symbol?
    Four installers link build/ objects blindly. If lib/provenientia.c referred to a symbol that only the generated file defines, every binary that links the library without that file would fail to link (fabrica plan 1a T7). So the binary names its own generated record and hands it in.
    symbolum provenientia_respondere ProvenientiaRelatio · via include/provenientia.h
  4. curwhy must provenientia_respondere be called before anything else in principale?
    bin/fabrica asks from whatever directory it is in, so the answer must not depend on the working directory, configuration or any other setup the tool does. It matches only when -provenientia is the ONE argument (argc == II); it then writes the report to stdout and returns VERUM, and the caller exits 0. In every other case it writes nothing and returns FALSUM. The smoke test checks it from a foreign directory.
    symbolum provenientia_respondere · via tools/fabrica_fumus.sh
  5. possumnecan the ingressus field be something other than a 64-character hex digest?
    Yes, despite the header's 'LXIV hex': the generator writes 'ignotum' when bin/fabrica is not built yet (bootstrap), or when 'bin/fabrica digestum' fails and PROVENIENTIA_TOLERANS=1 is set (without that variable a failing digest stops the generator with exit 1). commissum is 'ignotum' outside a git tree. A reader of the report should treat any value that is not 64 hex digits as unknown, never as fresh.
    symbolum ProvenientiaRelatio · via tools/provenientia_scribere.sh

pseudoterminale

include/pseudoterminale.h · lib/pseudoterminale.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I run a program in a pseudo-terminal and talk to it?
    Fill a PseudoterminaleConfiguratio with pseudoterminale_configuratio_initiare, set argumenta (a NIHIL-terminated argv, run through PATH with execvp - no shell), then call pseudoterminale_posix_creare. You get back a Pseudoterminale: a table of function pointers (legere, scribere, amplitudo, finitus, fossa, claudere) that you call with pt->datum as the first argument, e.g. pt->legere(pt->datum, buffer, n, mora_ms). The child gets a new session with the slave as its controlling terminal and as fds 0, 1 and 2, so stdout and stderr arrive mixed on one stream.
    symbolum pseudoterminale_configuratio_initiare pseudoterminale_posix_creare Pseudoterminale · via lib/terminale.c
  2. quomodohow do I tell "the binary does not exist" apart from a child that exited with 127?
    pseudoterminale_posix_creare waits on a CLOEXEC pipe until exec either succeeds (the pipe closes) or fails (the child writes the step and errno). A failed chdir or exec returns NIHIL with *error = PSEUDOTERMINALE_ERROR_EXEC and *descriptio such as "exec: No such file or directory"; the half-born child is already reaped. A real program that later exits 127 shows up instead through finitus with codex 127. One gap: if the child's dup2 onto fds 0-2 fails it exits 127 without writing to the pipe, so creare succeeds and the failure looks like an ordinary exit 127. Both error and descriptio may be NIHIL; pseudoterminale_error_nomen gives a fixed Latin name for the error code.
    symbolum pseudoterminale_posix_creare pseudoterminale_error_nomen PseudoterminaleError
  3. possumnecan scribere block if the child stops reading its input?
    No. The master fd is set O_NONBLOCK, and scribere returns how many bytes the kernel accepted: possibly 0 (EAGAIN), possibly fewer than you asked, -1 only on a real error. The host must keep the rest and send it later. The worklog measured a raw-mode child that never reads at 1022 accepted bytes; a canonical-mode child accepts everything (macOS discards past MAX_INPUT).
    symbolum pseudoterminale_posix_creare Pseudoterminale
  4. quomodohow do I wait for output from the child in my own event loop?
    Call pt->fossa(pt->datum) to get the master descriptor and put it in your poll set; never read or close it yourself. When it is readable call legere: mora_ms 0 does not wait, a positive value waits that many milliseconds, a negative value waits forever. legere returns the bytes read, 0 when nothing arrived in time (or EINTR/EAGAIN), and -1 when the child closed the slave side (read 0 or EIO). The memoriae bridge returns -1 from fossa, since it has no descriptor.
    symbolum Pseudoterminale pseudoterminale_posix_creare
  5. quomodohow do I find out whether the child exited and with what status?
    Call pt->finitus(pt->datum, &exitus): it reaps with waitpid WNOHANG, so it never blocks. It returns FALSUM while the child runs and VERUM once it has exited; later calls return VERUM and the same exitus. exitus.codex is WEXITSTATUS (0 when the child was killed) and exitus.signum is WTERMSIG or 0. If someone else reaped the pid (ECHILD) it still reports VERUM, but the exit is unknown and exitus reads codex 0, signum 0, which looks like a clean exit. exitus may be NIHIL for the posix bridge but not for the memoriae one. There are no signal handlers: the host polls finitus, typically after legere returned -1.
    symbolum PseudoterminaleExitus Pseudoterminale
  6. possumnecan I skip claudere if the child has already exited?
    No: claudere must always be called, and only it closes the master fd. It sends SIGHUP to the child's process group and the child (only if not yet reaped, so a reused pid is never signalled), closes the master, polls waitpid for up to XX steps of V ms, then sends SIGKILL and waits. Afterwards there is no zombie and no open fd, even for a child that ignores SIGHUP. It is idempotent.
    symbolum Pseudoterminale pseudoterminale_posix_creare
  7. quomodohow do I set or remove environment variables for the child without losing TERM?
    cfg.ambitus is a NIHIL-terminated list of changes applied on top of the inherited environment: "NAME=value" sets, a bare "NAME" unsets. The default from pseudoterminale_configuratio_initiare is { "TERM=xterm-256color", "COLORTERM=truecolor" }. Assigning your own array REPLACES that list, so include the TERM and COLORTERM lines yourself if you want them (lib/terminale.c passes its own ambitus_terminalis). A name of CCLV characters or more is silently skipped.
    symbolum pseudoterminale_configuratio_initiare PseudoterminaleConfiguratio · via lib/terminale.c
  8. quomodohow do I resize the terminal the child sees?
    Call pt->amplitudo(pt->datum, cols, rows, px_w, px_h): it sets TIOCSWINSZ on the master and the kernel sends SIGWINCH to the child. Pixel sizes may be 0 (unknown). It returns FALSUM after claudere, for a size below I, or above 0xFFFF. The starting size comes from cfg.latitudo and cfg.altitudo (LXXX x XXIV by default) and is checked the same way: a bad size makes creare fail with PSEUDOTERMINALE_ERROR_ARGUMENTA.
    symbolum Pseudoterminale PseudoterminaleConfiguratio
  9. quomodohow do I test a terminal host without spawning a real process?
    Use the memoriae bridge, the test seam: pseudoterminale_memoriae_creare takes a canned output that legere returns (then -1), and an exit code that finitus reports once all output has been read. Everything the host writes is captured and visible through pseudoterminale_memoriae_captum (a view, valid until the next scribere); pseudoterminale_memoriae_amplitudo reports the last size set. Because Pseudoterminale is a function table, host code calls both bridges the same way. They are not identical: the memoriae legere ignores mora_ms, its fossa is -1, its tty is NIHIL, its amplitudo has no 0xFFFF limit, and its finitus writes through exitus with no NIHIL check.
    symbolum pseudoterminale_memoriae_creare pseudoterminale_memoriae_captum pseudoterminale_memoriae_amplitudo · via probationes/probatio_pseudoterminale.c
  10. quidwhat is the tty field of a Pseudoterminale for?
    It is the slave device name returned by openpty, such as "/dev/ttys011", NUL-terminated and copied into the piscina. It was added for the colloquia plan (T4c) because tmux identifies its clients by tty; lib/terminale.c copies it into the terminal's state. It is NIHIL for the memoriae bridge.
    symbolum Pseudoterminale pseudoterminale_posix_creare · via lib/terminale.c
  11. possumnecan signals my host ignores or blocks leak into the child program?
    Partly. A disposition set to SIG_IGN survives exec, so the child resets HUP, INT, QUIT, PIPE, ALRM, TERM, CHLD, TSTP, TTIN, TTOU and WINCH to SIG_DFL before execvp (a host that ignores SIGPIPE does not pass that on). Any other signal the host ignores stays ignored, and the signal MASK is not touched: anything the host blocked with sigprocmask is still blocked in the program running in the terminal.
    symbolum pseudoterminale_posix_creare · via lib/pseudoterminale.worklog.md lib/pseudoterminale_posix.c

quadrans

include/quadrans.h · lib/quadrans.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I draw an image in a terminal with quadrant block characters?
    Take quadrans_optiones_ordinariae (quadrants, MEDIA colours, black fundus), ask quadrans_mensurare how many cells the pixel region needs, allocate that many QuadransCellula yourself, then call quadrans_computare. Each cell gives a runa (a codepoint, not a UTF-8 string) and two 0x00RRGGBB colours, foreground and background, in row order. tessellatio scales its pixel layer to columns * 2 by lines * 2 pixels with imago_scalare and then calls quadrans_computare, which yields exactly one cell per terminal cell.
    symbolum quadrans_optiones_ordinariae quadrans_mensurare quadrans_computare QuadransCellula · via lib/tessellatio.c
  2. quomodohow do I size an image so it fits a cell area without looking stretched?
    quadrans_aptare(img_lat, img_alt, max_lat, max_alt, modus, aspectus, &sub_lat, &sub_alt) gives the pixel size to scale the image to (up or down), keeping its proportions for a cell whose width / height is aspectus hundredths (QUADRANS_ASPECTUS_ORDINARIUS is L, i.e. 1:2). It does not scale anything: resize the image yourself (the effigies viewer uses imago_scalare with IMAGO_SCALA_AREA) and then call quadrans_computare. Any zero input gives 0 x 0.
    symbolum quadrans_aptare QuadransModus · via tessera/instrumenta/principalia/effigies.c
  3. possumnecan I get exact colours instead of two colours per 2x2 block?
    Yes, with modus QUADRANS_DIMIDIUM: each cell is 1x2 pixels, the top pixel is the foreground under U+2580 and the bottom the background, so colours are exact but horizontal resolution is halved. Measured on two photographs at the same terminal size (phase-log Q3) the quadrant mode had 3.8 and 7.3 times less error than half-blocks, which is why quadrants are the default.
    symbolum QuadransModus quadrans_computare · via lib/quadrans.phase-log.md
  4. curwhy is MEDIA the default colouring rather than the extreme pair OpenTUI uses?
    For a fixed split of the four pixels the mean of each group minimises the squared error, so QUADRANS_MEDIA is never worse than QUADRANS_EXTREMA, which uses the same split. quadrans_error measured MEDIA at about 46% of EXTREMA's error on the photographs; the numbers are a ratchet in aurum_errores.txt. Whether EXTREMA looks sharper is a matter of taste, so it stays selectable.
    symbolum quadrans_optiones_ordinariae QuadransColores quadrans_error · via lib/quadrans.phase-log.md probationes/fixa/quadrans/aurum_errores.txt
  5. quidwhat does quadrans do with transparent pixels and pixels outside the image?
    Both become QuadransOptiones.fundus. Alpha is composited against fundus FIRST, because terminal cells have no alpha, and pixels outside the image or past the region (an odd width or height, a region partly off the image) read as fundus. A NIHIL imago gives cells that are all fundus. Set fundus to the terminal background colour, as tessellatio does.
    symbolum quadrans_computare QuadransOptiones
  6. curwhy does a block of four equal pixels come out as a space instead of a full block?
    A uniform block (and in half-block mode two equal pixels) becomes U+0020 with that colour as the background, because a glyph drawn by the terminal font can leave a visible seam where a space with a background colour cannot. So runa 0x2588 never appears; a space cell carries its colour in color_fundi (color_litterae is the same value).
    symbolum quadrans_computare QuadransCellula
  7. quomodohow do I measure how faithful a quadrant rendering is?
    quadrans_error takes the same image, region and options plus the cells quadrans_computare produced, rebuilds the pixels from each cell's pattern and returns the mean squared error per channel in hundredths (0 is perfect, an empty region is 0). Passing cells computed with different options or a different region gives a meaningless number; nothing checks it.
    symbolum quadrans_error quadrans_computare
  8. possumnecan quadrans allocate the output or keep state between frames?
    No, by design it is pure: no allocation, no state, and it depends only on latina.h and imago_typus.h, so tessera, the terminal emulator and a window can share it. The caller provides the QuadransCellula array sized by quadrans_mensurare, and turns runa into UTF-8 and colours into its own cell format.
    symbolum quadrans_computare quadrans_mensurare · via include/quadrans.h

reactor

include/reactor.h · lib/reactor.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I get called back when a socket is readable or writable?
    Create a reactor with reactor_creare(piscina) and register the fd with reactor_adicere(r, fd, REACTOR_LEGERE, callback, data). The callback receives the fd, an event bitmask and your data. REACTOR_ERROR and REACTOR_CLAUSUM (POLLERR, POLLHUP) can appear in the mask even though you only ask for LEGERE and SCRIBERE. Calling reactor_adicere again for a registered fd replaces its events, callback and data; reactor_modificare changes only the events, for example to add REACTOR_SCRIBERE while output is pending.
    symbolum reactor_creare reactor_adicere ReactorCallback reactor_modificare
  2. quomodohow do I run something after a delay, or every N milliseconds?
    reactor_timer_adicere(r, ms, repetere, callback, data) returns a ReactorTimerId, or REACTOR_TIMER_ERROR (all CXXVIII slots in use, or NIHIL callback). Compare against REACTOR_TIMER_ERROR, not "< 0": the id is an unsigned i32. A one-shot timer is switched off before its callback runs, so the callback may add a new timer. A repeating timer is rescheduled from the moment it is processed, so lateness accumulates instead of being caught up. Cancel with reactor_timer_cancellare.
    symbolum reactor_timer_adicere reactor_timer_cancellare ReactorTimerCallback
  3. possumnecan a callback add or remove fds and timers while the reactor is dispatching?
    Yes, that is supported (http-vocatio T1). Each pass dispatches from a snapshot taken at its start, and every registration carries a generation number: an fd removed during the pass, or a slot reused by a new registration, is skipped for the rest of that pass, and an fd or timer added during the pass is first served on the next pass, so a callback that keeps adding 0 ms timers cannot keep reactor_poll from returning.
    symbolum reactor_poll reactor_removere reactor_adicere reactor_timer_adicere · via lib/reactor.worklog.md
  4. quomodohow do I drive the reactor from my own loop instead of reactor_currere?
    Call reactor_poll(r, timeout_ms) repeatedly (-1 waits without limit, 0 does not block). It shortens the wait to the next timer deadline, runs the ready fd callbacks and the expired timers, and returns how many it ran. reactor_currere is just that loop with a M ms (one second) cap per pass, until a callback calls reactor_sistere. reactor_currere clears the stop request when it starts, so a reactor_sistere called before reactor_currere is forgotten.
    symbolum reactor_poll reactor_currere reactor_sistere
  5. possumnecan I leave reactor_currere running while nothing is registered?
    No. With no active fd and no active timer reactor_poll returns 0 at once, without calling poll() or sleeping. reactor_currere then spins at full CPU, and since no callback can run, nothing can call reactor_sistere. A loop around reactor_poll has the same problem: check reactor_numerus_fd and reactor_numerus_timer and stop when both are zero.
    symbolum reactor_currere reactor_poll reactor_numerus_fd reactor_numerus_timer
  6. possumnecan I close an fd first and remove it from the reactor later?
    No: call reactor_removere before close. A closed fd that is still registered makes poll() report POLLNVAL, which the reactor does not translate, so your callback is called with an event mask of 0 on every pass and poll() never blocks. If the fd number is reused by a new socket, the old registration fires for it.
    symbolum reactor_removere
  7. quidwhat are the reactor's limits and how much memory does one take?
    At most REACTOR_FD_MAXIMA (M) fds registered at the same time and REACTOR_TIMER_MAXIMA (CXXVIII) active timers; freed slots are reused, so the limit is on concurrent registrations, not on the total ever made. reactor_adicere and reactor_timer_adicere fail past it. All tables are fixed arrays inside the Reactor struct, so reactor_creare takes one allocation of several tens of kilobytes from the piscina.
    symbolum reactor_creare reactor_adicere reactor_timer_adicere
  8. possumnecan reactor_destruere close my sockets or free the reactor's memory?
    No. It only clears the running flag and sets the fd and timer high-water marks to zero. It closes no fd (that stays your job), and the memory belongs to the piscina it was created from. It does not stop a reactor_currere in progress (that loop watches only the reactor_sistere request), and it leaves every timer slot active, so reactor_numerus_timer still counts them and reactor_timer_adicere still finds those slots taken: do not reuse a reactor after destroying it.
    symbolum reactor_destruere reactor_numerus_timer reactor_timer_adicere

registrum_commandi

include/registrum_commandi.h · lib/registrum_commandi.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I add a new $command that a pagina tag can run?
    Write a FunctioCommand (b32 fn(ContextusCommandi* ctx), VERUM on success) and register it under its name with registrum_commandi_registrare(reg, "nomen", fn, datum). Inside concha use concha_registrare_commandi, which forwards to the same call. Widgets do it in their _init function (see libro_paginarum_init registering rename, goto and new).
    symbolum registrum_commandi_registrare concha_registrare_commandi FunctioCommand
  2. quomodohow does a command handler get at the widget or state it belongs to?
    Two pointers arrive in the ContextusCommandi. ctx->datum_registratus is the datum given at registration (widgets pass their ContextusWidget and cast it back); registrum_commandi_executare writes it into the context on every call, overwriting whatever the caller left there. ctx->datum_custom is per call site: layout.c passes the libro for a pagina inside a libro and NIHIL otherwise, and schirmata.c passes its libro. The context also carries the pagina, a piscina, and the grid line and column where the tag ends.
    symbolum registrum_commandi_executare ContextusCommandi · via lib/libro_paginarum.c
  3. possumnecan I tell "command not found" apart from "the command failed" when I execute one?
    Not from registrum_commandi_executare alone: it returns FALSUM for an unknown name and otherwise returns whatever the handler returned, so a handler that fails also gives FALSUM. Ask registrum_commandi_habet first when the difference matters.
    symbolum registrum_commandi_executare registrum_commandi_habet
  4. possumnecan I re-register a command name or remove a command?
    Registering the same name again replaces the earlier handler and datum (the hash table overwrites the value; the old Command stays allocated in the piscina). There is no remove function. Registration returns nothing, so a refused registration is silent: a NIHIL registry, name or function returns early, and an empty name is refused by tabula_dispersa_inserere (key length zero) after the Command was already allocated. concha_registrare_commandi returns VERUM for those refusals too (FALSUM only when the concha or its registry is NIHIL).
    symbolum registrum_commandi_registrare tabula_dispersa_inserere
  5. possumnecan I list every registered command, for help text or completion?
    Not through this API: the only query is registrum_commandi_habet for one name. The commands live in reg->tabula, a chorda-keyed TabulaDispersa whose values are Command pointers, so walking that table directly with tabula_dispersa_iterator_initium and tabula_dispersa_iterator_proximum is the only way today. Command.titulus keeps at most XXXI characters of the name, but lookup uses the full name.
    symbolum RegistrumCommandi registrum_commandi_habet tabula_dispersa_iterator_initium tabula_dispersa_iterator_proximum

rivus_terminalis

include/rivus_terminalis.h · lib/rivus_terminalis.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I turn raw terminal input bytes into key, mouse and paste events?
    The library never reads or keeps a clock; you drive it. Loop: drain events with rivus_eventum until it returns FALSUM; then ask rivus_mora_ms how long to wait (0 = nothing pending, use your own timeout) and read at most rivus_spatium bytes with that timeout; if bytes came, give them to rivus_tradere and loop; if the wait ran out while something was pending (or rivus_spatium was 0), call rivus_moram and loop. The tempus argument of rivus_eventum and rivus_moram is only stamped onto the events (tessera passes 0). tessera/fontes/tessera_eventum.c is the reference loop.
    symbolum rivus_creare rivus_eventum rivus_mora_ms rivus_spatium rivus_tradere rivus_moram · via tessera/fontes/tessera_eventum.c
  2. curwhy must I call rivus_moram, and what does it do to a lone ESC?
    Bytes alone cannot tell the Escape key from the start of an escape sequence, so a pending ESC (or half a sequence, half a UTF-8 rune, an unfinished paste) waits for silence. rivus_mora_ms says how long: RIVUS_MORA_FUGAE_MS (25 ms) normally, RIVUS_MORA_GLUTINI_MS (3 s) inside a bracketed paste. When that silence has passed, rivus_moram resolves it: ESC becomes the Escape key, ESC ESC two of them, ESC x becomes Alt+x, other partial sequences (and a half X10 mouse report) are dropped, half a rune is dropped, and an unterminated paste is finished and marked truncated. A half SGR mouse report is kept in case the rest arrives, and a lone ESC, although already reported as Escape, is also kept so that a mouse report split right after it still decodes.
    symbolum rivus_moram rivus_mora_ms
  3. possumnecan I keep a pointer to an event's text after the next call?
    No. Event text (and coalesced mouse samples) live in the event queue's payload buffers, which are emptied when the queue is empty and decoding starts again (eventus_cauda_lectio_incipit). Views are valid until the next rivus_eventum (or rivus_eventum_coalitum); copy anything you need to keep.
    symbolum rivus_eventum rivus_eventum_coalitum eventus_cauda_lectio_incipit
  4. quomodohow do I turn on mouse reporting, bracketed paste, focus events or kitty keys?
    Call rivus_modos_intrare with RIVUS_MODUS_* bits and a buffer of at least RIVUS_MODI_MAXIMUM bytes; it returns the bytes YOU must write to the terminal (the library is pure and writes nothing). SUPER implies MUS, DEPOSITIO implies GLUTINUM. It returns 0 and changes nothing if modes are already entered, the buffer is too small, or no known bit is set (a caller cannot tell these apart from the return value). Entering also pushes an EVENTUS_FACULTATES event. On exit, rivus_modos_exire gives the reverse sequence (kitty popped first) and clears the state; rivus_modos_exeundi gives the same bytes without changing state, so a signal handler can keep them ready.
    symbolum rivus_modos_intrare rivus_modos_exire rivus_modos_exeundi
  5. quidwhat is the difference between rivus_eventum and rivus_eventum_coalitum?
    rivus_eventum decodes lazily, one lexeme at a time until an event exists, and never merges mouse motion: a consumer that must see every report uses it. rivus_eventum_coalitum, when the queue is empty, decodes ALL bytes already given (up to half the queue), so consecutive motion merges into one event with the samples attached, the way the window does it. Ordinary Eventus consumers want the coalesced form.
    symbolum rivus_eventum rivus_eventum_coalitum
  6. possumnecan a paste of a file path from Finder arrive as a file drop instead of text?
    Yes, if RIVUS_MODUS_DEPOSITIO is entered: a whole, untruncated paste that parses as absolute paths (shell quoting with backslash, single and double quotes, or file:// URIs with %XX escapes) is promoted to EVENTUS_DEPOSITIO. Anything else stays an ordinary paste text event. The drop position is the last mouse position seen BEFORE the drag, (0,0) if none, because the terminal cannot see a drag from another application; target by focus, not by position.
    symbolum rivus_modos_intrare interpres_depositio
  7. possumnecan I hand rivus_tradere a large read buffer?
    It takes at most rivus_spatium bytes (the internal buffer is RIVUS_BUFFER, 256) and returns how many it accepted; the rest is yours to keep and offer again after draining events, so read no more than rivus_spatium in the first place. A paste body is collected separately into a 64 KB buffer and is flagged truncated beyond that. rivus_creare takes those two 64 KB buffers and the event queue from the piscina you pass. rivus_pendentes reports bytes not yet consumed, to check that a stream ended cleanly.
    symbolum rivus_tradere rivus_spatium rivus_pendentes rivus_creare

runae

include/runae.h · lib/runae.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I measure how many terminal columns a UTF-8 string takes?
    runae_latitudo_textus(initium, finis, politica) sums the widths of the drawable units in the byte range [initium, finis); the range is a chorda's datum and datum + mensura, no NUL needed. It walks with runae_unitas_proxima, so a grapheme cluster counts as one unit of 0, 1 or 2 columns. Pass RUNAE_POLITICA_GRAPHEMATUM unless you are matching Terminal.app.
    symbolum runae_latitudo_textus runae_unitas_proxima
  2. curwhy does runae_latitudo give 0 for a tab or control character while runae_latitudo_textus counts it as 1?
    runae_latitudo is the true Unicode answer for a single rune: controls (Cc) are width 0. The text functions follow the house DRAWABLE UNIT rule (Fran 2026-09-29): a C0 control byte or DEL, and every invalid byte, is its own one-byte unit of one column, because the house painters draw a substitute for it (tessera '?', excerptum and saltuarius ' '). So "\r\n" measures 2 columns. The rule covers single BYTES only: a C1 control (U+0080 to U+009F, two bytes in UTF-8) is a valid rune and still measures 0 in text. Expand tabs yourself before measuring.
    symbolum runae_latitudo runae_unitas_proxima runae_latitudo_textus
  3. quomodohow do I walk a string one grapheme cluster at a time?
    Loop p = runae_graphema_proximum(p, finis, &latitudo) until p reaches finis; each call returns the end of the next extended grapheme cluster (UAX #29, Unicode 15.1) and its width. It is NOT streaming: give it the whole cluster or the logical end of the text, or a cluster split across buffers is measured as two. An invalid UTF-8 sequence is its own cluster of width I. For drawing prefer runae_unitas_proxima, which also splits off control bytes as one-column units.
    symbolum runae_graphema_proximum runae_graphema_ex_politica runae_unitas_proxima
  4. quomodohow do I find which character sits under a given column, for cursor placement or clicks?
    runae_columnam_quaerere(initium, finis, politica, columna, &columna_initii) returns the start of the unit covering that column and that unit's starting column. A column inside a wide (two-cell) grapheme snaps to the grapheme's start, zero-width units are skipped, and a column past the end returns finis with columna_initii set to the full text width.
    symbolum runae_columnam_quaerere
  5. quidwhat is the difference between RUNAE_POLITICA_GRAPHEMATUM and RUNAE_POLITICA_SIMPLEX?
    GRAPHEMATUM is Ghostty's rule (mode 2027, the default): a ZWJ emoji sequence is one cluster, at most 2 columns. SIMPLEX imitates Terminal.app: ZWJ does not join pictographs, so each emoji of a family is its own cluster; everything else, including spacing marks (Mc) widening a cluster to 2, is the same. SIMPLEX was derived from Terminal.app observations, not documentation. tessera does not detect the terminal; an app opts in through tessera_politicam_ponere with tessera_politica_ambitus().
    symbolum runae_graphema_ex_politica runae_unitas_proxima · via lib/runae.worklog.md tessera/fontes/tessera_opus.h
  6. quomodohow do I test grapheme boundaries rune by rune myself?
    Initialize a caller-owned RunaeRuptura with runae_rupturam_initiare, then call runae_rumpitur(prior, runa, &ruptura) for EVERY adjacent pair in order; it returns VERUM where a boundary falls between them. The small state (regional-indicator parity, emoji ZWJ and Indic conjunct sequences) is updated from prior on each call, so skipping a pair or reusing the state on another string gives wrong answers. Invalid runes break on both sides, like controls.
    symbolum runae_rupturam_initiare runae_rumpitur RunaeRuptura
  7. possumnecan I get case mapping, normalization or line breaking from runae?
    Not yet: today runae holds cell width and grapheme breaks only. Normalization, case, line breaks and bidi are planned here when needed; locale behaviour (collation, forms) never will be. The tables are generated from fixed Unicode RUNAE_VERSIO (15.1.0) data by tools/runae_generare.sh and checked against an ICU snapshot on every codepoint. Nothing allocates and there is no global state; the only mutable state is the caller-owned RunaeRuptura.
    symbolum runae_latitudo RUNAE_VERSIO · via tools/runae_generare.sh probationes/fixa/runae/aurum_icu.txt

scriba_applicatio

include/scriba_applicatio.h · lib/scriba_applicatio.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I start the scriba editor as a standalone app with its own window or terminal?
    Call thema_initiare yourself (the glue owns the theme), open the volume with scriba_volumen_aperire(piscina, argc, argv, &fumus), then scriba_applicatio_aedificare(&app, piscina, intern, vol, latitudo, altitudo) with the surface size in our pixels. It builds the repository, mount, dispatcher and gesture slot; the main then only attaches its window or tty to app.d. Close the volume with volumen_claudere at exit.
    symbolum scriba_volumen_aperire scriba_applicatio_aedificare thema_initiare volumen_claudere · via apps/scriba/scriba.c apps/scriba/scriba_terminalis.c
  2. quidwhat do -fumus and -volumen do on the scriba command line?
    scriba_volumen_aperire scans argv: -fumus opens a temporary volume (volumen_temporarium with prefix scriba_fumus, deleted on volumen_claudere) and sets *fumus; -volumen PATH opens or creates that file; otherwise scriba.volumen in the current directory. Unknown arguments are ignored, and -volumen with no path after it is ignored too. NIHIL means the volume could not be opened.
    symbolum scriba_volumen_aperire volumen_temporarium volumen_aperire_aut_creare
  3. quomodohow do I embed a scriba editor as one pane inside another app?
    Mount it into the host's InsulaRepositorium with scriba_montare(m, ..., repo, id, latitudo, altitudo, liber, systemata): id NIHIL uses the repository root, otherwise a child scriba element id is created under the root if absent. scriba_montare_in_ramo mounts under a parent branch instead (the calendar's day notes). Each mount has its own action and figura registries; its document is its own (in namespace id) when liber is NIHIL, else a page of the shared book chosen by the plagula scriba/visus/ID. systemata NIHIL means no persistence (tests).
    symbolum scriba_montare scriba_montare_in_ramo ScribaMontatio · via lib/vicus_applicatio.c lib/membrum__charta.c
  4. possumnecan I copy a ScribaMontatio or ScribaApplicatio struct by value?
    No. The action and figura contexts live INSIDE the ScribaMontatio and the registries point into them, so a copy leaves the registries pointing at the original. Keep the struct where you mounted it. ScribaApplicatio.doc, .actiones and .figurae are only aliases of the montatio fields.
    symbolum ScribaMontatio ScribaApplicatio scriba_montare
  5. curwhy is my nested scriba mount refused when its id contains a hyphen?
    The id becomes a canon nomen attribute on the branch, and the canon refuses '-' (write c1_notae, not c1-notae). The id also keys the page cursor plagula scriba/visus/ID and the saved state, so it must be unique in the whole volume. A NIHIL or empty id is refused with FALSUM.
    symbolum scriba_montare_in_ramo · via lib/scriba_applicatio.worklog.md
  6. quidwhat size is the sheet of a newly created scriba document?
    It follows the surface: the number of 6 x 8 cells in latitudo and altitudo minus one cell of margin on each side and, vertically, the status line, never less than 20 x 10. This applies only to a mount without a liber; an existing document, or a page of a book, keeps its own size. (The header's opening comment still says a new sheet is 68 x 56; that is no longer what the code does.)
    symbolum scriba_montare scriba_documentum_creare
  7. quomodohow do I keep two views of the same book in sync when one of them edits a page?
    Call scriba_reficere(m) on every pulse and BEFORE giving the view focus. It runs pending work and deferred commands, follows page navigation (m->doc catches up with the actions), refreshes the page indicator, and, when the document's cursor differs from the view's working cursor (another view committed), reloads the working sheet from the document. It returns VERUM when the pane must be repainted.
    symbolum scriba_reficere · via lib/vicus_applicatio.c
  8. quomodohow do I switch a mounted scriba view to a different book without remounting?
    scriba_librum_ponere(m, liber) shows the page named by the view's plagula if it exists in the new book, else its first page; registries and contexts stay. The scriba branch's owners only admit the writer pagina.clavis, so it sets that writer for the call and restores the previous one. FALSUM if liber is NIHIL or the view was never mounted.
    symbolum scriba_librum_ponere insula_scriptorem_ponere · via lib/membrum__charta.c

scriba_documentum

include/scriba_documentum.h · lib/scriba_documentum.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I open a text document from a volumen, or create it if it is not there?
    Try scriba_documentum_aperire first; it returns NIHIL when the manifest (key spatium/documentum, or just documentum for an empty spatium) is missing, does not parse, or has a width or height of 0 or less (and when the history cannot be opened). Only then call scriba_documentum_creare with the width, height and checkpoint interval (0 means LXIV; a width or height of 0 returns NIHIL). creare does not look for an existing document: it overwrites the manifest and starts a fresh history at cursor 0 while old acts stay in the volumen. It also ignores the result of the manifest write (volumen_plagulam_condere), so a failed write is not reported. lib/scriba_applicatio.c does exactly this aperire-then-creare.
    symbolum scriba_documentum_aperire scriba_documentum_creare volumen_plagulam_condere · via lib/scriba_applicatio.c
  2. quomodohow do I record an edit so it can be undone?
    Edit your own working TabulaCharacterum (same width and height), then call scriba_documentum_committere with it. The library diffs it against the projection, appends that act and returns its seq; 0 means nothing changed or the act was refused. Afterwards the projection equals your sheet. The projection from scriba_documentum_tabula is read-only: never edit it in place. Use scriba_documentum_committere_coniunctum to join the act to the previous one, so a run of typed chunks undoes as one step.
    symbolum scriba_documentum_committere scriba_documentum_committere_coniunctum scriba_documentum_tabula
  3. quidwhat does a mutatio act contain?
    The EFFECT of an edit, not the keys: after trimming trailing blank lines from both sheets, the common prefix and suffix are kept and the act says replace deletae lines at linea with the child linea elements (indentatio if set, textus if not all spaces). Because it is computed from before and after, it is exact for any pair of sheets no matter what vim did, and replay never runs vim. scriba_mutatio_computare returns an empty chorda when the sheets are equal or differ in size.
    symbolum scriba_mutatio_computare scriba_mutatio_applicare
  4. quidwhat counts as a blank line in a scriba sheet?
    Every cell a space AND indentation -1, which is what tabula_initiare and line insertion and deletion write. A NUL byte is content (vim can write one into the last column), and so is a line of spaces with indentation 0. Sheets built with tabula_ex_literis_cum_dimensionibus set indentation 0, so every line counts as content and every diff runs to the bottom (see the worklog).
    symbolum scriba_mutatio_computare tabula_initiare · via lib/scriba_documentum.worklog.md
  5. quomodohow do I escape arbitrary bytes into a safe STML attribute value and back?
    scriba_effugere writes backslash escapes for backslash, NUL, tab, byte 1, double quote (as \q) and \xHH for every other byte below 0x20 or from 0x7F up, so the result never holds a raw quote or control byte. scriba_solvere reverses it into a buffer you provide and returns the length, or -1 on a bad escape or when capacitas is too small. The hex digits must be lowercase: \x7F is rejected (scriba_effugere itself always writes lowercase, so its own output round-trips). Other code reuses this codec: lib/nomenclator.c, lib/membrum__prompta.c and lib/scriba_actiones.c.
    symbolum scriba_effugere scriba_solvere · via lib/nomenclator.c lib/membrum__prompta.c lib/scriba_actiones.c
  6. possumnecan a bad or oversized act damage the sheet?
    No. scriba_mutatio_applicare decodes all new lines into scratch first and returns FALSUM with the sheet untouched if the root is not mutatio, a child is not linea, the range runs past the content, a line is wider than the sheet, an escape is bad, or the result would have more lines than the sheet height.
    symbolum scriba_mutatio_applicare
  7. quomodohow do I undo, redo, and check that the projection is not corrupt?
    scriba_documentum_revocare and scriba_documentum_reficere move the history cursor (FALSUM at the start or end) and rebuild the projection by replaying from the nearest live checkpoint; each successful move also appends a <cursor ad="seq"/> note to the volumen, so aperire returns to that place (unless an act followed the note). A new commit after an undo appends a branch first, and the undone acts stay in the volumen as dead acts. scriba_documentum_verificare replays everything from nothing and compares seals. Compare scriba_documentum_cursor with the value you saw last to notice that another view committed, then copy the projection into your working sheet (lib/scriba_applicatio.c does this).
    symbolum scriba_documentum_revocare scriba_documentum_reficere scriba_documentum_verificare scriba_documentum_cursor scriba_documentum_finis
  8. possumnecan I keep one document open for a long session without the piscina growing?
    Not really: every commit computes the diff and every applied act parses STML and allocates scratch in the piscina given at creation, and undo or redo replays acts from the last checkpoint the same way. None of it is released until that piscina is destroyed, so memory grows with the number of edits and undos.
    symbolum scriba_documentum_committere scriba_mutatio_applicare scriba_documentum_revocare

scriba_liber

include/scriba_liber.h · lib/scriba_liber.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I open the page book and get the document for one page?
    Call scriba_liber_aperire(piscina, intern, volumen, latitudo, altitudo), then scriba_liber_pagina(l, titulus) for a name that is in the book, e.g. scriba_liber_nomen(l, ZEPHYRUM) for the first page. latitudo and altitudo (cells) are used only for pages created from now on. If the volume has no index yet, the book starts with the single page "1" and writes the index. aperire returns NIHIL when the index does not parse as STML, or when it is missing and writing it fails. scriba_liber_pagina returns NIHIL for a name that is not in the book, and also if the document can be neither opened nor created.
    symbolum scriba_liber_aperire scriba_liber_pagina scriba_liber_nomen
  2. curwhy does scriba_liber_pagina return the same ScribaDocumentum every time for a name?
    So that every scriba view of a page shares one object: two documents on one page's history in the same volume would write act logs that diverge. Documents open lazily on the first ask. The page's namespace is opened with scriba_documentum_aperire, and whenever that returns NIHIL (normally: no document yet, but any failure counts) a new one is made there with scriba_documentum_creare. The cache belongs to the ScribaLiber, so open the book ONCE per volume and prefix and share that pointer: a second book on the same index has its own cache and would hand out a second document for the same page. It also keeps its own name list and rewrites the whole index from it, so pages added through one book are dropped from the index when the other one adds a page.
    symbolum scriba_liber_pagina scriba_documentum_aperire scriba_documentum_creare · via lib/scriba_liber.worklog.md
  3. quomodohow do I add a page, numbered or named?
    scriba_liber_pagina_nova appends a page named with a number, starting at count + 1 and skipping names already used ("1", "3" gives "4"), rewrites the index and returns the new name. It returns an empty chorda and rolls the name back if the index write fails. It adds only the name: the page's document is made on the first scriba_liber_pagina for it. scriba_liber_paginam_condere(l, titulus) returns the document of a named page and appends the page first if it is new. The name must match [a-z0-9_-]+, otherwise it returns NIHIL; it also returns NIHIL when the index write fails or the document can be neither opened nor created. New pages always go at the end: index order is navigation order.
    symbolum scriba_liber_pagina_nova scriba_liber_paginam_condere
  4. possumnecan I delete, rename or reorder pages in a book?
    No. The API only adds pages, always at the end. Nothing removes a name from the index or moves one, and the book never calls volumen_plagulam_removere. Changing the order means rewriting the index plagula ('scriba/paginae', or '<prefix>/paginae') yourself and reopening the book.
    symbolum scriba_liber_pagina_nova scriba_liber_paginam_condere volumen_plagulam_removere
  5. quomodohow do I keep a separate book, for example one per calendar day?
    Use scriba_liber_aperire_in_spatio(piscina, intern, volumen, "dies/2026-10-14", latitudo, altitudo). Its index is '<prefix>/paginae' and each page's text lives in '<prefix>/paginae/<name>', apart from the shared vicus book that scriba_liber_aperire opens (index 'scriba/paginae', pages 'paginae/<name>'). A NIHIL or empty prefix returns NIHIL. The prefix is used verbatim, so do not end it with '/'.
    symbolum scriba_liber_aperire_in_spatio scriba_liber_aperire
  6. quidwhat do scriba_liber_nomen and scriba_liber_index return for a bad index or unknown name?
    scriba_liber_nomen returns an interned chorda. It is NOT NUL-terminated, and it is empty (datum NIHIL, mensura ZEPHYRUM) when the index is past the end or the book is NIHIL. The index argument is i32, which is unsigned, so a -1 passed in also comes back empty. scriba_liber_index returns s32 -1 for an unknown name. Names are interned on insertion, so the chorda you passed to paginam_condere does not have to outlive the book.
    symbolum scriba_liber_nomen scriba_liber_index scriba_liber_numerus

scrinium

include/scrinium.h · lib/scrinium.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I open a SQLite database and run a query with parameters?
    scrinium_aperire(piscina, via) opens or creates the file, then scrinium_praeparare compiles one statement. Bind with scrinium_ligare_textum / _numerum / _nihil / _massam (bind indices start at 1), step with scrinium_gradi until it stops returning SCRINIUM_ORDO, and read columns with scrinium_columna_* (column indices start at 0). Release the statement with scrinium_finire and the database with scrinium_claudere. Statements with no result rows can go through scrinium_exsequi in one call.
    symbolum scrinium_aperire scrinium_praeparare scrinium_ligare_textum scrinium_gradi scrinium_columna_textus scrinium_finire scrinium_claudere
  2. quomodohow do I open an in-memory database for a test?
    Pass ":memory:" (SQLite's own name). The header comment says ":memoria:", but the path goes to sqlite3_open unchanged, so ":memoria:" would create a file of that name in the current directory. The tests use ":memory:".
    symbolum scrinium_aperire · via probationes/probatio_scrinium.c
  3. possumnecan I bind a chorda directly, even though it is not NUL-terminated?
    Yes. scrinium_ligare_textum passes (datum, mensura) to sqlite3_bind_text with SQLITE_TRANSIENT, so a slice from the middle of a buffer binds correctly and SQLite copies it at once: the chorda may die right after the call. One trap: a chorda with datum NIHIL (the usual empty chorda) is passed as a NULL pointer, which SQLite binds as SQL NULL, not as ''. scrinium_ligare_massam does the same for a BLOB but guards that case: an empty massa (mensura 0 or datum NIHIL) binds an empty blob, not NULL.
    symbolum scrinium_ligare_textum scrinium_ligare_massam
  4. quidwhat do the column readers return, and who owns the memory?
    scrinium_columna_textus and scrinium_columna_massa COPY into the piscina you pass, so the chorda stays valid after the next scrinium_gradi; it is not NUL-terminated. An empty value and SQL NULL both come back as an empty chorda (datum NIHIL, mensura 0) for text as well as blobs: ask scrinium_columna_nihil_est first when the difference matters. scrinium_columna_numerus returns s64 (0 for NULL).
    symbolum scrinium_columna_textus scrinium_columna_massa scrinium_columna_nihil_est scrinium_columna_numerus
  5. curwhy are statements not allocated from my piscina?
    Statements are short-lived, and tying them to a piscina would keep their memory until the whole piscina dies. The wrapper is allocated with sqlite3_malloc beside SQLite's own statement memory (one allocator for ephemeral objects), so every scrinium_praeparare needs a scrinium_finire. Only the Scrinium handle lives in the piscina. scrinium_finire(NIHIL) is safe.
    symbolum scrinium_praeparare scrinium_finire · via lib/scrinium.worklog.md
  6. quomodohow do I reuse a prepared statement for many rows?
    After scrinium_gradi has finished, call scrinium_retexere: it resets the statement AND clears every binding (sqlite3_reset plus sqlite3_clear_bindings), so bind all parameters again before the next step. For a bulk insert wrap the loop in scrinium_incipere / scrinium_committere.
    symbolum scrinium_retexere scrinium_gradi scrinium_ligare_numerum
  7. quomodohow do I add a schema migration?
    Append a new SQL string to the END of your migrations array and pass the new count to scrinium_migrare. Migrations are numbered by array index (0-based) and the highest applied index is stored in the _migrationes table (one row per applied index); each call runs every migration after it once, all inside one BEGIN/COMMIT, and any failure rolls the whole batch back. Never edit, reorder or delete an applied entry: there are no down migrations. It returns VERUM when nothing is new.
    symbolum scrinium_migrare · via lib/volumen.c
  8. possumnecan I call scrinium_migrare or nest scrinium_incipere inside my own transaction?
    No. scrinium_incipere, scrinium_committere and scrinium_revolvere are plain BEGIN, COMMIT and ROLLBACK through scrinium_exsequi, with no nesting or savepoints, so a second scrinium_incipere fails. scrinium_migrare issues its own BEGIN whenever there is a migration to apply; inside an open transaction that BEGIN fails and the call returns FALSUM (with nothing new it returns VERUM before reaching BEGIN).
    symbolum scrinium_migrare scrinium_incipere scrinium_committere scrinium_revolvere
  9. quomodohow do I find out why a scrinium call failed?
    Calls report failure by FALSUM, NIHIL or SCRINIUM_ERROR (0) from scrinium_gradi; then scrinium_error returns SQLite's message for the last error, owned by SQLite and valid only until the next call. scrinium_aperire returns NIHIL with no message, because the handle is already closed. When one migration (or its _migrationes insert) fails, scrinium_migrare then runs ROLLBACK through sqlite3_exec, and a successful exec resets the error state, so the message of the failing migration is gone by the time the call returns FALSUM.
    symbolum scrinium_error scrinium_gradi scrinium_praeparare scrinium_aperire
  10. curwhy may only one process write to a scrinium database?
    It is a house discipline, not something scrinium enforces: one process writes, others only read. The vendored SQLite is built THREADSAFE=0 under the same rule, so one handle must also never be shared between threads. scrinium_aperire sets journal_mode=WAL, busy_timeout=5000, synchronous=NORMAL and foreign_keys=ON: readers in other processes run in parallel with the writer, and a second writer that does turn up waits up to five seconds for the lock and fails only if it is still held. The pragma results are not checked.
    symbolum scrinium_aperire · via lib/scrinium.worklog.md
  11. quomodohow do I generate a ULID for a new row?
    Give scrinium_ulid a character buffer of at least SCRINIUM_ULID_MENSURA (27) bytes: it writes 26 Crockford base32 characters plus a NUL. It delegates to moneta_ulid, which keeps static state: within one process, ids made in the same millisecond (or after the clock steps back) increment the random part, so they sort in creation order. Use it for row identity; content identity is a hash (sigillum).
    symbolum scrinium_ulid moneta_ulid SCRINIUM_ULID_MENSURA
  12. possumnecan FTS5 full-text search match Latin word forms?
    FTS5 is compiled in, but the porter stemmer only knows English, so 'parsuram' does not match 'parsura'. The working idiom for Latin text is a prefix query ('parsur*') with the unicode61 tokenizer.
    symbolum scrinium_aperire · via lib/scrinium.worklog.md

sigillum

include/sigillum.h · lib/sigillum.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I compute the SHA-256 of a buffer and print it as hex?
    Sigillum s = sigillum_computare(datum, mensura); then sigillum_hex(&s, hex) with a buffer of at least SIGILLUM_HEX_MENSURA (65) bytes: it writes 64 lowercase hex digits plus a NUL. For a chorda pass c.datum and c.mensura; no NUL is needed and none is read. The raw 32 bytes are in s.octeti.
    symbolum sigillum_computare sigillum_hex
  2. quomodohow do I hash data that arrives in pieces?
    Declare a SigillumContextus on the stack, call sigillum_incipere, then sigillum_addere for each piece (any sizes; the result equals the one-shot hash of the concatenation) and sigillum_finire once. finire pads the context in place and leaves massa_mensura at 64, so the context is spent: a further sigillum_addere or a second sigillum_finire on it writes past the 64-byte partial block (out of bounds, not just a wrong digest). Call sigillum_incipere again before reusing it. Nothing is allocated.
    symbolum sigillum_incipere sigillum_addere sigillum_finire
  3. possumnecan I hash a shared prefix once and then finish several different digests from it?
    Yes: SigillumContextus is a plain struct with no pointers (state, partial block, length), so copying it with = forks the hash. Feed the prefix, copy the context for each branch, add the branch data and finish each copy.
    symbolum sigillum_addere sigillum_finire SigillumContextus
  4. quomodohow do I compare two digests, for example to check a token or MAC?
    sigillum_aequale(&a, &b). It runs in constant time (it ORs the XOR of all 32 bytes instead of stopping at the first difference like memcmp), so it is safe where the digest guards a secret. Either pointer NIHIL gives FALSUM.
    symbolum sigillum_aequale
  5. quomodohow do I compute an HMAC-SHA256, for example to sign an AWS SigV4 request?
    sigillum_hmac(clavis, mensura_clavis, datum, mensura_datorum) implements RFC 2104: a key longer than 64 bytes is hashed first, a shorter one is zero-padded, and an empty key is allowed. Verified against RFC 4231 vectors. It is one-shot; there is no incremental HMAC context.
    symbolum sigillum_hmac
  6. possumnecan I parse a 64-character hex string back into a Sigillum?
    Not with this library: it only goes from digest to hex. lib/thesaurus.c has its own private _ex_hex (64 LOWERCASE hex digits, refusing anything else); copy that pattern or ask for a public sigillum_ex_hex rather than writing another private one.
    symbolum sigillum_hex · via lib/thesaurus.c
  7. curwhy is there both sigillum and friatio, two hash libraries?
    friatio hashes to DISPERSE keys over hash-table buckets, where speed matters and a collision only costs a probe. sigillum hashes to IDENTIFY content (content-addressed stores, ledger entities, versions of reducers), where a collision would merge two different things, so collision resistance is load-bearing and SHA-256 is used.
    symbolum sigillum_computare · via include/friatio.h lib/sigillum.worklog.md

silex

include/silex.h · lib/silex.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I scaffold a new project from the fabrica in code?
    Fill a SilexNovumOptiones (fons, destinatio = parent directory, titulus, vitrea) and call silex_novum. It refuses an empty titulus, one containing '/' or '.', a '-' when vitrea is set (the titulus becomes a C symbol), and a destinatio/titulus that already exists. Nothing else is checked: the titulus is pasted raw into generated C string literals and shell scripts, so keep it to letters, digits and '_' (a quote breaks fontes/<t>.c, a space breaks the vitrea scripts). It vendors the closure of latina.h piscina.h chorda.h credo.h (vitrea: the closures of its own templates), generates the sources and scripts, records everything in <destinatio>/<titulus>/<titulus>.volumen with a birth conditio, then writes the tree by reading it back from the volumen. On failure successus is FALSUM and erratum says why; a directory created before the failure is left behind, so the next attempt is refused until you delete it.
    symbolum silex_novum SilexNovumOptiones SilexNovumFructus silex_fons_disci
  2. quomodohow do I get every library file a set of headers depends on?
    silex_clausuram_colligere takes header names as they are cited ('chorda.h') and does a BFS over quoted includes, returning a Xar of SilexRes (via, contentum, origo 'vendicata:...'). For each include/X.h it adds lib/X.c, or failing that lib/X_macos.m, _macos.c, _posix.c, _impl.c, and then every lib/ file named by an aedilis corpus annotation in the header. Headers missing from include/ are looked up in SILEX_RADICES_CLIENTIUM; vendor/ files are leaves whose includes are never scanned. A NIHIL fons gives NIHIL. silex_clausuram_e_contentis does the same with seeds read from the include lines of source texts you pass, skipping the template's own <t>_pipa.h and capsula_* headers.
    symbolum silex_clausuram_colligere silex_clausuram_e_contentis SilexRes
  3. possumnecan an include that is commented out or inside #if 0 still pull a file into the closure?
    Yes for #if 0 and for a line inside a multi-line /* */ comment: the include scan is textual, line by line, and any line whose first non-blank character is '#' followed by include and a quoted name ending in .h counts, whatever surrounds it. A line that starts with something else (// #include, /* #include) is not seen, nor is an angle-bracket include. A cited header that does not exist in the source only prints a warning to stderr and is skipped (real dependencies always exist; phantom mentions do not); a header whose implementation is not found is kept and also warns. silex_monitiones_tacere(VERUM) silences those warnings (briar does).
    symbolum silex_clausuram_colligere silex_monitiones_tacere
  4. quidwhat is a SilexFons, and what is the difference between DISCUS and CORPUS?
    The one reader every verb uses for library sources. DISCUS (silex_fons_disci) reads a fabrica checkout on disk; it only checks that include/ exists and keeps your path pointer without copying it. CORPUS (silex_fons_corporis) opens a capsula embedded in the binary and takes its titulus from the key corpus.versio. silex_fons_legere sets *inventum FALSUM for an unknown path. silex_fons_enumerare lists names directly in one directory with a suffix, sorted so both kinds agree; a missing directory gives an empty Xar, NIHIL only when memory runs out.
    symbolum SilexFons silex_fons_disci silex_fons_corporis silex_fons_legere silex_fons_enumerare
  5. quomodohow does silex find the fabrica when I do not pass one?
    silex_fabricam_invenire climbs from the starting directory (at most 64 levels) and returns the first absolute path holding include/latina.h, a lib/ directory and fabrica.stml, or NIHIL. The declared marker fabrica.stml matters: scaffolded projects carry latina.h and lib/ too and once found themselves. The CLI tries -f/--fabrica first (an invalid one is an error, not a fallback), then this ascent from the current directory, then the embedded corpus.
    symbolum silex_fabricam_invenire · via tools/silex.c fabrica.stml
  6. quidwhich volumen file does silex use for a project directory?
    silex_volumen_viam_invenire first tries <dir>/<basename of dir>.volumen; if that is absent, a single *.volumen in the directory wins (so a renamed copy still opens). Two or more prints an ambiguity message to stderr and returns NIHIL; silex_status, silex_condere, silex_proicere, silex_renovare and the differentia verbs then fail with 'volumen deest', silex_historia returns NIHIL, and silex_partes falls back to the project's own files only.
    symbolum silex_volumen_viam_invenire
  7. quidwhat is a commit in silex, and what does silex status ignore?
    A commit is not an object: it is a named point, an actum 'conditio', in the volumen's event log. silex_condere runs silex_status, refuses an empty change, and absorbs every MUTATA, NOVA and ABSENS file plus the conditio in ONE transaction (all or nothing). silex_status never writes; it skips the project's top-level bin/ and build/, every *.volumen with its -wal and -shm, and every hidden file or directory (name starting with '.'), so dotfiles are invisible to the VCS. silex_historia lists the volumen's birth ('(ortus voluminis)') and then the conditiones, each with the number of file events since the previous one; SilexConditio.renovatio is read from the act's data, not its message.
    symbolum silex_condere silex_status silex_historia SilexConditio
  8. quomodohow do I put the working tree back the way it was at an earlier commit?
    silex_proicere(piscina, dir, ad_seq, scribere): ad_seq at or below 0 is the present manifest, N is the tree as of seq N. Without scribere it only plans. With scribere it refuses everything if any file it would overwrite holds content found in no stored massa (OBEX, 'conde prima'), then writes the SCRIBENDA and CREANDA files. Files on disk that the fold does not know (ALIENA) are listed but never touched or deleted, so files added after N stay. The log does not move; silex_condere afterwards makes the old tree a NEW point.
    symbolum silex_proicere SilexProiciendaStatus silex_condere
  9. quomodohow do I update vendored libraries after they change in the fabrica?
    silex_renovare compares three sigilla per vendored file: the last vendicata record in the log, the disk today, and the fabrica today through a recomputed closure (seeds = includes of your own files on disk plus the vendored headers). RENOVANDA (untouched by you, upstream moved) and ADDENDA (in the closure, never vendored) are written with scribere and recorded in one volumen transaction, plus a conditio whose data has renovatae (only if something was written). VULNUS (you edited or deleted it, upstream did not move), CONFLICTUS (both moved, or your own file in the way) and DERELICTA (dropped upstream) are only named, never touched, and do not block the rest. Timestamps are never used. Two limits: each file hits the disk before it is recorded, so a failure midway rolls back the volumen but not the files already written; and a vendored file whose removal you committed comes back as ADDENDA if its header is still in the closure.
    symbolum silex_renovare SilexRenovatioStatus
  10. quomodohow do I get a text diff of what changed in a silex project?
    silex_differentia_laborans diffs a fold (a_seq, at or below 0 = present) against the disk; silex_differentia_plicarum diffs two folds. Results come sorted by via with MUTATA/NOVA/ABSENS, a DifferentiaSumma, and the raw textus_vetus and textus_novus; identical files are only counted in aequales. textus is a unified diff (III lines of context, headers a/via b/via) only when cum_textu is VERUM; the summa is computed either way.
    symbolum silex_differentia_laborans silex_differentia_plicarum SilexDifferentiaRes
  11. quomodohow do I check which vendored files a project needs and whether they are present?
    silex_partes(piscina, dir, fons, plagula) computes the closure of the project's own files (plus the vendored headers in its manifest) or, when plagula is given, of that one file, and returns SilexPartesRes with adest = present on disk. An entry with adest FALSUM is what the header calls the files 'renovare -scribere' would vendor (one exception: a vendored file you deleted by hand while upstream did not move is VULNUS there and is not rewritten). It returns NIHIL for a NIHIL fons, when the given plagula is not on disk (so a typo looks like a broken fabrica), and when the closure cannot be collected.
    symbolum silex_partes SilexPartesRes
  12. quomodohow do I generate a build script for my sources and their closure?
    silex_ordinem_fingere returns a /bin/sh script compiling your fontes with SILEX_VEXILLA_COMPILATIONIS and -Iinclude into bin/<titulus>. Passing clausura NIHIL links the glob lib/*.c; passing a closure lists its lib .c and .m files (and client-root .c files) explicitly, adds -I for each client root it touches, and adds the Cocoa, Security and WebKit frameworks if any .m is present. silex_ordinem_probandi_fingere builds and execs bin/probatio_<titulus>. silex_ordinem_vitreum_fingere emits the four-stage vitrea build (capsula tool, vendor objects, app with frameworks, rm before mv) with SILEX_VEXILLA_VITREA and always an explicit file list, and silex_ordinem_probandi_vitreum_fingere the vitrea test build.
    symbolum silex_ordinem_fingere silex_ordinem_probandi_fingere silex_ordinem_vitreum_fingere silex_ordinem_probandi_vitreum_fingere SILEX_VEXILLA_COMPILATIONIS

similitudo

include/similitudo.h · lib/similitudo.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I rank a list of identifiers against a short fuzzy query, like fzf?
    Pass the query, an array of chordae and an output array of SimilitudoFructus to similitudo_optima. A candidate matches only if the query is a case-insensitive subsequence of it. Up to maxima results are written, best first (score descending, then the shorter candidate, then the lower index), and the count written is returned; each result's index points back into your candidate array.
    symbolum similitudo_optima SimilitudoFructus
  2. quomodohow do I suggest "did you mean" names for a mistyped full identifier?
    Use similitudo_optima_decurtata, not similitudo_optima. A wrongly guessed full name is usually a subsequence of nothing, so plain matching returns zero results. decurtata scores each candidate by its best prefix of the query of length III or more, so a wrong tail no longer kills the match. A query shorter than III characters returns ZEPHYRUM results.
    symbolum similitudo_optima_decurtata similitudo_optima
  3. quidwhat does a similitudo score reward and punish?
    Each matched character gains XVI at a word start (position 0, after one of _ - . / : or a space, or a lowercase-to-uppercase step), VIII when it directly follows the previous match, and I when its case is exact. A gap between two matches costs III plus I per skipped character beyond the first; characters skipped before the first match cost nothing. Scores can be negative (sparse matches). Larger means more similar; an empty query matches everything with score ZEPHYRUM.
    symbolum similitudo_punctare
  4. possumnecan I count on similitudo finding the best-scoring alignment of the query?
    No. The walk is greedy: each query character takes the leftmost candidate character that matches, and it never backtracks. The optimal alignment (dynamic programming) is a named deferral in the header; the API would stay the same if it were added.
    symbolum similitudo_punctare
  5. possumnecan I fuzzy-match non-ASCII text case-insensitively with similitudo?
    No. Only ASCII A-Z is folded to lowercase; every other byte, including UTF-8 sequences, must match exactly, byte for byte. Word boundaries are ASCII-only as well. The functions are pure: no piscina, no allocation and no state, so they are safe to call anywhere.
    symbolum similitudo_punctare
  6. curwhy does similitudo_optima_decurtata merge all prefix lengths instead of using the longest prefix that matches?
    Stopping at the longest matching prefix let one extra character of a guess's wrong tail win: 'legatus_tractare' suggested sloppy LEGATUS_* macros and never legatus_currere. The tail is the least trustworthy part of a guess, so every prefix from III up is scored in the same single greedy walk and the best score counts. A messy tail match lowers the score by itself, so no extra penalty term is needed (worklog 2026-07-30).
    symbolum similitudo_optima_decurtata · via lib/similitudo.worklog.md

sors

include/sors.h · lib/sors.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I get a random integer in a range that is the same on every run?
    Declare a plain Sors value, seed it once with sors_seminare(&s, semen, series), then draw. sors_intra(&s, n) gives [0, n) with no modulo bias (Lemire multiply-and-reject; n of 0 gives 0) and sors_inter(&s, imum, summum) gives the inclusive signed range (imum >= summum gives imum). The generator is integer-only PCG32 seeded through SplitMix64, so the same seed gives the same series on every machine and compiler.
    symbolum sors_seminare sors_intra sors_inter
  2. possumnecan I use a Sors without calling sors_seminare first?
    No. A zeroed Sors has incrementum 0 (the header says it must always be odd) and status 0, and PCG then stays at 0: every sors_proximum returns 0 forever. Always seed it; seed 0 is fine, SplitMix64 mixes it.
    symbolum sors_seminare sors_proximum Sors
  3. quomodohow do I give each stroke, asset or test case its own independent random stream?
    sors_derivare(&parens, clavis) returns a child Sors and does not touch the parent, so child k is the same no matter how many other children were derived before it. It does depend on the parent's CURRENT state: derive before drawing from the parent, or after the same number of draws, if you want the same child back. For fixed parallel streams from one seed, sors_seminare's series argument (0, 1, 2 ...) also gives disjoint streams.
    symbolum sors_derivare sors_seminare
  4. possumnecan I use sors for ids, tokens or anything that must be unguessable?
    No. sors is deliberately seedable and predictable (for replay, procedural assets and fuzzing). Use moneta, which reads /dev/urandom and cannot be seeded: moneta_ulid for unique ids (it falls back to a time mix if /dev/urandom fails, which keeps ids unique but not unguessable), and moneta_octeti_fortuiti for secrets, which has no fallback and returns FALSUM, leaving the output untouched, so the caller must refuse.
    symbolum moneta_octeti_fortuiti moneta_ulid sors_seminare · via include/moneta.h
  5. quomodohow do I get a random float, a coin flip or a shuffle?
    sors_f64 gives [0, 1) from LIII bits (two draws) and sors_f32 [0, 1) from XXIV bits. sors_casu(&s, numerator, denominator) is VERUM with probability numerator/denominator (denominator 0 gives FALSUM). sors_miscere is an in-place Fisher-Yates shuffle that swaps the raw bytes of numerus elements of magnitudo_elementi bytes each, allocating nothing.
    symbolum sors_f64 sors_f32 sors_casu sors_miscere
  6. curwhy does sors keep the old 1103515245 + 12345 LCG?
    sors_lcg_classicus is a legacy bridge: several fuzz suites had inline copies of the classic ANSI LCG, and keeping it under a name lets a suite move to the library without its generated cases changing (the migration rubric: use it only if the fixed cases must stay). In practice the fuzz suites migrated to sors_seminare and sors_intra instead, accepting that every generated case changed, and today only probatio_sors calls it. New code should use sors_intra: % n on the LCG's output is biased, and sors_intra is not.
    symbolum sors_lcg_classicus sors_intra · via lib/sors.worklog.md

stml

include/stml.h · lib/stml.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I parse an STML document and tell whether it succeeded?
    Call stml_legere(input, piscina, intern) (or stml_legere_ex_literis for a NUL-terminated C string). It returns an StmlResultus by value: check successus (status == STML_SUCCESSUS). radix is the DOCUMENTUM node and elementum_radix its first element child. On failure status names the class, linea_erroris and columna_erroris locate the FIRST error (later ones are treated as its cascade). The error chorda is filled only by the named refusals (MULTILINEA and the attribute-element ATTRIBUTUM ones); most errors, including a single-quoted value (ATTRIBUTUM), leave it empty. radix is still set after a parse error (a partial tree), so test successus, not radix; empty input gives STML_ERROR_VACUUM_INPUT with radix NIHIL. CRLF is turned into LF once on entry (crlf_canonicalizatum says so). Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): some malformed inputs still give successus with a silently corrupted tree (a closer missing '>' as in <a>t</a, a non-name byte in a tag name as in <a+b>x</a+b>, the fake doctype <!D><a/>b><c/>, capture operators missing '=>' or '>'), and nesting about 100000 levels deep kills the process with SIGSEGV instead of returning an error.
    symbolum stml_legere stml_legere_ex_literis StmlResultus
  2. possumnecan I empty the piscina I parsed into and keep the strings I read from the tree?
    Yes for the strings, no for the nodes. Nodes, their Xars, the error chorda and the results of the text accessors live in the piscina you pass in. Every titulus, valor, attribute value and trivia chorda is interned, and an InternamentumChorda copies into its OWN piscina (the one given to internamentum_creare), so those chordae survive piscina_vacare on the parse piscina (agenda.c reparses into a scratch piscina this way). They are chorda, not C strings: not NUL-terminated, so compare with chorda_aequalis_literis and convert with chorda_ut_cstr.
    symbolum stml_legere chorda_internare internamentum_creare piscina_vacare chorda_ut_cstr · via lib/agenda.c
  3. quomodohow do I read an attribute, and how do I tell a boolean attribute from a missing one?
    stml_attributum_capere(nodus, "name") returns the interned chorda* or NIHIL when absent. A boolean attribute (<a x>) stores the interned value "true", so <a x> and <a x="true"> are the same thing and the writer emits both as bare x, in both modes. Values are RAW: no entity decoding. With a duplicated attribute the FIRST one wins (stml_strictum reports it). If no inline attribute matches, capere also looks at attribute ELEMENTS (<@name=>text</>) at the start of the children and returns their text; an empty one is a tombstone and reads as NIHIL. stml_attributum_habet is just capere != NIHIL, so an attribute written v="" also reads as absent (see the next question).
    symbolum stml_attributum_capere stml_attributum_habet stml_strictum
  4. possumnecan I store an empty string or a double quote in an attribute value?
    Neither works inline.
