An empty chorda is never interned (chorda_internare returns NIHIL), so <a v=""/> parses with valor NIHIL: stml_attributum_capere returns NIHIL as if v were absent, and the writer emits bare v, which reads back as "true". A '"' cannot be written because inline values are raw and always wrapped in '"'; stml_attributum_addere does not check, and what stml_scribere emits either fails to parse or silently re-splits (v="x"y" reads back as v="x" plus a boolean y). For a value with '"' use an attribute element <@name=>text</> (its text child decodes entities). No constructor exists: like titulum_addere in lib/agenda.c or nomen_addere in lib/personae.c, call stml_elementum_creare(p, in, "@"), set attributum_titulus to the interned name, give it one text child, and place it before every other child. Only that leading run is read as attributes, and the parser refuses an attribute element that comes after any other child (a comment counts) unless it follows a template call <<#@...>>; nothing checks this when you build or write the tree.
    symbolum stml_attributum_addere stml_attributum_capere chorda_internare stml_scribere stml_elementum_creare · via lib/agenda.c lib/personae.c
  5. quomodohow do I find elements by tag name, or loop over all children of a node?
    stml_invenire_liberum and stml_invenire_omnes_liberos look at DIRECT children only and match element nodes by exact name. For descendants or CSS-style selectors (tag, #id, .label, [attr=val], >, +, ~, :first-child, :last-child, :empty) use stml_quaerere / stml_quaerere_omnes from selectio.h. To walk children, loop i over stml_numerus_liberorum with stml_liberum_ad_indicem and check genus: text, comment and element nodes are mixed, and attribute elements (<@name=>) are element children too, with titulus "@". Avoid stml_frater_proximus in a loop: each call rescans the parent's children to find its own index.
    symbolum stml_invenire_liberum stml_invenire_omnes_liberos stml_quaerere stml_quaerere_omnes stml_numerus_liberorum stml_liberum_ad_indicem stml_frater_proximus
  6. quomodohow do I get the text inside an element, and which of the four text accessors should I use?
    Use stml_textus_valor for data: the text nodes concatenated in order, comments and layout trivia excluded. It is stable under the fidelity writer and for raw or '\' content, but the pretty writer re-wraps flow prose, which changes the newlines inside a text value. stml_textus_fluxus reads prose: a whitespace run that holds a newline becomes one space, so it survives that re-wrap. stml_textus_normalizatus removes common indentation and leading/trailing blank lines from hand-indented blocks. stml_textus_internus rebuilds the layout bytes (trivia) as well; despite the header's wording, entities come back DECODED (x&amp;y reads x&y). All four allocate in the piscina you pass and return a chorda that is not NUL-terminated. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): the pretty writer drops a newline-bearing whitespace run after an inline child element or comment, so <p>one <b>two</b>\n three</p> reads back with fluxus "one twothree" and <p>a<!--c-->\n b</p> with "ab".
    symbolum stml_textus_valor stml_textus_fluxus stml_textus_normalizatus stml_textus_internus · via project-specs/stml-trivia-spec.md lib/stml.worklog.md
  7. quomodohow do I write a tree back to text, and what is the difference between pretty and non-pretty output?
    stml_scribere(nodus, piscina, pulchrum). With pulchrum FALSUM it re-emits the stored trivia, so a parsed document round-trips byte for byte over the LF-canonicalized input, apart from attributes: spaces around '=' are dropped, unquoted values gain quotes, x="true" becomes bare x and v="" becomes bare v. With pulchrum VERUM it is a reformatter: trivia are ignored and layout is regenerated (two-space indent, capture forms, prose re-filled to LXXII columns, wider when deeply nested). A tree built in code has no trivia, so FALSUM writes it on one line; use VERUM. stml_scribere_ad_aedificator appends to a ChordaAedificator, and stml_scribere_sedibus also records where each element landed in the output. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): pulchrum VERUM loses the space where a newline follows an inline child (<p>one <b>two</b>\n three</p> is written <p>one <b>two</b>three</>), and a tree nested about 100000 deep crashes stml_scribere with SIGSEGV.
    symbolum stml_scribere stml_scribere_ad_aedificator stml_scribere_sedibus · via project-specs/stml-trivia-spec.md
  8. curwhy does the pretty writer emit </> and <a(> forms instead of named closing tags?
    The pretty-layout decrees (worklog 2026-08-19 to 2026-08-25). A repeated name is noise while the open tag is still on screen, so the closer is the anonymous </> until the content passes XXX lines (STML_CLAUSURA_TACITA_LINEAE). This cannot oscillate because both closers take the same number of lines. Eligible single-child chains collapse to capture form (<a(> <b(> text), and elements whose two or three children are all simple elements, within XII lines, use <t((> / <t(((> with the children stacked, so closing tags remain only on the multi-child block form. Raw elements always keep their named closer, and attribute elements always close with </>. Non-pretty output keeps the author's own form.
    symbolum stml_scribere · via lib/stml.worklog.md
  9. quidwhat are the capture operators <tag (>, <) tag>, <= tag => and <tag! (>?
    Ways to give an element children without a closing tag; they are resolved after parsing. <tag (> takes the next N non-comment siblings, one per paren (chains such as <a(> <b(> x nest), and comments in between become its children too. <) tag> takes the previous N (comments in between stay siblings), <= tag => one before and one after. <tag! (> captures the rest of the LINE raw (no tags, no entities, newline excluded). Fragments capture too (<#id (>, sugar <(>). The node records captio_directio and captio_numerus. Known gap: a comment between <= tag => and its following captee is dropped. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): STML_ERROR_CAPTIO is never set, so a malformed operator (<r>x <= s y</r> with no '=>', <) z with no '>') or a captor with too few siblings (<r><a (></r>) parses with status 0 and the writer invents the missing terminator.
    symbolum StmlCaptioDirectio stml_legere
  10. possumnecan a raw <tag!> element contain its own closing sequence </tag>?
    Yes, through the escape ladder. A raw region ends at </T followed by >, ! or whitespace. The writer escapes such a sequence in the value by adding a backslash (<\/T>), and one more for each level already present; the reader removes one. Scanner and writer share one definition (stml_crudi_sequentia_est), and stml_crudi_terminatorem_fert tells you whether a value carries a terminator. One limit: a raw value that STARTS with </T followed by a name character (<code!></codex...) is not escaped and fails to parse, because the check at the very start of raw content compares only the </T prefix.
    symbolum stml_crudi_sequentia_est stml_crudi_terminator_est stml_crudi_terminatorem_fert
  11. quomodohow do I build an element in code with attributes, text and children?
    stml_elementum_creare gives an element with empty attributa and liberi Xars; then stml_attributum_addere (or _chorda, _boolean_addere), stml_textum_addere and stml_liberum_addere. Names and values are interned, so the C strings can be temporary. The add functions return FALSUM when the target Xar is NIHIL, as on any node from stml_duplicare_superficialiter (liberi is always NIHIL), on stml_duplicare copies of nodes that had no children or no attributes, and on parsed <#x/> fragments. stml_praeponere creates a missing liberi; nothing creates a missing attributa. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): no name is validated, so stml_elementum_creare with "a b", "", "9bad" or "-x" and stml_attributum_addere with ".k", a NIHIL titulus or a NIHIL valor all succeed, yet the written tree reads back changed or fails (titulus "" is written </> and closes the parent; a NIHIL valor is written as bare k while capere on the built node returns NIHIL).
    symbolum stml_elementum_creare stml_attributum_addere stml_attributum_boolean_addere stml_textum_addere stml_liberum_addere stml_praeponere stml_duplicare_superficialiter
  12. possumnecan I move a node to another parent with stml_liberum_addere or stml_inserere_post?
    Only after detaching it. No insert function (nor stml_substituere for its new node) removes the node from its old parent: it overwrites parens, the old parent still lists the node, and it is written twice. Call stml_removere first (it finds the parent through parens). stml_praeponere, stml_inserere_ante, stml_inserere_post and stml_removere build a NEW liberi Xar on every call (O(n), allocated from the piscina), so a pointer to the old parent->liberi goes stale; stml_liberum_addere and stml_substituere work in place. Nodes made with the creare functions carry no trivia, so non-pretty output glues them to their neighbours; moved or cloned parsed nodes keep their own.
    symbolum stml_removere stml_liberum_addere stml_inserere_ante stml_inserere_post stml_substituere
  13. quomodohow do I map byte positions in STML text to tokens or nodes, for highlighting or jump-to?
    For tokens, stml_lexemata_colligere returns a Xar of StmlLexema (genus, valor, initium/finis byte offsets [inclusive, exclusive), 1-based linea and byte columna) for ANY input, even half-typed text, with no parse and no error; it returns NIHIL for empty input or a NIHIL piscina/intern, and STML_TOKEN_FINIS is not added. Paint by the extent, not by valor (text token valor is the raw source slice). For parsed nodes, positus_initium and positus_finis span the opening tag through the closing tag, and linea is the start line; they are ZEPHYRUM on built nodes, and a captor's span covers only its own tag. All these offsets refer to the CRLF-to-LF canonicalized text. For written output, stml_scribere_sedibus fills a caller-made Xar of StmlSedesNodi for elements only, in post-order.
    symbolum stml_lexemata_colligere StmlLexema StmlNodus stml_scribere_sedibus StmlSedesNodi
  14. quidwhat does stml_strictum check that stml_legere lets through?
    The parser is deliberately lenient; stml_strictum(result.radix, piscina) judges the parsed DOCUMENT node and returns a Xar of StmlStrictumVitium: duplicated inline attribute, more than one root element, non-whitespace text outside the root, and a nameless element (< >, with a space; <> is an anonymous fragment). stml_strictum_nuntius gives the message. Accepted on purpose: unknown entities stay literal text (only &lt; &gt; &amp; &quot; &apos; decode, so &#60; is kept as written), and unquoted values. Unquoted values may only use letters, digits, '-' and '_': <a v=1.5> reads as v="1" and the rest of the tag becomes text, which stml_strictum does not catch.
    symbolum stml_strictum stml_strictum_nuntius StmlStrictumGenus

stml_macros

include/stml_macros.h · lib/stml_macros.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I load an STML document so templates, queries and anonymous lists are resolved before I read it?
    Run the chain with ONE InternamentumChorda and one piscina: stml_legere, then stml_expandere(resultus.radix, ...), then stml_distribuere(expansio.radix_expansa, ...). Pass the DOCUMENT node (resultus.radix), not elementum_radix, or document-level doctype, processing instructions and comments fall outside the subtree. stml_expandere and stml_distribuere each return a NEW tree and leave their input untouched, so elementum_radix still points into the original tree afterwards and must be found again in the result. tools/stml.c (the stml expandere verb) is the reference caller; spec section 3.1 lists the laws.
    symbolum stml_legere stml_expandere stml_distribuere · via project-specs/stml-instrumentum-spec.md tools/stml.c
  2. possumnecan I check radix_expansa != NIHIL to know that stml_expandere worked?
    No: test successus. Some vitia are soft. Anything that goes wrong while a reference in a string position (attribute value, interpolated text, an inner call's arguments) is substituted is recorded but does not stop the walk: a subtree argument there is ARGUMENTUM_ARBOREUM (VII), and a failing projection there is PROIECTIO_ABSENS or PROIECTIO_AMBIGUA (XXVII, XXVIII). The literal &@p; stays in place. A relation that no PER and no de= consumed is EXITUS_NON_CONSUMPTUS (XVIII), set by the end guard. In these cases radix_expansa is a non-NIHIL but wrong tree. Hard vitia stop the walk and return radix_expansa NIHIL. Only the first vitium is reported, with linea, fragmentum and loculus filled where they apply (empty chorda otherwise).
    symbolum stml_expandere StmlExpansioResultus
  3. curwhy does every template call fail with FRAGMENTUM_IGNOTUM even though the definition is right there?
    Most likely stml_expandere got a different InternamentumChorda from the one stml_legere used. Definitions keep the id the parser interned, calls intern the id with the intern you pass, and the two are compared by pointer, so with a second intern no call ever finds its definition. Create one intern before stml_legere and pass it to every stage.
    symbolum stml_expandere stml_legere internamentum_creare · via project-specs/stml-instrumentum-spec.md
  4. quidwhat is the difference between a #f fragment and a #@f template, and why does my call not expand?
    Only ids that start with @ are templates. <#@f> defines one; it is removed from the expanded tree, and each <<#@f ...>> is replaced by a fresh filled copy of its body (instantiation). <#f> and <<#f>> are content fragments and transclusions: stml_expandere copies them through untouched, because a plain transclusion is an alias (the same object seen twice), not a copy. <<#solus>> with no definition is therefore not an error. Decided 2026-08-26 when arbor's shared lexemes would otherwise have been deleted.
    symbolum stml_expandere · via lib/stml_macros.worklog.md
  5. quomodohow do I write a template with parameters and pass it text or a subtree?
    Declare each slot as an attribute VALUE starting with @ on the defining tag (the attribute name is ignored: k="@p" declares p), and refer to it in the body as &@p; in text or attribute values: <#@f p="@p"><a x="&@p;"/></#>. Call it inline with <<#@f p="v">>, or follow the call with block arguments <@p=>...</>. A block with no children is a tombstone (the slot counts as not passed). A block holding one text child is a scalar, unless that text is exactly &@x; naming a subtree argument of the enclosing template, which passes that subtree on. Any other block is a subtree, which may only stand alone as the whole text &@p; (it is spliced as a fresh copy each time). A subtree in an attribute or mixed text is vitium VII. Every required slot must be filled (IV), no unknown name may be passed (V), and no name twice, inline or block (VIII). Since any value of two or more characters starting with @ is a declaration, a defining tag cannot carry a literal value like "@home" ("@" alone and values starting "@." stay ordinary). The value "@?" declares a required slot named ? that no reference can name.
    symbolum stml_expandere
  6. possumnecan I leave a template argument out or pass it as an empty string?
    Leave it out: declare the slot optional with p="@p?", then omit it or pass the tombstone <@p=/>. Select on it with <COMMUTATIO de="&@p;"> and <CASUS nihil> / <CASUS non-nihil> arms (or est="value"). Using &@p; outside an arm that proves presence is vitium XI. Do NOT pass p="" inline: chorda_internare returns NIHIL for an empty chorda, so the argument has neither value nor subtree. In text or an attribute that dereferences NIHIL and crashes, and against an est arm it reports ARGUMENTUM_ARBOREUM (both seen 2026-10-17).
    symbolum stml_expandere chorda_internare
  7. curwhy does calling a template that is defined further down the document fail?
    Strata: a call can only reach definitions EARLIER in document order, so a later one is FRAGMENTUM_POSTERIUS (II), at document level and inside bodies alike. Termination is then guaranteed by construction, because each nested fill may only use strictly older definitions. Definitions are collected from any depth of the document, inside ordinary elements too, and dropped from the output. A template defined INSIDE another template's body is not collected: calls to it are FRAGMENTUM_IGNOTUM, and each instantiation copies the nested definition verbatim into the output. The one exception is a template calling itself: allowed only when every subtree argument is a strict descendant of the current instantiation's subtree arguments and there is at least one (else RECURSIO_NON_DESCENDENS, XXXI). Mutual recursion is still refused.
    symbolum stml_expandere · via lib/stml_macros.worklog.md
  8. quomodohow do I find elements in the document and generate output for each match?
    Use an EXEMPLAR to bind a relation and a PER to consume it: <EXEMPLAR output="$a"><q id="$i"/></EXEMPLAR> then <PER congruentia="$a"><v n="&@i;"/></PER> emits one <v> per match at the PER's place. A value written in the pattern is required and an omitted one is free. id="$i" captures a value, a bare $n attribute captures the node, a text child of only $t captures the text, and <*> matches any tag. An EXEMPLAR searches only the content ABOVE it in the document. modus is omnia (the default), unum (exactly one, else XVI), primum or optional. Both commands are consumed and never emitted. They work only at document level: inside a template body they are vitium XIII, except PER de="@n..." over a subtree argument. CATENA, DIRIBITIO and SINE chain, branch on and filter relations.
    symbolum stml_expandere · via project-specs/stml-exemplaria-spec.md
  9. quomodohow do I see what my EXEMPLAR, CATENA or PER commands actually matched?
    Put a bare <INDAGO/> at the top of the document. Every consumed EXEMPLAR, PER, CATENA or DIRIBITIO after it at document level leaves a comment in its place (TRANSPARENTIA does not), for example <!--indago EXEMPLAR $a (radix): ordines 1 (capturae: i)-->, with per-stage row counts for chains and taken arms for DIRIBITIO. The trace is a comment, so matching ignores it, but the output bytes do change. Commands BEFORE the element are not traced. INDAGO with any attribute or a non-comment child is vitium XXIV.
    symbolum stml_expandere
  10. quidwhat does stml_distribuere do, and do I need it if I already called stml_expandere?
    It dissolves list wrappers. A named element whose children (after any leading <@x=> attribute elements) are ALL anonymous fragments becomes one copy of itself per fragment: <nota t="a"><>lac</><>ianuam</></nota> becomes two <nota t="a"> elements. Wrapper attributes and attribute elements are copied into each item, and an item attribute of the same name wins. Mixing anonymous fragments with named children or text is STML_DISTRIBUTIO_MIXTA, with linea of the first offending child and the wrapper's titulus; comments pass through. Any element qualifies, the root included: <r><>x</></r> becomes <r>x</r>. Treat it as required: skipping it silently changes how many elements there are.
    symbolum stml_distribuere StmlDistributioResultus · via project-specs/stml-instrumentum-spec.md
  11. possumnecan I use stml_congruere_strictum as a general pattern matcher for STML trees?
    Only for exact shapes. Tag, fragment and raw flags must be equal, and the attribute lists must be equal IN THE SAME ORDER (x="1" y="2" does not match y="2" x="1"). Children must match one to one, except that a template whose only child is the whole text &@k; captures all the candidate's children (at least one) into an StmlCaptura. A repeated slot must capture byte-identical forests. paria (may be NIHIL) gets StmlCongruentiaPar entries as it goes and is not rolled back on failure, so save xar_numerus first and xar_truncare back to it. capturae (must not be NIHIL) is not rolled back either: pass a fresh Xar for each attempt, as silva_arbor.c does. For subset matching (omitted attributes free, children an ordered subsequence), use an EXEMPLAR inside the document instead.
    symbolum stml_congruere_strictum StmlCaptura StmlCongruentiaPar xar_truncare
  12. quomodohow do I find which template call produced a node in the expanded tree?
    Read tabula_expansionum, a Xar of StmlExpansioNota in splice order. nodus is the first node the call spliced (NIHIL if the body produced nothing), vocatio is the call node in the ORIGINAL tree (keep that tree alive while you use it), fragmentum_id is the interned id and stratum the 1-based nesting depth. PER voca="#@f" adds one entry per row. Calls inside block arguments are recorded before the call that receives them, and their nodus points into the argument's stored subtree, not into radix_expansa: that subtree is cloned at each place it is spliced, so look such a node up through the receiving call's entry instead.
    symbolum StmlExpansioNota stml_expandere
  13. possumnecan I get a printable name for the vitium in a failed StmlExpansioResultus?
    Not from the library: there is no public name function, only the enum (I to XXXI for expansion, MIXTA and MEMORIA for distribution), each value with its comment in the header. tools/stml.c keeps a private switch (_vitium_expansionis_titulus) that prints the name with linea, fragmentum and loculus; copy it, and keep it in step when a vitium is added.
    symbolum StmlExpansioVitium StmlDistributioVitium · via tools/stml.c include/stml_macros.h

tabellarius

include/tabellarius.h · lib/tabellarius.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I write a JSON-RPC server loop over stdin and stdout?
    Make one piscina per message, read a message, classify it, answer, then destroy (or empty) the piscina. For MCP-style line framing: tabellarius_lineam_legere, then tabellarius_nuntium_legere, then tabellarius_responsum or tabellarius_errorem written back with tabellarius_lineam_scribere. For LSP framing use tabellarius_epistulam_legere and tabellarius_epistulam_scribere instead. Every result points into that piscina, so it dies with it. The main loop at the end of gesta/fontes/tabularium.c is a complete line-framed server (piscina_generare_dynamicum per message, piscina_destruere after); fori_principale.c shows the same loop as a proxy that forwards requests instead of answering them.
    symbolum tabellarius_lineam_legere tabellarius_nuntium_legere tabellarius_responsum tabellarius_lineam_scribere piscina_generare_dynamicum piscina_destruere · via gesta/fontes/tabularium.c gesta/instrumenta/fori_principale.c
  2. quomodohow do I tell end of input apart from a bad message?
    Both readers return an empty chorda and set *fluxus_finitus to VERUM both at EOF and when the stream is corrupt (a header line of 255 bytes or more counting its \r, no or a negative Content-Length, short body, body over TABELLARIUS_CORPUS_MAXIMUM, or a last line without a newline; empty lines are skipped, not errors). Either way the framing is lost, so the caller should exit, not try to resync. Test *fluxus_finitus, not the chorda: a Content-Length of 0 also gives an empty body, with *fluxus_finitus FALSUM. A Content-Length value that is not a number is NOT detected: strtol reads it as 0 (or its numeric prefix), the body is taken as empty and the stream silently desyncs. A message that frames correctly but is not valid JSON-RPC comes back from tabellarius_nuntium_legere as TABELLARIUS_PRAVUM (radix NIHIL if the JSON did not parse); answer it with an error.
    symbolum tabellarius_epistulam_legere tabellarius_lineam_legere tabellarius_nuntium_legere
  3. quidwhat are PETITIO, NUNTIATIO, RESPONSUM and PRAVUM?
    The message kinds, judged only by structure (the jsonrpc field is not checked): a string method with an id is a PETITIO (request, must be answered); a string method without an id is a NUNTIATIO (notification, never answered); an id with result or error and no string method is a RESPONSUM; anything else is PRAVUM, including batches (JSON arrays), which this library does not support. A JSON null id still counts as present.
    symbolum tabellarius_nuntium_legere
  4. curwhy is the request id kept as a JsonValor instead of a number?
    JSON-RPC allows the id to be an integer or a string, and the reply must carry it back exactly. Converting it would corrupt it (a string id or a big integer), so TabellariusNuntius.id is the parsed JSON node, verbatim, and you pass it straight to tabellarius_responsum or tabellarius_errorem. NIHIL there writes id null.
    symbolum tabellarius_nuntium_legere tabellarius_responsum tabellarius_errorem
  5. possumnecan I send pretty-printed JSON with line framing?
    No. With line framing one message is one line, so tabellarius_lineam_scribere refuses a body that contains a raw newline: it prints a complaint to stderr, writes nothing and returns FALSUM (it also returns FALSUM for an empty body). Write compact JSON, which escapes newlines inside strings. LSP framing (tabellarius_epistulam_scribere) has a byte count, so any body is fine there.
    symbolum tabellarius_lineam_scribere tabellarius_epistulam_scribere
  6. possumnecan a hostile client make the reader allocate huge amounts of memory?
    Partly. With LSP framing the Content-Length is checked against TABELLARIUS_CORPUS_MAXIMUM (64 MB) before allocating, but a claim just under the cap is allocated in full from the piscina before any body byte arrives (and piscina_allocare exits the process if that piscina cannot grow). With line framing the cap is checked only after the whole line has been read into memory, so a line that never ends grows without limit.
    symbolum tabellarius_epistulam_legere tabellarius_lineam_legere piscina_allocare
  7. quomodohow do I send a notification or an error reply?
    tabellarius_nuntiationem builds the method plus optional params object (params NIHIL omits the field); tabellarius_errorem builds an error reply from the id, a code and a C string message. Both return the body as a chorda in your piscina (empty if a required argument is NIHIL); frame it yourself with the matching scribere function. Standard codes are TABELLARIUS_ERROR_ANALYSIS (-32700) through TABELLARIUS_ERROR_INTERNUS (-32603), plus the LSP code TABELLARIUS_ERROR_NONDUM_INITIATUM (-32002).
    symbolum tabellarius_nuntiationem tabellarius_errorem

tabula_characterum

include/tabula_characterum.h · lib/tabula_characterum.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I create a text grid and type a character into it the way an editor would?
    tabula_initiare(&tabula, piscina, latitudo, altitudo) allocates the cells (row-major) and the per-line indentation array from the piscina; it has no return value (piscina_allocare exits on failure). tabula_inserere_characterem(&tabula, linea, columna, c) is the typing operation: in blank space it paints, at or on content it pushes the rest of the line right and wraps the overflow onto the next line. Read and write raw cells with the tabula_cellula(t, linea, columna) macro. The grid has no cursor; lib/vim.c keeps one.
    symbolum tabula_initiare tabula_inserere_characterem tabula_cellula · via lib/vim.c
  2. quidwhat does an empty cell contain, a space or a NUL?
    Usually a space: tabula_initiare, tabula_inserere_lineam and tabula_delere_lineam fill with ' ' and set indentation to -1 (the header's "initialized to '\0'" is wrong). But tabula_trahere_sinistram, and so tabula_delere_characterem, writes '\0' into the last column. tabula_est_cellula_vacua treats ' ', '\0', '\t' and TAB_CONTINUATIO as blank; tabula_invenire_finem_contenti treats only ' ' and '\0' as blank, so tabs count as content there. Compare a cell with tabula_est_cellula_vacua, not with ' '.
    symbolum tabula_initiare tabula_trahere_sinistram tabula_est_cellula_vacua tabula_invenire_finem_contenti · via lib/scriba_documentum.worklog.md
  3. quidwhat is the barrier (obex) and when does typing push text instead of overwriting?
    The obex is the first non-blank column at or after the given column (tabula_invenire_obicem, -1 if none). tabula_inserere_characterem only paints when there is no content to the right, or when at least one blank cell lies between the column and the obex (the whitespace "cushion" is used up). When the column is at the obex or right before it, the rest of the line is pushed one cell right.
    symbolum tabula_invenire_obicem tabula_inserere_characterem
  4. possumnecan I treat a FALSUM from tabula_inserere_characterem as "nothing happened"?
    No. FALSUM means the page is full, but the grid has already changed: the line was pushed and the new character painted before the overflow was handled, and on the last line (or when tabula_tractare_overflow cannot insert a line) the pushed-out character is simply lost. The same holds for tabula_inserere_tab and tabula_inserere_spatium. tabula_est_plena (content in the last column of the last line) does not predict every failure: when the overflow must open a new line before an indented line, tabula_inserere_lineam refuses as soon as the last line has any content at all. Keep a copy of the cells if you need to undo.
    symbolum tabula_inserere_characterem tabula_tractare_overflow tabula_inserere_tab tabula_inserere_spatium tabula_est_plena tabula_inserere_lineam
  5. quomodohow do I insert and delete a tab without leaving half of it behind?
    A tab is two cells: '\t' followed by TAB_CONTINUATIO (0x01). Insert it with tabula_inserere_tab (column at most latitudo - 2) and remove it with tabula_delere_tab, which accepts either cell. tabula_delere_characterem knows nothing about tabs and removes only one of the two cells, so look at the cell first (for '\t' or TAB_CONTINUATIO) as lib/vim.c's backspace does. Pushing text off the end of a line can also drop the TAB_CONTINUATIO and leave a lone '\t'.
    symbolum tabula_inserere_tab tabula_delere_tab tabula_est_tab_continuatio TAB_CONTINUATIO
  6. quidwhat is the indentatio array and who keeps it up to date?
    Sticky indentation per line (s32, -1 = not set). The library only moves it along with tabula_inserere_lineam / tabula_delere_lineam and sets it in the tabula_ex_literis helpers (the first column that is neither a space nor a tab). Typing never updates it: lib/vim.c sets it after a successful tabula_inserere_characterem. tabula_tractare_overflow decides indentation from the cells (tabula_invenire_initium_contenti), not from this array.
    symbolum tabula_initiare tabula_inserere_lineam tabula_delere_lineam tabula_ex_literis_cum_dimensionibus · via lib/vim.c
  7. quomodohow do I write a test that checks the grid against expected text?
    Build the start state with tabula_ex_literis (default LXVIII x LVI) or tabula_ex_literis_cum_dimensionibus ('\n' = next line, '\t' becomes the two-cell tab), act, then tabula_asserere(&t, piscina, expectatum, descriptio). It calls CREDO_VERUM and, on failure, prints both grids with tabula_imprimere ('.' for '\0', '_' for space, '>' for tab). The comparison is strict for characters you list (a ' ' you write does not match a '\0' cell) and only requires blank cells, in the tabula_est_cellula_vacua sense, after them.
    symbolum tabula_ex_literis tabula_ex_literis_cum_dimensionibus tabula_aequalis_literis tabula_asserere tabula_imprimere
  8. quomodohow do I copy text in and out of a grid region without the push logic?
    tabula_scribere_ad_positionem overwrites cells from a NUL-terminated string and returns how many it wrote (it stops at the line end; tabs are not expanded). tabula_legere_ad_positionem copies longitudo cells into a buffer that must hold longitudo + 1 bytes, NUL-terminated. To make room first, tabula_inserere_spatium pushes the line right N times. lib/concha.c's $date command uses all three.
    symbolum tabula_scribere_ad_positionem tabula_legere_ad_positionem tabula_inserere_spatium · via lib/concha.c

tabula_dispersa

include/tabula_dispersa.h · lib/tabula_dispersa.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I make a string-keyed map and look a key up?
    tabula_dispersa_creare_chorda(piscina, capacitas) gives an open-addressing table with FNV-1a hashing and byte comparison; the capacity is rounded up to a power of two (at least XVI). Values are vacuum*. tabula_dispersa_invenire returns VERUM and writes *valor_out only when the key is present (valor_out may be NIHIL); tabula_dispersa_continet is the same test without the value. A stored NIHIL value is still "found", so test the return value, not the pointer.
    symbolum tabula_dispersa_creare_chorda tabula_dispersa_inserere tabula_dispersa_invenire tabula_dispersa_continet
  2. possumnecan I reuse or free the key's bytes after inserting it?
    No. The table stores the chorda by value, so it keeps the pointer to your bytes, not a copy. If those bytes are a stack buffer, are rolled back with piscina_reficere, or are overwritten, lookups quietly stop matching. Copy the key first (chorda_transcribere into a piscina that lives as long as the table) or intern it (chorda_internare).
    symbolum tabula_dispersa_inserere chorda_transcribere chorda_internare
  3. quidwhat happens when I insert a key that is already in the table?
    Only the value is replaced. The key chorda stored the first time stays (with its pointer), the count does not change, and the call returns VERUM. Empty keys (mensura 0) are refused: inserere, invenire and delere all return FALSUM for them.
    symbolum tabula_dispersa_inserere
  4. possumnecan I look up with a C string literal instead of building a chorda?
    Yes, with tabula_dispersa_invenire_literis and tabula_dispersa_continet_literis, but only on a table made by tabula_dispersa_creare_chorda. The _literis path always hashes with FNV-1a and compares raw bytes, whatever friatio and comparatio the table was created with. On a table with a custom hash (tabula_friare_multiplicatio, or your own) it never finds anything.
    symbolum tabula_dispersa_invenire_literis tabula_dispersa_continet_literis tabula_dispersa_creare_chorda
  5. quomodohow do I key a table by something other than string contents, such as pointer identity?
    Pass your own TabulaFriatio and TabulaComparatio to tabula_dispersa_creare (it returns NIHIL if either is NIHIL). The comparator must return 0 for equal keys and is only called when the cached hashes match, so hash and equality must agree. lib/insula.c keys a table by the address and length of a chorda (friatio_sedis, comparatio_sedis).
    symbolum tabula_dispersa_creare TabulaFriatio TabulaComparatio · via lib/insula.c
  6. quomodohow do I walk every entry, and can I change the table while iterating?
    tabula_dispersa_iterator_initium then tabula_dispersa_iterator_proximum until FALSUM; entries come in slot order, not insertion order (sort them yourself if the output must be stable). Deleting during the walk is safe: tabula_dispersa_delere only marks the slot as a tombstone. Inserting is not: an insert can rehash into a new slot array and the iterator, which holds only the table and a slot index, then skips or repeats entries; even without a rehash a new entry is visited or not depending on its slot.
    symbolum tabula_dispersa_iterator_initium tabula_dispersa_iterator_proximum tabula_dispersa_delere
  7. curwhy does a table with few live entries keep using more memory as I insert and delete?
    Deletion leaves a tombstone (SLOT_DELETUM). When live entries pass 75% of capacity, or tombstones pass 25%, the next insert rehashes into a new array of DOUBLE the capacity, even when the rehash was caused only by tombstones. The old array stays in the piscina (it is an arena), so heavy insert/delete churn grows memory with the number of operations, not with the live count. tabula_dispersa_vacare empties every slot in place without allocating.
    symbolum tabula_dispersa_delere tabula_dispersa_inserere tabula_dispersa_vacare tabula_dispersa_factor_deletorum
  8. possumnecan tabula_dispersa_inserere fail because memory ran out?
    Not in a way you can handle: growth allocates with piscina_allocare, which exits the process when memory runs out, so the "FALSUM on allocation failure" path in the header never actually runs. FALSUM in practice means a NIHIL table or an empty key.
    symbolum tabula_dispersa_inserere piscina_allocare

tempus

include/tempus.h · lib/tempus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quidwhat does tempus_nunc return, and can I use it as a date or timestamp?
    Seconds (f64) since the global clock started, which happens lazily on the first tempus_* call (or explicitly with tempus_initiare). It counts the platform's high-resolution pulses (fenestra_tempus_obtinere_pulsus), so it is for measuring intervals, timeouts and animation, not wall-clock time. For milliseconds since the Unix epoch use mensura_momentum_nunc; for calendar dates use fasti.
    symbolum tempus_nunc tempus_initiare fenestra_tempus_obtinere_pulsus mensura_momentum_nunc fasti_nunc
  2. quomodohow do I run a frame loop at a fixed frame rate and read the frame delta?
    Set the rate once with tempus_ponere_quadra_destinata (default 60). Each frame call tempus_quadrum first: it records the frame start and updates the delta that tempus_delta returns. After the work, tempus_exspectare_quadrum sleeps until 1/rate seconds after that tempus_quadrum (coarse sleeps, then 100-microsecond naps for the last two milliseconds); a rate of 0 or less disables the wait. tempus_obtinere_statisticas gives the current frame rate, the mean rate over the last CXX frames, and the minimum and maximum frame times in seconds. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): the mean rate comes back inf when two tempus_quadrum calls land in the same clock tick (delta 0, common in a tight loop), and a target below about 2.3e-4 fps (a frame period over about 4295 s) overflows the sleep conversion in tempus_exspectare_quadrum, which is undefined behaviour.
    symbolum tempus_ponere_quadra_destinata tempus_quadrum tempus_delta tempus_exspectare_quadrum tempus_obtinere_statisticas
  3. possumnecan I call tempus_initiare again to restart the clock?
    You can, but it resets everything global: the start time, every frame statistic and the target rate (back to 60), so a rate set earlier is lost. To time something on its own, make an independent clock with tempus_creare(piscina), restart it with tempus_restituere, read it with tempus_elapsum, and call tempus_actualizare per step to fill its delta_tempus and tempus_totale.
    symbolum tempus_initiare tempus_creare tempus_restituere tempus_elapsum tempus_actualizare
  4. quomodohow do I animate a value smoothly toward a target?
    animare_ad moves at a constant speed (celeritas times the delta) and lands exactly on the target. elastrum_ad is a damped spring: keep the velocity in your own f32 and pass its address each frame. For a timed tween compute t from 0 to 1, shape it with one of the lenire_* easing curves and feed it to interpolare; gradus_lenis is smoothstep and remappare maps one range onto another. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): elastrum_ad is explicit Euler and blows up on large deltas, e.g. k=100, d=10 at a 0.25 s frame reaches -4e33 within 40 steps and k=10, d=30 at 0.1 s diverges, so it is only stable while damnum times delta stays below 2 and delta times sqrt(k) stays small.
    symbolum animare_ad elastrum_ad interpolare gradus_lenis lenire_cubicus_egressus remappare
  5. possumnecan I pass values outside 0..1 to the easing functions, or negative time to the unda waves?
    Nothing is clamped. The lenire_* curves are only defined for t in 0..1 (clamp with cohibere first). The waves take fmod of t times the frequency, which keeps the sign of a negative t, so unda_triangulus(-0.25, 1, a) gives -2a, outside its range, unda_dens_serrae also leaves its range, unda_quadratus stays high for all negative t, and oscillare goes negative. Use non-negative time.
    symbolum lenire_linearis unda_triangulus unda_dens_serrae unda_quadratus oscillare cohibere
  6. possumnecan I use circumvolvere to wrap any value into a range?
    Only values a few ranges away: it adds or subtracts the width in a loop, so the time grows with the distance, and it never returns when the width is 0 or when the value is so large that adding the width does not change the f32 (1e9 into 0..1). For far values wrap with fmod yourself and add the width when the result is negative. interpolare_angulum is no substitute: it does not wrap its result, and because C fmod keeps the sign it only takes the shorter way round when b - a is at least -pi (0 toward -4 goes the long way). Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): circumvolvere also never returns for a reversed range (minimum greater than maximum, e.g. circumvolvere(0.5, 1, 0)) or for an infinite value.
    symbolum circumvolvere interpolare_angulum
  7. quidwhat colour layout does interpolare_colorem expect?
    A packed i32 with red in bits 0-7, green in 8-15, blue in 16-23 and alpha in 24-31; each channel is interpolated linearly and truncated. t is not clamped, so keep it in 0..1. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): for any result alpha of 0x80 or more (every opaque colour, e.g. 0xFF000000 toward 0xFF0000FF) the alpha byte is shifted left 24 as a signed int, which is undefined behaviour that only happens to give the right value.
    symbolum interpolare_colorem
  8. quidwhat kind of noise does unda_tumultus produce?
    White noise in [-1, 1) that is a pure function of t: time is cut into buckets of about 81 microseconds (floor of t times 12345.6789), and each bucket seeds sors, so the same t always gives the same value and neighbouring buckets are independent. It is not smooth noise.
    symbolum unda_tumultus sors_seminare

terminale

include/terminale.h · lib/terminale.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I run a standalone terminal window with this library?
    Build the child yourself: terminale_argumenta (argv; -fumus gives a short fixed script, otherwise $SHELL -l, falling back to /bin/zsh) and terminale_ambitus (TERM=xterm-256color, COLORTERM=truecolor, TERM_PROGRAM=terminale, TMUX and TMUX_PANE removed) go into pseudoterminale_posix_creare. Then terminale_applicatio_aedificare(app, piscina, intern, pt, latitudo, altitudo) builds the store, registries, dispatcher and emulator host; app.d and app.figurae feed the window loop. apps/terminale/terminale.c is the reference.
    symbolum terminale_argumenta terminale_ambitus pseudoterminale_posix_creare terminale_applicatio_aedificare · via apps/terminale/terminale.c
  2. quomodohow do I embed a terminal pane inside another app's store, as vicus does?
    terminale_montare(app, piscina, intern, repo, id, argumenta, systemata, latitudo, altitudo) spawns the child itself (argumenta NIHIL = $SHELL -l) and mounts the branch with element terminale and the given id through the embedded organum manifest. It creates NO dispatcher: the host routes events and calls terminale_componere with the app as ctx to build the pane's components.
    symbolum terminale_montare terminale_componere · via lib/vicus_applicatio.c
  3. quomodowhat does my main loop have to call every frame?
    The library owns no loop. Each frame call terminale_pulsare(app, mora_ms): it reads superficies_latitudo / superficies_altitudo from the app's branch, resizes the emulator and child when the cell grid changed, then pulses the host. Redraw when the result has mutatum; finitus means the shell exited. Afterwards terminale_titulus(app, &mutatus) gives the OSC 0/2 title, with mutatus VERUM once per change.
    symbolum terminale_pulsare aemulator_hospes_pulsare terminale_titulus
  4. possumnecan I keep the chorda that terminale_titulus returns?
    Only until the next pulse. It points into a fixed CCLVI-byte buffer inside the app's private context (longer titles are cut to CCLVI bytes) and is overwritten whenever the program sets a new title. Copy it if you need it later; the window app copies it into a stack buffer rather than the piscina, because zsh retitles on every prompt and a pool copy per change would grow for the whole session.
    symbolum terminale_titulus
  5. curwhy does a key press reach the shell only after the next event arrives?
    Real input sources send key down, then its text, then key up, but the encoder wants the key and its text as one series. So a key-down is held as pending; a following committed text event is encoded together with it, and any other event (key up, mouse, focus, wheel, another key down) first flushes the pending key alone. Keys with no text (Enter, Ctrl-C, arrows) therefore go out with the next event, normally their own key-up.
    symbolum codificator_eventa · via lib/terminale.worklog.md
  6. curwhy does Cmd+V or any Cmd key never reach the program?
    Every key with MOD_SUPER is dropped, down and up, which is the macOS terminal convention (Terminal.app, iTerm). The menu handles the shortcut (Cmd+V pastes through it); forwarding the key as well made kitty-keyboard programs such as Claude Code type a stray v before the pasted text.
    via lib/terminale.c lib/terminale.worklog.md
  7. curwhy do my program's colours look different from the colours it asked for?
    A minimum contrast of 3.0 (WCAG) is applied: text too close to its background is nudged the smallest step toward black or white that reaches the ratio, because programs assume a dark background and the house theme is light. Faint (SGR 2) text is first blended halfway into its background, and bold 0-7 uses the bright palette. Box drawing and block elements (U+2500-259F) skip the contrast floor, since their colour is the picture.
    via lib/terminale.c lib/terminale.worklog.md
  8. quidwhat is ornamenta_pixelorum and when should it be FALSUM?
    A flag on TerminaleApplicatio, VERUM after terminale_applicatio_aedificare. It draws underlines (five forms), strike-through, overline, fake bold (glyph drawn again one pixel right) and box/block/braille glyphs as pixel shapes. The terminal twin (terminale_terminalis) sets it FALSUM because its rasteriser turns a horizontal line into box-drawing cells, which would erase the letter under an underline.
    symbolum terminale_applicatio_aedificare · via apps/terminale/terminale_terminalis.c
  9. quidwhat happens on failure, and who closes the pseudoterminal?
    Once the host exists it owns the pseudoterminal, and terminale_claudere closes the child through it. A FALSUM from terminale_applicatio_aedificare closes pt only when the failure comes BEFORE the host is created (bad arguments, no memory for the context, or aemulator_hospes_creare failing, which closes pt itself). A later failure (organum missing, branch refused, store, registries or scratch pool missing) returns FALSUM with the child still running, despite the header's promise; terminale_montare passes on the same result. So zero the TerminaleApplicatio before the call (it is zeroed only after the argument check) and call terminale_claudere after any FALSUM that left app.hospes set.
    symbolum terminale_applicatio_aedificare terminale_montare terminale_claudere aemulator_hospes_creare
  10. quomodohow do I find which tty the child shell is on?
    terminale_tty(app) returns the child's tty path (for example /dev/ttys011), copied into the piscina when the app is built; vicus uses it to match tmux clients to panes. It is an empty chorda when the pseudoterminal has no tty, such as the in-memory bridge.
    symbolum terminale_tty
  11. possumnecan I mount and close terminals over and over without leaking memory?
    Not completely. Everything else lives in the piscina you pass, but every build also creates its own dynamic scratch piscina (terminale_scrutinium, first block LXIV * MXXIV bytes) for encoding key bytes, and nothing ever destroys it: terminale_claudere only closes the emulator host. vicus mounts a new terminale on every pane opening, so each one leaves that pool behind for the life of the process.
    symbolum terminale_claudere terminale_montare piscina_generare_dynamicum

thema

include/thema.h · lib/thema.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I get the RGB colour for a semantic UI colour like the background or text?
    Call thema_initiare once at startup, then thema_color(COLOR_BACKGROUND) (or any ColorThema). It returns a Color struct, not a packed integer: semantic colour -> palette index -> Color. The palette stores 6-bit components (0x00 to 0x3F) and color_ex_palette scales them to 8 bits (times CCLV, divided by LXIII) with alpha CCLV.
    symbolum thema_initiare thema_color color_ex_palette
  2. possumnecan I call thema_color without calling thema_initiare first?
    You can, but every colour comes out black: the semantic map is a zeroed static array until thema_initiare fills it, so every ColorThema maps to palette index 0 (PALETTE_BLACK). The theme is one process-wide global, not per window, and calling thema_initiare again resets the map to the defaults.
    symbolum thema_initiare thema_color thema_palette_index
  3. possumnecan I switch to a different palette with thema_ponere_palette?
    Not today. thema_ponere_palette copies the 48 bytes into a private array that nothing reads: thema_color goes through color_ex_palette, which always reads thema_palette_aquinas. What does take effect is remapping a semantic colour to another of the 16 palette indices with thema_ponere_mappam, because thema_color reads that map.
    symbolum thema_ponere_palette thema_ponere_mappam color_ex_palette thema_palette_aquinas
  4. quidwhat is the difference between a ColorThema, a palette index and a coloratio index?
    A ColorThema (COLOR_BACKGROUND ... COLOR_SUPERFICIES, COLOR_COUNT entries) is a semantic role. A palette index (PALETTE_BLACK ... PALETTE_WARM_GRAY, 0 to 15) is a slot in the 16-colour palette; thema_palette_index maps a role to its slot. A coloratio index is a syntax-highlighting class from coloratio.h, resolved by thema_color_ex_indice_colorationis; 0 and any unknown value give the COLOR_TEXT colour. In drawing commands, ColorMandati of genus COLOR_MANDATI_THEMA carries a ColorThema and COLOR_MANDATI_INDEX a coloratio index; color_ex_mandato resolves both.
    symbolum ColorThema thema_palette_index thema_color_ex_indice_colorationis color_ex_mandato
  5. possumnecan a bad colour value make thema read out of bounds?
    Yes, nothing is range-checked. thema_color, thema_palette_index and thema_ponere_mappam index the semantic map directly, so a value at or past COLOR_COUNT reads or writes past it. A palette index above 15 given to thema_ponere_mappam makes color_ex_palette read past the 48-byte palette. Keep roles below COLOR_COUNT and slots within 0 to 15.
    symbolum thema_color thema_ponere_mappam thema_palette_index color_ex_palette

thesaurus

include/thesaurus.h · lib/thesaurus.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I cache a computed result by its inputs and get it back next run?
    Hash your inputs into a Sigillum key. On a miss, store the output bytes with thesaurus_ponere (it writes their content sigillum to sigillum_out), put those sigilla in a Xar of Sigillum and record key -> outputs with thesaurus_actio_ponere. On the next run thesaurus_actio_capere(key) returns that Xar, and thesaurus_via gives each blob's file path. On a hit AND on a miss also call thesaurus_generationem_notare(key), or the next purge deletes the entry. compilator and aedilis_silva do exactly this.
    symbolum thesaurus_aperire thesaurus_ponere thesaurus_actio_ponere thesaurus_actio_capere thesaurus_via thesaurus_generationem_notare · via lib/compilator.c lib/aedilis_silva.c
  2. quomodohow do I read the bytes of a stored blob?
    There is no read call: thesaurus_via returns the blob's path as a chorda (not NUL-terminated), then read it yourself with filum_legere_totum(chorda_ut_cstr(via, p), p) or link it into place. thesaurus_via returns FALSUM when the blob is absent, and also when a sampled verification finds the bytes do not match their sigillum: it deletes the bad blob and you must recompute.
    symbolum thesaurus_via chorda_ut_cstr filum_legere_totum
  3. quomodohow do I control how often reads are verified against the content hash?
    thesaurus_verificationem_ponere(t, N) re-hashes one thesaurus_via call in N (the counter is per handle, not per blob). The default is THESAURUS_VERIFICATIO_RATIO (XVI); I verifies every read (fabrica -plenus) and ZEPHYRUM turns verification off. A verification reads the whole blob into the piscina you passed to thesaurus_via.
    symbolum thesaurus_verificationem_ponere THESAURUS_VERIFICATIO_RATIO thesaurus_via
  4. possumnecan two processes write the same blob or action at the same time?
    Yes. Every write goes to a temporary name in the same directory (.temporarium.pid.n) and is then renamed into place, so a reader never sees half a file and two writers of the same bytes do not conflict. A blob that already exists is never rewritten (its name is its content), and an action whose file already holds the same bytes is not rewritten either.
    symbolum thesaurus_ponere thesaurus_actio_ponere
  5. quidwhat is a generation and why must I set THESAURUS_GENERATIO?
    A generation is one orchestrating run (one bin/fabrica invocation or one gate run), not one process. thesaurus_generationem_notare appends the key to generationes/NAME.lst, where NAME comes from the THESAURUS_GENERATIO environment variable (it must start with a UTC time so name order is time order; only [A-Za-z0-9_-], at most 120 characters). With the variable unset or malformed nothing is recorded, so that process's keys are not protected from the next purge. Caution (audit 2026-10-18, unfixed): the two limits do not add up - thesaurus_aperire accepts a radix of up to 383 characters, and once radix plus THESAURUS_GENERATIO exceed 493 characters (e.g. a 383-character radix with a 120-character name) the first thesaurus_generationem_notare sprintfs the list path past the 512-byte generatio field, off the end of the Thesaurus struct into the next allocation in its piscina.
    symbolum thesaurus_generationem_notare thesaurus_purgare · via lib/thesaurus.worklog.md
  6. possumnecan a purge delete a blob I stored but never referenced from an action?
    Yes. thesaurus_purgare keeps only the newest generationes_servandae generation lists, the actions whose keys appear in them, and the blobs those actions name; every other action and blob is deleted, along with leftover temporary files. With verificare it also re-hashes every kept blob and deletes broken ones. So: put the blob, record the action, AND note the key in a generation.
    symbolum thesaurus_purgare thesaurus_generationem_notare thesaurus_actio_ponere
  7. possumnecan I run thesaurus_purgare while builds are writing to the store?
    No. A purge between a writer's thesaurus_ponere and its thesaurus_actio_ponere can delete the fresh blob. It only ever costs a cache miss, never a wrong result, but callers should run it under their own lock: bin/fabrica purgare takes the fabrica lock.
    symbolum thesaurus_purgare · imperium bin/fabrica purgare
  8. possumnecan I keep one Thesaurus handle open for a long-running process?
    Yes, but watch the memory: thesaurus_actio_ponere and thesaurus_purgare allocate from the piscina given to thesaurus_aperire (they have no piscina parameter), and nothing is ever returned. Each action write leaves a buffer plus a read of the old action file there, and a verifying purge reads every kept blob into it. Open the store in a piscina you can throw away.
    symbolum thesaurus_aperire thesaurus_actio_ponere thesaurus_purgare

tls

include/tls.h · lib/tls.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I open an HTTPS-style connection, send a request and read the reply?
    Call tls_connectere(hospes, portus, piscina) with a DNS name (it is sent as SNI and checked against the certificate) and test .successus; on failure .error and .error_descriptio say why. Send with tls_mittere_omnia, then loop tls_recipere until it returns 0 (clean EOF) or a negative value, and always finish with tls_claudere, which frees the context and closes the TCP socket and may be called twice. Most callers want http.h instead.
    symbolum tls_connectere tls_mittere_omnia tls_recipere tls_claudere
  2. quomodohow do I set a timeout, trust a private CA, or turn off certificate checks?
    Start from tls_optiones_default() (timeout_ms XXX * M, verification on, system trust store) and pass it to tls_connectere_cum_optionibus. ancora_via names a DER CA certificate that becomes the ONLY trust anchor (chain, dates and host name are still checked). verificare_certificatum FALSUM skips the trust decision. A zero-filled TlsOptiones selects the default transport but also means no verification.
    symbolum tls_optiones_default tls_connectere_cum_optionibus TlsOptiones
  3. possumnecan I get a connection with no timeout by setting timeout_ms to 0?
    No, despite the header comment. Every transport copies timeout_ms into the TCP options only when it is greater than 0, so 0 leaves the TCP default from tcp_optiones_default, which is XXX * M ms (30 seconds). Pass a large value if you need a long wait.
    symbolum TlsOptiones tcp_optiones_default
  4. quidwhat is a TLS vectura and which one do I get?
    The backend that carries the connection. TlsOptiones.vectura wins when not ORDINARIA; otherwise the RHUBARB_TLS environment variable (bearssl, securetransport or tls13), read on every connection; otherwise the house default, the in-house TLS 1.3 client. tls_vecturam_eligere tells you the choice in advance, tls_vectura the backend actually in use, tls_versio the negotiated version (0x0304 or 0x0303, 0 before the handshake).
    symbolum tls_vecturam_eligere tls_vectura tls_versio TlsVectura
  5. curwhy does tls_vectura say BEARSSL when I asked for the TLS 1.3 client?
    The server answered as TLS 1.2 only. The 1.3 client then hands the socket back, the dispatcher reconnects on the same fd number and finishes with BearSSL TLS 1.2, checking the DOWNGRD sentinel in the server random so a downgrade attack is refused. Set sine_descensu VERUM to get TLS_ERROR_HANDSHAKE instead of the fallback.
    symbolum tls_vectura tls_versio tls_tls13_tcp_relinquere · via lib/tls_tls13.worklog.md project-specs/tls13-spec.md
  6. quomodohow do I do a non-blocking TLS handshake inside an event loop?
    Connect TCP yourself (tcp_incipere, then tcp_incipere_perficere), then call tls_incipere; nothing is sent yet. Call tls_gradus whenever the socket is ready: LEGERE or SCRIBERE says what to wait for next, PERFECTUS means done, ERROR means read tls_gradus_error. The TLS connection owns the TcpConnexio from then on, and on failure tls_incipere has already closed it, so do not close it again (http_vocatio.c is the real example).
    symbolum tls_incipere tls_gradus tls_gradus_error tcp_incipere tcp_incipere_perficere · via lib/http_vocatio.c
  7. possumnecan I tell a receive timeout apart from would-block when tls_recipere returns -2?
    Not by the value: TLS_ITERUM and TCP_ITERUM are both -2. On a blocking connection (tls_connectere) it means the receive timeout expired; on a stepped one (tls_incipere) it means nothing is ready now. A stepped caller must keep reading until TLS_ITERUM on each wakeup, because decrypted bytes can be held inside the backend where the socket no longer signals them. Only 0 means the peer closed; -1 is an error.
    symbolum tls_recipere tls_mittere
  8. possumnecan I send a partial buffer with tls_mittere and continue later?
    Yes: tls_mittere returns how many bytes it took (or -1); on TLS_ITERUM call it again with the SAME remaining bytes once the socket is writable. tls_mittere_omnia loops for you, sleeping 1 ms on 0 or TLS_ITERUM, with no overall deadline: on a blocking connection a send that times out also comes back as TLS_ITERUM, so a peer that stops reading keeps it looping. It returns FALSUM for an empty buffer (mensura 0), the same answer as an error.
    symbolum tls_mittere tls_mittere_omnia
  9. quomodohow do I read the server certificate's expiry date?
    Call tls_certificatum_obtinere while the connection is still open (after tls_claudere nothing is left). It fills a TlsCertificatum with the leaf's notBefore and notAfter as Unix epoch seconds and the subject CN as a chorda allocated in the connection's piscina; days left = (non_post - now) / 86400. FALSUM, with valida FALSUM, when the certificate could not be read.
    symbolum tls_certificatum_obtinere TlsCertificatum

unitates

include/unitates.h · lib/unitates.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I find out what a file in the repository is (its unit, custody, lifecycle, role, language)?
    From the shell: bin/fabrica quid VIA (add -machina for TAB lines). From C: build a sutura with unitates_sutura_arboris(radix, piscina) (git ls-files -co plus disk reads), read the policy and declarations once with unitates_legere, then call unitates_quid per path. Each field of the UnitatisResponsum is a valor plus the causa (the rule that decided it), and responsum.unitas is the innermost unit, with parens links up to the root.
    symbolum unitates_legere unitates_quid unitates_sutura_arboris UnitatisResponsum · imperium bin/fabrica quid lib/xar.c
  2. quomodohow do I make my tool skip vendor, fixtures and generated files without a path list?
    Add an iudex line to unitates.stml (titulus, descriptio, and the curae, vitae, munera, provenientiae, indoles and linguae it accepts, each a space-separated list of known values), then ask it: unitates_iudicat(u, iudex, via, ...) per path, unitates_viae_iudicatae for a newline-separated list, or tools/iudex_unitatum.sh [-causae] JUDGE from a shell script. A file is judged only if ALL six properties are in the judge's lists; a file with no munus is never judged. Do not add an exclusion regex or path list: the judges replace the nine exclusion lists the spec counted.
    symbolum unitates_iudicat unitates_viae_iudicatae · via unitates.stml tools/iudex_unitatum.sh
  3. quomodohow do I see why unitates_legere returned NIHIL?
    NIHIL is a refusal, never a silent default. Pass causa_out to get every cause at once, one line each in via:linea: form, and recusationes_out (NIHIL is allowed) for the same causes as a Xar of UnitatesRecusatio. Typical causes: unknown attribute or element, a genus that is not citable, an unknown cura, vita or usus, the same title twice in one genus, a family with no library, no root unitas.stml, or unreadable STML.
    symbolum unitates_legere UnitatesRecusatio
  4. possumnecan I free the sutura (or its piscina) after unitates_legere returns?
    No. The Unitates keeps the UnitatesSutura pointer, because provenance is read lazily: unitates_quid, unitates_iudicat and unitates_interrogationes read files through it later. The sutura and its datum must outlive the Unitates; allocate them in the same piscina, as unitates_sutura_arboris does.
    symbolum unitates_legere unitates_quid unitates_sutura_arboris · via lib/unitates.worklog.md
  5. quidwhat unit does a file belong to, for example a test or a macos backend?
    The innermost one, and a library beats the directory around it. A library stem S (include/S.h, lib/S.c or lib/S.m exists) claims those files, lib/S.*.md (worklog, phase-log), lib/S.*.stml (such as this file), docs/bibliothecae/S.html and anything under lib/S_assets/. A stem ending in a part (_macos _posix _interna _impl _tabula) belongs to its base library. probationes/probatio_X.c goes to the longest _-prefix of X that names a library or a directory unit (the library wins at equal length), else to the innermost directory unit (when that is the root, the census lists the test under orphani). Everything else goes to the innermost DIR/unitas.stml, and that declaration file itself belongs to the CONTAINING unit.
    symbolum unitates_quid unitates_legere · via include/unitates.h unitates.stml
  6. quomodohow do I mark a file as generated so the formatter and lint treat it that way?
    Put GENERATUM in its FIRST line, or in the second line when the first must be a declaration (<?xml or <!doctype). The position is fixed on purpose: a hand-written header that mentions GENERATUM in its prose on line 2 stays manu. A judge that admits both provenances never reads the file at all.
    symbolum unitates_quid unitates_iudicat · via include/unitates.h
  7. quomodohow do I mark a big table of data (string literals, initializers) so code judges like silva's corpus gates skip it?
    Put the whole word DATA in the same signature line as GENERATUM (first line, or second after a required declaration), e.g. /* DATA: biblia_dr.c - ... */ or /* GENERATUM DATA: generator - ... */. bin/fabrica quid then reports indoles data, and a judge whose indoles list is "codex" (corpus_silvae) refuses the file with that cause. DATA is independent of GENERATUM: hand-written data needs no generator. Keep logic out of a DATA file (lib/biblia.c holds the Bible's accessors, biblia_dr.c only its tables).
    symbolum unitates_quid unitates_iudicat UnitatisResponsum · via unitates.stml lib/biblia_dr.c
  8. quomodohow do I declare a new directory as a unit (an app, a vendored library, an archive)?
    Create DIR/unitas.stml with a root unitas element carrying titulus, genus (a natura path that unitates.stml makes citable) and descriptio, plus optional cura, vita, successor and usus; foreign code adds origo children (via, fons, versio, licentia). vita vetusta and successor must come together. Any unknown value is a refusal naming the known ones.
    symbolum unitates_legere · via unitas.canon apps/vicus/unitas.stml vendor/unitas.stml
  9. curwhy does a file deep inside archivum count as archived when its own unit declares nothing?
    Inheritance through the parens chain: vita is the unit's own declaration, else the nearest ancestor that declares one, else viva, and archivum/unitas.stml declares vita archivata. cura works the same way, except that when no unit in the chain DECLARES a cura the default comes from the genus of the innermost unit (genus lines in unitates.stml), not from an ancestor's genus. So a unit inside archivum is archived unless it declares its own vita, and the causa field names the declaration (or genus) that decided.
    symbolum unitates_quid UnitatisResponsum · via include/unitates.h archivum/unitas.stml
  10. quidwhat decides a file's munus (role), and why is it sometimes empty?
    The munus rules in unitates.stml, in order; the FIRST rule whose directory segment and extension both match wins. Extensions are suffix matches on the file name, so .interrogationes.stml works as an extension. If no rule matches, munus is empty with causa nulla regula munus congruit, and no judge will accept the file.
    symbolum unitates_quid · via unitates.stml
  11. quomodohow do I list units with missing pieces, orphan tests or undeclared directories?
    unitates_census (or bin/fabrica quid -census) counts every enumerated file per unit and role without reading any file, then checks the policy's exspectatio lines per genus: desunt lists unmet expectations (archived units are skipped), orphani lists root tests matching no unit, indeclarata lists directories with code that fall to the root. It is a report, not a gate.
    symbolum unitates_census UnitatesCensus · imperium bin/fabrica quid -census
  12. possumnecan unitates_interrogationes tell me that an answer's anchor symbol no longer exists?
    No: it only reads and checks the form. It never returns NIHIL, reports problems per file in recusationes (wrong root, unitas not the owner, missing or doubled quaestio or responsum, no anchor) and still returns an entry that has a question and an answer. Whether symbolum, via and imperium name real things is judged by the interrogationes gate, tools/interrogationes_fumus.sh.
    symbolum unitates_interrogationes UnitatesInterrogationes · via tools/interrogationes_fumus.sh
  13. curwhy are cura, vita, munus and lingua chorda values instead of enums?
    They are open sets declared in unitates.stml, so adding a role or a language is a policy edit, not a C change; an unknown value is a refusal listing the known ones. provenientia (manu, generatum) and indoles (codex, data) are the closed sets, because they are derived from the GENERATUM and DATA markers rather than declared.
    symbolum UnitatisResponsum UnitatesProprietas · via lib/unitates.worklog.md

utf8

include/utf8.h · lib/utf8.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I loop over the codepoints of a chorda?
    Keep a cursor and an end pointer: p = s.datum, finis = s.datum + s.mensura, then while (p < finis) { runa = utf8_decodere(&p, finis); ... }. Inside that loop utf8_decodere always moves the cursor forward, by at least one byte even on bad input, so the loop ends (it moves nothing only when p is NIHIL or already at finis). It returns -1 (an s32) for invalid input; check runa < 0 and decide whether to skip it or draw U+FFFD.
    symbolum utf8_decodere
  2. quidwhat does utf8_decodere reject, and how far does it skip on an error?
    It returns -1 for stray continuation bytes, the lead bytes F8-FF, a sequence cut off by finis, a lead byte not followed by enough continuation bytes, overlong forms (including the C0 and C1 leads), surrogates (U+D800-DFFF) and values above U+10FFFF. The skip is not uniform: a bad lead, a truncated sequence or a missing continuation byte advances one byte, but an overlong form, a surrogate or a value too large advances over the whole sequence (II-IV bytes).
    symbolum utf8_decodere utf8_longitudo_byte
  3. quomodohow do I encode a codepoint into UTF-8 bytes?
    utf8_codere(runa, buffer) writes one to four bytes and returns how many; give it a buffer of at least IV bytes. It returns 0 and writes nothing for a NIHIL buffer, negative values, surrogates (U+D800-DFFF) and values above U+10FFFF. It does not NUL-terminate.
    symbolum utf8_codere
  4. possumnecan I use utf8_numerare_runas to work out how wide text is on screen?
    No: it counts codepoints, not terminal cells. A CJK character is one codepoint but two cells, and a combining accent is a codepoint of width zero. Use runae_latitudo for one codepoint and runae_latitudo_textus (which walks grapheme clusters under a RunaePolitica) for a run of text; both come from lib/runae.c, built from fixed Unicode tables. Invalid input also counts unevenly, whatever the header says: each step of utf8_decodere counts as one, so a three-byte overlong form counts once but a sequence cut short counts once per byte.
    symbolum utf8_numerare_runas runae_latitudo runae_latitudo_textus
  5. possumnecan I use utf8_proxima_runa and utf8_decodere interchangeably to step through text?
    Only on valid UTF-8. utf8_proxima_runa looks at the lead byte alone and jumps its full length without checking the continuation bytes, so on the bytes E2 'A' 'B' it skips straight past both letters, while utf8_decodere returns -1 and advances one byte to 'A'. utf8_prior_runa steps back over at most three continuation bytes and validates nothing either. Use utf8_decodere when the input may be broken.
    symbolum utf8_proxima_runa utf8_decodere utf8_prior_runa
  6. curwhy do the utf8 functions take i8 pointers instead of char?
    i8 is unsigned char in the house types and chorda.datum is i8*, so bytes 0x80-0xFF compare and shift as positive values and a chorda can be passed in without casts. The whole library is pure functions over a byte range: no allocation, no piscina, nothing outside latina.h, so it is cheap to use anywhere.
    symbolum utf8_decodere utf8_longitudo_byte chorda · via include/latina.h

vates

include/vates.h · lib/vates.interrogationes.stml

  1. quomodohow do I send one message to Claude and read the text of the reply?
    Open a provider with vates_aperire(piscina, provisor, clavis, &optiones) (provisor anthropic or fictus; an unknown name returns NIHIL and lists the known ones on stderr), build a request with vates_petitio_creare(piscina, exemplar), add a user turn with vates_nuntium_addere(p, VATES_USOR) and vates_textum_addere, then call vates_mittere(v, p, propositum, piscina). r->bloci is a Xar of VatesBlocus STRUCTS (xar_obtinere gives a VatesBlocus*); collect textus from blocks whose genus is VATES_TEXTUS. Text you add is NOT copied: the chorda must stay valid while the request is in use.
    symbolum vates_aperire vates_petitio_creare vates_nuntium_addere vates_textum_addere vates_mittere · via lib/quaestio.c
  2. quomodohow do I run a tool-use loop with vates?
    Declare tools with vates_instrumentum_addere(p, &instrumentum), a VatesInstrumentum holding titulus, descriptio and a JSON Schema JsonValor (copied by value, so the chordae must outlive p). When r->causa_finis is VATES_FINIS_INSTRUMENTUM, append the reply with vates_responsum_addere(p, r) (every block, OPACUM included, becomes an assistant turn), then open a VATES_USOR turn and add one vates_effectum_addere(n, id, contentum, erratum) per VATES_INSTRUMENTUM_PETITUM block, using that block's id. Send the same petitio again.
    symbolum vates_instrumentum_addere vates_responsum_addere vates_effectum_addere vates_nuntium_addere
  3. quomodohow do I tell a failed call from a successful one?
    vates_mittere never returns NIHIL (except with a NIHIL piscina): check r->successus. On failure r->error says which kind (RETE, TEMPUS, STATUS, PARSE, or LIMES for a local refusal before anything was sent: NIHIL vates or petitio, too many cache points), status_http the HTTP code, and error_genus and error_nuntius carry the provider's own error type (such as overloaded_error) and message; when the error body is not JSON, error_nuntius is its first CCLVI bytes. Only status CC counts as success. vates_error_descriptio names the kind. A refusal is a SUCCESS with causa_finis VATES_FINIS_RECUSATIO; recusatio_categoria holds stop_details.category when the API sends it.
    symbolum vates_mittere vates_error_descriptio VatesResponsum
  4. quidwhat does vates_mittere retry, and how long can it block?
    Up to conatus_maximi attempts (0 means III). Network errors are always retried, a timeout only on the first attempt (a long call is expensive to repeat), and HTTP 408, 409, 429 and every status from D up. Between attempts it sleeps for the retry-after seconds or mora_iterandi_ms (0 means II s), always capped by mora_iterandi_maxima_ms (0 means LX s); each attempt has tempus_ms (0 means X minutes). For the three durations zero or a negative value means the default, so the sleep cannot be switched off (use I ms). With the defaults one call can block about XXX minutes (III attempts of X minutes) plus the sleeps. r->usus is summed over all attempts and r->conatus counts them. The streaming calls retry only before the first delta, and a stream that returned CC is retried only for an overloaded_error or api_error event.
    symbolum vates_mittere vates_optiones_ordinariae VatesOptiones
  5. possumnecan vates read the API key from the environment for me?
    No, by design: the caller reads the key and passes its bytes as clavis. Vates copies it once, NUL-terminated, for the x-api-key header, and never writes it to the ledger, the herbarium captures or any error message (a test reads the ledger and every herbarium file back to prove it). The fictus provider ignores the key.
    symbolum vates_aperire vates_anthropic_aperire
  6. quomodohow do I know what a call cost?
    Give VatesOptiones.pretia (a VatesPretium table with micro-dollars per million tokens for input, output, cache read and 5m and 1h cache writes) and pretia_numerus. Prices come from the caller because they change; they are never compiled in. r->usus.pretium is then the cost in micro-dollars, or -I when the model is not in the table; the model is matched exactly against the name the API returns (r->exemplar), falling back to the request's exemplar. Set rationarium_via to also append one JSON line per attempt (time, model, purpose, status, tokens, price, latency), written with fsync.
    symbolum vates_mittere VatesPretium VatesUsus
  7. quomodohow do I use prompt caching?
    Mark a breakpoint with the signum_thesauri argument of vates_systema_addere, or set signum_thesauri on a VatesBlocus returned by vates_textum_addere or vates_effectum_addere; vates_petitio_caudam_signare adds the automatic tail point. The API allows IV breakpoints, so more than IV makes the call fail with VATES_ERROR_LIMES before anything is sent. OPACUM blocks are never marked and are not counted. The request body is byte-deterministic, so an unchanged prefix hits the cache.
    symbolum vates_systema_addere vates_textum_addere vates_petitio_caudam_signare vates_mittere
  8. quomodohow do I stream the reply as it is generated?
    vates_mittere_rivum blocks like vates_mittere but calls your VatesRivus with each text delta (cogitatio FALSUM) or thinking delta (VERUM); the chorda is invalid once the callback returns. The final response is the same as the non-streaming one. It retries only before the first delta; a stream that ends without message_stop is VATES_ERROR_RETE, and an error event is VATES_ERROR_STATUS. vates_vocare_rivum does the same on your Reactor without blocking (retry waits are reactor timers); it returns NIHIL only when an argument is NIHIL and reports every failure, LIMES included, through finis; then read vates_vocatio_responsum. finis is meant to run exactly once (vates_vocationem_abrumpere calls it synchronously), but if the reactor has no free timer slot when a retry or the LIMES refusal is scheduled it never runs, and vates_vocatio_finita stays FALSUM.
    symbolum vates_mittere_rivum vates_vocare_rivum vates_vocatio_responsum vates_vocationem_abrumpere
  9. quomodohow do I test code that calls vates without the network?
    Open the fictus provider (vates_fictus_aperire, or vates_aperire with provisor fictus), queue replies with vates_fictus_textum, vates_fictus_instrumentum, vates_fictus_crudum (any status and body) or vates_fictus_rivum, then read the exact bodies sent with vates_fictus_petitio. Replies are rendered as real Anthropic JSON and parsed by the real reader. An exhausted queue is a network error (fictus exhaustum), which IS retried: each retry sleeps mora_iterandi_ms (II s when 0) and records one more request, so set conatus_maximi to I or mora_iterandi_ms to I. Fictus only intercepts vates_mittere and vates_mittere_rivum: vates_vocare_rivum calls http_vocare directly, so on a fictus Vates it goes to the real URL. Set sine_herbario too, or any reply the judge finds unusual (non-CC status, unknown field or block) is captured to disk under host fictus.
    symbolum vates_fictus_aperire vates_fictus_textum vates_fictus_instrumentum vates_fictus_crudum vates_fictus_rivum vates_fictus_petitio vates_vocare_rivum
  10. quidwhat is the herbarium, and why do files appear in ~/.rhubarb/herbarium?
    Response capture, ON by default: a judge inside the transport saves any response that is not an ordinary 200 (other status, non-JSON body, unknown stop_reason, a field or block the declared schema in lib/vates_responsum.norma does not know) as a specimen before vates reads it; a CC text/event-stream body is not judged yet. The directory is herbarium_via used as given, else $RHUBARB_HERBARIUM or ~/.rhubarb/herbarium plus /host. The host is always that of the built-in API URL (api.anthropic.com), even when VatesOptiones.url points elsewhere; fictus uses host fictus. sine_herbario VERUM turns it off. vates_vocare_rivum bypasses the transport and is never captured.
    symbolum vates_anthropic_aperire herbarium_sedes_ordinaria · via lib/vates.worklog.md
  11. quidwhat is an OPACUM block, and why does vates keep blocks it does not understand?
    Nothing is thrown away.
